8494 — Abū Rajā’ al-‘Uṭāridī said: We used to worship a stone. If we found a better stone than it, we would throw it away and take the other. If we could not find a stone, we would gather a mound of earth, then bring a sheep and milk it over it, then circumambulate it. When the month of Rajab entered, we would say: “Remove the iron points,” and we would not leave any spear with an iron tip, nor any arrow with an iron head, except that we removed it and discarded it during the month of Rajab. He also said: I heard Abū Rajā’ saying: On the day the Prophet ﷺ was sent, I was a boy grazing camels for my family. When we heard of his emergence, we fled to the Fire — to Musaylimah the Liar.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4376–4377), from al-Ṣalt ibn Muḥammad, who said: I heard Mahdī ibn Maymūn say: I heard Abū Rajā’ al-‘Uṭāridī say it.

8495 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: If it pleases you to know the ignorance of the Arabs, then recite what comes after one hundred and thirty in Sūrat al-An‘ām:
 “Lost indeed are those who killed their children foolishly without knowledge, and forbade what Allah had provided for them, inventing lies against Allah. They have gone astray and were not guided.” [al-An‘ām: 140]
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3524).

8496 — Abū Hurayrah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I saw ‘Amr ibn Luḥayy ibn Qama‘ah ibn Khindif, the brother of these Banū Ka‘b, dragging his intestines in the Fire.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (2856:50) and al-Bukhari (4623).

8497 — Zaynab bint Abī Salamah — the stepdaughter of the Prophet ﷺ — was asked: “Was the Prophet ﷺ from Muḍar?” She said: “From whom else would he be except from Muḍar? He was from Banū al-Naḍr ibn Kinānah.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3491).

8498 — Wāthilah ibn al-Asqa‘ said: I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say: “Allah chose Kinānah from the children of Ismā‘īl, chose Quraysh from Kinānah, chose Banū Hāshim from Quraysh, and chose me from Banū Hāshim.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2276).

8499 — Al-Ash‘ath ibn Qays said: I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in a delegation, and they did not think that I was the best of them. I said: “O Messenger of Allah, we claim that you are from us.” He said: “We are Banū al-Naḍr, Banū Kinānah. We do not follow our mother in lineage, nor do we disown our father.”
 Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2612), Aḥmad (21839), al-Ḍiyā’ in al-Mukhtārah (1488, 1489), and Ibn Abī ‘Āṣim in al-Āḥād wa al-Mathānī (897, 2425).
 His saying, “We claim that you are from us,” was said because the Prophet ﷺ had a grandmother from Kindah, the mother of Kilāb ibn Murrah, and that is what al-Ash‘ath intended. His saying, “We do not follow our mother,” means: we do not follow mothers in lineage and leave fathers. It was also said that it means: we do not accuse her or slander her.

8500 — Abū Qatādah said: A Bedouin said: “O Messenger of Allah, what do you say about fasting on Monday?” He said: “That is the day on which I was born, and on which revelation was sent down to me.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1162:198).

8501 — Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Deeds are presented on Monday and Thursday, and I love for my deed to be presented while I am fasting.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (747), Ibn Mājah (1740), and Aḥmad (8361).
 The latter two added: “So Allah, Mighty and Majestic, forgives every Muslim — or every believer — except two who have abandoned one another. He says: Delay these two.” This is the wording of the Musnad. The wording of Ibn Mājah is: “Leave them until they reconcile.”

8502 — Al-Muṭṭalib ibn ‘Abdullah ibn Qays ibn Makhramah narrated from his father, from his grandfather, who said: I and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ were born in the Year of the Elephant, so we are peers; we were born in the same year.
 Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3619), Aḥmad (17891), and al-Ḥākim (2/603).

8503 — Ibn ‘Abbas said: The Prophet ﷺ was born in the Year of the Elephant.
 Good: Narrated by al-Bazzar — Kashf al-Astar (226) — al-Tabarani (12432), and al-Hakim (2/603).
 His saying “the Day of the Elephant” means “the Year of the Elephant,” as Ibn Sa‘d said in al-Ṭabaqāt (1/101). This is the well-known and widespread view in the books of sīrah and history. The matter of the elephant was that a king in Yemen had overcome it, and he was originally from Abyssinia. He was called Abraha. He built a church in Ṣan‘ā’ and named it al-Qullays, claiming that he would divert the pilgrimage of the Arabs to it. He swore that he would march to the Ka‘bah and demolish it, until what happened of his destruction occurred. See the details of that in Thiqāt Ibn Ḥibbān (1/15–21).

8504 — Qays ibn Makhramah said: I and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ were born in the Year of the Elephant, so we are peers.
 Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq, and through him al-Ḥākim (2/605).

8505 — Al-‘Irbāḍ ibn Sāriyah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Indeed, with Allah I was the Seal of the Prophets while Adam, peace be upon him, was still cast down in his clay. I will inform you of the beginning of that: the supplication of my father Ibrahim, the glad tidings of ‘Isa concerning me, and the vision which my mother saw; and likewise the mothers of the prophets see such visions.”
 In another narration: “And the vision which my mother saw, that a light came out from her by which the palaces of al-Shām were illuminated.”
 Good: Narrated by Imam Ahmad (17150), al-Tabarani in al-Kabir (18/629), and al-Bazzar — Kashf al-Astar (2365).
 His saying “al-munjadil” means cast down. His saying “the supplication of Ibrahim” refers to Allah’s saying: “Our Lord, and send among them a messenger from themselves” [al-Baqarah: 129]. His saying “the glad tidings of ‘Isa” refers to Allah’s saying: “and giving glad tidings of a messenger to come after me, whose name is Ahmad” [al-Ṣaff: 6].

8506 — Khālid ibn Ma‘dān narrated from the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that they said: O Messenger of Allah, tell us about yourself. He said: “I am the supplication of my father Ibrahim, the glad tidings of ‘Isa, and my mother saw, when she carried me, that a light came out from her by which Buṣrā was illuminated. Buṣrā is in the land of al-Shām.”
 Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/600), and it is in Sīrat Ibn Isḥāq, section (33).

8507 — ‘A’ishah said: A Jewish man had settled in Makkah and traded there. On the night when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was born, he said in an assembly of Quraysh: “O people of Quraysh, has a child been born among you tonight?” The people said: “By Allah, we do not know.” He said: “Allah is greater. If you missed it, then there is no harm. Look and remember what I say to you: Tonight the prophet of this last nation has been born among you. Between his shoulders is a sign with consecutive hairs like the mane of a horse. He will not nurse for two nights, because an ‘ifrit from the jinn placed his finger in his mouth and prevented him from nursing.” The people dispersed from their gathering, amazed at what he said and narrated. When they reached their homes, each of them informed his family, and they said: “A boy has been born to ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib; they named him Muhammad.” The people met and said: “Did you hear the speech of this Jew? Has the birth of this boy reached you?” So they went until they came to the Jew and informed him of the news. He said: “Go with me so I may look at him.” They went with him until they brought him in to Āminah. He said: “Bring out your son to us.” She brought him out, and they uncovered his back for him. He saw that mark, and the Jew fell unconscious. When he regained consciousness, they said: “Woe to you, what is wrong with you?” He said: “By Allah, prophethood has gone from the Children of Israel. Are you happy about him, O people of Quraysh? By Allah, he will surely strike you with a mighty blow, the news of which will spread from the east to the west.”
 Among those to whom the Jew said what he said were Hishām and al-Walīd, the two sons of al-Mughīrah, Musāfir ibn Abī ‘Amr, ‘Ubaydah ibn al-Ḥārith, and ‘Uqbah ibn Rabī‘ah — a young man above the age of puberty — along with a group from Banū ‘Abd Manāf and others from Quraysh.
 Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/601–602), and through him al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (2/108–109).

8508 — ‘Urwah said: Thuwaybah was the freedwoman of Abu Lahab. Abu Lahab had freed her, and she nursed the Prophet ﷺ.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (5101), and this is his wording, and Muslim (1449).

8509 — ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Abbas said: It was said to the Prophet ﷺ: “Will you not marry the daughter of Ḥamzah?” He said: “She is the daughter of my brother through nursing.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (5100) and Muslim (1447).
Muṣ‘ab al-Zubayrī said: Thuwaybah nursed the Prophet ﷺ after she had nursed Ḥamzah, then she nursed Abu Salamah. Al-Fatḥ (9/142).
 Thuwaybah — in the diminutive form — nursed the Prophet ﷺ for some days until Ḥalīmah arrived. Thuwaybah had nursed Ḥamzah before him and Abu Salamah ibn ‘Abd al-Asad after him. There was disagreement concerning her Islam. Ibn Mandah mentioned her among the Companions, while Abu Nu‘aym said: We do not know anyone who mentioned her Islam besides him.
 What is found in the sīrah is that the Prophet ﷺ used to honor her. She would enter upon him after he married Khadījah, and he would send gifts to her from Medina until she died after the conquest of Khaybar; her son Masrūḥ also died. Al-Fatḥ (9/145).
2 — Ḥalīmah al-Sa‘diyyah:

8510 — Khālid ibn Ma‘dān narrated from the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that they said to him: “Tell us about yourself.” He said: “Yes. I am the supplication of my father Ibrahim and the glad tidings of ‘Isa, peace be upon them both. My mother saw, when she was carrying me, that a light came out from her by which the palaces of al-Shām were illuminated. I was suckled among Banū Sa‘d ibn Bakr. While I was with some small livestock belonging to us, two men came to me wearing white garments, carrying a golden basin full of snow. They laid me down, split open my abdomen, then took out my heart and split it open. They removed from it a black clot and threw it away. Then they washed my heart and abdomen with that snow until they purified them, then returned them as they had been. Then one of them said to his companion: Weigh him against ten of his Ummah. So he weighed me against ten, and I outweighed them. Then he said: Weigh him against one hundred of his Ummah. So he weighed me against one hundred, and I outweighed them. Then he said: Weigh him against one thousand of his Ummah. So he weighed me against one thousand, and I outweighed them. He said: Leave him, for if you weighed him against his entire Ummah, he would outweigh them.”
 Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, as Ibn Kathīr mentioned in al-Bidāyah wa al-Nihāyah (2/275).
 The nursing mother was Ḥalīmah bint Abī Dhu’ayb al-Sa‘diyyah. She took him with her to her land, and he stayed with her among Banū Sa‘d ibn Bakr for about five years. Other women were also mentioned as having nursed the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, but what I have mentioned is the correct account.

8511 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that Jibrīl came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was playing with the boys. He took him, threw him down, and split open his chest over his heart. He took out the heart, removed a clot from it, and said: “This is Satan’s portion of you.” Then he washed it in a golden basin with Zamzam water, stitched it back together, and returned it to its place. The boys came running to his mother — meaning his wet nurse — saying: “Muḥammad has been killed.” They met him while his color had changed. Anas said: I used to see the mark of that stitching on his chest.
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (162:261).
 His saying “la’amahu” — with fatḥah on the lām followed by hamzah, on the pattern of ḍarabahu — means he joined it together.
 His saying “ẓi’ruhu” means his nursing woman. His age ﷺ at that time was four years, and in the fifth year Ḥalīmah returned him to his mother Āminah.

8512 — ‘Utbah ibn ‘Abd al-Sulamī narrated that a man asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ: “How was the beginning of your affair, O Messenger of Allah?” He said: “My nurse was from Banū Sa‘d ibn Bakr. I went out with a son of hers among some small livestock belonging to us, and we had not taken any provision with us. I said: My brother, go and bring us some provision from our mother. So my brother went, and I remained with the small livestock. Then two white birds came toward me, as though they were eagles. One said to the other: Is it he? He said: Yes. They hastened toward me, took me, and laid me flat on my back. They split open my abdomen, then took out my heart and split it. They removed two black clots from it. One said to the other — Yazīd said in his narration: Bring me snow water — and they washed my inside with it. Then he said: Bring me hail water, and they washed my heart with it. Then he said: Bring me tranquility, and he sprinkled it into my heart. Then one of them said to his companion: Stitch it. So he stitched it and sealed it with the Seal of Prophethood — and Ḥaywah said in his narration: Stitch it, so he stitched it, and seal it with the Seal of Prophethood. Then one of them said to his companion: Place him in one pan of the scale, and place one thousand of his Ummah in the other pan. I then saw the thousand above me, and I feared that some of them would fall upon me. He said: If his Ummah were weighed against him, he would outweigh them. Then they departed and left me, and I became extremely frightened. I went to my mother and told her what I had experienced. She feared that something had confused me, and said: I seek refuge for you in Allah. She saddled a camel of hers and placed me — and in another narration: carried me — on the saddle, and rode behind me until we reached my mother. She said: Have I fulfilled my trust and responsibility? And she told her what I had experienced. That did not frighten her. She said: I saw a light come out from me by which the palaces of al-Shām were illuminated.”
 Good: Narrated by Imam Ahmad (17648), al-Tabarani in al-Shāmiyyīn (1181), and al-Ḥākim (2/616–617).

8513 — Anas ibn Mālik said: Abu Dharr used to narrate that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “The roof of my house was opened while I was in Makkah. Jibrīl descended, opened my chest, then washed it with Zamzam water. Then he brought a golden basin full of wisdom and faith, poured it into my chest, and then closed it.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (349) and Muslim (163).

8514 — Jubayr ibn Muṭ‘im said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I have five names: I am Muḥammad, and Aḥmad. I am al-Māḥī, through whom Allah erases disbelief. I am al-Ḥāshir, at whose feet people will be gathered. And I am al-‘Āqib.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (1), al-Bukhari (3532), and Muslim (2354:124).

8515 — Abu Hurayrah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Do you not marvel at how Allah turns away from me the insults and curses of Quraysh? They insult Mudhammam and curse Mudhammam, while I am Muḥammad.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3533).

8516 — Abu Musa al-Ash‘ari said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to name himself for us with names. He said: “I am Muḥammad, al-Muqaffā, al-Ḥāshir, the Prophet of Repentance, and the Prophet of Mercy.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2355).

8517 — Ḥudhayfah ibn al-Yamān said: I heard the Prophet ﷺ say in one of the streets of Medina: “I am Muḥammad, I am Aḥmad, al-Ḥāshir, al-Muqaffā, and the Prophet of Mercy.”
 Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhi in al-Shamā’il (361), Ibn Abī Shaybah (32350), and Aḥmad (23443).
 The meaning of “al-Muqaffā” is the one who follows others and comes after them; meaning that he is the last of the prophets, peace and blessings be upon them.

8518 — Anas said: A man called another man in al-Baqī‘: “O Abu al-Qāsim!” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ turned toward him. The man said: “O Messenger of Allah, I did not mean you; I only called so-and-so.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Name yourselves with my name, but do not use my kunyah.”
 In another narration: The Prophet ﷺ was in the market.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (2121) and Muslim (2131:1).
 The other narration was narrated by al-Bukhari (2120) and (3537).

8519 — Jābir ibn ‘Abdullah said: A boy was born to a man among us, and he named him al-Qāsim. The Anṣār said: “We will not give you the kunyah Abu al-Qāsim, nor will we let your eye be delighted.” The Prophet ﷺ said: “The Anṣār have done well. Name yourselves with my name, but do not use my kunyah, for I am only a distributor.”
 In Muslim’s narration: “For I am Abu al-Qāsim; I distribute among you.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3115) and Muslim (2133:5).

8520 — Abu Hurayrah said: Abu al-Qāsim ﷺ said: “Name yourselves with my name, but do not use my kunyah.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3538) and Muslim (2134:8).

8521 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that a man said: “O Messenger of Allah, where is my father?” He said: “In the Fire.” When he turned away, he called him and said: “Indeed, my father and your father are in the Fire.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (203).

8522 — Abu Hurayrah said: The Prophet ﷺ visited the grave of his mother, so he wept and made those around him weep. Then he said: “I sought permission from my Lord to visit the grave of my mother, and He permitted me. I sought permission from Him to ask forgiveness for her, but He did not permit me. So visit graves, for they remind you of death.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (976:108).

8523 — Buraydah said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ conquered Makkah, he came to the base of a grave and sat beside it. He sat as though addressing someone, while the people sat around him. Then he stood while weeping. ‘Umar met him — and he was among the boldest of people with him — and said: “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah, what made you weep?” He said: “This is the grave of my mother. I asked my Lord for permission to visit it, and He permitted me. I asked Him for permission to seek forgiveness for her, but He did not permit me. I remembered her, my heart softened, and I wept.” He said: No day was seen in which he wept more than that day.
 Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Shaybah (11930).

8524 — Anas ibn Mālik said: When the Emigrants came from Makkah to Medina, they came with nothing in their hands, while the Anṣār were people of land and property. The Anṣār shared with them on the condition that they would give them half of the fruits of their property every year, and the Emigrants would spare them the work and expenses. The mother of Anas ibn Mālik, who was called Umm Sulaym, and who was also the mother of ‘Abdullah ibn Abī Ṭalḥah — he was Anas’s brother through his mother — had given the Messenger of Allah ﷺ some date-palms of hers. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave them to Umm Ayman, his freedwoman, the mother of Usāmah ibn Zayd.
 Ibn Shihāb said: Anas ibn Mālik informed me that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ finished fighting the people of Khaybar and returned to Medina, the Emigrants returned to the Anṣār the grants of fruit that they had given them. He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned to my mother her date-palms, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave Umm Ayman in their place from his orchard.
 Ibn Shihāb said: The matter of Umm Ayman, the mother of Usāmah ibn Zayd, was that she was a young slave-girl of ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib, and she was from Abyssinia. When Āminah gave birth to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ after his father had died, Umm Ayman cared for him until the Messenger of Allah ﷺ grew up. He freed her, then married her to Zayd ibn Ḥārithah. She died five months after the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (2630) and Muslim (1771:70). The wording is Muslim’s.
His saying: “Then she died five months after the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died.”
 This conflicts with what Ibn Sa‘d narrated in his Ṭabaqāt (8/226) with an authentic chain from Ṭāriq ibn Shihāb, who said: When the Prophet ﷺ passed away, Umm Ayman wept. It was said to her: “What makes you weep?” She said: “I weep over the news of heaven.” And with his chain he said: When ‘Umar was killed, Umm Ayman wept. It was said to her, and she said: “Today Islam has weakened.” Al-Wāqidī said: Umm Ayman died during the caliphate of ‘Uthmān.
 Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said in al-Iṣābah under the biography of “Umm Ayman”: “Ibn al-Sakan reconciled between the two views by saying that the one mentioned by al-Zuhrī was the freedwoman of the Prophet ﷺ, and the one mentioned by Ṭāriq ibn Shihāb was the freedwoman of Umm Ḥabībah, and that each of them was named Barakah and had the kunyah Umm Ayman. This is possible, though remote.” End quote.

8525 — Jābir ibn ‘Abdullah said: When the Ka‘bah was being built, the Prophet ﷺ and al-‘Abbās went to carry stones. Al-‘Abbās said to the Prophet ﷺ: “Place your lower garment over your neck; it will protect you from the stones.” He fell to the ground, and his eyes looked upward toward the sky. Then he regained consciousness and said: “My lower garment.” So he tied his lower garment around himself.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3829) and Muslim (340).
 In a narration of both of them: He was never seen naked after that.

8526 — Abu Musa al-Ash‘ari said: Abu Ṭālib went out to al-Shām, and the Prophet ﷺ went out with him among the elders of Quraysh. When they came near the monk, they stopped and unloaded their mounts. The monk came out to them, though before that they used to pass by him and he would neither come out to them nor pay attention. He said: While they were unloading their mounts, the monk began moving among them until he came and took the hand of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said: “This is the master of the worlds. This is the messenger of the Lord of the worlds. Allah will send him as a mercy to the worlds.” The elders of Quraysh said to him: “How do you know?” He said: “When you came near from the mountain pass, there was no tree or stone except that it fell in prostration, and they only prostrate to a prophet. I recognize him by the Seal of Prophethood below the cartilage of his shoulder, like an apple.” Then he returned and prepared food for them. When he brought it to them, the Prophet ﷺ was among the camels. He said: “Send for him.” He came with a cloud shading him. When he came near the people, he found that they had preceded him to the shade of the tree. When he sat down, the shade of the tree moved over him. The monk said: “Look at the shade of the tree; it has moved over him.” He said: While he was standing over them and imploring them not to take him to the Romans — for if the Romans saw him, they would recognize him by his description and kill him — he turned and saw seven men approaching from the Romans. He met them and said: “What has brought you?” They said: “We have come because this prophet is emerging in this month. No road has been left except that men were sent to it, and we were informed of his news, so we were sent to this road of yours.” He said: “Is there anyone behind you who is better than you?” They said: “We were only informed of his news on this road of yours.” He said: “Tell me, if Allah wants to decree a matter, can any person repel it?” They said: “No.” He said: So they pledged allegiance to him and stayed with him. He said: “I adjure you by Allah, which of you is his guardian?” They said: “Abu Ṭālib.” He kept imploring him until Abu Ṭālib sent him back. Abu Bakr sent Bilāl with him, and the monk provided him with cakes and olive oil.
 Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhi (3620), who graded it good, al-Ḥākim (2/615–616), and al-Bayhaqī in his Dalā’il (2/42).

8527 — Abu Hurayrah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “There is no prophet except that he herded sheep.” They said: “And you, O Messenger of Allah?” He said: “Yes, I used to herd them for qirāṭs for the people of Makkah.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (2262).

8528 — Jābir ibn ‘Abdullah said: We were with the Prophet ﷺ at Marr al-Ẓahrān, gathering kabāth, and the Prophet ﷺ said: “Take the black ones.” He said: We said: “O Messenger of Allah, it is as though you herded sheep.” He said: “Yes, and was there any prophet except that he herded them?” or words similar to this.
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2050).
 His saying “kabāth” refers to the ripe fruit of the arāk tree.

8529 — ‘Abdah ibn Ḥazn said: The camel-owners and the sheep-owners boasted to one another. The Prophet ﷺ said: “Musa was sent while he was a shepherd of sheep. Dawud was sent while he was a shepherd of sheep. And I was sent while I was herding sheep for my family at Ajyād.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari in al-Adab al-Mufrad (577), and al-Nasa’i in al-Kubrā (11262).

8530 — ‘Abd al-Raḥmān ibn ‘Awf said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I witnessed with my uncles the alliance of al-Muṭayyibīn. I would not love to have red camels in exchange for breaking it.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1676), al-Bukhari in al-Adab al-Mufrad (567), and Ibn Ḥibbān authenticated it (4373).

8531 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “There is no new alliance in Islam, and every alliance that existed in the Jāhiliyyah was only increased in strength by Islam. It would not please me to have red camels in exchange for breaking the alliance that took place in Dār al-Nadwah.”
 Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (6/683).

8532 — Abu Hurayrah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I did not witness any alliance of Quraysh except the alliance of al-Muṭayyibīn, and I would not love to have red camels in exchange for having broken it.” He said: The Muṭayyibūn were Hāshim, Umayyah, Zuhrah, and Makhzūm.
 Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (3474), al-Bayhaqī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (6/366), and in al-Dalā’il (2/38).

8533 — Jābir said: Khadījah hired the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for two journeys to Jurash, each journey for a young she-camel.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/182), and through him al-Bayhaqī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (6/118).

8534 — Mujāhid narrated from his patron that he told him he was among those who built the Ka‘bah during the Jāhiliyyah. He said: I had a stone that I carved with my own hand and worshiped besides Allah, Blessed and Most High. I would bring thick milk that I preferred for myself and pour it over it, then a dog would come and lick it, then lift its leg and urinate. We built until we reached the place of the Stone, while no one could see the Stone. Then it was in the middle of our stones, like the head of a man, and a person’s face could almost be seen in it. One clan of Quraysh said: “We will place it.” Others said: “We will place it.” They said: “Appoint a judge between you.” They said: “The first man to appear from the mountain path.” Then the Prophet ﷺ came, and they said: “The Trustworthy One has come to you.” They told him. He placed it in a cloth, then called their clans, and they took its sides along with him. Then he ﷺ placed it.
 Good: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (15504) and al-Ḥākim (1/458).

8535 — ‘Alī ibn Abī Ṭālib said: When they wanted to raise the Black Stone, they disputed over it. They said: “We will appoint as judge between us the first man who comes out from this lane.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was the first to come out to them. He judged between them that they should place it in a cloak, then all the tribes would lift it together. Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ took it and placed it.
 Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (1/458–459), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (2/55–56).

8536 — Abu al-Ṭufayl said: In the Jāhiliyyah, the Ka‘bah was built of stacked stones, without clay. Its height was about what a young goat could leap over. It was not roofed; its coverings were only placed upon it and then let down over it. The Black Corner was placed visibly upon its wall, and it had two corners like the form of this ring. A ship came from the land of the Romans, and when they were near Jeddah, the ship broke apart. Quraysh went out to take its wood, and they found a Roman man beside it. They took the wood; he gave it to them. The ship had been heading toward Abyssinia, and the Roman who was on the ship was a carpenter. They came with the wood and brought the Roman man. Quraysh said: “We will build the House of our Lord with this wood.”
When they wanted to demolish it, there was a snake upon the wall of the House, like a piece of a large beam, black-backed and white-bellied. Whenever anyone came near the House to demolish it or take any of its stones, it would rush at him with its mouth open. Quraysh gathered at the Sacred Precinct and cried out to Allah, saying: “Our Lord! We are not seeking harm. We only intend to honor Your House and set it in order. If You are pleased with that, then let it be; otherwise, do what You will.” They heard a cry in the sky, and there was a bird greater than an eagle, black-backed with white belly and legs. It dug its claws into the back of the snake’s neck, then flew away dragging it, its tail hanging down greatly, until it took it toward Ajyād.
Then Quraysh demolished it and began building it with stones from the valley, which Quraysh carried on their necks. They raised it twenty cubits high. While the Prophet ﷺ was carrying stones from Ajyād and wearing a striped cloak, the cloak became tight on him, so he went to place it on his shoulder. Because of the smallness of the cloak, his nakedness appeared, so he was called: “O Muhammad! Cover your nakedness.” He was never seen naked after that. Between the rebuilding of the Ka‘bah and the revelation Allah sent down to him were five years, and between his departure from Makkah and its rebuilding were fifteen years.
 Good: Narrated by ‘Abd al-Razzāq (9106), Imam Aḥmad (23794, 23800), and al-Ḥākim (4/179).

8537 — Zayd ibn Ḥārithah said: I circumambulated with the Prophet ﷺ one day, and I touched some of the idols. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me: “Do not touch them.” I said: “I will do it again so that I may see what he says.” Then I touched them, and he said: “Were you not forbidden from this?” He said: By the One who honored him and sent down the Book upon him, he never touched any idol until Allah honored him and sent down the Book upon him.
 Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (5/88), al-Bazzār — Kashf al-Astār (2755), and Abū Ya‘lā (7212).

8538 — ‘Urwah said: A neighbor of Khadījah bint Khuwaylid narrated to me that he heard the Prophet ﷺ saying to Khadījah: “O Khadījah, by Allah, I will never worship al-Lāt; by Allah, I will never worship al-‘Uzzā.” He said: Khadījah would say: “Leave al-Lāt; leave al-‘Uzzā.” He said: “Their idols which they used to worship, then lie down.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (17947).

8539 — Jubayr ibn Muṭ‘im said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ before revelation was sent down to him, standing upon a camel of his at ‘Arafāt with the people until he departed from it with them, by Allah’s guidance for him.
 Good: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (16757), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (2/142–143), and Ibn Khuzaymah authenticated it (3057).

8540 — The Mother of the Believers ‘Ā’ishah said: Quraysh and those who followed their religious practice — and they were the ḥums — used to stand on the evening of ‘Arafah at Muzdalifah, saying: “We are the dwellers of the House.” The rest of the people and the Arabs would stand at ‘Arafāt. So Allah, Mighty and Majestic, sent down: “Then depart from where the people depart, and seek forgiveness from Allah” [al-Baqarah: 199]. So they moved forward and stood with the people at ‘Arafāt.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4520) and Muslim (1219).
The word “ḥums” is the plural of aḥmas, meaning one who is firm and severe in zeal. They were called this because, according to their claim, they were strict in their religion. ‘Urwah said: In the Jāhiliyyah, people used to circumambulate naked except for the ḥums. The ḥums were Quraysh and those born from them. The ḥums would withhold clothing from people: a man would give another man clothes in which to circumambulate, and a woman would give another woman clothes in which to circumambulate. Whoever was not given clothes by the ḥums would circumambulate naked. The general body of people would depart from ‘Arafāt, while the ḥums would depart from Jam‘, meaning Muzdalifah. Al-Bukhari (1665) and Muslim (1219) mentioned it.

8541 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: A woman would circumambulate the House while naked and would say: “Who will lend me a garment for circumambulation?” She would place it over her private part and say: “Today some of it or all of it may appear, but whatever appears of it I do not permit.” Then this verse was revealed:
 “O children of Adam, take your adornment at every mosque.” [al-A‘rāf: 31]
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3028).

8542 — ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Umar said: I never heard ‘Umar say about anything, “I think it is such-and-such,” except that it was as he thought. While ‘Umar was sitting, a handsome man passed by him. He said: “My thought has failed — or else this man is still upon his religion from the Jāhiliyyah, or he was their soothsayer. Bring the man to me.” He was summoned, and ‘Umar said that to him. The man said: “I have never seen such a reception given to a Muslim man as today.” He said: “I insist that you tell me.” He said: “I was their soothsayer in the Jāhiliyyah.” He said: “What was the most amazing thing your female jinn brought to you?” He said: “One day, while I was in the market, she came to me and I recognized fear in her. She said: Have you not seen the jinn and their despair, their hopelessness after their reversal, and their joining the small she-camels and their saddle-cloths?” ‘Umar said: “He spoke the truth. While I was near their deities, a man came with a calf and slaughtered it. Then a caller shouted, and I never heard any caller with a louder voice than him, saying: O Jalīḥ, a successful matter, an eloquent man, saying: There is no god but You. The people leapt up. I said: I will not leave until I know what is behind this. Then he called again: O Jalīḥ, a successful matter, an eloquent man, saying: There is no god but Allah. I stood up, and soon it was said: This is a prophet.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3866).

8543 — Salamah ibn Salām ibn Waqsh — and Salamah was one of the people of Badr — said: We had a Jewish neighbor among Banū ‘Abd al-Ashhal. He said: One day, shortly before the mission of the Prophet ﷺ, he came out to us from his house and stood before the gathering of Banū ‘Abd al-Ashhal. Salamah said: At that time I was the youngest among them in age, wearing a cloak and lying in it in the courtyard of my family. He mentioned resurrection, the Day of Judgment, reckoning, the scales, Paradise, and Hellfire. He said that to a people of polytheism, idol worshipers, who did not believe that any resurrection would occur after death. They said to him: “Woe to you, so-and-so! Do you think this will happen — that people will be resurrected after their deaths to an abode containing Paradise and Hellfire, and that they will be recompensed there for their deeds?” He said: “Yes, by the One by whom oaths are sworn. He would wish that he had, in place of his share of that Fire, the greatest oven in the world heated for him, then that he be put into it and it be closed over him, and that he thereby be saved from that Fire tomorrow.” They said to him: “Woe to you, what is the sign of that?” He said: “A prophet will be sent from the direction of these lands,” and he gestured with his hand toward Makkah and Yemen. They said: “When do you think he will appear?” He looked at me, while I was among the youngest of them in age, and said: “If this boy lives out his natural lifespan, he will see him.” Salamah said: By Allah, not much time passed before Allah Most High sent His Messenger ﷺ while that man was still alive among us. We believed in him, but he disbelieved in him out of transgression and envy. We said: “Woe to you, so-and-so! Are you not the one who said to us about him what you said?” He said: “Yes, but it is not him.”
 Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (15841), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (7/47), al-Ḥākim (3/417–418), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (2/78).

8544 — Ibn ‘Umar said: Banū al-Naḍīr and Qurayẓah fought the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He expelled Banū al-Naḍīr and left Qurayẓah in place and showed them favor, until Qurayẓah fought. Then he killed their men and divided their women, children, and wealth among the Muslims, except that some of them joined the Prophet ﷺ; he granted them safety and they accepted Islam. He expelled all the Jews of Medina: Banū Qaynuqā‘, who were the people of ‘Abdullah ibn Salām, the Jews of Banū Ḥārithah, and all the Jews of Medina.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4028) and Muslim (1766).
 His saying, “some of them joined the Prophet ﷺ; he granted them safety and they accepted Islam”: among them were Tha‘labah ibn Sa‘yah, Usayd ibn Sa‘yah, Asad ibn ‘Ubayd, and a group from Banū Hudhal, the brothers of Banū Qurayẓah.

8545 — Abu Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I was sent from the best generations of the children of Adam, generation after generation, until I was from the generation in which I was.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3557).

8546 — Jābir ibn Samurah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Indeed, I know a stone in Makkah that used to greet me before I was sent. Indeed, I know it now.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2277).

8547 — ‘Alī ibn Abī Ṭālib said: I went out with the Prophet ﷺ, and he did not pass by any stone or tree except that it greeted him.
 Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Awsaṭ — Majma‘ al-Baḥrayn (3519).

8548 — ‘Abdullah ibn al-Zubayr said to ‘Ubayd ibn ‘Umayr ibn Qatādah al-Laythī: “Narrate to us, O ‘Ubayd, how the beginning of what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ began with of prophethood occurred, when Jibrīl, peace be upon him, came to him.” He said: ‘Ubayd said — while I was present as he narrated to ‘Abdullah ibn al-Zubayr and those with him among the people —: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would spend a month in seclusion at Ḥirā’ every year. That was among the acts of taḥannuth that Quraysh practiced in the Jāhiliyyah; and taḥannuth means devotion and righteousness.
 ‘Ubayd said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would spend that month in seclusion every year and would feed any poor person who came to him. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ completed his seclusion for that month, the first thing he would begin with when he left his seclusion was the Ka‘bah before entering his house. He would circumambulate it seven times, or however much Allah willed of that, then return to his house.
 Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq, as in Sīrat Ibn Hishām (1/235).

8549 — Ibn ‘Umar narrated that Zayd ibn ‘Amr ibn Nufayl went out to al-Shām, asking about the religion and seeking to follow it. He met a Jewish scholar and asked him about their religion, saying: “Perhaps I will adopt your religion, so inform me.” He said: “You will not be upon our religion until you take your share of Allah’s anger.” Zayd said: “I flee only from Allah’s anger, and I will never bear anything of Allah’s anger. How could I bear it? Can you direct me to something else?” He said: “I do not know of anything except that you be a ḥanīf.” Zayd said: “What is a ḥanīf?” He said: “The religion of Ibrahim; he was neither Jewish nor Christian, and he worshiped none but Allah.” Zayd went out and met a Christian scholar. He mentioned something similar, and the scholar said: “You will not be upon our religion until you take your share of Allah’s curse.” He said: “I flee only from Allah’s curse, and I will never bear anything of Allah’s curse or His anger. How could I bear it? Can you direct me to something else?” He said: “I do not know of anything except that you be a ḥanīf.” He said: “What is a ḥanīf?” He said: “The religion of Ibrahim; he was neither Jewish nor Christian, and he worshiped none but Allah.” When Zayd saw what they said about Ibrahim, peace be upon him, he left. When he emerged into the open, he raised his hands and said: “O Allah, I bear witness that I am upon the religion of Ibrahim.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3827).

8550 — ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Umar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ met Zayd ibn ‘Amr ibn Nufayl at the lower part of Baldaḥ before revelation came down to the Prophet ﷺ. A meal was presented to the Prophet ﷺ, but he refused to eat from it. Then Zayd said: “I do not eat from what you slaughter upon your stone altars, and I eat only what Allah’s name has been mentioned over.” Zayd ibn ‘Amr used to criticize Quraysh for their slaughtered animals, saying: “The sheep was created by Allah; He sent down water from the sky for it and caused vegetation to grow for it from the earth. Then you slaughter it upon other than the name of Allah,” rejecting that and magnifying it.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3826).

8551 — Asmā’ bint Abī Bakr said: I saw Zayd ibn ‘Amr ibn Nufayl leaning his back against the Ka‘bah, saying: “O people of Quraysh, none of you today is upon the religion of Ibrahim, peace be upon him, besides me.” He used to pray in the Ka‘bah and say: “My God is the God of Ibrahim, and my religion is the religion of Ibrahim, peace be upon him.” He used to save girls who were to be buried alive. When a man wanted to kill his daughter, he would say: “Do not kill her. Give her to me, and I will bear her expenses for you.” When she grew up, he would say: “If you wish, take her now; and if you wish, leave her, and I will bear her expenses for you.” The Prophet ﷺ was asked about him, and he said: “He will be resurrected on the Day of Resurrection as a nation by himself.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī ‘Āṣim in al-Āḥād wa al-Mathānī (771), and al-Nasa’ī in al-Kubrā (8131).
 His saying, “He will be resurrected on the Day of Resurrection as a nation by himself,” means that no one in his era was upon the religion of Ibrahim, peace be upon him, besides him — not even the Christians, for they had become followers of the monk Paul, who altered the religion of the Messiah and made him the son of Allah — until the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sent with the religion of Ibrahim, peace be upon him.

8552 — Zayd ibn Ḥārithah said: I went out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he had me riding behind him, on a hot day from the days of Makkah. We had with us a sheep that we had slaughtered and prepared, and we placed it in a travel food container. Zayd ibn ‘Amr ibn Nufayl met him, and each of them greeted the other with the greeting of the Jāhiliyyah. The Prophet ﷺ said: “O Zayd” — meaning Zayd ibn ‘Amr — “why do I see that your people have become hostile toward you?” He said: “By Allah, O Muhammad, that is not because of any blood-feud I have with them. Rather, I went out seeking this religion until I came to the rabbis of Khaybar. I found them worshiping Allah while associating partners with Him, so I said: This is not the religion I seek. Then I went until I came to the rabbis of al-Shām. I found them worshiping Allah while associating partners with Him, so I said: This is not the religion I seek. Then one of them said: You are asking about a religion by which we know no one worships Allah except a shaykh in al-Jazīrah. So I went until I came to him. When he saw me, he said: All whom you have seen are in misguidance. Where are you from? I said: I am from the people of the House of Allah, from the people of thorn-trees and acacia. He said: What you seek has appeared in your land. A prophet has been sent; his star has risen. But I have not yet perceived anything, O Muhammad.”
 He said: The food container was brought near to him, and he said: “What is this?” He said: “A sheep that we slaughtered for one of these stone altars.” He said: “I would not eat anything slaughtered for other than Allah.” Then they parted.
Zayd ibn Ḥārithah said: Then the Prophet ﷺ came to the House, and I was with him. He circumambulated it. Near the House there were two idols, one of copper; one was called Isāf and the other Nā’ilah. When the polytheists circumambulated, they would touch them. The Prophet ﷺ said: “Do not touch them, for they are filth.” He said: I said to myself: I will touch them so that I may see what he says. So I touched them, and he said: “O Zayd, were you not forbidden?” He said: Revelation was sent down upon the Prophet ﷺ. He said: Zayd ibn ‘Amr died, and the Prophet ﷺ said: “He will be resurrected as a nation by himself.”
 Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in his Musnad (1331), al-Nasa’ī in al-Kubrā (8132), and al-Ḥākim (3/54).
 His saying, “A sheep that we slaughtered for one of these stone altars,” al-Dhahabī said: “There is an objectionable element in it.” This is answered by what we clarified in al-Jāmi‘ al-Kāmil, Book of Hunting and Slaughtered Animals, Chapter: The Prohibition of Slaughtering for Other than Allah; namely, that the sheep belonged to Quraysh, who slaughtered it and offered it to the Prophet ﷺ, but he refused to eat from it. The Prophet ﷺ then offered it to Zayd ibn ‘Amr, thinking that he followed the customs of Quraysh, but he also refused it.

8553 — Sa‘īd ibn Zayd ibn ‘Amr ibn Nufayl said: I and ‘Umar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb asked — meaning, we asked the Prophet ﷺ — about Zayd ibn ‘Amr ibn Nufayl. He said: “He will come on the Day of Resurrection as a nation by himself.”
 Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī ‘Āṣim in al-Āḥād wa al-Mathānī (775).

8554 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sent at the age of forty. He remained in Makkah for thirteen years while revelation came to him. Then he was commanded to migrate, so he migrated and lived ten years, and he died when he was sixty-three years old.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3902).

8555 — ‘Ā’ishah said: The first thing with which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ began of revelation was true dreams in sleep. He would not see any dream except that it came like the break of dawn… the full hadith.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3) and Muslim (60).

8556 — ‘Ā’ishah, the Mother of the Believers, said: The first thing with which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ began of revelation was righteous dreams in sleep. He would not see any dream except that it came like the break of dawn. Then seclusion was made beloved to him. He would seclude himself in the Cave of Ḥirā’, practicing taḥannuth there — meaning worship — for a number of nights before returning to his family. He would take provisions for that, then return to Khadījah and take provisions for a similar period, until the truth suddenly came to him while he was in the Cave of Ḥirā’. The angel came to him and said: “Read.” He said: “I am not one who reads.” He said: He took me and pressed me until I reached the limit of my strength, then released me and said: “Read.” I said: “I am not one who reads.” He took me and pressed me a second time until I reached the limit of my strength, then released me and said: “Read.” I said: “I am not one who reads.” He took me and pressed me a third time until I reached the limit of my strength, then released me and said: “Read in the name of your Lord who created; created man from a clinging clot. Read, and your Lord is the Most Generous.” [al-‘Alaq: 1–3] The Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned with it, his shoulders trembling, until he entered upon Khadījah and said: “Cover me, cover me.” They covered him until the fear left him. Then he said to Khadījah: “O Khadījah, what is wrong with me?” He informed her of what had happened and said: “I feared for myself.” Khadījah said to him: “No. Rejoice, for by Allah, Allah will never disgrace you. By Allah, you maintain ties of kinship, speak the truth, bear the burdens of others, provide for the needy, honor the guest, and help in the calamities of truth.” Khadījah then took him to Waraqah ibn Nawfal ibn Asad ibn ‘Abd al-‘Uzzā, who was the cousin of Khadījah, the brother of her father. He was a man who had become Christian in the Jāhiliyyah. He used to write the Arabic script and write from the Gospel in Arabic whatever Allah willed him to write. He was an old man who had become blind. Khadījah said to him: “O uncle, listen to your nephew.” Waraqah ibn Nawfal said to him: “O nephew, what do you see?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ informed him of what he had seen. Waraqah said to him: “This is the Namus that was sent down upon Musa, peace be upon him. If only I were young then! If only I were alive when your people drive you out!” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Will they drive me out?” Waraqah said: “Yes. No man has ever brought what you have brought except that he was opposed. If your day reaches me, I will support you with strong support.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3) and Muslim (160). The wording is Muslim’s.

8557 — Yaḥyā ibn Abī Kathīr said: I asked Abū Salamah ibn ‘Abd al-Raḥmān about the first thing revealed from the Qur’an, and he said: “O you who is wrapped up” [al-Muddaththir: 1]. I said: “They say: ‘Read in the name of your Lord who created.’” Abū Salamah said: I asked Jābir ibn ‘Abdullah about that and said to him what you said. Jābir said: I will only narrate to you what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ narrated to us. He said: “I secluded myself in Ḥirā’. When I completed my seclusion, I descended. Then I was called. I looked to my right and saw nothing; I looked to my left and saw nothing; I looked in front of me and saw nothing; I looked behind me and saw nothing. Then I raised my head and saw something. I came to Khadījah and said: Wrap me up, and pour cold water over me.” He said: They wrapped me up and poured cold water over me. He said: Then it was revealed: “O you who is wrapped up, arise and warn, and magnify your Lord.” [al-Muddaththir: 1–3]
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4922) and Muslim (160:257).
 His saying, “the first thing revealed from the Qur’an,” means after the period in which revelation stopped, as is explicitly stated in the following hadith. As for the manner in which revelation descended, see the Book of Revelation.

8558 — Jābir ibn ‘Abdullah said: I heard the Prophet ﷺ narrating about the period in which revelation stopped. In his hadith he said: “While I was walking, I heard a voice from the sky. I raised my head, and there was the angel who had come to me at Ḥirā’, sitting on a chair between heaven and earth. I was terrified of him, so I returned and said: Cover me, cover me. They wrapped me up, and Allah, Mighty and Majestic, sent down: ‘O you who is wrapped up’ up to His saying: ‘And abandon idols’ [al-Muddaththir: 1–5] — before prayer was prescribed — and it refers to idols. Then revelation became intense and continued.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4925) and Muslim (161).

8559 — Jundub ibn ‘Abdullah ibn Sufyān said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ became ill and did not stand for prayer for two or three nights. A woman came and said: “O Muhammad, I hope that your devil has left you; I have not seen him approach you for two or three nights.” So Allah, Mighty and Majestic, sent down: “By the morning brightness, and by the night when it grows still, your Lord has not forsaken you, nor has He hated you.” [al-Ḍuḥā: 1–3]
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4950) and Muslim (1797:114).

8560 — Ibn ‘Abbās said concerning the saying of Allah Most High: “Do not move your tongue with it to hasten with it” [al-Qiyāmah: 16]: The Prophet ﷺ used to experience hardship from the revelation, and he would move his lips. Sa‘īd ibn Jubayr said: Ibn ‘Abbās said to me: “I will move them as the Prophet ﷺ used to move them.” Sa‘īd said: “I will move them as Ibn ‘Abbās used to move them.” Then he moved his lips. So Allah Most High sent down: “Do not move your tongue with it to hasten with it. Indeed, upon Us is its collection and its recitation” [al-Qiyāmah: 16–17]. He said: Its collection in your chest, then that you recite it. “So when We recite it, follow its recitation” [al-Qiyāmah: 18]. He said: Listen and remain silent. Then it is upon Us that you recite it. So when Jibrīl came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he would listen, and when Jibrīl departed, the Prophet ﷺ would recite it as he had taught him.
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (448:148).

8561 — Anas narrated that Allah Most High caused revelation to come successively to His Messenger ﷺ before his death until Allah took him, and revelation was at its greatest amount; then he died.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4982) and Muslim (3016). The wording is al-Bukhari’s.
 In Muslim’s wording: “Revelation was most frequent on the day the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died,” meaning the period near his death, not the exact day of his death specifically.

8562 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: When this verse was revealed: “And warn your closest relatives” [al-Shu‘arā’: 214], and your sincere clan among them, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out until he climbed al-Ṣafā and called out: “O morning alarm!” They said: “Who is this calling out?” They said: “Muḥammad.” So they gathered to him. He said: “O Banū so-and-so! O Banū so-and-so! O Banū so-and-so! O Banū ‘Abd Manāf! O Banū ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib!” They gathered to him, and he said: “Tell me, if I informed you that cavalry were coming out from the foot of this mountain, would you believe me?” They said: “We have never experienced any lie from you.” He said: “Then I am a warner to you before a severe punishment.”
 He said: Abū Lahab said: “May you perish! Did you gather us only for this?” Then he stood, and this surah was revealed: “Perish the two hands of Abū Lahab, and perish he” [al-Masad: 1]. Al-A‘mash recited it this way to the end of the surah.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4971) and Muslim (208).

8563 — Abū Hurayrah said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had revealed to him: “And warn your closest relatives,” he said: “O people of Quraysh! Purchase yourselves from Allah; I cannot avail you against Allah at all. O Banū ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib! I cannot avail you against Allah at all. O ‘Abbās ibn ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib! I cannot avail you against Allah at all. O Ṣafiyyah, aunt of the Messenger of Allah! I cannot avail you against Allah at all. O Fāṭimah, daughter of the Messenger of Allah! Ask me of my wealth whatever you wish; I cannot avail you against Allah at all.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (2753) and Muslim (206).

8564 — Abū Hurayrah said: When this verse was revealed: “And warn your closest relatives,” the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called Quraysh, and they gathered. He addressed them generally and specifically, saying: “O Banū Ka‘b ibn Lu’ayy! Save yourselves from the Fire. O Banū Murrah ibn Ka‘b! Save yourselves from the Fire. O Banū ‘Abd Shams! Save yourselves from the Fire. O Banū ‘Abd Manāf! Save yourselves from the Fire. O Fāṭimah! Save yourself from the Fire, for I possess nothing for you against Allah, except that you have ties of kinship which I will maintain.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (204).

8565 — ‘Ā’ishah said: When “And warn your closest relatives” was revealed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood upon al-Ṣafā and said: “O Fāṭimah daughter of Muḥammad! O Ṣafiyyah daughter of ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib! O Banū ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib! I possess nothing for you against Allah. Ask me of my wealth whatever you wish.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (205).

8566 — Qabīṣah ibn al-Mukhāriq and Zuhayr ibn ‘Amr both said: When “And warn your closest relatives” was revealed, the Prophet of Allah ﷺ went to a rocky part of a mountain and climbed its highest stone. Then he called out: “O Banū ‘Abd Manāf! I am a warner. My example and your example is only like that of a man who saw the enemy, so he went to watch out for his people, but feared that they would reach them before he could, so he began calling out: O morning alarm!”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (207).

8567 — ‘Abdullah ibn Mas‘ūd said: The first to openly display their Islam were seven: the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, Abū Bakr, ‘Ammār, his mother Sumayyah, Ṣuhayb, Bilāl, and al-Miqdād. As for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, Allah protected him through his uncle Abū Ṭālib. As for Abū Bakr, Allah protected him through his people. As for the rest of them, the polytheists seized them, dressed them in iron armor, and left them to burn in the sun. There was not a single one of them except that he yielded to them in what they wanted, except Bilāl. He considered his own soul insignificant for the sake of Allah, and his people considered him insignificant, so they handed him over to the boys, and they dragged him around the mountain paths of Makkah while he was saying: “One, One.”
 Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (150), Aḥmad (3832), and Ibn Ḥibbān (7083) and al-Ḥākim (3/283) authenticated it.
 His saying, “he yielded to them in what they wanted,” means leaving the open display of Islam, except for Bilāl, for he continued to display it openly.

8568 — ‘Ammār said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when none was with him except five slaves, two women, and Abū Bakr.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3660).
 The five slaves were: Bilāl, Zayd ibn Ḥārithah, ‘Āmir ibn Fuhayrah the freedman of Abū Bakr, Abū Fukayhah who was also a freedman of Abū Bakr, and Shuqran. It was also said that the fifth was ‘Ammār ibn Yāsir. As for the two women, they were Khadījah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, and Umm Ayman, the nursemaid of the Prophet ﷺ. It was also said that one of them was Sumayyah, the mother of ‘Ammār ibn Yāsir, for she was the first woman to be martyred in Islam; Abū Jahl stabbed her in her private part with a spear, and she died. Al-Fatḥ (7/24).

8569 — Sa‘d ibn Abī Waqqāṣ said: I remember myself when I was one third of Islam.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3726).

8570 — Sa‘d ibn Abī Waqqāṣ said: No one accepted Islam except on the day I accepted Islam; and I remained for seven days while I was one third of Islam.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3727).
 His saying, “one third of Islam,” means that he was the third after Abū Bakr and Khadījah. But this conflicts with the hadith of ‘Ammār, so perhaps Sa‘d ibn Abī Waqqāṣ did not know that others had also accepted Islam, because they concealed their Islam.

8571 — Muṣ‘ab ibn Sa‘d ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated from his father that verses of the Qur’an were revealed concerning him. He said: Sa‘d’s mother swore that she would never speak to him until he disbelieved in his religion, and that she would neither eat nor drink. She said: “You claim that Allah has instructed you regarding your parents, and I am your mother, and I command you with this.” He said: She remained for three days until she fainted from exhaustion. A son of hers named ‘Umārah stood and gave her drink. She began supplicating against Sa‘d. Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed in the Qur’an this verse: “But if they strive against you to make you associate with Me that of which you have no knowledge, then do not obey them,” and in it: “But accompany them in this world with kindness.” [Luqmān: 15]
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1748:43).

8572 — ‘Amr ibn ‘Abasah al-Sulamī said: In the Jāhiliyyah, I used to think that the people were upon misguidance and that they were upon nothing while they worshiped idols. Then I heard of a man in Makkah who was reporting matters, so I mounted my riding animal and came to him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was in hiding, while his people were bold against him. I acted gently until I entered upon him in Makkah. I said to him: “What are you?” He said: “I am a prophet.” I said: “And what is a prophet?” He said: “Allah has sent me.” I said: “With what has He sent you?” He said: “He sent me with maintaining family ties, breaking idols, and that Allah be worshiped alone without anything being associated with Him.” I said to him: “Who is with you upon this?” He said: “A free man and a slave.” He said: With him that day were Abū Bakr and Bilāl among those who had believed in him. I said: “I will follow you.” He said: “You are not able to do that on this day of yours. Do you not see my condition and the condition of the people? Rather, return to your family, and when you hear that I have prevailed, then come to me.” He said: I went to my family, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to Medina while I was with my family. I began seeking news and asking people when he came to Medina, until a group from the people of Yathrib, the people of Medina, came to me. I said: “What happened to this man who came to Medina?” They said: “The people are rushing to him, and his people wanted to kill him but were unable to do so.” So I came to Medina and entered upon him. I said: “O Messenger of Allah, do you recognize me?” He said: “Yes, you are the one who met me in Makkah.” He said: I said: “Yes…”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (832).

8573 — Abū Ḥamzah — a man from the Anṣār — said: I heard Zayd ibn Arqam saying: The first to accept Islam was ‘Alī. ‘Amr ibn Murrah said: I mentioned that to Ibrāhīm al-Nakha‘ī, and he rejected it and said: The first to accept Islam was Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq.
 Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3735), Aḥmad (19281), and al-Nasa’ī in al-Kubrā (8081).

8574 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: The first to pray was ‘Alī.
 Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3734), al-Ṭayālisī (2875), and through him Aḥmad (3542).

8575 — ‘Aqīl ibn Abī Ṭālib said: Quraysh came to Abū Ṭālib and said: “Your nephew harms us in our gathering place and in our mosque, so stop him from harming us.” He said: “O ‘Aqīl, bring Muḥammad to me.” I went and brought him to him. He said: “O my nephew, your cousins claim that you harm them in their gathering place and in their mosque, so stop doing that.”
 He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ lifted his gaze to the sky and said: “Do you see this sun?” They said: “Yes.” He said: “I am no more able to leave that for you than you are able to bring me a flame from it.” Abū Ṭālib said: “My nephew has not lied to us. Go back.”
 Good: Narrated by Abū Ya‘lā (6804) and al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (2/186–187).

8576 — Anas narrated that the people of Makkah asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to show them a sign, so he showed them the splitting of the moon.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3637) and Muslim (2802).

8577 — ‘Abdullah ibn Mas‘ūd said: The moon split during the time of the Prophet ﷺ and became two parts. He said to us: “Bear witness, bear witness.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4865) and Muslim (2800).

8578 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: The people of Makkah asked the Prophet ﷺ to make al-Ṣafā gold for them and to remove the mountains from around them so that they could farm. It was said to him: “If you wish, you may delay and be patient with them; and if you wish, We will give them what they asked for, but if they disbelieve after that, they will be destroyed as those before them were destroyed.” He said: “No, rather I will be patient with them.” So Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed this verse: “Nothing prevented Us from sending signs except that the former peoples denied them. And We gave Thamūd the she-camel as a visible sign.” [al-Isrā’: 59]
 Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (2333), al-Bazzār — Kashf al-Astār (2225), and al-Ḥākim (2/362).

8579 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: Quraysh said to the Prophet ﷺ: “Call upon your Lord for us to make al-Ṣafā gold for us, and we will believe in you.” He said: “Will you do so?” They said: “Yes.” He supplicated, and Jibrīl came to him and said: “Your Lord sends greetings of peace to you and says to you: If you wish, al-Ṣafā will become gold for them by morning, but whoever among them disbelieves after that, I will punish him with a punishment with which I have not punished anyone among the worlds. And if you wish, I will open for them the door of repentance and mercy.” He said: “Rather, the door of repentance and mercy.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (2166, 3223), al-Bazzār — Kashf al-Astār (2224), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (12736).

8580 — ‘Abdullah ibn Mas‘ūd said: While the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was praying near the House, and Abū Jahl and some of his companions were sitting, some of them said to others: “Which of you will bring the abdominal contents of a camel belonging to Banū so-and-so and place it on Muḥammad’s back when he prostrates?” The most wretched of the people went and brought it. He watched until the Prophet ﷺ prostrated, then placed it on his back between his shoulders. I was watching and could do nothing; if only I had strength. He said: They began laughing and leaning upon one another, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained prostrating and did not raise his head. Then he said: “O Allah, deal with Quraysh,” three times. This distressed them when he supplicated against them. He said: They used to believe that supplication in that land would be answered. Then he named them: “O Allah, deal with Abū Jahl; deal with ‘Utbah ibn Rabī‘ah, Shaybah ibn Rabī‘ah, al-Walīd ibn ‘Utbah, Umayyah ibn Khalaf, and ‘Uqbah ibn Abī Mu‘ayṭ,” and he counted a seventh, but I did not remember him. He said: By the One in whose hand is my soul, I saw those whom the Messenger of Allah ﷺ counted lying dead in the well, the well of Badr.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (240) and Muslim (1794).

8581 — ‘Urwah ibn al-Zubayr said: I said to ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Amr ibn al-‘Āṣ: “Tell me of the worst thing the polytheists did to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” He said: While the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was praying in the courtyard of the Ka‘bah, ‘Uqbah ibn Abī Mu‘ayṭ came, took the shoulder of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, twisted his garment around his neck, and strangled him severely. Abū Bakr came, took him by the shoulder, pushed him away from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and said: “Would you kill a man because he says, ‘My Lord is Allah,’ while he has brought you clear proofs from your Lord?” [Ghāfir: 28]
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4815).

8582 — ‘Urwah ibn al-Zubayr narrated from ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Amr ibn al-‘Āṣ that he said: I said to him: “What was the worst you saw Quraysh inflict upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, while they displayed hostility toward him?” He said: I was present with them one day when their nobles had gathered in the Ḥijr. They mentioned the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said: “We have never seen anything like what we have endured from this man. He has declared our minds foolish, insulted our fathers, criticized our religion, divided our unity, and reviled our gods. We have endured from him a grave matter,” or as they said. He said: While they were like that, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ appeared before them. He came walking until he touched the Corner, then passed by them while circumambulating the House. When he passed by them, they made insulting remarks to him with some of what they were saying. He said: I recognized that in his face, then he continued. When he passed by them a second time, they made similar remarks, and I recognized that in his face, then he continued. Then he passed by them a third time, and they made similar remarks, so he said: “Do you hear, O people of Quraysh? By the One in whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, I have come to you with slaughter.” His words seized the people, until there was no man among them except that it was as though a bird had landed upon his head. Even the one who had been most severe against him before that began soothing him with the best words he could find, saying: “Go away, O Abū al-Qāsim. Go away rightly guided. By Allah, you were never ignorant.” He said: So the Messenger of Allah ﷺ left. The next day, they gathered in the Ḥijr, and I was with them. Some of them said to others: “You mentioned what has reached you from him and what has reached him from you, until when he confronted you with what you dislike, you left him.” While they were like that, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ appeared before them. They leapt toward him as one man, surrounded him, and said to him: “Are you the one who says such-and-such?” referring to what had reached them from him of criticism of their gods and religion. He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Yes, I am the one who says that.” He said: I saw a man among them take hold of the gathered part of his cloak. Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq stood in front of him, saying while weeping: “Would you kill a man because he says, ‘My Lord is Allah’?” [Ghāfir: 28] Then they left him. That was the worst I ever saw Quraysh reach against him.
 Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (7036).

8583 — Anas said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I was harmed for the sake of Allah when no one else was harmed, and I was frightened for the sake of Allah when no one else was frightened. Three days and nights passed over me, and neither I nor Bilāl had any food that a living creature with a liver could eat, except what could be hidden under Bilāl’s armpit.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2472), Ibn Mājah (151), and Aḥmad (12212); Ibn Ḥibbān (6560) authenticated it. Al-Tirmidhī said: “Good, authentic.”
 His saying, “what could be hidden under Bilāl’s armpit,” means that it was a small amount, only as much as Bilāl could carry under his armpit.

8584 — Anas ibn Mālik said: They once beat the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until he lost consciousness. Abū Bakr stood and began calling out: “Woe to you! Would you kill a man because he says, ‘My Lord is Allah’?” [Ghāfir: 28] They said: “Who is this?” They said: “The mad son of Abū Quḥāfah.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Abū Ya‘lā (3691) and al-Ḥākim (3/67).

8585 — Khabbāb said: I came to the Prophet ﷺ while he was reclining on a cloak in the shade of the Ka‘bah, and we had met severe hardship from the polytheists. I said: “Will you not supplicate to Allah?” He sat up, his face red, and said: “Among those before you, a man would be combed with iron combs, stripping away the flesh or nerves beneath his bones, yet that would not turn him away from his religion. A saw would be placed on the parting of his head and he would be split in two, yet that would not turn him away from his religion. Allah will surely complete this matter until a rider travels from Ṣan‘ā’ to Ḥaḍramawt fearing none but Allah.”
 Bayān added: “And the wolf regarding his sheep.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3852).

8586 — ‘Ā’ishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, said to the Prophet ﷺ: “Did any day come upon you that was more severe than the day of Uḥud?” He said: “I met from your people what I met, and the severest thing I met from them was on the Day of al-‘Aqabah, when I presented myself to Ibn ‘Abd Yālīl ibn ‘Abd Kulāl, but he did not respond to what I wanted. I departed, distressed and not knowing where I was heading, and I did not regain awareness until I was at Qarn al-Tha‘ālib. I raised my head, and there was a cloud shading me. I looked, and Jibrīl was in it. He called me and said: Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has heard what your people said to you and how they responded to you. He has sent to you the Angel of the Mountains so that you may command him concerning them with whatever you wish. He said: The Angel of the Mountains called me, greeted me with peace, then said: O Muḥammad, Allah has heard what your people said to you. I am the Angel of the Mountains, and your Lord has sent me to you so that you may command me with your command. What do you wish? If you wish, I will crush them between the two mountains. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him: Rather, I hope that Allah will bring forth from their loins those who worship Allah alone and do not associate anything with Him.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3231) and Muslim (1795:11).

8587 — Usāmah ibn Zayd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ rode a donkey with a Fadakī cloak beneath him and had Usāmah ibn Zayd riding behind him, going to visit Sa‘d ibn ‘Ubādah among Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj before the Battle of Badr. He said: He passed by a gathering in which was ‘Abdullah ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl, and that was before ‘Abdullah ibn Ubayy accepted Islam. In the gathering were a mixture of Muslims, polytheist idol worshipers, Jews, and Muslims, and ‘Abdullah ibn Rawāḥah was in the gathering. When the dust of the animal covered the gathering, ‘Abdullah ibn Ubayy covered his nose with his cloak and said: “Do not raise dust over us.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ greeted them with peace, then stopped and dismounted. He called them to Allah and recited the Qur’an to them. ‘Abdullah ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl said: “O man, there is nothing better than what you say if it is true, but do not annoy us with it in our gathering. Return to your camp, and whoever comes to you, then narrate to him.” ‘Abdullah ibn Rawāḥah said: “Rather, O Messenger of Allah, come to us with it in our gatherings, for we love that.” The Muslims, polytheists, and Jews began insulting one another until they almost fought. The Prophet ﷺ continued calming them until they became quiet. Then the Prophet ﷺ mounted his animal and went until he entered upon Sa‘d ibn ‘Ubādah. The Prophet ﷺ said to him: “O Sa‘d, did you not hear what Abū Ḥubāb said?” — meaning ‘Abdullah ibn Ubayy — “He said such-and-such.” Sa‘d ibn ‘Ubādah said: “O Messenger of Allah, pardon him and overlook him. By the One who sent down the Book upon you, Allah has brought the truth that He sent down upon you. The people of this settlement had agreed to crown him and bind the headband upon him. When Allah refused that by the truth He gave you, he was choked by that, and that is why he did what you saw.” So the Messenger of Allah ﷺ pardoned him. The Prophet ﷺ and his Companions used to pardon the polytheists and the People of the Book as Allah commanded them, and they remained patient over harm. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: “You will surely hear much harm from those who were given the Book before you and from those who associate partners” [Āl ‘Imrān: 186]. And Allah said: “Many of the People of the Book wish they could turn you back to disbelief after your faith, out of envy from themselves” [al-Baqarah: 109] to the end of the verse. The Prophet ﷺ would interpret the command as pardon, as Allah had commanded him, until Allah gave permission concerning them. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ fought at Badr and Allah killed through it the chiefs of the disbelievers of Quraysh, Ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl and those with him from the polytheists and idol worshipers said: “This matter has now become established.” So they pledged allegiance to the Messenger ﷺ upon Islam, and they accepted Islam.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4566) and Muslim (1798:116).

8588 — Ṭāriq ibn ‘Abdullah al-Muḥāribī said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the market of Dhū al-Majāz, wearing a red suit of garments. I heard him saying: “O people, say: There is no god but Allah, and you will succeed.” A man was following him, throwing stones at him, having made his heels and ankles bleed, saying: “O people, do not obey this man, for he is a liar.” I said: “Who is this?” It was said: “This is the young man of Banū ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib.” I said: “And who is this one following him and throwing stones at him?” He said: “This is ‘Abd al-‘Uzzā, Abū Lahab.” He said: When Islam became manifest, we went out in that direction until we settled near Medina, and with us was a woman traveling in a litter. While we were sitting, a man came to us wearing two garments. He greeted us with peace and said: “From where have the people come?” We said: “From al-Rabadhah.” He said: We had a camel with us. He said: “Will you sell this camel?” We said: “Yes.” He said: “For how much?” We said: “For such-and-such ṣā‘s of dates.” He took it and did not bargain us down. He said: “I have taken it.” Then he disappeared among the walls of Medina. We began blaming one another, saying: “You gave your camel to a man you do not know.” The woman in the litter said: “Do not blame one another. I saw the face of a man who would not wrong you. I have never seen anything more resembling the full moon than his face.” He said: When evening came, a man came to us, greeted us with peace, and said: “I am the messenger of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He says: You may eat until you are full and measure out until you receive your full due.” He said: So we ate until we were full and measured out until we had received our due. He said: Then we came to Medina the next day, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was standing, delivering a sermon on the pulpit. He was saying: “The giving hand is the upper hand. Begin with those you support: your mother, your father, your sister, your brother, then the nearest and the nearest.” A man stood and said: “O Messenger of Allah, these Banū Tha‘labah ibn Yarbu‘ killed so-and-so in the Jāhiliyyah, so take vengeance for us from him.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ raised his hands until I saw the whiteness of his armpits and said: “Indeed, a mother does not bear guilt for her child. Indeed, a mother does not bear guilt for her child.”
 Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6562), al-Dāraquṭnī (3/44–45), and al-Ḥākim (2/611–612).

8589 — Abū Hurayrah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Do you not marvel at how Allah turns away from me the insults and curses of Quraysh? They insult Mudhammam and curse Mudhammam, while I am Muḥammad.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3533).

8590 — Ibn ‘Abbās said concerning Allah Most High’s saying: “A human being only teaches him” [al-Naḥl: 103]: They said: “Only the slave of Ibn al-Ḥaḍramī teaches Muḥammad, and he is the one who has books.” So Allah said: “The tongue of the one they refer to is foreign, while this is a clear Arabic tongue,” and: “Only those who do not believe in the signs of Allah fabricate lies.” [al-Naḥl: 103, 105]
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/357), through Ādam ibn Abī Iyās, from Warqā’, from Abū Najīḥ, from Mujāhid, from Ibn ‘Abbās, who mentioned it.

8591 — Ibn ‘Abbās said regarding the saying of Allah, Mighty and Majestic: “Indeed, We are sufficient for you against the mockers” [al-Ḥijr: 95]: The mockers were al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīrah, al-Aswad ibn ‘Abd Yaghūth al-Zuhrī, al-Aswad ibn al-Muṭṭalib Abū Zam‘ah from Banū Asad ibn ‘Abd al-‘Uzzā, al-Ḥārith ibn al-Ṭalāṭilah al-Sahmī, and al-‘Āṣ ibn Wā’il.
 Jibrīl, peace be upon him, came to him, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ complained to him about them. He showed him al-Walīd, Abū ‘Amr ibn al-Mughīrah, and Jibrīl, peace be upon him, pointed to his vein, and said: “What have you done?” He said: “I have sufficed you against him.” Then he showed him al-Aswad ibn al-Muṭṭalib, and Jibrīl pointed to his eyes and said: “What have you done?” He said: “I have sufficed you against him.” Then he showed him al-Aswad ibn ‘Abd Yaghūth al-Zuhrī, and he pointed to his head and said: “What have you done?” He said: “I have sufficed you against him.” Then he showed him al-Ḥārith ibn al-Ṭalāṭilah al-Sahmī, and he pointed to his head — or he said: to his belly — and said: “What have you done?” He said: “I have sufficed you against him.” Then al-‘Āṣ ibn Wā’il passed by him, and he pointed to the hollow of his foot and said: “What have you done?” He said: “I have sufficed you against him.”
 As for al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīrah, he passed by a man from Khuzā‘ah who was feathering arrows, and one struck his vein and severed it. As for al-Aswad ibn al-Muṭṭalib, he became blind. Some say he became blind in this way, and others say that he sat beneath an acacia tree and began saying: “O my sons, will you not defend me? I have been killed.” They kept saying: “We do not see anything.” He kept saying: “O my sons, will you not protect me? I have perished; here I am being stabbed with thorns in my eyes.” They kept saying: “We do not see anything,” and he remained like that until both his eyes became blind. As for al-Aswad ibn ‘Abd Yaghūth al-Zuhrī, sores appeared on his head and he died from them. As for al-Ḥārith ibn al-Ṭalāṭilah, yellow water afflicted his stomach until it came out of his mouth, and he died from it. As for al-‘Āṣ ibn Wā’il, one day a thorny plant entered his head until it filled it, and he died from it. Others said in this report that he rode to al-Ṭā’if on a donkey, and it knelt upon a thorny plant, so a thorn entered the hollow of his foot and killed him.
