 Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Awsaṭ (5/515–516) and al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (2/316–318).

8592 — Sa‘d ibn Abī Waqqāṣ said: We were with the Prophet ﷺ, six people, and the polytheists said to the Prophet ﷺ: “Drive these people away so that they will not be bold toward us.” He said: I was among them, along with Ibn Mas‘ūd, a man from Hudhayl, Bilāl, and two men whom I will not name. Something occurred in the mind of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ as Allah willed, and he thought to himself, so Allah revealed: “And do not drive away those who call upon their Lord morning and evening, seeking His Face. You are not accountable for them in anything” [al-An‘ām: 52].
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2413:46).

8593 — Masrūq said: We were sitting with ‘Abdullah while he was reclining among us. A man came to him and said: “O Abū ‘Abd al-Raḥmān, there is a storyteller at the gates of Kindah telling stories and claiming that the sign of the smoke will come and seize the breath of the disbelievers, while it will affect the believers like a cold.” ‘Abdullah sat up angrily and said: “O people! Fear Allah. Whoever among you knows something, let him speak according to what he knows; and whoever does not know, let him say: Allah knows best. For it is more knowledgeable for one of you, regarding what he does not know, to say: Allah knows best. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said to His Prophet ﷺ: ‘Say: I do not ask you for any reward for it, and I am not one of those who pretend’ [Ṣād: 86].
 When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw people turning away, he said: ‘O Allah, seven years like the seven of Yusuf.’ He said: A drought seized them and wiped out everything, until they ate hides and carrion from hunger. One of them would look toward the sky and see something like smoke. Abū Sufyān came to him and said: ‘O Muḥammad, you came commanding obedience to Allah and maintaining family ties, and your people have perished, so supplicate to Allah for them.’
 Allah Most High said: ‘So watch for the Day when the sky will bring a clear smoke, covering the people. This is a painful punishment’ [al-Dukhān: 10–11], until His saying: ‘Indeed, you will return’ [al-Dukhān: 15]. He said: ‘Will the punishment of the Hereafter be removed?’ ‘The Day We will seize with the greatest seizure; indeed, We will take revenge’ [al-Dukhān: 16]. The seizure was the Day of Badr. The sign of the smoke, the seizure, the binding punishment, and the sign of the Romans have already passed.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (1007) and Muslim (2798).

8594 — Ibn Mas‘ūd said: Five have already passed: the binding punishment, the Romans, the seizure, the moon, and the smoke. Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Tafsīr (4825) and Muslim in The Description of the Day of Resurrection (2798:4), both from al-A‘mash, from Abū al-Ḍuḥā, from Masrūq, from ‘Abdullah, who mentioned it.

8595 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: The Prophet ﷺ prostrated during Sūrat al-Najm, and the Muslims, the polytheists, the jinn, and mankind prostrated with him; meaning when the saying of Allah Most High was revealed: “So prostrate to Allah and worship” [al-Najm: 62].
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4862).

8596 — Umm Salamah, daughter of Abū Umayyah ibn al-Mughīrah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, said: When we settled in the land of Abyssinia, we lived under the best neighbor there, the Negus. We were safe in our religion, we worshiped Allah Most High without being harmed, and we did not hear anything we disliked. When that reached Quraysh, they plotted to send two strong men to the Negus concerning us, and to send gifts to the Negus from the rare goods of Makkah. Among the things he most admired from it was leather, so they gathered much leather for him and did not leave any of his bishops without giving him a gift. Then they sent that with ‘Abdullah ibn Abī Rabī‘ah ibn al-Mughīrah al-Makhzūmī and ‘Amr ibn al-‘Āṣ ibn Wā’il al-Sahmī, and instructed them with their plan. They said to them: “Give every bishop his gift before you speak to the Negus about them, then present the Negus his gifts, then ask him to hand them over to you before he speaks to them.”
 She said: So they left and came to the Negus, while we were with him in the best dwelling and with the best neighbor. They did not leave any of his bishops except that they gave him his gift before speaking to the Negus. Then they said to every bishop among them: “Some foolish young men from us have fled to the king’s land. They have left the religion of their people and have not entered your religion. They have come with an invented religion that neither we nor you know. The nobles of their people have sent us to the king concerning them so that we may return them to them. When we speak to the king about them, advise him to hand them over to us and not speak to them, for their people have better insight about them and know best what they blame them for.” They said to the two of them: “Yes.” Then the two of them brought their gifts to the Negus, and he accepted them. Then they spoke to him and said: “O king, some foolish young men from us have fled to your land. They have left the religion of their people and have not entered your religion. They have come with an invented religion that neither we nor you know. The nobles of their people — their fathers, uncles, and clansmen — have sent us to you concerning them so that you may return them to them. They have better insight about them and know best what they blame and reproach them for.”
 She said: Nothing was more hated to ‘Abdullah ibn Abī Rabī‘ah and ‘Amr ibn al-‘Āṣ than that the Negus should hear their speech. The bishops around him said: “They have spoken truthfully, O king. Their people have better insight about them and know best what they blame them for, so hand them over to the two of them, and let them return them to their land and their people.” She said: The Negus became angry and said: “No, by Allah, then I will not hand them over to them. I will not nearly hand over a people who have sought my protection, settled in my land, and chosen me over others, until I call them and ask them about what these two say concerning them. If they are as the two say, I will hand them over to them and return them to their people. But if they are otherwise, I will protect them from the two and treat them well as long as they remain under my protection.”
 She said: Then he sent for the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and summoned them. When his messenger came to them, they gathered and said to one another: “What will you say to the man when you come to him?” They said: “By Allah, we will say what we know and what our Prophet ﷺ commanded us, whatever may happen.” When they came to him, the Negus had summoned his bishops, and they had spread their scriptures around him. He asked them: “What is this religion by which you have separated from your people, while you have not entered my religion nor the religion of any of these nations?”
 She said: The one who spoke to him was Ja‘far ibn Abī Ṭālib. He said to him: “O king, we were a people of ignorance. We worshiped idols, ate carrion, committed immoral deeds, cut off family ties, treated neighbors badly, and the strong among us consumed the weak. We remained upon that until Allah sent to us a Messenger from among us, whose lineage, truthfulness, trustworthiness, and chastity we knew. He called us to Allah Most High, to single Him out and worship Him, and to abandon what we and our fathers used to worship besides Him of stones and idols. He commanded us to speak truthfully, fulfill trusts, maintain family ties, treat neighbors well, and refrain from forbidden things and bloodshed. He forbade us from immoralities, false speech, consuming the wealth of orphans, and slandering chaste women. He commanded us to worship Allah alone, not associating anything with Him, and he commanded us to pray, give zakah, and fast.”
 She said: He listed to him the matters of Islam. “So we believed him, had faith in him, and followed him in what he brought. We worshiped Allah alone and did not associate anything with Him. We made unlawful what he made unlawful for us, and lawful what he made lawful for us. Then our people attacked us, tortured us, and tried to tempt us away from our religion, to return us from the worship of Allah to the worship of idols, and to make us consider lawful what we used to consider lawful of foul things. When they overpowered us, wronged us, made things difficult for us, and came between us and our religion, we came out to your land, chose you over others, desired your protection, and hoped that we would not be wronged in your presence, O king.”
 She said: The Negus said to him: “Do you have anything with you from what he brought from Allah?” Ja‘far said: “Yes.” The Negus said: “Recite it to me.” So he recited to him the beginning of “Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād” [Maryam: 1]. She said: By Allah, the Negus wept until his beard became wet, and his bishops wept until their scriptures became wet when they heard what was recited to them. Then the Negus said: “Indeed, this and what Mūsā brought come from one lamp.” He said to the two men: “Go, for by Allah, I will never hand them over to you, nor will I come close to doing so.”
 Umm Salamah said: When they left him, ‘Amr ibn al-‘Āṣ said: “By Allah, tomorrow I will inform him of something about them that will disgrace them in his sight, and by it I will uproot their foundation.” She said: ‘Abdullah ibn Abī Rabī‘ah, who was the more mindful of the two men regarding us, said to him: “Do not do that, for they have family ties, even if they have opposed us.” He said: “By Allah, I will tell him that they claim that ‘Īsā ibn Maryam is a slave.” She said: The next morning he went to him and said: “O king, they say a grave statement about ‘Īsā ibn Maryam, so send for them and ask them what they say about him.”
 She said: He sent for them and asked them about him, and nothing like that had ever come upon us. The people gathered and said to one another: “What will you say about ‘Īsā if he asks you about him?” They said: “By Allah, we will say about him what Allah said and what our Prophet brought, whatever may happen.” When they entered upon him, he said to them: “What do you say about ‘Īsā ibn Maryam?” Ja‘far ibn Abī Ṭālib said to him: “We say about him what our Prophet brought: he is the servant of Allah, His Messenger, His spirit, and His word which He cast to Maryam, the virgin and pure one.” She said: The Negus struck his hand to the ground, picked up a stick from it, and said: “‘Īsā ibn Maryam did not exceed what you have said by the measure of this stick.” His bishops snorted around him when he said what he said. He said: “Even if you snort, by Allah. Go, for you are safe in my land.” The word “suyūm” means “safe.” “Whoever insults you will be fined; whoever insults you will be fined; whoever insults you will be fined. I would not love to have a mountain of gold while I harm a man among you.” In the language of Abyssinia, “dabr” means mountain. “Return their gifts to the two of them; we have no need of them. By Allah, Allah did not take a bribe from me when He returned my kingdom to me, so should I take a bribe concerning it? And the people did not obey concerning me, so should I obey them concerning it?”
 She said: They left him disgraced, with what they had brought returned to them, and we remained with him in the best dwelling and with the best neighbor.
 She said: By Allah, while we were upon that, someone came against him, meaning someone who contested him in his kingdom. She said: By Allah, we never experienced any grief more severe than the grief we felt at that time, fearing that this person might prevail over the Negus, and that a man would come who would not know our right as the Negus knew it. She said: The Negus went out, and between them was the width of the Nile. She said: The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Who is a man who will go out and attend the battle of the people, then bring us the news?” She said: al-Zubayr ibn al-‘Awwām said: “I will.” She said: He was among the youngest of the people. They inflated a waterskin for him, and he placed it on his chest, then swam upon it until he reached the side of the Nile where the two groups were meeting. Then he went on until he attended them. She said: We supplicated to Allah for the Negus to prevail over his enemy and to be established in his land, and the affair of Abyssinia became firm for him. We remained with him in the best lodging until we came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was in Makkah.
 Good: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (1740, 22498) and al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (2/301–306).

8597 — Ibn Mas‘ūd said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us to the Negus, and we were about eighty men. Among them were ‘Abdullah ibn Mas‘ūd, Ja‘far, ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Arfaṭah, ‘Uthmān ibn Maẓ‘ūn, and Abū Mūsā. They came to the Negus, and Quraysh sent ‘Amr ibn al-‘Āṣ and ‘Umārah ibn al-Walīd with a gift. When the two entered upon the Negus, they prostrated to him, then hastened to sit on his right and left. Then they said to him: “A group from our cousins have settled in your land, so send for them.” He sent for them. Ja‘far said: “I am your spokesman today.” So they followed him. He greeted him with peace and did not prostrate. They said to him: “Why do you not prostrate to the king?” He said: “We do not prostrate except to Allah, Mighty and Majestic.” He said: “What is that?” He said: “Allah, Mighty and Majestic, sent to us His Messenger ﷺ and commanded us not to prostrate to anyone except Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and commanded us with prayer and zakah.” ‘Amr ibn al-‘Āṣ said: “They oppose you regarding ‘Īsā ibn Maryam!” He said: “What do you say about ‘Īsā ibn Maryam and his mother?” They said: “We say as Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: He is the word of Allah and His spirit which He cast to the virgin and pure one, whom no human being had touched and from whom no child had been born before.” He lifted a stick from the ground and said: “O people of Abyssinia, priests, and monks, by Allah, they do not add to what we say about him even the measure of this.” Then he said: “Welcome to you and to the one from whom you have come. I testify that he is the Messenger of Allah. He is the one we find in the Gospel, and he is the Messenger about whom ‘Īsā ibn Maryam gave glad tidings. Settle wherever you wish. By Allah, were it not for the kingdom I am in, I would go to him until I myself carried his sandals and poured water for his ablution.” He ordered that the gift of the two others be returned to them. Then ‘Abdullah ibn Mas‘ūd hurried back until he caught Badr. He claimed that the Prophet ﷺ sought forgiveness for the Negus when news of his death reached him.
 Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (4400), al-Ḥākim (2/623), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (2/298).

8598 — Abū Mūsā al-Ash‘arī said: News of the Prophet’s ﷺ emergence reached us while we were in Yemen, so we set out migrating to him — myself and two brothers of mine, I being the youngest of them: one was Abū Burdah and the other Abū Ruhm — either he said among a few, or he said among fifty-three or fifty-two men from my people. We boarded a ship, and our ship took us to the Negus in Abyssinia. We found Ja‘far ibn Abī Ṭālib and his companions with him. Ja‘far said: “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us here and commanded us to remain, so stay with us.” We stayed with him until we all came together, and we met the Prophet ﷺ when he conquered Khaybar. He assigned us shares — or he said: he gave us from it. He did not divide anything from Khaybar for anyone who was absent from its conquest except those who had witnessed it with him, except for the people of our ship along with Ja‘far and his companions; he gave them shares with them.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3136).

8599 — ‘Ā’ishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, said: I never knew my parents except that they were practicing the religion, and no day would pass except that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would come to us at both ends of the day: morning and evening. When the Muslims were tested, Abū Bakr set out as a migrant toward the land of Abyssinia. When he reached Bark al-Ghimād, Ibn al-Daghinah — the chief of al-Qārah — met him and said: “Where are you going, O Abū Bakr?” Abū Bakr said: “My people have driven me out, so I want to travel through the earth and worship my Lord.” Ibn al-Daghinah said: “A man like you, O Abū Bakr, should not leave and should not be driven out. You earn for the destitute, maintain family ties, bear burdens, host guests, and assist in calamities of truth. I am your protector, so return and worship your Lord in your town.” So he returned, and Ibn al-Daghinah traveled back with him. That evening, Ibn al-Daghinah went around to the nobles of Quraysh and said to them: “A man like Abū Bakr should not leave and should not be driven out. Will you drive out a man who earns for the destitute, maintains family ties, bears burdens, hosts guests, and assists in calamities of truth?” Quraysh did not reject the protection of Ibn al-Daghinah, and they said to Ibn al-Daghinah: “Tell Abū Bakr to worship his Lord in his house, to pray there, and to recite whatever he wishes, but not to harm us with that and not to do it openly, for we fear he will put our women and children to trial.” Ibn al-Daghinah told that to Abū Bakr.
 Abū Bakr remained like that, worshiping his Lord in his house, not making his prayer public and not reciting outside his house. Then something occurred to Abū Bakr, so he built a small mosque in the courtyard of his house. He would pray in it and recite the Qur’an. The women and children of the polytheists would crowd around him, amazed by him and looking at him. Abū Bakr was a man who wept often; he could not control his eyes when he recited the Qur’an. This alarmed the nobles of Quraysh among the polytheists, so they sent for Ibn al-Daghinah, and he came to them. They said: “We granted Abū Bakr protection through your protection on the condition that he worship his Lord in his house, but he has gone beyond that. He built a mosque in the courtyard of his house and openly performs prayer and recitation in it. We fear that he will put our women and children to trial, so stop him. If he wants to limit himself to worshiping his Lord in his house, let him do so. But if he refuses except to do that openly, then ask him to return your covenant of protection to you. We dislike violating your protection, but we will not allow Abū Bakr to practice openly.”
 ‘Ā’ishah said: Ibn al-Daghinah came to Abū Bakr and said: “You know what I covenanted for you. Either limit yourself to that, or return my covenant of protection to me, for I do not want the Arabs to hear that I have been betrayed regarding a man for whom I made a covenant.” Abū Bakr said: “Then I return your protection to you, and I am content with the protection of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”
 The Prophet ﷺ was in Makkah at that time, and he said to the Muslims: “I have been shown the land of your migration: a place of palm trees between two lava plains,” and they were the two ḥarrahs. So whoever migrated before Medina migrated, and most of those who had migrated to Abyssinia returned to Medina. Abū Bakr prepared himself to go to Medina, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him: “Wait, for I hope that permission will be granted to me.” Abū Bakr said: “Do you hope for that, may my father be sacrificed for you?” He said: “Yes.” So Abū Bakr kept himself waiting for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ so that he could accompany him, and he fed two riding animals that he had with the leaves of the samur tree — meaning khabaṭ — for four months.
 Ibn Shihāb said: ‘Urwah said: ‘Ā’ishah said: While we were sitting one day in the house of Abū Bakr at the height of midday, someone said to Abū Bakr: “This is the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, veiled, at an hour when he would not usually come to us.” Abū Bakr said: “May my father and mother be sacrificed for him. By Allah, nothing has brought him at this hour except an important matter.” She said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came and asked permission to enter, and permission was granted to him. He entered, and the Prophet ﷺ said to Abū Bakr: “Send out whoever is with you.” Abū Bakr said: “They are only your family, may my father be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah.” He said: “I have been granted permission to leave.” Abū Bakr said: “Companionship, may my father be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Yes.” Abū Bakr said: “Then take, may my father be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah, one of these two riding animals of mine.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “For a price.”
 ‘Ā’ishah said: We prepared them as quickly and completely as possible, and we prepared provisions for them in a leather bag. Asmā’ bint Abī Bakr cut a piece from her waistband and tied the mouth of the bag with it, and for that she was called Dhāt al-Niṭāq. She said: Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr reached a cave in Mount Thawr and hid in it for three nights. ‘Abdullah ibn Abī Bakr, a clever and perceptive young man, would spend the night with them, then leave them before dawn and wake up in Makkah among Quraysh as though he had spent the night there. He would not hear any plot they were devising except that he memorized it, then he would bring them the news when darkness mixed in. ‘Āmir ibn Fuhayrah, the freedman of Abū Bakr, would graze a milch flock of sheep for them and bring it to them when some time of the night had passed, so they would spend the night with fresh milk — the milk of their milch animals and what was warmed with hot stones — until ‘Āmir ibn Fuhayrah would drive it away before dawn. He did that every night of those three nights. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr hired a man from Banū al-Dīl, from Banū ‘Abd ibn ‘Adī, as a skilled guide; “khirrīt” means one expert in guidance. He had entered into a covenant with the family of al-‘Āṣ ibn Wā’il al-Sahmī, and he was upon the religion of the disbelievers of Quraysh. They trusted him and handed over their two riding animals to him, agreeing to meet him at the cave of Thawr after three nights with their two mounts on the morning of the third. ‘Āmir ibn Fuhayrah and the guide departed with them, and he took them along the coastal route.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3905).

8600 — Abū Hurayrah said: The Prophet ﷺ said the next day, the Day of Sacrifice, while he was at Minā: “Tomorrow we will camp at the valley of Banū Kinānah, where they made a pact upon disbelief.”
 By this he meant al-Muḥaṣṣab. That was because Quraysh and Kinānah had allied themselves against Banū Hāshim and Banū ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib — or Banū al-Muṭṭalib — that they would not marry into them, nor buy and sell with them, until they surrendered the Prophet ﷺ to them.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1590) and Muslim (1314).

8601 — al-‘Abbās ibn ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib said to the Prophet ﷺ: “What benefit did you give your uncle, for he used to protect you and become angry on your behalf?” He said: “He is in a shallow part of the Fire. Were it not for me, he would have been in the lowest depth of the Fire.” Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3883) and Muslim (209). In another narration: “I found him in the depths of the Fire, so I brought him out to a shallow part.” “Ḍaḥḍāḥ” refers to shallow water spread over the surface of the ground up to around the ankles; meaning that he is in the Fire reaching his ankles. His saying, “Were it not for me, he would have been in the lowest depth of the Fire,” means the bottom of Hell and its furthest lowest level.

8602 — al-Musayyib ibn Ḥazn said: When death approached Abū Ṭālib, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to him and found Abū Jahl ibn Hishām and ‘Abdullah ibn Abī Umayyah ibn al-Mughīrah with him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to Abū Ṭālib: “O my uncle, say: There is no god but Allah — a word by which I will testify for you before Allah.” Abū Jahl and ‘Abdullah ibn Abī Umayyah said: “O Abū Ṭālib, will you turn away from the religion of ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ continued presenting it to him, and the two of them kept repeating that statement, until the last thing Abū Ṭālib said to them was: “He is upon the religion of ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib,” and he refused to say: “There is no god but Allah.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “By Allah, I will surely seek forgiveness for you as long as I am not forbidden from doing so.” Then Allah Most High revealed: “It is not for the Prophet and those who believe to seek forgiveness for the polytheists, even if they are near relatives, after it has become clear to them that they are the companions of the Blaze” [al-Tawbah: 113]. And He revealed regarding Abū Ṭālib, saying to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ: “Indeed, you do not guide whom you love, but Allah guides whom He wills, and He knows best those who are guided” [al-Qaṣaṣ: 56].
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4772) and Muslim (24).

8603 — Abū Hurayrah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to his uncle: “Say: There is no god but Allah, and I will testify for you by it on the Day of Resurrection.” He said: “Were it not that Quraysh would shame me, saying: ‘Only panic drove him to that,’ I would have comforted your eye with it.” So Allah revealed: “Indeed, you do not guide whom you love, but Allah guides whom He wills, and He knows best those who are guided” [al-Qaṣaṣ: 56].
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (25).

8604 — Abū Sa‘īd heard the Prophet ﷺ, when his uncle was mentioned in his presence, say: “Perhaps my intercession will benefit him on the Day of Resurrection, so he will be placed in a shallow part of the Fire reaching his ankles, from which his brain will boil.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3885) and Muslim (210). In another narration: “The core of his brain will boil from it.”

8605 — Ibn ‘Abbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “The lightest of the people of the Fire in punishment is Abū Ṭālib. He will be wearing two sandals from which his brain will boil.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (212).

8606 — ‘Urwah narrated that ‘Ā’ishah told him that she said to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ: “O Messenger of Allah, has there ever come upon you a day more severe than the Day of Uḥud?” He said: “I certainly faced from your people what I faced, and the most severe of what I faced from them was on the Day of al-‘Aqabah, when I presented myself to Ibn ‘Abd Yālīl ibn ‘Abd Kulāl, but he did not respond to what I wanted. I departed, overwhelmed with grief, not knowing where I was going, and I did not regain awareness until I reached Qarn al-Tha‘ālib. I raised my head and there was a cloud shading me. I looked and there was Jibrīl in it. He called me and said: Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has heard what your people said to you and how they responded to you, and He has sent to you the Angel of the Mountains so that you may command him regarding them as you wish. Then the Angel of the Mountains called me, greeted me with salām, and said: O Muḥammad, Allah has heard what your people said to you. I am the Angel of the Mountains, and your Lord has sent me to you so that you may command me with your command. What do you wish? If you wish, I will crush them between the two mountains.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him: “Rather, I hope that Allah will bring forth from their loins those who will worship Allah alone and not associate anything with Him.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3231) and Muslim (1795).

8607 — ‘Abdullah ibn Ja‘far ibn Abī Ṭālib said: When Abū Ṭālib died, the Prophet ﷺ went out walking on his feet to al-Ṭā’if and called them to Allah, but they did not respond to him. He came to the shade of a tree, prayed two rak‘ahs, then said: “O Allah, to You I complain of my weakness, my lack of means, and my lowliness before the people. O Most Merciful of the merciful, You are the Lord of the weak, and You are my Lord. To whom will You entrust me? To a distant person who receives me harshly? Or to an enemy whom You have given control over my affair? If You are not angry with me, then I do not mind; but Your protection is broader for me. I seek refuge in the light of Your Face, by which darknesses shine and the affairs of this world and the Hereafter are set right, from Your anger descending upon me or Your displeasure coming down upon me. I seek to please You until You are pleased. There is no might and no power except through You.” Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (14/139–140) and in al-Du‘ā’ (2/1280); through him al-Ḍiyā’ in al-Mukhtārah (9/181); Ibn ‘Adī in al-Kāmil (6/2124); and al-Khaṭīb in al-Jāmi‘ li-Akhlāq al-Rāwī wa Ādāb al-Sāmi‘ (2/275), all through Wahb ibn Jarīr ibn Ḥāzim, from his father, from Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, from Hishām ibn ‘Urwah, from his father, from ‘Abdullah ibn Ja‘far ibn Abī Ṭālib.

8608 — Abū Dharr used to narrate that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “The roof of my house was opened while I was in Makkah, and Jibrīl, peace be upon him, descended. He opened my chest, then washed it with the water of Zamzam. Then he brought a basin of gold filled with wisdom and faith, poured it into my chest, then closed it. Then he took my hand and ascended with me to the heaven. When we came to the lowest heaven, Jibrīl, peace be upon him, said to the gatekeeper of the lowest heaven: ‘Open.’ He said: ‘Who is this?’ He said: ‘This is Jibrīl.’ He said: ‘Is anyone with you?’ He said: ‘Yes, Muḥammad ﷺ is with me.’ He said: ‘Has he been sent for?’ He said: ‘Yes.’ So he opened. When we rose into the lowest heaven, there was a man, with dark multitudes on his right and dark multitudes on his left. When he looked to his right, he laughed, and when he looked to his left, he wept. He said: ‘Welcome to the righteous Prophet and righteous son.’ I said: ‘O Jibrīl, who is this?’ He said: ‘This is Ādam, peace be upon him, and these dark multitudes on his right and left are the souls of his children. The people of the right are the people of Paradise, and the dark multitudes on his left are the people of the Fire. When he looks to his right he laughs, and when he looks to his left he weeps.’
 Then Jibrīl ascended with me until he came to the second heaven. He said to its gatekeeper: ‘Open.’ Its gatekeeper said to him the same as the gatekeeper of the lowest heaven had said, and he opened.”
 Anas ibn Mālik said: He mentioned that he found in the heavens Ādam, Idrīs, ‘Īsā, Mūsā, and Ibrāhīm, may the prayers of Allah be upon them all, but he did not establish exactly how their stations were, except that he mentioned that he found Ādam, peace be upon him, in the lowest heaven, and Ibrāhīm in the sixth heaven. He said: When Jibrīl and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed by Idrīs, may Allah’s prayers be upon him, he said: “Welcome to the righteous Prophet and righteous brother.” He said: Then he passed on, and I said: “Who is this?” He said: “This is Idrīs.” Then I passed by Mūsā, peace be upon him, and he said: “Welcome to the righteous Prophet and righteous brother.” I said: “Who is this?” He said: “This is Mūsā.” Then I passed by ‘Īsā, and he said: “Welcome to the righteous Prophet and righteous brother.” I said: “Who is this?” He said: “This is ‘Īsā ibn Maryam.” Then I passed by Ibrāhīm, peace be upon him, and he said: “Welcome to the righteous Prophet and righteous son.” I said: “Who is this?” He said: “This is Ibrāhīm.”
 Ibn Shihāb said: Ibn Ḥazm informed me that Ibn ‘Abbās and Abū Ḥabbah al-Anṣārī used to say: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Then I was ascended until I reached a level where I heard the scratching of the pens.”
 Ibn Ḥazm and Anas ibn Mālik said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Allah obligated upon my Ummah fifty prayers.” He said: “I returned with that until I passed by Mūsā, and Mūsā, peace be upon him, said: ‘What has your Lord obligated upon your Ummah?’ I said: ‘He has obligated upon them fifty prayers.’ Mūsā, peace be upon him, said to me: ‘Return to your Lord, for your Ummah will not be able to bear that.’ So I returned to my Lord, and He removed half of it. Then I returned to Mūsā, peace be upon him, and informed him. He said: ‘Return to your Lord, for your Ummah will not be able to bear that.’ So I returned to my Lord, and He said: ‘They are five, and they are fifty. The word is not changed with Me.’ I returned to Mūsā, and he said: ‘Return to your Lord.’ I said: ‘I have become shy before my Lord.’ Then Jibrīl took me until he came to Sidrat al-Muntahā, and it was covered with colors that I did not know. Then I was admitted into Paradise, and there were domes of pearl, and its soil was musk.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (349) and Muslim (163), and the wording is Muslim’s.

8609 — Mālik ibn Ṣa‘ṣa‘ah, a man from his people, said: The Prophet of Allah ﷺ said: “While I was at the House, between sleep and wakefulness, I heard someone saying: ‘One of the three, between the two men.’ I was brought and taken away, and a basin of gold containing water from Zamzam was brought. My chest was opened to such-and-such.” Qatādah said: I said to the one with me: “What does he mean?” He said: “To the bottom of his abdomen.” “My heart was taken out and washed with Zamzam water, then returned to its place, then filled with faith and wisdom. Then I was brought a white beast called al-Burāq, larger than a donkey and smaller than a mule, whose step would land at the farthest point of its sight, and I was carried upon it. Then we set out until we came to the lowest heaven. Jibrīl, peace be upon him, asked for it to be opened. It was said: ‘Who is this?’ He said: ‘Jibrīl.’ It was said: ‘Who is with you?’ He said: ‘Muḥammad ﷺ.’ It was said: ‘Has he been sent for?’ He said: ‘Yes.’ It was opened for us, and it was said: ‘Welcome to him, and what an excellent arrival he has made.’ Then we came upon Ādam, peace be upon him.”
 He then narrated the story of the ḥadīth and mentioned that in the second heaven he met ‘Īsā and Yaḥyā, peace be upon them both; in the third, Yūsuf; in the fourth, Idrīs; in the fifth, Hārūn — may the prayers and peace of Allah be upon them. He said: “Then we set out until we reached the sixth heaven. I came upon Mūsā, peace be upon him, and greeted him with salām. He said: ‘Welcome to the righteous brother and righteous Prophet.’ When I passed him, he wept, and he was called: ‘What makes you weep?’ He said: ‘My Lord, this young man whom You sent after me — more of his Ummah will enter Paradise than of my Ummah!’ Then we set out until we reached the seventh heaven, and I came upon Ibrāhīm.”
 In the ḥadīth, the Prophet of Allah ﷺ narrated that he saw four rivers emerging from its base: two outward rivers and two inward rivers. I said: “O Jibrīl, what are these rivers?” He said: “As for the inward rivers, they are two rivers in Paradise, and as for the outward ones, they are the Nile and the Euphrates.” Then al-Bayt al-Ma‘mūr was raised for me. I said: “O Jibrīl, what is this?” He said: “This is al-Bayt al-Ma‘mūr. Every day seventy thousand angels enter it, and when they leave it, they never return to it again; that is the last obligation upon them.” Then I was brought two vessels: one containing wine and the other milk. They were presented to me, and I chose the milk. It was said: “You have chosen correctly; through you, Allah has made your Ummah upon the fiṭrah.” Then fifty prayers every day were obligated upon me. Then he mentioned the story until the end of the ḥadīth.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3887) and Muslim (164), and the wording is Muslim’s.
 In al-Bukhārī’s wording, after his saying: “They will not return to it; that is the last obligation upon them,” it says: “Sidrat al-Muntahā was raised for me, and its fruits were like the jars of Hajar, and its leaves were like the ears of elephants.”
 His saying, “Then fifty prayers every day were obligated upon me,” then he mentioned the story until the end of the ḥadīth — that is his saying ﷺ, as in Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī: “I came forward until I came to Mūsā. He said: ‘What did you do?’ I said: ‘Fifty prayers have been obligated upon me.’ He said: ‘I know people better than you; I dealt with the Children of Israel with the most difficult treatment, and your Ummah will not be able to bear it. Return to your Lord and ask Him.’ So I returned and asked Him, and He made it forty. Then the same occurred, then thirty. Then the same occurred, and He made it twenty. Then the same occurred, and He made it ten. I came to Mūsā, and he said the same, so He made it five. I came to Mūsā, and he said: ‘What did you do?’ I said: ‘He made it five.’ He said the same. I said: ‘I have submitted with goodness.’ Then it was announced: ‘I have carried out My obligation, lightened it for My servants, and I reward each good deed tenfold.’”

8610 — Anas narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Sidrat al-Muntahā was raised for me, and there were four rivers: two outward rivers and two inward rivers. As for the outward rivers, they are the Nile and the Euphrates, and as for the inward rivers, they are two rivers in Paradise. I was brought three cups: a cup containing milk, a cup containing honey, and a cup containing wine. I took the one containing milk and drank it, and it was said: You and your Ummah have attained the fiṭrah.”
 Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Ṣaghīr (1139).

8611 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I was brought al-Burāq, a tall white animal, larger than a donkey and smaller than a mule, placing its hoof at the farthest point of its sight. I rode it until I came to Bayt al-Maqdis. I tied it to the ring to which the Prophets tie their mounts. Then I entered the mosque and prayed two rak‘ahs in it. Then I came out, and Jibrīl, peace be upon him, brought me a vessel of wine and a vessel of milk, and I chose the milk. Jibrīl, peace be upon him said: ‘You have chosen the fiṭrah.’ Then we were taken up to the heaven, and Jibrīl asked for it to be opened. It was said: ‘Who are you?’ He said: ‘Jibrīl.’ It was said: ‘Who is with you?’ He said: ‘Muḥammad.’ It was said: ‘Has he been sent for?’ He said: ‘He has been sent for.’ It was opened for us, and there I was with Ādam, who welcomed me and supplicated good for me. Then we ascended to the second heaven, and Jibrīl, peace be upon him, asked for it to be opened. It was said: ‘Who are you?’ He said: ‘Jibrīl.’ It was said: ‘Who is with you?’ He said: ‘Muḥammad.’ It was said: ‘Has he been sent for?’ He said: ‘He has been sent for.’ It was opened for us, and there I was with the two maternal cousins: ‘Īsā ibn Maryam and Yaḥyā ibn Zakariyyā, may Allah’s prayers be upon them both. They welcomed me and supplicated good for me. Then I was ascended to the third heaven, and Jibrīl asked for it to be opened. It was said: ‘Who are you?’ He said: ‘Jibrīl.’ It was said: ‘Who is with you?’ He said: ‘Muḥammad.’ It was said: ‘Has he been sent for?’ He said: ‘He has been sent for.’ It was opened for us, and there I was with Yūsuf ﷺ, who had been given half of beauty. He welcomed me and supplicated good for me. Then we ascended to the fourth heaven, and Jibrīl, peace be upon him, asked for it to be opened. It was said: ‘Who is this?’ He said: ‘Jibrīl.’ It was said: ‘Who is with you?’ He said: ‘Muḥammad.’ It was said: ‘Has he been sent for?’ He said: ‘He has been sent for.’ It was opened for us, and there I was with Idrīs, who welcomed me and supplicated good for me. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: ‘And We raised him to a high place’ [Maryam: 57]. Then we ascended to the fifth heaven, and Jibrīl asked for it to be opened. It was said: ‘Who is this?’ He said: ‘Jibrīl.’ It was said: ‘Who is with you?’ He said: ‘Muḥammad.’ It was said: ‘Has he been sent for?’ He said: ‘He has been sent for.’ It was opened for us, and there I was with Hārūn ﷺ, who welcomed me and supplicated good for me. Then we ascended to the sixth heaven, and Jibrīl, peace be upon him, asked for it to be opened. It was said: ‘Who is this?’ He said: ‘Jibrīl.’ It was said: ‘Who is with you?’ He said: ‘Muḥammad.’ It was said: ‘Has he been sent for?’ He said: ‘He has been sent for.’ It was opened for us, and there I was with Mūsā ﷺ, who welcomed me and supplicated good for me. Then we ascended to the seventh heaven, and Jibrīl asked for it to be opened. It was said: ‘Who is this?’ He said: ‘Jibrīl.’ It was said: ‘Who is with you?’ He said: ‘Muḥammad ﷺ.’ It was said: ‘Has he been sent for?’ He said: ‘He has been sent for.’ It was opened for us, and there I was with Ibrāhīm ﷺ, reclining his back against al-Bayt al-Ma‘mūr. Every day seventy thousand angels enter it and never return to it. Then I was taken to Sidrat al-Muntahā, and its leaves were like the ears of elephants, and its fruits were like large jars. When there covered it from the command of Allah what covered it, it changed, and no one from the creation of Allah could describe its beauty. Allah then revealed to me what He revealed, and He obligated upon me fifty prayers every day and night. I descended to Mūsā ﷺ, and he said: ‘What has your Lord obligated upon your Ummah?’ I said: ‘Fifty prayers.’ He said: ‘Return to your Lord and ask Him to lighten it, for your Ummah will not be able to bear that. I tested and experienced the Children of Israel.’ So I returned to my Lord and said: ‘My Lord, lighten it for my Ummah.’ He removed five from me. I returned to Mūsā and said: ‘He removed five from me.’ He said: ‘Your Ummah will not be able to bear that, so return to your Lord and ask Him to lighten it.’ I continued going back and forth between my Lord, Blessed and Most High, and Mūsā, peace be upon him, until He said: ‘O Muḥammad, they are five prayers every day and night. For every prayer there are ten, so that is fifty prayers. Whoever intends a good deed but does not do it, one good deed is written for him; if he does it, ten are written for him. Whoever intends an evil deed but does not do it, nothing is written; if he does it, one evil deed is written.’ Then I descended until I reached Mūsā and informed him. He said: ‘Return to your Lord and ask Him to lighten it.’ The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: ‘I said: I have returned to my Lord until I have become shy before Him.’”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (162).

8612 — Anas ibn Mālik said: On the night when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was taken by night from the Mosque of the Ka‘bah, three individuals came to him before revelation had come to him, while he was sleeping in al-Masjid al-Ḥarām. The first of them said: “Which of them is he?” The middle one said: “He is the best of them.” The last one said: “Take the best of them.” That was that night, and he did not see them again until they came to him on another night, while his heart was seeing, though his eyes slept and his heart did not sleep; and likewise the Prophets, their eyes sleep but their hearts do not sleep. They did not speak to him until they carried him and placed him by the well of Zamzam. Jibrīl took charge of him from among them and split from his throat to his lower chest until he finished his chest and abdomen. He washed him with Zamzam water by his hand until he purified his inside. Then he brought a basin of gold containing a vessel of gold filled with faith and wisdom, and he filled his chest and his throat veins with it, then closed it. Then he ascended with him to the lowest heaven and knocked on one of its gates. The people of heaven called out to him: “Who is this?” He said: “Jibrīl.” They said: “Who is with you?” He said: “Muḥammad ﷺ is with me.” They said: “Has he been sent?” He said: “Yes.” They said: “Welcome to him.” The people of heaven rejoiced at him, and the people of heaven do not know what Allah intends for him on earth until He informs them. He found Ādam in the lowest heaven, and Jibrīl said to him: “This is your father, so greet him with salām.” He greeted him, and Ādam returned the greeting and said: “Welcome to my son; what an excellent son you are.” Then in the lowest heaven there were two rivers flowing. He said: “What are these two rivers, O Jibrīl?” He said: “These are the source of the Nile and Euphrates.” Then he took him onward in heaven, and there was another river upon which was a palace of pearl and emerald. He struck it with his hand, and it was fragrant musk. He said: “What is this, O Jibrīl?” He said: “This is al-Kawthar, which your Lord has stored for you.” Then he ascended with him to the second heaven, and the angels said to him the same as the first had said: “Who is this?” He said: “Jibrīl.” They said: “Who is with you?” He said: “Muḥammad ﷺ.” They said: “Has he been sent to?” He said: “Yes.” They said: “Welcome to him.” Then he ascended with him to the third heaven, and they said to him the same as the first and second. Then he ascended with him to the fourth, and they said the same. Then he ascended with him to the fifth heaven, and they said the same. Then he ascended with him to the sixth heaven, and they said the same. Then he ascended with him to the seventh heaven, and they said the same. In every heaven there were Prophets whom he named. I remembered among them: Idrīs in the second, Hārūn in the fourth, another in the fifth whose name I did not preserve, Ibrāhīm in the sixth, and Mūsā in the seventh, due to the virtue of Allah speaking to him. Mūsā said: “My Lord, I did not think that anyone would be raised above me.” Then He took him above that to what none knows except Allah, until he came to Sidrat al-Muntahā, and the Almighty, Lord of Might, drew near and descended until he was at the distance of two bow-lengths or nearer. Allah revealed among what He revealed to him: fifty prayers upon your Ummah every day and night. Then he descended until he reached Mūsā, and Mūsā detained him and said: “O Muḥammad, what covenant did your Lord give you?” He said: “He gave me the covenant of fifty prayers every day and night.” He said: “Your Ummah cannot bear that, so return and let your Lord lighten it for you and for them.” The Prophet ﷺ turned to Jibrīl as though consulting him about that, and Jibrīl indicated to him: “Yes, if you wish.” He took him up to the Almighty, and he said, while still in his place: “My Lord, lighten it for us, for my Ummah cannot bear this.” He removed ten prayers from him. Then he returned to Mūsā, who detained him, and Mūsā continued sending him back to his Lord until they became five prayers. Then Mūsā detained him at the five and said: “O Muḥammad, by Allah, I urged the Children of Israel, my people, regarding something less than this, but they weakened and abandoned it. Your Ummah is weaker in bodies, hearts, bodies, sight, and hearing, so return and let your Lord lighten it for you.” Each time, the Prophet ﷺ would turn to Jibrīl for his counsel, and Jibrīl did not dislike that. He raised him at the fifth, and he said: “My Lord, my Ummah are weak in their bodies, hearts, hearing, and bodies, so lighten it for us.” The Almighty said: “O Muḥammad.” He said: “At Your service and obedience.” He said: “The word is not changed with Me, as I prescribed it for you in the Mother of the Book. Every good deed is multiplied tenfold, so they are fifty in the Mother of the Book, though they are five upon you.” He returned to Mūsā, who said: “What did you do?” He said: “He lightened it for us and gave us tenfold for every good deed.” Mūsā said: “By Allah, I urged the Children of Israel regarding less than that and they abandoned it. Return to your Lord and let Him lighten it for you also.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “O Mūsā, by Allah, I have become shy before my Lord because of how often I went back to Him.” He said: “Then descend in the name of Allah.” He said: He woke up while he was in al-Masjid al-Ḥarām.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7517) and Muslim (162), and the wording is al-Bukhārī’s.

8613 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “When my Lord caused me to ascend, I passed by people who had nails with which they were scratching their faces and chests. I said: ‘Who are these, O Jibrīl?’ He said: ‘These are the ones who ate the flesh of people and attacked their honor.’”
 Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4878) and Aḥmad (3340).

8614 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “On the night I was taken by night, I passed by people whose lips were being cut with shears of fire. I said: ‘Who are these?’ They said: ‘Preachers from the people of the world who used to command people to righteousness and forget themselves, while they recited the Book. Do they not understand?’”
 Authentic: Narrated by Abū Ya‘lā (4069) and al-Bayhaqī in Shu‘ab al-Īmān (4965).

8615 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Prophet ﷺ was brought al-Burāq on the night he was taken by night, saddled and bridled for him to ride, but it became difficult for him. Jibrīl said to it: “What makes you do this? By Allah, no one more honored by Allah than him has ever ridden you.” Then it flowed with sweat.
 Authentic: Narrated by ‘Abd al-Razzāq in his Tafsīr (1533), and from him Imam Aḥmad (12672), and al-Tirmidhī (3131), who graded it good.
 His saying “farfaḍḍa” means: it flowed.

8616 — Abū Hurayrah said: The Prophet ﷺ said: “When I was taken by night, I met Mūsā, peace be upon him, and he was a man — I think he said: tall and lean — with straight hair, as though he were from the men of Shanū’ah. He said: I met ‘Īsā, and the Prophet ﷺ described him: he was of medium height, reddish, as though he had just come out of a dīmās, meaning a bathhouse. He said: I saw Ibrāhīm, may the prayers of Allah be upon him, and I am the child of his who most resembles him. He said: I was brought two vessels, one containing milk and the other containing wine. It was said to me: Take whichever you wish. I took the milk and drank it. It was said to me: You have been guided to the fiṭrah, or you have attained the fiṭrah. If you had taken the wine, your Ummah would have gone astray.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3437) and Muslim (168).
His saying “rab‘ah” means a man of medium height, neither tall nor short.
 His saying “dīmās”: al-Jawharī said in his Ṣiḥāḥ regarding this ḥadīth that the phrase “as though he had come out of a dīmās” refers to his freshness and the abundance of brightness in his face, as though he had come out from a sheltered place, because he said in describing him: “as though his head was dripping water.”
 In al-Bukhārī (4709), through another route, it states: Abū Hurayrah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was brought on the night of the Isrā’, at Īliyā’, two cups: one of wine and one of milk. He looked at them and took the milk. Jibrīl said: “Praise be to Allah who guided you to the fiṭrah. Had you taken the wine, your Ummah would have gone astray.”

8617 — Ibn ‘Abbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said: “On the night I was taken by night, I saw Mūsā as a dark-complexioned, tall, sturdy man, as though he were from the men of Shanū’ah. I saw ‘Īsā as a man of medium stature and build, inclined to reddishness and whiteness, with straight hair. And I saw Mālik, the keeper of the Fire, and the Dajjāl, among the signs that Allah showed him.” Allah Most High said: “So do not be in doubt concerning meeting him” [al-Sajdah: 23].
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3239) and Muslim (165), and the wording is al-Bukhārī’s.
His saying “ṭuwāl” means tall; both forms are used in Arabic.
 His saying “ja‘d” here refers to the compactness and firmness of the body, not curly hair.
 His saying “Shanū’ah” refers to a known tribe in Yemen; from it is Azd Shanū’ah, a clan from Yemen.

8618 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: They mentioned the Dajjāl, and someone present said: “It is written between his eyes: disbeliever.” Ibn ‘Abbās said: “I did not hear him say that, but he said: ‘As for Ibrāhīm, look at your companion. As for Mūsā, he was a dark-complexioned, sturdy man upon a red camel bridled with palm fiber; it is as though I can see him descending into the valley reciting the talbiyah.’”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (166:270).

8619 — Ibn ‘Abbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed by Wādī al-Azraq and said: “What valley is this?” They said: “This is Wādī al-Azraq.” He said: “It is as though I can see Mūsā, peace be upon him, descending from the mountain pass, raising his voice to Allah with the talbiyah.” Then he came to the mountain pass of Harshā and said: “What mountain pass is this?” They said: “The mountain pass of Harshā.” He said: “It is as though I can see Yūnus ibn Mattā, peace be upon him, upon a red, sturdy she-camel, wearing a wool cloak, the halter of his camel made of palm fiber, while he is reciting the talbiyah.”
 Ibn Ḥanbal said in his narration: Hushaym said: meaning palm fiber, “while he is reciting the talbiyah.” Thus it appears in the Musnad.
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (166). His saying “al-ju’ār” means raising the voice and calling out. Muslim also narrated it through another route, in which Ibn ‘Abbās said: “We traveled with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ between Makkah and Medina,” and in it also: “The halter of his she-camel was palm fiber, khulbah.”

8620 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “When it was the night I was taken by night, and I awoke in Makkah, I became distressed about my affair, and I knew that the people would deny me.” So he sat apart, saddened. He said: The enemy of Allah, Abū Jahl, passed by and came until he sat with him and said mockingly: “Did anything happen?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Yes.” He said: “What?” He said: “I was taken by night tonight.” He said: “To where?” He said: “To Bayt al-Maqdis.” He said: “Then you woke up among us?” He said: “Yes.” He did not show that he was denying him, fearing that he would deny the report if he called his people to him. He said: “What do you think, if I call your people, will you tell them what you told me?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Yes.” He said: “Come, O Banū Ka‘b ibn Lu’ayy!” The gatherings stirred and they came until they sat with the two of them. He said: “Tell your people what you told me.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I was taken by night tonight.” They said: “To where?” He said: “To Bayt al-Maqdis.” They said: “Then you woke up among us?” He said: “Yes.” Some clapped, and some placed their hands on their heads, out of amazement at what they claimed was a lie. They said: “Can you describe the mosque to us?” Among the people were those who had traveled to that land and seen the mosque. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I began describing it, and I continued describing it until some of the description became unclear to me.” He said: “Then the mosque was brought while I was looking until it was placed near the house of ‘Aqīl, and I described it while looking at it.” He said: “Along with this there was a description that I did not preserve.” He said: The people said: “As for the description, by Allah, he has spoken accurately.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (2819), al-Bazzār — Kashf al-Astār (56), and al-Ṭabarānī (12782).

8621 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “On the night in which I was taken by night, a pleasant fragrance came upon me. I said: ‘O Jibrīl, what is this pleasant fragrance?’ He said: ‘This is the fragrance of the hairdresser of Pharaoh’s daughter and her children.’ I said: ‘What was her story?’ He said: ‘One day, while she was combing the hair of Pharaoh’s daughter, the comb fell from her hand, and she said: In the name of Allah. Pharaoh’s daughter said to her: My father? She said: No, but my Lord and the Lord of your father is Allah. She said: Shall I inform him of that? She said: Yes. So she informed him, and he summoned her and said: O so-and-so, do you have a lord other than me? She said: Yes, my Lord and your Lord is Allah. He ordered a copper cauldron to be heated, then ordered that she and her children be thrown into it. She said to him: I have a request from you. He said: What is your request? She said: I would like you to gather my bones and the bones of my children in one cloth and bury us. He said: That is your right upon us. He ordered her children to be thrown in before her, one by one, until it came to a nursing infant of hers, and it was as though she hesitated because of him. He said: O my mother, plunge in, for the punishment of this world is easier than the punishment of the Hereafter. So she plunged in.’”
 Ibn ‘Abbās said: Four infants spoke: ‘Īsā ibn Maryam, peace be upon him; the companion of Jurayj; the witness of Yūsuf; and the son of the hairdresser of Pharaoh’s daughter.
 Authentic: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (2821).

8622 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: The Prophet ﷺ was taken by night to Bayt al-Maqdis, then returned that same night and told them about his journey, the sign of Bayt al-Maqdis, and their camel. Some people said — Ḥasan said: “Are we to believe Muḥammad in what he says?” — and they apostatized as disbelievers, so Allah struck their necks along with Abū Jahl. Abū Jahl said: “Muḥammad frightens us with the tree of Zaqqūm! Bring dates and butter and eat zaqqūm.” He saw the Dajjāl in his actual form, with the vision of the eye, not a dream vision; and he saw ‘Īsā, Mūsā, and Ibrāhīm, may Allah’s prayers be upon them. The Prophet ﷺ was asked about the Dajjāl, and he said: “He is white and bright — Ḥasan said: He said: I saw him as huge-bodied, white and bright — one of his eyes protruding, as if it were a shining star, and the hair of his head like the branches of a tree. I saw ‘Īsā as a young man, white, with curly hair, sharp sight, and a lean body. I saw Mūsā as dark brown, with much hair — Ḥasan said: hair — and a strong build. I looked at Ibrāhīm, and I did not look at any limb of his except that I saw it resembling mine, as though he were your companion. Jibrīl, peace be upon him, said: Greet Mālik with salām, so I greeted him.”
 Good: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (3546) and Abū Ya‘lā (2720). His saying “al-faylamānī” is attributed to “faylam,” meaning one who is great and massive in body. His saying “al-aqmar” means white. His saying “al-irb” means limb.

8623 — Ibn ‘Abbās said regarding the saying of Allah Most High: “And We did not make the vision which We showed you except as a trial for the people” [al-Isrā’: 60]: It was a vision of the eye that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was shown on the night he was taken by night to Bayt al-Maqdis. He said regarding His saying: “And the cursed tree in the Qur’an” [al-Isrā’: 60]: It is the tree of Zaqqūm.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3888). It was also said that this vision has no connection to the Isrā’, because the Isrā’ occurred while awake; therefore ‘Ikrimah interpreted the vision in this verse as the entry into al-Masjid al-Ḥarām, and the trial as the prevention at al-Ḥudaybiyah. This was mentioned by al-Qurṭubī in al-Mufhim (1/385).

8624 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: On the night the Prophet of Allah ﷺ was taken by night, he entered Paradise and heard a faint sound at one side of it. He said: “O Jibrīl, what is this?” He said: “This is Bilāl the mu’adhdhin.” When the Prophet of Allah ﷺ came to the people, he said: “Bilāl has succeeded; I saw for him such-and-such.” He said: He met Mūsā, peace be upon him, who welcomed him and said: “Welcome to the unlettered Prophet.” He said: “Who is this, O Jibrīl?” He said: “This is Mūsā, peace be upon him.” He continued and met ‘Īsā, who welcomed him. He said: “Who is this, O Jibrīl?” He said: “This is ‘Īsā.” He continued and met a venerable, awe-inspiring old man, who welcomed him and greeted him with salām; all of them greeted him with salām. He said: “Who is this, O Jibrīl?” He said: “This is your father Ibrāhīm.” He then looked into the Fire and saw people eating carcasses. He said: “Who are these, O Jibrīl?” He said: “These are those who eat the flesh of people.” He also saw a reddish, blue-eyed, curly-haired, disheveled man, and when he saw him, he said: “Who is this, O Jibrīl?” He said: “This is the hamstringer of the she-camel.”
 He said: When the Prophet ﷺ entered al-Masjid al-Aqṣā, he stood to pray, then turned and saw all the Prophets praying with him. When he finished, two cups were brought, one on the right and one on the left. In one was milk and in the other honey. He took the milk and drank from it, and the one who was with the cup said: “You have attained the fiṭrah.”
 Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2324). The word “al-wajs” means a faint sound.

8625 — Jābir narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “The Prophets were shown to me. Mūsā was a lean, tall man, as though he were from the men of Shanū’ah. I saw ‘Īsā ibn Maryam, peace be upon him, and the person I have seen who most resembled him was ‘Urwah ibn Mas‘ūd. I saw Ibrāhīm, may Allah’s prayers be upon him, and the one I have seen who most resembled him was your companion,” meaning himself. “And I saw Jibrīl, peace be upon him, and the one I have seen who most resembled him was Diḥyah.” In another narration: “Diḥyah ibn Khalīfah.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (167).

8626 — Zirr ibn Ḥubaysh said: I came upon Ḥudhayfah ibn al-Yamān while he was narrating about the night Muḥammad ﷺ was taken by night, and he was saying: “Then I set out — or we set out — until we came to Bayt al-Maqdis.” But they did not enter it. He said: I said: “Rather, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered it that night and prayed in it.” He said: “What is your name, O bald one? I recognize your face but do not know your name.” I said: “I am Zirr ibn Ḥubaysh.” He said: “What is your evidence that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed in it that night?” I said: “The Qur’an informs me of that.” He said: “Whoever speaks with the Qur’an has prevailed. Recite.” So I recited: “Glory be to the One who took His servant by night from al-Masjid al-Ḥarām…” [al-Isrā’: 1]. He said: “I did not find in it that he prayed there.” He said: “O bald one, do you find that he prayed there?” I said: “No.” He said: “By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not pray there that night. Had he prayed there, prayer there would have been prescribed upon you just as prayer at the Ancient House was prescribed upon you. By Allah, they did not leave al-Burāq until the gates of heaven were opened for them, and they saw Paradise, the Fire, and the promise of the Hereafter altogether, then they returned along the same route by which they had begun.” He said: Then he laughed until I saw his molars.
 He said: “They narrate that he tied it — so that it would flee from him?! Rather, the Knower of the unseen and the witnessed had subjected it to him.” He said: I said: “Abū ‘Abdullah, what sort of animal was al-Burāq?” He said: “A tall white animal; its stride was as far as the eye could see.”
 Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3147), Imam Aḥmad (23285), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (45).
As for Ḥudhayfah’s denial that the Prophet ﷺ prayed in Bayt al-Maqdis, it was based on his own reasoning. Otherwise, it is established in the ḥadīths of Ibn Mas‘ūd, Anas, Abū Hurayrah, and others that he did pray there, and the one who affirms is given precedence over the one who negates.
 As for his saying, “If the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had prayed there, it would have become obligatory upon his Ummah to come to that place and pray there as he ﷺ did,” there is no proof in that for Ḥudhayfah, because the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would come to places and pray in them, yet coming to them and praying in them was not prescribed upon us. For more, see Mushkil al-Āthār (12/544) by al-Ṭaḥāwī.

8627 — Buraydah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “When we reached Bayt al-Maqdis, Jibrīl indicated with his finger, pierced the stone with it, and tied al-Burāq to it.”
 Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3132), who graded it good, and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (47) and al-Ḥākim (2/360).
 In the wording of Ibn Ḥibbān: “Jibrīl pierced the rock with his finger.”

8628 — Shaddād ibn Aws said: We said: “O Messenger of Allah, how were you taken by night?” He said: “I prayed the night prayer in Makkah after darkness had set in, and Jibrīl, peace be upon him, came to me with a white animal, larger than a donkey and smaller than a mule. He said: ‘Ride.’ It became difficult for me, so he twisted its ear, then carried me upon it. It set off swiftly with us, its hoof landing wherever its sight reached, until we reached a land of palm trees. He made me dismount and said: ‘Pray.’ So I prayed. Then we rode, and he said: ‘Do you know where you prayed?’ I said: ‘Allah knows best.’ He said: ‘You prayed in Yathrib; you prayed in Ṭaybah.’ It set off swiftly with us, its hoof landing wherever its sight reached. Then we reached a land, and he said: ‘Dismount.’ I dismounted, then he said: ‘Pray.’ So I prayed. Then we rode, and he said: ‘Do you know where you prayed?’ I said: ‘Allah knows best.’ He said: ‘You prayed in Madyan; you prayed near the tree of Mūsā, peace be upon him.’ Then it set off swiftly with us, its hoof landing wherever its sight reached. Then we reached a land in which palaces appeared to us. He said: ‘Dismount.’ I dismounted, and he said: ‘Pray.’ So I prayed. Then we rode. He said: ‘Do you know where you prayed?’ I said: ‘Allah knows best.’ He said: ‘You prayed in Bethlehem, where ‘Īsā, peace be upon him, the Messiah son of Maryam, was born.’ Then he took me until we entered the city from its southern gate. He came to the qiblah of the mosque and tied his animal there. We entered the mosque through a gate where the sun and moon incline. I prayed in the mosque wherever Allah willed. I was overcome by extreme thirst, and two vessels were brought to me: one containing milk and the other honey. Both were sent to me. I considered between them, then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, guided me, so I took the milk and drank until I struck my forehead with it. Before me was an old man reclining on his cushion, and he said: ‘Your companion has taken the fiṭrah; indeed, he is guided.’ Then he took me until we came to the valley in the city, and behold, Hell was being unveiled like carpets. I said: ‘O Messenger of Allah, how did you find it?’ He said: ‘Like a hot, black spring.’ Then he returned with me, and we passed by a caravan of Quraysh at such-and-such place. They had lost a camel, and so-and-so had gathered it. I greeted them, and some of them said: ‘This is the voice of Muḥammad.’ Then I came to my Companions before dawn in Makkah, and Abū Bakr came to me and said: ‘O Messenger of Allah, where were you tonight? I looked for you in your place.’ He said: ‘You knew that I came to Bayt al-Maqdis tonight.’ He said: ‘O Messenger of Allah, it is a month’s journey, so describe it to me.’ He said: A path was opened for me, as though I were looking into it; he did not ask me about anything except that I informed him of it. Abū Bakr said: ‘I bear witness that you are the Messenger of Allah.’ The polytheists said: ‘Look at Ibn Abī Kabshah; he claims that he came to Bayt al-Maqdis tonight.’ He said: ‘Among the signs of what I say to you is that I passed by a caravan of yours at such-and-such place. They had lost a camel, and so-and-so gathered it. Their route is that they will stop at such-and-such, then such-and-such, and they will come to you on such-and-such day, led by a dark camel carrying a black covering and two black sacks.’ When that day came, the people looked out waiting, until it was near midday, and then the caravan approached, led by that camel which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had described.”
 Good: Narrated by al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (2/355–357).

8629 — ‘Ā’ishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, said: When the Prophet ﷺ was taken by night to al-Masjid al-Aqṣā, the people began speaking about it in the morning. Some of those who had believed in him and affirmed him apostatized, and they went with that to Abū Bakr. They said: “What do you think of your companion? He claims that he was taken by night to Bayt al-Maqdis.” He said: “Did he say that?” They said: “Yes.” He said: “If he said that, then he has spoken the truth.” They said: “Do you believe him that he went tonight to Bayt al-Maqdis and returned before morning?” He said: “Yes, I believe him in what is farther than that. I believe him regarding news from the heaven in the morning or evening.” For that reason Abū Bakr was called al-Ṣiddīq.
 Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/62), and through him al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (2/360–361).

8630 — Jābir ibn ‘Abdullah heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say: “When Quraysh denied me, I stood in al-Ḥijr, and Allah made Bayt al-Maqdis clear to me, so I began informing them of its signs while I was looking at it.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3886) and Muslim (170).

8631 — Abū Hurayrah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I saw myself in al-Ḥijr while Quraysh were asking me about my night journey. They asked me about things in Bayt al-Maqdis that I had not memorized, and I became distressed with a distress unlike any I had ever felt. Then Allah raised it up for me so that I could look at it, and they did not ask me about anything except that I informed them of it. I also saw myself among a group of Prophets. Mūsā was standing and praying; he was a lean, sturdy man, as though he were from the men of Shanū’ah. ‘Īsā ibn Maryam, peace be upon him, was standing and praying; the person who most resembled him was ‘Urwah ibn Mas‘ūd al-Thaqafī. Ibrāhīm, peace be upon him, was standing and praying; the one who most resembled him was your companion,” meaning himself. “Then the prayer time came, and I led them in prayer. When I finished the prayer, someone said: ‘O Muḥammad, this is Mālik, the keeper of the Fire, so greet him with salām.’ I turned toward him, and he began by greeting me with salām.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (172).

8632 — ‘Abdullah said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was taken by night, he was brought to Sidrat al-Muntahā. It is in the sixth heaven. To it ends whatever ascends from the earth and is taken from it, and to it ends whatever descends from above it and is taken from it. He said regarding: “When there covered the lote tree that which covered it” [al-Najm: 16]: “Moths of gold.” He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was given three things: he was given the five prayers, he was given the concluding verses of Sūrat al-Baqarah, and the major destructive sins were forgiven for those of his Ummah who did not associate anything with Allah.
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (173).

8633 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said: “Sidrat al-Muntahā was raised for me in the seventh heaven. Its fruits were like the jars of Hajar, and its leaves were like the ears of elephants. From its trunk emerge two outward rivers and two inward rivers. I said: ‘O Jibrīl, what are these?’ He said: ‘As for the inward rivers, they are in Paradise, and as for the outward rivers, they are the Nile and the Euphrates.’”
 Authentic: Narrated by ‘Abd al-Razzāq in his Tafsīr, and from him Imam Aḥmad (12673).
al-Qurṭubī said in al-Mufhim (1/394): The ḥadīth of Anas indicates that the lote tree is in the seventh heaven or above it, due to his saying, “Then I was taken to the lote tree,” after he had sought entry into the seventh heaven, it was opened for him, and he entered. In the ḥadīth of ‘Abdullah, it is in the sixth heaven, and there is undoubtedly a conflict here. What is in the ḥadīth of Anas is more authentic, and it is the view of most scholars. This is also required by its description as that at which the knowledge of every near angel and every sent Prophet ends, as Ka‘b said, and he said: what lies beyond it is unseen, known only to Allah. Likewise, al-Khalīl ibn Aḥmad said that the souls of the martyrs end there. Ibn ‘Abbās said: It is to the right of the Throne. Also, the ḥadīth of Anas is marfū‘, while the ḥadīth of ‘Abdullah is mawqūf from his own statement, and the connected marfū‘ report is given precedence. End quote.
 al-Nawawī attempted to reconcile the two ḥadīths, and Ibn Ḥajar followed him, by saying that its root is in the sixth heaven and its branches in the seventh, but this is questionable.

8634 — Jābir ibn ‘Abdullah said: The Prophet ﷺ used to present himself to the people at the standing place, saying: “Is there any man who will take me to his people? For Quraysh have prevented me from conveying the speech of my Lord.”
 A man from Hamdān came to him, and he said: “Who are you from?” The man said: “From Hamdān.” He said: “Do your people have strength and protection?” He said: “Yes.” Then the man feared that his people would break their pledge to him, so he came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said: “I will go to them and inform them, then come to you next year.” He said: “Yes.” So he departed, and the delegation of the Anṣār came in Rajab.
 Authentic: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (15192), and this wording is his; Abū Dāwūd (4734), Ibn Mājah (201), and al-Tirmidhī (2925).

8635 — Jābir said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained in Makkah for ten years, following the people in their camps at ‘Ukāẓ and Majannah, and during the seasons at Minā, saying: “Who will shelter me? Who will support me so that I may convey the message of my Lord — and he will have Paradise?” A man would even come from Yemen or from Egypt — so he said — and his people would come to him and say: “Beware of the young man of Quraysh; do not let him tempt you.” He would walk among their men while they pointed at him with their fingers, until Allah sent us to him from Yathrib. We sheltered him and believed him. A man from us would go out, believe in him, and he would recite the Qur’an to him; then he would return to his family and they would embrace Islam because of his Islam, until there remained no house among the houses of the Anṣār except that in it was a group of Muslims openly manifesting Islam. Then they all consulted together, and we said: “How long will we leave the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to be driven through the mountains of Makkah and to live in fear?” So seventy men from us traveled to him until they came to him during the season, and we made an appointment with him at the mountain pass of al-‘Aqabah. We gathered with him, one man and two men at a time, until we all came together. We said: “O Messenger of Allah, upon what shall we pledge allegiance to you?” He said: “You shall pledge allegiance to me upon hearing and obeying in energy and reluctance, spending in hardship and ease, commanding what is right and forbidding what is wrong, speaking for Allah without fearing the blame of any blamer, and supporting me by protecting me, when I come to you, from what you protect yourselves, your wives, and your children from — and you will have Paradise.” He said: We stood to him and pledged allegiance. As‘ad ibn Zurārah, one of the youngest of them, took his hand and said: “Slowly, O people of Yathrib. We did not strike the livers of camels except knowing that he is the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Taking him out today means separating from all the Arabs, the killing of your best men, and that swords will bite you. If you are a people who will be patient upon that, then your reward is with Allah. But if you are a people who fear cowardice from yourselves, then make that clear now, for that is more excusable for you before Allah.” They said: “Move away from us, O As‘ad. By Allah, we will never abandon this pledge, nor will we ever ask to be released from it.” He said: We stood to him and pledged allegiance. He took conditions from us and stipulated them, and in return he gave us Paradise.
 Good: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (14456), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7012) and al-Ḥākim (2/624).

8636 — Ash‘ath said: An elderly man from Banū Mālik ibn Kinānah narrated to me: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the market of Dhū al-Majāz, moving through it and saying: “O people, say: There is no god but Allah, and you will succeed.” He said: Abū Jahl was throwing dust upon him and saying: “O people, do not let this man deceive you away from your religion. He only wants you to leave your gods and leave al-Lāt and al-‘Uzzā.” He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not turn toward him. He said: We said: “Describe the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to us.” He said: He was wearing two red cloaks, of medium stature, with much flesh, handsome in face, intensely black hair, very white, and with full hair.
 Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (16603, 23192).

8637 — Rabī‘ah ibn ‘Ibād al-Dīlī said: I saw Abū Lahab at ‘Ukāẓ following the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and saying: “O people, this man has gone astray, so do not let him lead you away from the gods of your fathers.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would move away from him, while he followed behind him, and we were following as boys. It is as though I can still see him: cross-eyed, with two braided locks, the whitest and most handsome of people.
 Authentic: Narrated by ‘Abdullah ibn Aḥmad in his father’s Musnad (16020), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (4588).
 ‘Ukāẓ, Dhū al-Majāz, and Majannah were all markets in the pre-Islamic period.

8638 — Rabī‘ah ibn ‘Ibād al-Dīlī, who had lived in the pre-Islamic period and then embraced Islam, said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with my own eyes in the market of Dhū al-Majāz, saying: “O people, say: There is no god but Allah, and you will succeed,” while entering its pathways, and the people gathered around him. I did not see anyone saying anything while he did not remain silent; he kept saying: “O people, say: There is no god but Allah, and you will succeed.” Behind him was a cross-eyed man with a bright face and two braided locks, saying: “He is a Ṣābi’ and a liar.” I said: “Who is this?” They said: “Muḥammad ibn ‘Abdullah, and he is mentioning prophethood.” I said: “Who is this man who is calling him a liar?” They said: “His uncle, Abū Lahab.” I said: “You were young that day.” He said: “No, by Allah, I understood that day.”
 Good: Narrated by ‘Abdullah ibn Aḥmad (16023), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (4582), and al-Ḥākim (1/15).

8639 — Rabī‘ah ibn ‘Ibād narrated from his father, who said: I was a young boy with my father at Minā, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was saying: “O Banū so-and-so, I am the Messenger of Allah to you. He commands you to worship Allah and not associate anything with Him, to renounce those rivals that you worship besides Him, to shelter me, to believe me, and to protect me until I make clear from Allah what He has sent me with.” He said: Behind him was a cross-eyed, bright-faced man with two braided locks, wearing an Adeni garment. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ finished his words and what he was calling to, that man would say: “O Banū so-and-so, this man only calls you to strip al-Lāt and al-‘Uzzā from your necks, and your allies from the jinn of Banū Mālik ibn Aqyas, for what he has brought of innovation and misguidance. So do not obey him and do not listen to him.”
 He said: I said to my father: “My father, who is this man who follows him and refutes what he says?” He said: “This is his uncle, ‘Abd al-‘Uzzā ibn ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib, Abū Lahab.”
 Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq in his Sīrah, Ibn Hishām (1/423).

8640 — Ṭāriq ibn ‘Abdullah al-Muḥāribī said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the market of Dhū al-Majāz wearing a red garment and saying: “O people, say: There is no god but Allah, and you will succeed.” A man was following him, throwing stones at him, having bloodied his heels and ankles, while saying: “O people, do not obey him, for he is a liar.” I said: “Who is this?” It was said: “This is the young man of Banū ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib.” I said: “Who is this man following him and throwing stones at him?” He said: “This is ‘Abd al-‘Uzzā, Abū Lahab.”
 Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6562), al-Nasā’ī (8/55), and al-Ḥākim (2/611–612).

8641 — ‘Ā’ishah said: The Day of Bu‘āth was a day that Allah had brought forward for His Messenger ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived when their leaders had become divided, their noblemen had been killed, and they had been wounded. Allah brought it forward for His Messenger ﷺ as a cause for their entering Islam.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3777).
