 Bu‘āth is the place where the war between al-Aws and al-Khazraj occurred. The leader of al-Aws, Ḥuḍayr, the father of Usayd ibn Ḥuḍayr, was killed in it; and the leader of al-Khazraj, ‘Amr ibn al-Nu‘mān al-Bayāḍī, was also killed in it. Victory was first for al-Khazraj, then for al-Aws. This took place five years before the Prophet ﷺ arrived.
 Her saying “sarawāt” is the plural of “sarāh,” meaning a nobleman.

8642 — Maḥmūd ibn Labīd, the brother of Banū ‘Abd al-Ashhal, said: When Abū al-Ḥaysar Anas ibn Rāfi‘ came to Makkah with young men from Banū ‘Abd al-Ashhal, among them Iyās ibn Mu‘ādh, seeking an alliance from Quraysh against their people from al-Khazraj, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard of them, came to them, and sat with them. He said to them: “Would you like something better than what you came for?” They said: “What is that?” He said: “I am the Messenger of Allah. He sent me to the servants, calling them to worship Allah and not associate anything with Him, and a Book has been revealed to me.” Then he mentioned Islam and recited the Qur’an to them. Iyās ibn Mu‘ādh, who was a young boy, said: “O people, by Allah, this is better than what you came for.” Abū al-Ḥaysar Anas ibn Rāfi‘ took a handful of gravel from the plain and struck the face of Iyās ibn Mu‘ādh with it. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then rose from them, and they returned to Medina. Then the battle of Bu‘āth occurred between al-Aws and al-Khazraj. He said: Soon after, Iyās ibn Mu‘ādh died. Maḥmūd ibn Labīd said: Those of my people who were present at his death informed me that they continued hearing him declaring Allah’s oneness, magnifying Him, praising Him, and glorifying Him until he died. They had no doubt that he died as a Muslim, for he had felt Islam enter his heart in that gathering when he heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ what he heard.
 Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (23619), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (1/251), and al-Ḥākim (3/180–181).

8643 — Ibn Isḥāq said: ‘Āṣim ibn ‘Umar ibn Qatādah narrated to me from elders of his people, who said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ met them, he said to them: “Who are you?” They said: “A group from al-Khazraj.” He said: “From the allies of the Jews?” They said: “Yes.” He said: “Will you not sit so that I may speak to you?” They said: “Yes.” They sat with him, and he called them to Allah, Mighty and Majestic, presented Islam to them, and recited the Qur’an to them. He said: Among what Allah had prepared for them in Islam was that the Jews lived with them in their land, and they were people of Scripture and knowledge, while they were people of shirk and idol worship. They had fought them in their land, and whenever something occurred between them, the Jews would say to them: “A Prophet is now being sent; his time has drawn near. We will follow him and kill you with him as ‘Ād and Iram were killed.” When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ spoke to those men and called them to Allah, some of them said to others: “O people, by Allah, you know that he is the Prophet with whom the Jews have threatened you, so do not let them precede you to him.” They responded to what he called them to, believed him, accepted from him what he presented of Islam, and said: “We have left our people, and no people have between them the enmity and evil that they have. Perhaps Allah will unite them through you. We will go to them, call them to your matter, and present to them what we have responded to from this religion. If Allah unites them upon it, then no man will be dearer than you.” Then they departed from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, returning to their land, having believed and affirmed.
 Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq with his chain, as in Sīrat Ibn Hishām (1/428–429).
Its chain is good because of Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq. As for the unknown identity of the elders, it does not harm the report, because they were a large number who strengthen one another.
Those whom the Messenger of Allah ﷺ met at al-‘Aqabah were six men from al-Khazraj:
As‘ad ibn Zurārah, Abū Umāmah.
‘Awf ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Rifā‘ah.
Rāfi‘ ibn Mālik ibn al-‘Ajlān.
Quṭbah ibn ‘Āmir ibn Ḥadīdah.
‘Uqbah ibn ‘Āmir ibn Nābī.
Jābir ibn ‘Abdullah ibn Ri’āb.
 When they returned to Medina to their people, they mentioned the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to them and called them to Islam until it spread among them. There remained no house among the houses of the Anṣār except that in it there was mention of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Sīrat Ibn Hishām (1/429–430).

8644 — ‘Ubādah ibn al-Ṣāmit, who had witnessed Badr and was one of the naqībs on the night of al-‘Aqabah, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said while a group of his Companions were around him: “Pledge allegiance to me that you will not associate anything with Allah, that you will not steal, that you will not commit fornication, that you will not kill your children, that you will not bring a slander you fabricate between your hands and feet, and that you will not disobey in what is right. Whoever among you fulfills this, his reward is with Allah. Whoever commits any of that and is punished for it in this world, it is an expiation for him. Whoever commits any of that and Allah conceals him, then his matter is with Allah: if He wills, He will pardon him, and if He wills, He will punish him.” So we pledged allegiance to him upon that.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (18) and Muslim (1709).
 ‘Ubādah ibn al-Ṣāmit was one of the naqībs who pledged allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, as he said about himself: “I was among the naqībs who pledged allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” Al-Bukhārī (3893) and Muslim (1709:44).

8645 — Jābir ibn ‘Abdullah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained for ten years following the pilgrims in their camps, during the season, at Majannah and ‘Ukāẓ, and in their camps at Minā, saying: “Who will shelter me? Who will support me so that I may convey the messages of my Lord — and he will have Paradise?” He found no one to support him and shelter him. A man would even travel from Muḍar or from Yemen to his relative, and his people would come to him and say: “Beware of the young man of Quraysh; do not let him tempt you.” He would walk among their camps calling them to Allah, Mighty and Majestic, while they pointed at him with their fingers, until Allah, Mighty and Majestic, sent us to him from Yathrib. A man would come to him, believe in him, and he would recite the Qur’an to him; then he would return to his family, and they would embrace Islam because of his Islam, until there remained no house among the houses of Yathrib except that in it was a group of Muslims openly manifesting Islam. Then Allah sent us, and we consulted together, seventy men from us gathered, and we said: “How long will we leave the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to be driven through the mountains of Makkah and to live in fear?” We traveled until we came to him during the season and arranged to meet him at the mountain pass of al-‘Aqabah. His uncle al-‘Abbās said: “My nephew, I do not know who these people are who have come to you, and I have knowledge of the people of Yathrib.” We gathered with him, one man and two men at a time. When al-‘Abbās looked at our faces, he said: “These are people I do not know; these are young men.” We said: “O Messenger of Allah, upon what shall we pledge allegiance to you?” He said: “You shall pledge allegiance to me upon hearing and obeying in energy and reluctance, upon spending in hardship and ease, upon commanding what is right and forbidding what is wrong, upon speaking for Allah without the blame of any blamer taking hold of you, and upon supporting me when I come to Yathrib by protecting me from what you protect yourselves, your wives, and your children from — and you will have Paradise.” We stood to pledge allegiance to him, and As‘ad ibn Zurārah, the youngest of the seventy, took his hand and said: “Slowly, O people of Yathrib. We did not strike the livers of the riding camels to come to him except knowing that he is the Messenger of Allah. Taking him out today means separating from all the Arabs, the killing of your best men, and that swords will bite you. If you are a people who will be patient upon swords when they touch you, upon the killing of your best men, and upon separating from all the Arabs, then take him, and your reward is with Allah. But if you fear something within yourselves, then leave him, for that is more excusable before Allah.” They said: “O As‘ad ibn Zurārah, remove your hand from us. By Allah, we will not abandon this pledge, nor will we ask to be released from it.” So we stood to him one man at a time. He took from us according to the condition of al-‘Abbās, and in exchange gave us Paradise.
 Good: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (14653), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7012) and al-Ḥākim (2/624–625).

8646 — Jābir ibn ‘Abdullah said: My two maternal uncles attended al-‘Aqabah on my behalf.
 Abū ‘Abdullah — al-Bukhārī — said: Ibn ‘Uyaynah said: One of them was al-Barā’ ibn Ma‘rūr.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Manāqib (3890).

8647 — ‘Abdullah ibn Ka‘b, who used to guide Ka‘b when he became blind, said: I heard Ka‘b ibn Mālik narrating at length about when he remained behind from the Prophet ﷺ during the expedition of Tabūk.
 Ibn Bukayr said in his narration: “I indeed witnessed with the Prophet ﷺ the night of al-‘Aqabah, when we pledged ourselves upon Islam, and I would not prefer to have witnessed Badr instead of it, even though Badr is more remembered among the people than it.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Manāqib (3889).

8648 — Ka‘b ibn Mālik said: We set out among the pilgrims of our people from the polytheists, though we had prayed and gained understanding. With us was al-Barā’ ibn Ma‘rūr, our chief and elder. When we set out on our journey and departed from Medina, al-Barā’ said: “O people, I have formed an opinion, and by Allah I do not know whether you will agree with me or not.” We said: “What is it?” He said: “I think that I should not place this structure behind my back,” meaning the Ka‘bah, “and that I should pray toward it.” We said: “By Allah, we have not heard that our Prophet ﷺ prays except toward Syria, and we do not wish to oppose him.” He said: “I will pray toward it.” We said to him: “But we will not do so.”
 Whenever prayer time came, we prayed toward Syria, while he prayed toward the Ka‘bah, until we arrived in Makkah. We had criticized him for what he did, but he refused except to persist in that. When we arrived in Makkah, he said to me: “My nephew, come with us to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ so that I may ask him about what I did on this journey of mine, for something has entered my heart because I saw you disagreeing with me concerning it.” We went out asking about the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and we did not know him and had not seen him before. We met a man from the people of Makkah and asked him about the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He said: “Do you know him?” We said: “No.” He said: “Do you know al-‘Abbās ibn ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib, his uncle?” We said: “Yes.” We used to know al-‘Abbās, for he often came to us as a merchant. He said: “When you enter the mosque, he is the man sitting with al-‘Abbās.” We entered the mosque, and there was al-‘Abbās sitting, with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sitting with him. We greeted them with salām and then sat with him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to al-‘Abbās: “Do you know these two men, Abū al-Faḍl?” He said: “Yes. This is al-Barā’ ibn Ma‘rūr, the chief of his people, and this is Ka‘b ibn Mālik.” He said: By Allah, I will never forget the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saying: “The poet?” He said: “Yes.”
 Al-Barā’ ibn Ma‘rūr then said to him: “O Prophet of Allah, I set out on this journey after Allah Most High had guided me to Islam. I thought that I should not place this structure behind my back, so I prayed toward it. My companions disagreed with me about that, until something entered my heart about it. What do you think, O Messenger of Allah?” He said: “You had been upon a qiblah, if only you had remained patient upon it.” So al-Barā’ returned to the qiblah of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and prayed with us toward Syria. His family claims that he prayed toward the Ka‘bah until he died, but that is not as they say; we know him better than they do.
 Ka‘b ibn Mālik said: Then we went out for Ḥajj, and we arranged to meet the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at al-‘Aqabah in the middle of the Days of Tashrīq. When we finished the Ḥajj, on the night for which we had arranged to meet the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, we had with us ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Amr ibn Ḥarām, Abū Jābir, one of our leaders and nobles. We took him aside, while we were concealing our affair from the polytheists among our people who were with us. We spoke to him and said: “O Abū Jābir, you are one of our leaders and one of our nobles, and we dislike for you to remain in your present state and become fuel for the Fire tomorrow.” Then we called him to Islam and informed him of our appointment with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at al-‘Aqabah. He embraced Islam, witnessed al-‘Aqabah with us, and became a naqīb.
 He said: We slept that night with our people in our camps. When a third of the night had passed, we left our camps for our appointment with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, slipping away quietly like sandgrouse, concealing ourselves, until we gathered in the mountain pass at al-‘Aqabah. We were seventy-three men, and with us were two women: Nusaybah bint Ka‘b, Umm ‘Umārah, one of the women of Banū Māzin ibn al-Najjār; and Asmā’ bint ‘Amr ibn ‘Adī ibn Nābī, one of the women of Banū Salamah, who was Umm Manī‘.
 He said: We gathered in the mountain pass waiting for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until he came to us, accompanied by al-‘Abbās ibn ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib. At that time, al-‘Abbās was still upon the religion of his people, but he wished to attend the affair of his nephew and secure assurance for him. When he sat, the first to speak was al-‘Abbās ibn ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib. He said: “O people of al-Khazraj” — he said: the Arabs used to refer to this group of the Anṣār as al-Khazraj, both their Khazraj and their Aws — “Muḥammad is from us, as you know. We have protected him from our people, those who share our view concerning him. He is in honor among his people and protection in his land. Yet he has refused except to join you and attach himself to you. If you believe that you will fulfill for him what you have called him to, and protect him from those who oppose him, then you and what you have undertaken are as you are. But if you believe that you will surrender him and abandon him after he has gone out to you, then leave him now, for he is in honor and protection among his people and in his land.” We said to him: “We have heard what you said. Speak, O Messenger of Allah, and take for yourself and your Lord whatever you wish.”
 The Messenger of Allah ﷺ spoke, recited the Qur’an, called to Allah, encouraged Islam, then said: “I pledge with you that you will protect me from what you protect your women and children from.”
 Al-Barā’ ibn Ma‘rūr took his hand and said: “Yes, by the One who sent you with the truth as a Prophet, we will surely protect you from what we protect our families from. So accept our pledge, O Messenger of Allah, for by Allah, we are sons of wars and people of armor; we inherited it elder from elder.”
 Then Abū al-Haytham ibn al-Tayyihān interrupted while al-Barā’ was speaking to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said: “O Messenger of Allah, between us and men there are ties, and we will be cutting them,” meaning the Jews. “Do you think that if we do this and then Allah gives you victory, you will return to your people and leave us?”
 The Messenger of Allah ﷺ smiled, then said: “Rather, blood is blood, and destruction is destruction. I am from you and you are from me. I will fight those you fight and make peace with those you make peace with.”
 Ka‘b said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Bring forth from among you twelve naqībs, so that they may be responsible over their people regarding what is among them.” So they brought forth twelve naqībs: nine from al-Khazraj and three from al-Aws.
 Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq, as in Sīrat Ibn Hishām (1/440–443).
The names of the twelve naqībs:
Abū Umāmah As‘ad ibn Zurārah, from al-Khazraj.
Sa‘d ibn al-Rabī‘ ibn ‘Amr, from al-Khazraj.
‘Abdullah ibn Rawāḥah ibn Tha‘labah, from al-Khazraj.
Rāfi‘ ibn Mālik ibn al-‘Ajlān, from al-Khazraj.
al-Barā’ ibn Ma‘rūr, from al-Khazraj.
‘Abdullah ibn ‘Amr ibn Ḥarām, from al-Khazraj.
‘Ubādah ibn al-Ṣāmit ibn Qays, from al-Khazraj.
Sa‘d ibn ‘Ubādah ibn Dulaym, from al-Khazraj.
al-Mundhir ibn ‘Amr ibn Khunays, from al-Khazraj.
Usayd ibn Ḥuḍayr ibn Simāk, from al-Aws.
Sa‘d ibn Khaythamah ibn al-Ḥārith, from al-Aws.
Rifā‘ah ibn ‘Abd al-Mundhir ibn Zubayr, from al-Aws.

8649 — Abū Mūsā narrated from the Prophet ﷺ that he said: “I saw in a dream that I was migrating from Makkah to a land containing palm trees, and my mind first went to al-Yamāmah or Hajar, but it was Medina, Yathrib. In this dream of mine, I saw that I shook a sword and its front part broke; that represented what befell the believers on the Day of Uḥud. Then I shook it again and it returned better than it had been; that represented what Allah brought of victory and the gathering of the believers. I also saw cows in it — and Allah is good — and they were the group of believers on the Day of Uḥud; and the good was what Allah brought afterward of good and the reward of truthfulness that Allah gave us after the Day of Badr.”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3622) and Muslim (2273:20).

8650 — ‘Ā’ishah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to the Muslims: “I have been shown the land of your migration: a place with palm trees between two lava fields,” meaning the two ḥarrahs. So those who migrated set out toward Medina, and most of those who had migrated to Abyssinia returned to Medina.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3905).
The inhabitants of Medina: Among the inhabitants of Medina were Arabs and Jews. The historical documents do not explicitly establish which of them lived there first, but the geography of the Arabs indicates that Medina was never at any time empty of inhabitants, because it was the nature of the Arabs to move constantly from one place to another in search of water and pasture.
 The Arab tribes in Medina were known as al-Aws and al-Khazraj, and they belonged to the Azd tribe of Yemen. History does not specify exactly when they left Yemen, but given their numbers and their firm establishment in the land of Medina, it is estimated that they migrated there two and a half to three centuries before the Prophet ﷺ was sent. Wars continued between them over control of the cultivated lands of Medina and its leadership until Islam came and united their hearts.
 As for the Jews, no reason appears for their settlement in Medina except that they found in their books and heard from their scholars that the description of the land that would be the place of migration for the Prophet of the end of time was Medina. When the time of the Prophet’s appearance drew near, they went out in search of this land. They reached Taymā’ and found palm trees there, so a group of them settled there. Another group thought it was Khaybar and settled there. The noblest and largest group continued until they saw Yathrib with its palm trees, and they said: “This is the town to which the Prophet will migrate.” Banū al-Naḍīr settled in Buṭḥān, and Banū Qurayẓah and al-Naḍīr settled in Mudhaynīb and Mahzūr. Wafā’ al-Wafā by al-Samhūdī (1/160).
 They were people of crafts and industry, and in a short time they were able to take control of wide agricultural lands. They fortified themselves in their forts out of fear that the Arabs would turn against them, because their tyrannical nature always drove them toward cheating and deception against their neighbors. It is established that they used to threaten their neighbors with the awaited Prophet. But when the Prophet ﷺ was sent, they disbelieved in him, although they recognized him as they recognized their own sons. Razīn narrated from al-Sharqī that the Jews were more than twenty tribes. Wafā’ al-Wafā (1/165).
 The large tribes, however, were Banū al-Naḍīr, whose men numbered seven hundred; Banū Qurayẓah, whose men also numbered seven hundred; and Banū Qaynuqā‘, whose men numbered nine hundred.

8651 — Sa‘d ibn Abī Waqqāṣ said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ visited me during the Farewell Ḥajj because of an illness from which I had nearly died. He mentioned the story of the will, then I said: “O Messenger of Allah, will I be left behind after my Companions?” He said: “You will not be left behind and then do any deed seeking the Face of Allah except that you will increase thereby in rank and elevation. Perhaps you will be left behind so that some people benefit through you and others are harmed through you. O Allah, complete the migration of my Companions and do not turn them back on their heels. But the unfortunate one is Sa‘d ibn Khawlah.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ lamented for him because he died in Makkah.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4409) and Muslim (1628).
 His saying, “You will not be left behind and then do any deed,” means here being left alive for a long life and many righteous deeds.
 His saying, “Perhaps you will be left behind so that some people benefit through you,” is one of the miracles, because he lived until Iraq was conquered, many people entered Allah’s religion, and many disbelievers were harmed through him.
 A migrant’s remaining in Makkah due to necessity and dying there does not nullify the reward of migration. Sa‘d ibn Khawlah was among those who migrated to Abyssinia in the second migration. Mūsā ibn ‘Uqbah mentioned that he was among those who attended Badr and that he died in Makkah during the Farewell Ḥajj, so the Messenger of Allah ﷺ lamented for him because he died in the land from which he had migrated. For this reason, the Prophet ﷺ made a general supplication, saying: “O Allah, complete the migration of my Companions and do not turn them back on their heels,” meaning: do not let death come to them while they are in the land from which they migrated.

8652 — ‘Ā’ishah said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Medina, Abū Bakr and Bilāl fell ill with fever. She said: I entered upon them and said: “My father, how do you feel? Bilāl, how do you feel?” When the fever seized Abū Bakr, he would say:
 Every person wakes in the morning among his family,
 while death is closer than the strap of his sandal.
 And when Bilāl’s fever subsided, he would raise his voice and say:
 Would that I knew whether I shall spend a night
 in a valley with idhkhir and jalīl around me;
 and whether one day I shall return to the waters of Majannah,
 and whether Shāmah and Ṭafīl will appear to me.
 ‘Ā’ishah said: I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him, so he said: “O Allah, make Medina beloved to us as we love Makkah, or even more. Make it healthy, bless us in its ṣā‘ and its mudd, and transfer its fever and place it in al-Juḥfah.”
 Authentic: Narrated by Mālik (14) and al-Bukhārī (3926).

8653 — ‘Ā’ishah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to the Muslims, while he was in Makkah at that time: “I have been shown the land of your migration. I was shown a salty land with palm trees between two lava fields,” meaning the two ḥarrahs. So whoever migrated set out toward Medina after the Messenger of Allah ﷺ mentioned that, and some of those who had migrated to Abyssinia returned to Medina. Abū Bakr prepared to migrate, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him: “Wait calmly, for I hope that permission will be granted to me.” Abū Bakr said: “Do you hope for that, may my father be sacrificed for you?” He said: “Yes.” So Abū Bakr held himself back for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ so that he could accompany him, and he fed two riding camels that he had with him on the leaves of the samur tree for four months.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2297). The word “sabkhah” means a salty, marshy land.

8654 — al-Barā’ ibn ‘Āzib said: The first to come to us were Muṣ‘ab ibn ‘Umayr and Ibn Umm Maktūm. Then ‘Ammār ibn Yāsir and Bilāl, may Allah be pleased with them, came to us.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3924).

8655 — al-Barā’ ibn ‘Āzib said: The first to come to us were Muṣ‘ab ibn ‘Umayr and Ibn Umm Maktūm, and they used to teach the people Qur’an. Then Bilāl, Sa‘d, and ‘Ammār ibn Yāsir came. Then ‘Umar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb came with twenty of the Prophet’s Companions. Then the Prophet ﷺ came. I never saw the people of Medina rejoice over anything as they rejoiced over the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, until even the slave girls were saying: “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has arrived.” He did not arrive until I had recited, “Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High” [al-A‘lā: 1], among sūrahs from the Mufaṣṣal.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3925).

8656 — Umm Salamah said: I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say: “No Muslim is afflicted by a calamity and then says, ‘Indeed we belong to Allah and indeed to Him we return. O Allah, reward me in my calamity and replace it for me with something better than it,’ except that Allah will replace it for him with something better than it.” She said: When Abū Salamah died, I said: “Which Muslim is better than Abū Salamah? The first household to migrate to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” Then I said it, and Allah replaced him for me with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (918).
 Her saying, “the first to migrate,” can be reconciled with the statement of al-Barā’ and the statement of Umm Salamah regarding who first migrated to Medina by saying that Abū Salamah migrated there fleeing with his religion, while Muṣ‘ab ibn ‘Umayr came to Medina as a teacher for the people of Medina after al-‘Aqabah. Thus the absolute priority belongs to Abū Salamah.

8657 — ‘Umar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said: When we wanted to migrate to Medina, I, ‘Ayyāsh ibn Abī Rabī‘ah, and Hishām ibn al-‘Āṣ ibn Wā’il al-Sahmī agreed to meet at al-Tanāḍub, near the pool of Banū Ghifār above Sarif. We said: “Whoever does not arrive there by morning has been detained, so his two companions should go on.” He said: In the morning, I and ‘Ayyāsh ibn Abī Rabī‘ah were at al-Tanāḍub, while Hishām had been detained from us and was put to trial, and he succumbed to the trial. When we arrived in Medina, we stayed among Banū ‘Amr ibn ‘Awf in Qubā’. Abū Jahl ibn Hishām and al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām went out to ‘Ayyāsh ibn Abī Rabī‘ah — he was their cousin and their brother through their mother — until they came to us in Medina, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was still in Makkah. They spoke to him and said: “Your mother has vowed that no comb will touch her head until she sees you, and that she will not shade herself from the sun until she sees you.” So he felt pity for her. I said to him: “O ‘Ayyāsh, by Allah, these people only want to tempt you away from your religion, so beware of them. By Allah, if lice were to harm your mother, she would comb her hair; and if the heat of Makkah became severe upon her, she would seek shade.” He said: “I will fulfill my mother’s oath, and I have wealth there which I will take.” I said: “By Allah, you know that I am among the wealthiest of Quraysh, so you may have half my wealth, but do not go with them.” But he refused except to go out with them. When he refused except to do that, I said to him: “Since you have done what you have done, take this she-camel of mine. It is a noble, docile camel. Stay on its back, and if anything suspicious from the people alarms you, escape on it.” He set out on it with them, until they were on part of the road. Abū Jahl said to him: “My nephew, by Allah, I find my camel too rough. Will you let me ride behind you on your camel?” He said: “Yes.” He made it kneel, and they both made their mounts kneel so he could transfer to it. When they were all on the ground, they attacked him, bound him tightly, tied him up, and brought him into Makkah, where they put him to trial and he succumbed to the trial. He said: We used to say: “Allah will not accept from anyone who has been put to trial any compensation, ransom, or repentance; they were people who knew Allah, then returned to disbelief because of an affliction that struck them.” They used to say that about themselves as well. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Medina, Allah Most High revealed concerning them, and concerning what we said and what they said about themselves: “Say: O My servants who have transgressed against themselves, do not despair of the mercy of Allah. Indeed, Allah forgives all sins. Indeed, He is the Forgiving, the Merciful. And turn in repentance to your Lord and submit to Him before the punishment comes to you, then you will not be helped. And follow the best of what has been revealed to you from your Lord before the punishment comes upon you suddenly while you do not perceive” [al-Zumar: 53–55]. ‘Umar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said: I wrote it with my own hand on a sheet and sent it to Hishām ibn al-‘Āṣ. Hishām ibn al-‘Āṣ said: When it reached me, I began reading it at Dhū Ṭuwā, going up and down with it and not understanding it, until I said: “O Allah, make me understand it.” He said: Allah Most High cast into my heart that it had been revealed concerning us, and concerning what we had been saying about ourselves and what was being said about us. He said: I returned to my camel, mounted it, and caught up with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was in Medina.
 Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, as in Sīrat Ibn Hishām (1/474–476).

8658 — ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Abbās informed him that ‘Abd al-Raḥmān ibn ‘Awf returned to his family while he was at Minā during the last Ḥajj that ‘Umar performed, and he found me. ‘Abd al-Raḥmān said: I said: “O Commander of the Believers, the pilgrimage season gathers the rabble and the common masses. I think you should wait until you arrive in Medina, for it is the land of migration and the Sunnah, and you will be with the people of understanding, the nobles, and those of sound judgment.” ‘Umar said: “I will surely stand in the first place where I stand in Medina.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3928).
 His saying, “I will surely stand in the first place,” means: I will stand as a speaker when I return to Medina instead of doing so at Minā.
 His saying, “the land of migration and the Sunnah,” means that the source of the Sunnah was Medina because the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ were there, and they are the foundation while the people depend on them. This is a shortened ḥadīth whose meaning is not fully clear from this wording. al-Bukhārī narrated it fully in al-I‘tiṣām (7323): Ibn ‘Abbās, may Allah be pleased with them both, said: I used to teach ‘Abd al-Raḥmān ibn ‘Awf to recite. During the last Ḥajj that ‘Umar performed, ‘Abd al-Raḥmān said at Minā: “If only you had seen the Commander of the Believers. A man came to him and said: ‘So-and-so says: If the Commander of the Believers dies, we will pledge allegiance to so-and-so.’ ‘Umar said: ‘I will stand this evening and warn those people who wish to usurp their affair.’ I said: ‘Do not do so, for the season gathers the rabble of the people, and they will dominate your gathering. I fear that they will not place your words in their proper context and that every carrier will carry them off. Wait until you come to Medina, the land of migration and the land of Sunnah. Then you will be alone with the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ from the Muhājirīn and Anṣār, and they will preserve your statement and put it in its proper context.’ He said: ‘By Allah, I will surely stand with it in the first place where I stand in Medina.’” Ibn ‘Abbās said: We arrived in Medina, and he said: “Allah sent Muḥammad ﷺ with the truth and revealed the Book to him, and among what He revealed was the verse of stoning.”

8659 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: The Prophet ﷺ was sent at the age of forty. He remained in Makkah for thirteen years receiving revelation, then he was commanded to migrate. He then lived for ten years after migration, and died at the age of sixty-three.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3902).

8660 — ‘Abdullah ibn ‘Adī ibn al-Ḥamrā’ said that he heard the Prophet ﷺ while he was standing at al-Ḥazwarah in the market of Makkah: “By Allah, you are the best land of Allah and the most beloved land of Allah to Allah, Mighty and Majestic. Were it not that I was expelled from you, I would not have left.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3925), Ibn Mājah (3108), and Aḥmad (18715), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (3708).

8661 — ‘Ā’ishah said: Sa‘d said: “O Allah, You know that there is no one whom I would love more to fight in Your cause than a people who denied the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and expelled him. O Allah, I think that You have ended the war between us and them.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3901).

8662 — ‘Ā’ishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, said: I never knew my parents except that they followed the religion, and not a day passed except that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would come to us at both ends of the day: morning and evening. When the Muslims were put to trial, Abū Bakr set out as a migrant toward Abyssinia, until he reached Bark al-Ghimād. There he met Ibn al-Dughnah, the chief of al-Qārah, who said: “Where are you going, Abū Bakr?” Abū Bakr said: “My people have driven me out, so I want to travel through the land and worship my Lord.” Ibn al-Dughnah said: “A man like you, Abū Bakr, should neither leave nor be driven out. You earn for the one who has nothing, maintain ties of kinship, carry the burden of the weak, host guests, and help in calamities of truth. I will grant you protection. Return and worship your Lord in your own town.” So he returned, and Ibn al-Dughnah traveled back with him. That evening Ibn al-Dughnah went around among the nobles of Quraysh and said to them: “A man like Abū Bakr should neither leave nor be driven out. Will you drive out a man who earns for the one who has nothing, maintains ties of kinship, carries the burden of the weak, hosts guests, and helps in calamities of truth?” Quraysh did not reject Ibn al-Dughnah’s protection, but they said to Ibn al-Dughnah: “Tell Abū Bakr to worship his Lord inside his house. Let him pray there and recite whatever he wishes, but he must not harm us by that or do it openly, for we fear that he will tempt our women and children.”
 Ibn al-Dughnah said that to Abū Bakr. Abū Bakr remained like that, worshiping his Lord in his house, not performing the prayer openly and not reciting anywhere other than his house. Then something occurred to Abū Bakr, so he built a small mosque in the courtyard of his house. He would pray in it and recite the Qur’an, and the women and children of the polytheists would gather around him, amazed by him and looking at him. Abū Bakr was a man who wept much and could not hold back his tears when he recited the Qur’an. This alarmed the noble polytheists of Quraysh, so they sent for Ibn al-Dughnah, and he came to them. They said: “We granted protection to Abū Bakr on the condition that he worship his Lord in his house, but he has gone beyond that and built a mosque in the courtyard of his house, publicly performing prayer and reciting in it. We fear that he will tempt our women and children, so forbid him. If he wishes to restrict himself to worshiping his Lord in his house, let him do so. But if he refuses except to do it openly, then ask him to return your covenant of protection to you. We dislike breaking your protection, but we will not allow Abū Bakr to act publicly.”
 ‘Ā’ishah said: Ibn al-Dughnah came to Abū Bakr and said: “You know the covenant upon which I gave you protection. Either restrict yourself to that, or return my covenant of protection to me, for I do not want the Arabs to hear that I was betrayed regarding a man for whom I had made a covenant.” Abū Bakr said: “I return your protection to you, and I am content with the protection of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.” The Prophet ﷺ was then in Makkah, and the Prophet ﷺ said to the Muslims: “I have been shown the land of your migration: a place with palm trees between two lava fields,” meaning the two ḥarrahs. So those who migrated set out toward Medina, and most of those who had migrated to Abyssinia returned to Medina. Abū Bakr prepared to go toward Medina, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him: “Wait calmly, for I hope that permission will be granted to me.” Abū Bakr said: “Do you hope for that, may my father be sacrificed for you?” He said: “Yes.” So Abū Bakr held himself back for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ so that he could accompany him, and he fed two riding camels that he had with him on the leaves of the samur tree — which is al-khabaṭ — for four months.
 Ibn Shihāb said: ‘Urwah said: ‘Ā’ishah said: One day, while we were sitting in the house of Abū Bakr at the hottest part of midday, someone said to Abū Bakr: “This is the Messenger of Allah ﷺ coming, covering his head, at an hour when he did not usually come to us.” Abū Bakr said: “May my father and mother be sacrificed for him. By Allah, nothing has brought him at this hour except an important matter.” She said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came and sought permission, and permission was granted to him. He entered, and the Prophet ﷺ said to Abū Bakr: “Remove whoever is with you.” Abū Bakr said: “They are only your family, may my father be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah.” He said: “Permission has been granted to me to leave.” Abū Bakr said: “Companionship, may my father be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Yes.” Abū Bakr said: “Then take, may my father be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah, one of these two riding camels of mine.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “For its price.”
 ‘Ā’ishah said: We prepared them as quickly and thoroughly as possible, and we prepared for them a food-bag in a leather pouch. Asmā’ bint Abī Bakr cut a piece from her waist-belt and tied the mouth of the pouch with it, and for that reason she was called Dhāt al-Niṭāq. She said: Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr went to the cave in Mount Thawr and hid in it for three nights. ‘Abdullah ibn Abī Bakr, a young, intelligent, quick-witted boy, would spend the night with them. He would leave them before dawn, then wake up among Quraysh in Makkah as though he had slept there, and he would not hear any plot being planned against them except that he would memorize it, until he brought them the news when darkness mixed in. ‘Āmir ibn Fuhayrah, the freedman of Abū Bakr, would graze a milch flock of sheep for them, and would bring it to them when part of the night had passed after ‘ishā’. They would spend the night with fresh milk from their sheep and heated milk, until ‘Āmir ibn Fuhayrah would drive the sheep away before dawn. He did that each night of those three nights. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr hired a man from Banū al-Dīl, from Banū ‘Abd ibn ‘Adī, as a skilled guide — and a khirrīt is an expert guide. He had entered into an alliance with the family of al-‘Āṣ ibn Wā’il al-Sahmī and was upon the religion of the disbelievers of Quraysh. They trusted him, gave him their two riding camels, and arranged to meet him at the cave of Thawr after three nights, in the morning of the third night, with their two camels. ‘Āmir ibn Fuhayrah and the guide set out with them, and he took them along the coastal route.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3905).

8663 — Asmā’ bint Abī Bakr said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ departed and Abū Bakr departed with him, Abū Bakr carried all of his wealth with him: five thousand dirhams or six thousand dirhams. She said: He set out with it. She said: Our grandfather Abū Quḥāfah entered upon us, and he had lost his sight. He said: “By Allah, I think he has distressed you by taking his wealth along with himself.” She said: I said: “No, my father, he has left us much good.” She said: I took some stones and placed them in the niche of the house where my father used to put his wealth, then I placed a cloth over them. Then I took his hand and said: “My father, place your hand on this wealth.” She said: He placed his hand on it and said: “There is no harm. If he has left this for you, then he has done well, and this is enough for you.” She said: No, by Allah, he had left us nothing, but I only wanted to calm the old man by that.
 Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (26957), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (24/88), and al-Ḥākim (3/5–6).

8664 — Asmā’ said: I prepared a food-bag for the Prophet ﷺ and Abū Bakr when they intended to go to Medina. I said to my father: “I cannot find anything to tie it with except my waist-belt.” He said: “Tear it in two.” So I did that, and I was called Dhāt al-Niṭāqayn, the woman of the two waist-belts.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3907).

8665 — Asmā’ was pregnant with ‘Abdullah ibn al-Zubayr, and she said: I set out while I was near full term. I came to Medina and stopped at Qubā’, and I gave birth to him at Qubā’. Then I brought him to the Prophet ﷺ, and he placed him in his lap. He then called for a date, chewed it, and put some of his saliva into his mouth. So the first thing to enter his stomach was the saliva of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Then he performed taḥnīk for him with a date, supplicated for him, and blessed him. He was the first child born in Islam.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3909) and Muslim (2146:26).

8666 — Ibn ‘Abbās said: A group of Quraysh, from the nobles of every tribe, gathered to enter Dār al-Nadwah. Iblīs came across them in the form of a venerable old man. When they saw him, they said: “Who are you?” He said: “An old man from Najd. I heard that you had gathered, so I wished to attend with you, and you will not lack opinion and advice from me.” They said: “Yes, enter.” So he entered with them and said: “Consider the matter of this man. By Allah, he is soon to leap upon you in your affairs with his affair.” One person said: “Bind him in chains, then wait for the calamity of death to overtake him until he perishes as those poets before him perished, like Zuhayr and al-Nābighah. He is only like one of them.” The enemy of Allah, the Najdī old man, cried out and said: “By Allah, this is not a good opinion for you. By Allah, his Lord will surely bring him out of his prison to his Companions, and they will soon pounce upon you until they take him from your hands and protect him from you. I do not feel safe that they will expel you from your land.” They said: “Then consider something else.” Another person said: “Expel him from among you and be relieved of him. Once he leaves, whatever he does and wherever he goes will not harm you. When his harm is absent from you, you will be relieved, and his affair will be among others.” The Najdī old man said: “By Allah, this is not a good opinion for you. Have you not seen the sweetness of his speech, the eloquence of his tongue, and how hearts are seized by what they hear of his words? By Allah, if you do that and he then presents himself to the Arabs, they will surely unite against you, then come to you until they expel you from your land and kill your nobles.” They said: “He has spoken the truth, by Allah. So consider an opinion other than this.” Abū Jahl said: “By Allah, I will suggest to you an opinion which I do not think you have yet considered, and I see no other.” They said: “What is it?” He said: “We take from every tribe a young, strong, middle-ranking man, then each young man is given a sharp sword, and they strike him as one man. When they kill him, his blood will be dispersed among all the tribes. I do not think this clan of Banū Hāshim will be able to fight all of Quraysh. When they see that, they will accept blood money, and we will be relieved and cut off his harm from us.” The Najdī old man said: “This, by Allah, is the opinion. The correct word is what the young man has said; I see no other.” So they dispersed upon that, having agreed upon it. He said: Jibrīl came to the Prophet ﷺ and commanded him not to sleep in the bed in which he usually slept that night. Allah then permitted him to depart. After his arrival in Medina, Allah revealed al-Anfāl to him, reminding him of His blessing upon him and His favor with him: “And when those who disbelieved plotted against you to restrain you, kill you, or expel you. They plot, and Allah plots, and Allah is the best of plotters” [al-Anfāl: 30]. And He revealed concerning their saying, “Wait for the calamity of death to overtake him until he perishes as the poets before him perished”: “Or do they say: A poet for whom we await the calamity of time?” [al-Ṭūr: 30]. That day was called the Day of al-Zaḥmah because of the plan upon which they gathered.
 Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/134–135).

8667 — Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq, may Allah be pleased with him, said: I said to the Prophet ﷺ while I was in the cave: “If one of them were to look beneath his feet, he would see us.” He said: “What do you think, O Abū Bakr, of two when Allah is their third?”
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3653) and Muslim (2381).
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr stayed in the cave for three nights so that the pursuit of them would subside. That was because when the polytheists lost them, they went in every direction searching for them. They assigned one hundred camels for whoever returned both of them or either one of them, and they followed their tracks. The one who tracked footprints for Quraysh was Surāqah ibn Mālik ibn Ju‘shum, until they reached the mountain in which the two of them were.

8668 — Jundub ibn Sufyān said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was in a cave, and his finger was struck.
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1796:113).
 The word “cave” here is a scribal error for “on a military expedition,” as appears in another narration: “He was in one of the battles.” See al-Bukhārī (2802) and Muslim (1796:112). However, some scholars considered the cave here to be the cave of Thawr during the Prophet’s migration from Makkah to Medina.

8669 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Prophet ﷺ approached Medina with Abū Bakr riding behind him. Abū Bakr was an older man who was recognized, while the Prophet of Allah ﷺ was a young man who was not recognized. A man would meet Abū Bakr and say: “Abū Bakr, who is this man in front of you?” He would say: “This man guides me along the way.” The listener would think he meant the road, while he meant the way of goodness.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3911).

8670 — Ibn Shihāb reported:
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Mālik al-Mudlijī—the nephew of Surāqah ibn Mālik ibn Juʿshum—informed me that his father told him that he heard Surāqah ibn Juʿshum say:
Messengers from the disbelievers of Quraysh came to us offering the blood-money of each of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr to anyone who killed or captured either of them.
While I was sitting in one of the gatherings of my people, Banū Mudlij, a man from among them came and stood over us while we were seated. He said:
“O Surāqah, I have just seen some dark figures near the coast, and I think they are Muḥammad and his companions.”
Surāqah said:
I knew that it was them, but I said to him:
“They are not them. You only saw so-and-so and so-and-so, who set out before our eyes.”
I remained in the gathering for a while, then got up and entered my house. I ordered my slave girl to take my horse out and keep it ready for me behind a hillock. I took my spear and went out through the back of the house, dragging its lower end along the ground and keeping its upper end lowered, until I reached my horse.
I mounted it and urged it onward until I drew near them. My horse stumbled and I fell from it. I stood up, reached into my quiver, took out the divining arrows, and sought a decision through them as to whether I should harm them or not. The result came out with what I disliked.
I mounted my horse again and disobeyed the divining arrows. It carried me closer until I could hear the recitation of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He did not turn around, while Abū Bakr frequently looked back.
Then the forelegs of my horse sank into the earth up to the knees, and I fell from it. I urged it, and it rose, barely managing to pull out its forelegs.
Once it stood upright, a cloud of dust rose from the marks of its forelegs into the sky like smoke. I again sought a decision through the divining arrows, and again the result came out with what I disliked.
So I called out to them offering safety, and they stopped.
I rode until I reached them. Because of what had happened to me in being prevented from reaching them, it occurred to me that the cause of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would prevail.
I said to him:
“Your people have placed a blood-price upon you.”
I informed them of what the people intended against them and offered them provisions and goods, but they took nothing from me and asked nothing of me except that he said:
“Keep our whereabouts concealed.”
I asked him to write me a document granting me safety. He instructed ʿĀmir ibn Fuhayrah, who wrote it upon a piece of tanned leather. Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ continued on his journey.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3906).

8671 — ‘Abd al-Raḥmān ibn Mālik ibn Ju‘shum al-Mudlijī narrated that his father Mālik told him that his brother Surāqah ibn Ju‘shum told him: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ left Makkah migrating to Medina, Quraysh offered one hundred camels to whoever returned him to them. He said: While I was sitting in the gathering of my people, a man from us came and said: “By Allah, I just saw three riders pass by me, and I think it is Muḥammad.” He said: I signaled to him with my eye to be quiet and said: “It is only Banū so-and-so, seeking a lost animal of theirs.” He said: “Perhaps.” Then he became silent. He said: I remained for a short while, then I rose and entered my house. I ordered my horse, and it was led to me in the middle of the valley. I took out my weapon from behind my rooms, then took my arrows to seek a decision with them. Then I put on my armor and went out with my arrows, seeking a decision with them. The arrow I disliked came out: “Do not harm him.” I had hoped to return him and take the one hundred camels. He said: I rode after him. While my horse was carrying me along, it stumbled and I fell from it. He said: I took out my arrows and sought a decision with them, and the arrow I disliked came out: “Do not harm him.” But I refused except to follow him. I mounted again, and when the people appeared to me and I fixed my gaze upon them, my horse stumbled with me, and its forelegs sank into the ground. I fell from it, then it pulled out its forelegs, and smoke like dust followed them. I knew then that he had been protected from me and that he would prevail. So I called out to them and said: “Wait for me. By Allah, I will not harm you, and nothing you dislike will come from me.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Say to him: What do you seek?” I said: “Write for me a document that will be a sign between me and you.” He said: “Write for him, Abū Bakr.” He said: So he wrote it for me and threw it to me. I returned and kept silent, not mentioning anything of what had happened, until Allah, Mighty and Majestic, opened Makkah and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had finished with the people of Khaybar. I went out to meet the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, carrying the document he had written for me. While I was heading toward him, I entered between the two sides of one of the battalions of the Anṣār. They began striking me with their spears and saying: “Away, away,” until I came near the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was on his she-camel. I could see his shin in the stirrup, like the pith of a palm tree. I raised my hand with the document and said: “O Messenger of Allah, this is your document.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “A day of fulfillment and righteousness. Bring him near.” He said: So I embraced Islam. Then I remembered something to ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about. Ibn Shihāb said: He only asked him about stray animals and something he had done during the journey he had been on. I do not remember anything except that I said: “O Messenger of Allah, stray animals come to my water-basins, which I have filled for my camels. Is there reward for me if I give them water?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Yes, in every moist liver there is reward.” He said: Then I went back and drove my charity to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
 Good: Narrated by al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (2/487) and Abū Nu‘aym in al-Dalā’il (2/428–429).

8672 — al-Barā’ ibn ‘Āzib said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came from Makkah to Medina, Surāqah ibn Mālik ibn Ju‘shum pursued him. He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated against him, and his horse sank into the ground. He said: “Supplicate to Allah for me, and I will not harm you.” He said: So he supplicated to Allah. He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ became thirsty, and they passed by a shepherd. Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq said: I took a cup and milked into it a small amount of milk for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. I brought it to him, and he drank until I was satisfied.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3908) and Muslim (2009).

8673 — al-Barā’ ibn ‘Āzib said: Abū Bakr bought a saddle from ‘Āzib for thirteen dirhams. Abū Bakr said to ‘Āzib: “Tell al-Barā’ to carry it to my house.” He said: “No, not until you tell us what you did when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ left and you were with him.” Abū Bakr said: We set out by night and pushed on through our day and night until midday came and the sun stood high. I looked around to see whether I could find shade to take refuge in, and I saw a rock. I went toward it and found some shade remaining beside it. I prepared it for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, spread out a fur cloak for him, and said: “Lie down, O Messenger of Allah.” So he lay down. Then I went out to see whether I could see anyone pursuing us, and I saw a shepherd. I said: “Whose are you, young man?” He said: “I belong to a man from Quraysh.” He named him, and I recognized him. I said: “Is there milk in your sheep?” He said: “Yes.” I said: “Will you milk for me?” He said: “Yes.” I commanded him, so he restrained one of the sheep. Then I commanded him to wipe the dust from its udder. Then I commanded him to wipe the dust from his hands. I had with me a waterskin with a cloth tied over its mouth. He milked a small amount of milk for me, and I poured water over the cup until its lower part became cool. Then I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and found that he had awakened. I said: “Drink, O Messenger of Allah.” He drank until I was satisfied. Then I said: “Has the time to depart come?” So we set out while the people were searching for us. None of them caught up with us except Surāqah ibn Mālik ibn Ju‘shum on a horse of his. I said: “O Messenger of Allah, this pursuer has caught up with us.” He said: “Do not grieve; indeed Allah is with us.” When he came close to us, and the distance between us and him was about one spear-length or two — or he said two or three — I said: “O Messenger of Allah, this pursuer has caught up with us,” and I cried. He said: “Why are you crying?” I said: “By Allah, I am not crying for myself, but I am crying for you.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated against him and said: “O Allah, suffice us against him however You will.” The legs of his horse sank up to its belly in hard ground. He jumped off and said: “Muḥammad, I know that this is your doing. Supplicate to Allah to save me from what I am in. By Allah, I will mislead those behind me who are pursuing you. This is my quiver; take an arrow from it, for you will pass by my camels and sheep at such-and-such place, so take from them whatever you need.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I have no need of them.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated for him, and he was released. He returned to his companions, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ continued on, and I was with him, until we arrived in Medina. The people met him, coming out into the roads and onto the rooftops. The servants and children ran quickly through the road saying: “Allah is greatest! The Messenger of Allah has come! Muḥammad has come!” The people disputed about who he would stay with. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Tonight I will stay with Banū al-Najjār, the maternal uncles of ‘Abd al-Muṭṭalib, to honor them by that.” When morning came, he went where he was commanded.
 al-Barā’ said: The first of the Muhājirīn to come to us was Muṣ‘ab ibn ‘Umayr, the brother of Banū ‘Abd al-Dār. Then Ibn Umm Maktūm, the blind man, one of Banū Fihr, came to us. Then ‘Umar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb came with twenty riders. We said: “What has happened to the Messenger of Allah?” He said: “He is following after me.” Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived, with Abū Bakr accompanying him. al-Barā’ said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not arrive until I had recited several sūrahs from the Mufaṣṣal.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by Imam Aḥmad (3), and this is his wording; al-Bukhārī (3615); and Muslim (2009:75).
 al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said: al-Barā’s entering upon the family of Abū Bakr was certainly before the ruling of ḥijāb was revealed. Also, at that time he had not yet reached puberty, and the same applies to ‘Ā’ishah.

8674 — al-Barā’ narrated that Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq said: When we set out with the Prophet ﷺ from Makkah to Medina, we passed by a shepherd, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had become thirsty. He said: So I milked a small amount of milk for him and brought it to him, and he drank until I was satisfied.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5607) and Muslim (2009:90), and the wording is Muslim’s. His saying “kuthbah” means a small amount.

8675 — Abū Bakr said: I set out and found a shepherd driving his sheep. I said: “Whose are you?” He said: “I belong to a man from Quraysh,” and he named him, so I recognized him. I said: “Is there milk in your sheep?” He said: “Yes.” I said: “Will you milk for me?” He said: “Yes.” So I ordered him to restrain a sheep from his flock. Then I ordered him to wipe the dust from its udder. Then I ordered him to wipe his hands, and he did like this: he struck one palm against the other. He milked a small amount of milk. I had prepared for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ a waterskin with a cloth tied over its mouth, so I poured water over the milk until its lower part became cool. Then I came to the Prophet ﷺ and said: “Drink, O Messenger of Allah.” He drank until I was satisfied.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2439).
 His saying, “Will you milk?” means: do you have permission to milk for travelers, according to the custom of the Arabs?

8676 — Ibn Mas‘ūd said: I was a young boy tending sheep for ‘Uqbah ibn Abī Mu‘ayṭ when the Prophet ﷺ and Abū Bakr, may Allah be pleased with him, came, having fled from the polytheists. They said: “Young boy, do you have any milk to give us to drink?” I said: “I am entrusted, and I will not give you drink.” The Prophet ﷺ said: “Do you have a young she-goat that no male has mounted?” I said: “Yes.” I brought it to them. The Prophet ﷺ restrained it, wiped the udder, and supplicated. The udder filled with milk. Then Abū Bakr brought him a bowl from a hollowed stone, and he milked into it. He drank, Abū Bakr drank, and then I drank. Then he said to the udder: “Shrink.” So it shrank. I came to him after that and said: “Teach me from this speech.” He said: “You are a boy who will be taught.” He said: I took from his mouth seventy sūrahs concerning which no one disputed with me.
 Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (4412), Abū Ya‘lā (4985), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (8455).

8677 — Qays ibn al-Nu‘mān said: When the Prophet ﷺ and Abū Bakr set out in concealment, they passed by a slave tending sheep and asked him for milk to drink. He said: “I do not have any sheep that can be milked, except that there is here a young she-goat that became pregnant at the beginning of winter, then gave birth prematurely, and no milk remains for it.” He said: “Call it.” So he called it. The Prophet ﷺ restrained it, wiped its udder, and supplicated until it produced milk. Abū Bakr came with a shield, and he milked into it. He gave Abū Bakr to drink, then milked and gave the shepherd to drink, then milked and drank himself. The shepherd said: “By Allah, who are you? By Allah, I have never seen anyone like you.” He said: “Will you keep my matter secret if I tell you?” He said: “Yes.” He said: “I am Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.” He said: “Are you the one Quraysh claim is a Ṣābi’?” He said: “They do say that.” He said: “I bear witness that you are a Prophet, and I bear witness that what you have brought is true, and no one does what you have done except a Prophet. I will follow you.” He said: “You will not be able to do that today. When news reaches you that I have prevailed, then come to us.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (18/343), al-Bazzār — Kashf al-Astār (1743), and al-Ḥākim (3/8–9).
 An ‘anāq is a young female goat. His saying “akhdajat” means that it delivered its young prematurely and incompletely formed.

8678 — Ḥubaysh ibn Khālid al-Khuzā‘ī said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ left Makkah, along with Abū Bakr, the freedman of Abū Bakr, ‘Āmir ibn Fuhayrah, and their Laythī guide ‘Abdullah ibn Urayqiṭ, they passed by the tent of Umm Ma‘bad al-Khuzā‘iyyah. She was a strong, visible woman who would sit wrapped in her garment in the courtyard of the tent, giving drink and food. They asked her for meat and dates to buy from her, but they found nothing of that with her. The people were exhausted and afflicted by drought. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at a sheep in the corner of the tent and said: “What is this sheep, Umm Ma‘bad?” She said: “A sheep that weakness has kept behind from the flock.” He said: “Does it have any milk?” She said: “It is too weak for that.” He said: “Do you permit me to milk it?” She said: “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you; if you see any milk in it, then milk it.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated, wiped its udder with his hand, mentioned the Name of Allah, Blessed and Most High, and supplicated for her sheep. It spread its legs for him, gave milk, and began chewing. He called for a vessel large enough to satisfy the group, and he milked into it abundantly until foam rose over it. Then he gave her to drink until she was satisfied, and he gave his Companions to drink until they were satisfied. He drank last, and they were all fully satisfied. Then he milked into it a second time after the first until he filled the vessel, left it with her, pledged with her, and departed from her.
 Not long after, her husband Abū Ma‘bad came driving lean goats that were swaying from thinness, with little marrow in them. When Abū Ma‘bad saw the milk, he was amazed and said: “Where did you get this, Umm Ma‘bad, when the sheep are far away in the pasture and there is no milking animal in the house?” She said: “No, by Allah, except that a blessed man passed by us, of such-and-such condition.” He said: “Describe him to me, Umm Ma‘bad.” She said: “I saw a man radiant in brightness, with a luminous face, fine in form. He was not marred by a large belly, nor disfigured by a small head. He was handsome and well-proportioned. His eyes had deep blackness, his eyelashes were long, his voice had a slight hoarseness, his neck was long, his beard was thick, his eyebrows were arched and joined. If he was silent, dignity rested upon him; and if he spoke, beauty rose over him and crowned him. He was the most beautiful and splendid of people from afar, and the finest and sweetest of them from near. His speech was sweet, clear, neither too little nor too much, as though his words were a string of pearls descending. He was of medium height: not disliked for being too tall, nor looked down upon for being short. He was like a branch between two branches, the most radiant of the three in appearance and the finest of them in stature. He had companions surrounding him. If he spoke, they listened attentively to his words; if he commanded, they hastened to his command. He was served and surrounded, neither frowning nor foolish in speech.”
 Abū Ma‘bad said: “By Allah, this is the man of Quraysh whose matter has been mentioned to us in Makkah. I had wished to accompany him, and I will certainly do so if I find a way.” In the morning, a loud voice was heard in Makkah. They heard the voice but did not know who its owner was, and it was saying:
 May Allah, Lord of mankind, reward with His best reward
 two companions who stopped at the tent of Umm Ma‘bad.
 They descended upon her with guidance, so she was guided by it;
 successful indeed is the one who becomes the companion of Muḥammad.
 O people of Quṣayy, what Allah withheld from you
 through him of deeds unmatched and noble rank!
 Congratulations to Banū Ka‘b for the place of their young woman,
 and her seat as a watch-post for the believers.
 Ask your sister about her sheep and her vessel,
 for if you ask the sheep, it will testify.
 He called it, though it was barren, and it poured forth milk for him,
 pure and abundant from the udder of the sheep.
 He left it with her as a pledge for the milker,
 who would return to it at each watering and return.
 When Ḥassān ibn Thābit al-Anṣārī, the poet of the Prophet ﷺ, heard of that, he composed verses answering the unseen caller and said:
 A people have failed whose Prophet departed from them;
 blessed is the one to whom he travels by night and by morning.
 He moved away from a people whose minds were deficient,
 and settled among a people with renewed light.
 Their Lord guided them through him after misguidance,
 and whoever follows the truth is rightly guided.
 Can the misguidance of foolish, blind people be equal
 to guided ones who are guided by a guide?
 From him there descended upon the people of Yathrib
 riders of guidance who came down upon them with good fortune.
 A Prophet who sees what people around him do not see,
 and recites the Book of Allah in every gathering.
 If he says a statement one day about what is absent,
 its confirmation comes that day or the next morning.
 Congratulations to Abū Bakr for the good fortune of his destiny,
 by companionship with him; whoever Allah makes happy is happy.
 Congratulations to Banū Ka‘b for the place of their young woman,
 and her seat as a watch-post for the believers.
 Good: Narrated by al-Baghawī in Mu‘jam al-Ṣaḥābah, and the wording is his; and al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalā’il (1/277).
 Umm Ma‘bad’s name was ‘Ātikah bint Khālid, and she was the sister of Ḥubaysh ibn Khālid, the Companion of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ who was killed at al-Baṭḥā’ on the Day of the Conquest.
 Explanation of difficult words:
 “Yurbiḍ al-rahṭ” means that it quenches them until they become heavy and sit down; a rahṭ is from three to ten people. “Thajj” means a flowing stream.
 “al-Bahā’” refers to the whiteness of the milk foam. They drank until they were fully satisfied; “arāḍū” means they were satisfied, as appears in some routes.
 “Tasāwakna” means they swayed from weakness. It is also narrated as “tashārakna,” meaning that emaciation had overtaken them all. “The sheep are ‘āzib” means they are far away in the pasture.
 “Ablaj al-wajh” means bright and radiant of face. “Thajlah” means a large belly with the lower part hanging loosely.
 “Ṣa‘lah” means a small head. It is also narrated as “ṣaqlah,” meaning slimness and leanness; and “ṣaql” refers to the end of the ribs near the flank.
 “Wasīm” means known for beauty, as though beauty had become his mark. “Qasīm” means having a well-proportioned face. “Waṭaf” means length.
 “Ṣaḥal” means a slight hoarseness. “Saṭa‘” means a long neck.
 “Lā taqtaḥimuhu ‘ayn min qiṣar” means that the eye would not look down on him because of shortness and pass beyond him to another; rather, it would revere and accept him.
 “Maḥfūd” means served. “Maḥshūd” means someone around whom people gather.

8679 — ‘Ā’ishah said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ left the cave as a migrant, accompanied by Abū Bakr and ‘Āmir ibn Fuhayrah, with Abū Bakr riding behind him and ‘Abdullah ibn Urayqiṭ al-Laythī behind him, he took them along the lower part of Makkah. Then he continued until he brought them down to the coast below ‘Usfān. Then he passed with them by the lower part of Amaj. Then he crossed the route after passing with them through Qudayd. Then he took them through al-Ḥijāz, then passed through Thaniyyat al-Marār, then took them through al-Ḥafyā’, then passed with them through Mudlijat Liqf, then took them into the interior of Mudlijat Ṣiḥāḥ, then took them through Madhḥij, then through the valley of Madhḥij from Dhī al-Ghaḍwayn, then through the valley of Kashd, then he took al-Jabājib, then Dhā Salam from the upper valley of Mudlijah, then took al-Qāḥah, then descended al-‘Arj, then took Thaniyyat al-Ghā’ir to the right of Rakūbah, then descended the valley of Rīm, and arrived at Qubā’ among Banū ‘Amr ibn ‘Awf.
 Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, as in Sīrat Ibn Hishām (1/491–492).

8680 — Ibn Shihāb said: ‘Urwah ibn al-Zubayr informed me that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ met al-Zubayr among a group of Muslims who were merchants returning from Syria. al-Zubayr clothed the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr in white garments. The Muslims in Medina heard that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had left Makkah, so every morning they would go out to the lava field and wait for him until the heat of midday drove them back. One day, after they had waited a long time, they returned. When they had gone back to their houses, a Jewish man climbed one of their forts for something he was looking at, and he saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions clothed in white, the mirage moving around them. The Jew could not restrain himself and cried out at the top of his voice: “O assembly of Arabs, this is your fortune that you have been waiting for!” The Muslims rushed to their weapons and met the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at the back of the lava field. He turned with them to the right until he stopped among Banū ‘Amr ibn ‘Awf, and that was on a Monday in the month of Rabī‘ al-Awwal. Abū Bakr stood for the people, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sat silently. Those of the Anṣār who came and had not seen the Messenger of Allah ﷺ began greeting Abū Bakr, until the sun struck the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Then Abū Bakr came forward and shaded him with his cloak, and the people then recognized the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
 The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained among Banū ‘Amr ibn ‘Awf for more than ten nights. He founded the mosque that was founded upon taqwā, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed in it. Then he mounted his riding animal and traveled on while the people walked with him, until it knelt at the place of the Messenger’s mosque in Medina. At that time, some Muslim men used to pray there. It had been a drying-ground for dates belonging to Sahl and Suhayl, two orphan boys under the guardianship of As‘ad ibn Zurārah. When his riding animal knelt, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “This, if Allah wills, is the place of residence.” Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called the two boys and negotiated with them regarding the drying-ground so that he could take it as a mosque. They said: “Rather, we give it to you as a gift, O Messenger of Allah.” But the Messenger of Allah ﷺ refused to accept it from them as a gift until he purchased it from them. Then he built it as a mosque. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ began carrying bricks with them during its construction, saying while carrying the bricks:
 This load is not the load of Khaybar;
 this is more righteous, our Lord, and purer.
 And he would say:
 O Allah, the true reward is the reward of the Hereafter;
 so have mercy on the Anṣār and the Muhājirah.
 He recited the poetry of a Muslim man whose name was not mentioned to me. Ibn Shihāb said: It has not reached us in the ḥadīths that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recited a complete line of poetry other than this line.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3906), and al-Ḥākim connected it as in al-Fatḥ (7/243), and ‘Abd al-Razzāq (5/395).

8681 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Prophet ﷺ approached Medina with Abū Bakr riding behind him. Abū Bakr was an older man who was recognized, while the Prophet of Allah ﷺ was a young man who was not recognized. A man would meet Abū Bakr and say: “Abū Bakr, who is this man in front of you?” He would say: “This man guides me along the way.” The listener would think he meant the road, while he meant the way of goodness. Then Abū Bakr turned and saw a horseman who had caught up with them. He said: “O Messenger of Allah, this horseman has caught up with us.” The Prophet of Allah ﷺ turned and said: “O Allah, throw him down.” The horse threw him down, then stood neighing. He said: “O Prophet of Allah, command me with whatever you wish.” He said: “Stand in your place, and do not let anyone catch up with us.” He said: At the beginning of the day, he was striving against the Prophet of Allah ﷺ, and by the end of the day he was a guard for him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stopped by the side of the lava field, then sent to the Anṣār. They came to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ, greeted both of them, and said: “Ride safely and obeyed.” So the Prophet of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr rode, while they surrounded them with weapons. It was said in Medina: “The Prophet of Allah has come! The Prophet of Allah ﷺ has come!” The people looked out, saying: “The Prophet of Allah has come! The Prophet of Allah ﷺ has come!” He continued until he stopped near the house of Abū Ayyūb. While he was speaking to his family, ‘Abdullah ibn Salām heard of him. He was in his family’s palm grove harvesting dates for them, so he hurried to put down what he was harvesting, came while carrying it, and listened to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ. Then he returned to his family. The Prophet of Allah ﷺ said: “Which of our family’s houses is closest?” Abū Ayyūb said: “I am, O Prophet of Allah. This is my house, and this is my door.” He said: “Go and prepare for us a resting place.” He said: “Rise, upon the blessing of Allah.” When the Prophet of Allah ﷺ came, ‘Abdullah ibn Salām came and said: “I bear witness that you are the Messenger of Allah and that you have come with the truth. The Jews know that I am their chief and the son of their chief, and the most knowledgeable of them and the son of the most knowledgeable of them. Call them and ask them about me before they know that I have embraced Islam, for if they know that I have embraced Islam, they will say about me what is not true.” The Prophet of Allah ﷺ sent for them. They came and entered upon him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to them: “O assembly of Jews, woe to you, fear Allah. By Allah, besides Whom there is no deity, you know that I am truly the Messenger of Allah and that I have come to you with the truth, so embrace Islam.” They said: “We do not know that.” They said this to the Prophet ﷺ three times. He said: “What kind of man among you is ‘Abdullah ibn Salām?” They said: “He is our chief and the son of our chief, the most knowledgeable of us and the son of the most knowledgeable of us.” He said: “What do you think if he has embraced Islam?” They said: “Allah forbid! He would never embrace Islam.” He said: “What do you think if he has embraced Islam?” They said: “Allah forbid! He would never embrace Islam.” He said: “What do you think if he has embraced Islam?” They said: “Allah forbid! He would never embrace Islam.” He said: “‘Ibn Salām, come out to them.” He came out and said: “O assembly of Jews, fear Allah. By Allah, besides Whom there is no deity, you know that he is the Messenger of Allah and that he has come with the truth.” They said: “You lie.” So the Messenger of Allah ﷺ expelled them.
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3911).

8682 — al-Barā’ ibn ‘Āzib said: The first to come to us were Muṣ‘ab ibn ‘Umayr and Ibn Umm Maktūm, and they used to teach the people Qur’an. Then Bilāl, Sa‘d, and ‘Ammār ibn Yāsir came. Then ‘Umar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb came with twenty of the Prophet’s Companions. Then the Prophet ﷺ came. I never saw the people of Medina rejoice over anything as they rejoiced over the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, until even the slave girls were saying: “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has arrived.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3925).

8683 — Anas ibn Mālik said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Medina, the Abyssinians played with their spears out of joy at his arrival.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4923), Aḥmad (12649), and al-Ḍiyā’ in al-Mukhtārah (1781), all through ‘Abd al-Razzāq — and it is in his Muṣannaf (19723) — who said: Ma‘mar narrated to us, from Thābit, from Anas; then he mentioned it. Its chain is authentic.

8684 — Anas said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ migrated, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was riding and Abū Bakr was behind him. Abū Bakr was known along the road because of his frequent travel to Syria. He would pass by people, and they would say: “Who is this in front of you, Abū Bakr?” He would say: “A guide who guides me.” When they drew near Medina, they sent to the people of the Anṣār who had embraced Islam, to Abū Umāmah and his companions, so they came out to the two of them and said: “Enter, safe and obeyed.” So they entered. Anas said: I never saw any day brighter or more beautiful than the day the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr entered Medina. And I witnessed his death, and I never saw any day darker or uglier than the day on which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died.
 Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (12234) and Abū Ya‘lā (3486).

8685 — ‘Urwah ibn al-Zubayr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ met al-Zubayr among a group of Muslims. Then he mounted his riding animal and traveled on while the people walked with him, until it knelt at the place of the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ mosque in Medina.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Manāqib (3906) with a connected chain from Ibn Shihāb, who said: ‘Urwah ibn al-Zubayr informed me; then he mentioned it within a long hadith. Its outward form is mursal, but it is connected through ‘Urwah’s hearing from his father.

8686 — ‘Abdullah ibn al-Zubayr narrated that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Medina, his riding animal was made to kneel between the house of Ja‘far ibn Muḥammad ibn ‘Alī and the house of al-Ḥasan ibn Zayd. The people came to him and said: “O Messenger of Allah, the lodging!” Then his riding animal moved on with him, and he said: “Leave it, for it has been commanded.” It continued with him until it brought him to the place of the pulpit, where it knelt. Then it shifted slightly, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ dismounted from his riding animal and took shelter in the shade, where he settled. Abū Ayyūb came to him and said: “O Messenger of Allah, my house is the closest of houses.” Then another man came and said: “O Messenger of Allah, stay with me.” He said: “A man remains with his belongings wherever they are.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained in the shelter until he prayed there with the people for twelve nights, until the mosque was built.
 Good: Narrated by Sa‘īd ibn Manṣūr (2/347).

8687 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stopped by the side of the lava field, then sent to the Anṣār. They came to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr, greeted both of them, and said: “Ride, safe and obeyed.” So the Prophet of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr rode, while they surrounded them with weapons. It was said in Medina: “The Prophet of Allah has come! The Prophet of Allah ﷺ has come!” The people looked out, saying: “The Prophet of Allah has come!” He continued until he stopped near the house of Abū Ayyūb. While he was speaking to his family, ‘Abdullah ibn Salām heard of him. He was in his family’s palm grove harvesting dates for them, so he hurried to put down what he was harvesting, came while carrying it, and listened to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ.
