Then he returned to his family. The Prophet of Allah ﷺ said: “Which of our family’s houses is closest?” Abū Ayyūb said: “I am, O Prophet of Allah. This is my house, and this is my door.” He said: “Go and prepare for us a resting place.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3911).

8688 — Abū Ayyūb narrated that the Prophet ﷺ stayed with him. The Prophet ﷺ stayed on the lower floor, while Abū Ayyūb was on the upper floor. He said: One night Abū Ayyūb awoke and said: “Are we walking above the head of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?” So they moved aside and spent the night on one side. Then he spoke to the Prophet ﷺ about that, and the Prophet ﷺ said: “The lower floor is more convenient.” He said: “I will not be above a ceiling while you are beneath it.” So the Prophet ﷺ moved to the upper floor, and Abū Ayyūb moved to the lower floor. He used to prepare food for the Prophet ﷺ, and when it was brought back to him, he would ask about the place where his fingers had touched and follow the place of his fingers. He prepared food for him that contained garlic, and when it was returned to him he asked about the place where the Prophet’s ﷺ fingers had touched. He was told: “He did not eat.” He became alarmed, went up to him, and said: “Is it forbidden?” The Prophet ﷺ said: “No, but I dislike it.” He said: “Then I dislike what you dislike,” or “what you disliked.” He said: The Prophet ﷺ used to receive revelation.
 Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2053:171).

8689 — Abū Ayyūb said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stayed with me, I said: “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you. I dislike being above you while you are below me.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “It is more convenient for me to be on the lower floor because of the people who come to us.” I remember that a jar of ours broke and its water spilled. I and Umm Ayyūb rose with a cloak of ours — we had no blanket other than it — and used it to soak up the water out of fear that anything of it might reach the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and harm him.
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/460–461), through Abū al-Walīd al-Imām → Muḥammad ibn Nu‘aym → Isḥāq ibn Ibrāhīm al-Ḥanẓalī → Wahb ibn Jarīr → his father → Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq → Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb → Marthad ibn ‘Abdullah al-Yazanī → Abū Umāmah al-Bāhilī → Abū Ayyūb. Al-Ḥākim said: “Authentic according to the criterion of Muslim.”
The Prophet ﷺ initially stayed on the lower floor of the house because, as he said, it was more convenient for him, his Companions, and those who came to visit him. When Abū Ayyūb insisted and disliked being above while the Prophet ﷺ was below, the Prophet ﷺ moved to the upper floor. This shows Abū Ayyūb’s beautiful etiquette toward the Prophet ﷺ, and this was specific to him. His saying, “he used to receive revelation,” means that the angels and revelation would come to him, as stated in the authentic hadith: “The angels are harmed by what harms the children of Adam.” Thus the Prophet ﷺ would continually avoid eating garlic because he expected the angels and revelation to come to him at any time. This does not apply to others; rather, eating it is disliked when attending congregational prayer. The Prophet ﷺ remained in the house of Abū Ayyūb for about seven months.

8690 — Anas ibn Mālik said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Medina, he stayed in the upper part of Medina among a clan called Banū ‘Amr ibn ‘Awf. He said: He stayed among them for fourteen nights. Then he sent for the leaders of Banū al-Najjār. He said: They came wearing their swords. He said: It is as though I am looking at the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on his riding animal, with Abū Bakr riding behind him and the leaders of Banū al-Najjār around him, until he stopped at the courtyard of Abū Ayyūb. He said: He would pray wherever the prayer reached him, and he would pray in the sheep pens. He said: Then he commanded that the mosque be built. He sent for the leaders of Banū al-Najjār, and they came. He said: “O Banū al-Najjār, name a price for me for this garden of yours.” They said: “No, by Allah, we seek its price only from Allah.” He said: In it was what I will tell you: there were graves of polytheists, ruins, and palm trees. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ commanded that the graves of the polytheists be dug up, that the ruins be leveled, and that the palm trees be cut down. He said: They arranged the palm trees toward the qiblah of the mosque. He said: They made its two doorposts from stones. He said: They began carrying those stones while chanting, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was with them, saying:
 O Allah, there is no good except the good of the Hereafter;
 so support the Anṣār and the Muhājirah.
 Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3932) and Muslim (524).

8691 — Abū Sa‘īd al-Khudrī said: We were carrying one brick at a time, while ‘Ammār was carrying two bricks at a time. The Prophet ﷺ saw him, wiped the dust from him, and said: “Woe to ‘Ammār. He will be killed by the rebellious group. He calls them to Paradise, while they call him to the Fire.”
 Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (447).

8692 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that when ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām heard that the Prophet ﷺ had arrived in Madinah, he came to him to ask him about certain matters. He said, “I will ask you about three things which no one knows except a prophet: What is the first of the signs of the Hour? What is the first food the people of Paradise will eat? And why does a child resemble his father or his mother?” He said, “Jibrīl has just informed me of them.” ʿAbdullāh said, “He is the enemy of the Jews among the angels.” The Prophet ﷺ said, “As for the first of the signs of the Hour, it is a fire that will gather them from the east to the west. As for the first food the people of Paradise will eat, it is the extra lobe of the liver of the fish. As for the child, when the man’s fluid precedes the woman’s fluid, the child resembles his father; and when the woman’s fluid precedes the man’s fluid, the child resembles his mother.” He said, “I bear witness that there is no god but Allah and that you are the Messenger of Allah.” Then he said, “O Messenger of Allah, the Jews are a people of slander, so ask them about me before they learn of my Islam.” The Jews came, and the Prophet ﷺ said, “What kind of man is ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām among you?” They said, “He is the best of us and the son of the best of us, and the most virtuous of us and the son of the most virtuous of us.” The Prophet ﷺ said, “What do you think if ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām embraces Islam?” They said, “May Allah protect him from that.” He repeated the question, and they said the same. Then ʿAbdullāh came out to them and said, “I bear witness that there is no god but Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah.” They said, “He is the worst of us and the son of the worst of us,” and they disparaged him. He said, “This is what I feared, O Messenger of Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3938).

8693 — Ibn ʿAbbās said regarding the verse, “And for everyone We have appointed heirs” [al-Nisāʾ: 33], that it means inheritors. Regarding the verse, “And those with whom your right hands have made a covenant” [al-Nisāʾ: 33], he said: When the Muhājirūn came to Madinah, a Muhājir would inherit from an Anṣārī instead of the Anṣārī’s blood relatives, because of the brotherhood that the Prophet ﷺ had established between them. When the verse, “And for everyone We have appointed heirs,” was revealed, it abrogated that. Then he said that the verse, “And those with whom your right hands have made a covenant,” refers to support, assistance, and sincere counsel; inheritance has passed away, but one may make a bequest for him.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Tafsīr (4580), from al-Ṣalt ibn Muḥammad: Abū Usāmah narrated to us, from Idrīs, from Ṭalḥah ibn Muṣarrif, from Saʿīd ibn Jubayr, from Ibn ʿAbbās, and he mentioned it. Al-Bukhārī said: Abū Usāmah heard from Idrīs, and Idrīs heard from Ṭalḥah. For more, see the Book of Inheritance.

8694 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf arrived, and the Prophet ﷺ established brotherhood between him and Saʿd ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Anṣārī. Saʿd offered to divide his family and wealth with him equally, but ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said, “May Allah bless your family and wealth for you; show me the marketplace.” He earned some dried yogurt and clarified butter. The Prophet ﷺ saw him with traces of yellow perfume on him and said, “What is this, O ʿAbd al-Raḥmān?” He said, “O Messenger of Allah, I married a woman from the Anṣār.” He said, “What dowry did you give her?” He said, “The weight of a date-stone in gold.” The Prophet ﷺ said, “Hold a wedding feast, even if only with a sheep.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Manāqib (3937), from Muḥammad ibn Yūsuf: Sufyān narrated to us, from Ḥumayd, from Anas, and he mentioned it.

8695 — Anas ibn Mālik was asked, “Has it reached you that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, ‘There is no alliance in Islam’?” Anas replied, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ made an alliance between Quraysh and the Anṣār in my house.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Kafālah (2294) and Muslim in Faḍāʾil al-Ṣaḥābah (2529), both from the hadith of ʿĀṣim al-Aḥwal, who said: I said to Anas ibn Mālik: “Has it reached you that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said...?” and he mentioned it.
The phrase “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ made an alliance…” means that if the intended meaning is an alliance of inheritance, then it was abrogated by the verse: “And blood relatives are more entitled to one another” [al-Anfāl: 75]. But if the intended meaning is brotherhood, mutual support in religion, cooperation in righteousness and piety, and establishing the truth, then this remains until the Day of Resurrection.

8696 — Khārijah ibn Zayd al-Anṣārī narrated that Umm al-ʿAlāʾ, a woman of theirs who had pledged allegiance to the Prophet ﷺ, told him that ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn’s lot had fallen to her household when the Anṣār cast lots to host the Muhājirūn. Umm al-ʿAlāʾ said: ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn stayed with us, then he became ill, and we nursed him until he died. When we had shrouded him in his garments, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered upon us, and I said, “May Allah have mercy on you, Abū al-Sāʾib. My testimony concerning you is that Allah has honored you.” The Prophet ﷺ said to me, “How do you know that Allah has honored him?” I said, “I do not know—may my father and mother be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “As for ʿUthmān, by Allah, certainty has come to him, and I hope for good for him. But by Allah, I do not know, though I am the Messenger of Allah, what will be done with him.” She said, “By Allah, I will never declare anyone pure after him.” That saddened me. She said: Then I slept and saw for ʿUthmān a flowing spring. I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and told him, and he said, “That is his deed.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Shahādāt (2687), from Abū al-Yamān: Shuʿayb informed us, from al-Zuhrī, who said: Khārijah ibn Zayd al-Anṣārī narrated to me, and he mentioned it.

8697 — Anas narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ established brotherhood between Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ and Abū Ṭalḥah.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Virtues of the Companions (2528), from Ḥajjāj ibn al-Shāʿir: ʿAbd al-Ṣamad narrated to us; Ḥammād, meaning Ibn Salamah, narrated to us from Thābit, from Anas, and he mentioned it.

8698 — Anas narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ made an alliance between Quraysh and the Anṣār in his house in Madinah.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in Faḍāʾil al-Ṣaḥābah (205:2528), through routes from ʿAbdah ibn Sulaymān, from ʿĀṣim, from Anas, and he mentioned it.

8699 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated: When the Muhājirūn came to Madinah from Makkah, they had nothing in their hands, while the Anṣār owned land and property. So the Anṣār shared with them, giving them the fruits of their properties each year, while the Anṣār would spare them the labor and expenses. His mother, Umm Anas—Umm Sulaym, the mother of ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Ṭalḥah—had given the Messenger of Allah ﷺ some date-palms, and the Prophet ﷺ gave them to Umm Ayman, his freedwoman, the mother of Usāmah ibn Zayd.
Ibn Shihāb said: Anas ibn Mālik informed me that when the Prophet ﷺ finished fighting the people of Khaybar and returned to Madinah, the Muhājirūn returned to the Anṣār the grants of fruit they had been given. The Prophet ﷺ returned to Anas’s mother her date-palms, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave Umm Ayman in their place from his own orchard.
Aḥmad ibn Shabīb said: My father informed us from Yūnus with this narration, and he said: “in their place from his private share.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Hibah (2630), from ʿAbdullāh ibn Yūsuf: Ibn Wahb informed us; Yūnus narrated to us, from Ibn Shihāb, from Anas ibn Mālik, and he mentioned it.

8700 — Anas narrated: When the Prophet ﷺ came to Madinah, the Muhājirūn came to him and said, “O Messenger of Allah, we have never seen any people more generous with much, nor better in sharing when they have little, than the people among whom we have settled. They have spared us the expenses and shared with us the fruits and comforts, until we feared that they would take all the reward.” The Prophet ﷺ said, “No, so long as you supplicate to Allah for them and praise them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2487), Aḥmad (13122, 13074), and al-Bayhaqī (6/183), through routes from Ḥumayd, from Anas, and he mentioned it. The wording is al-Tirmidhī’s.

8701 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated: The Prophet ﷺ wrote down for every clan their blood-money responsibilities, then he wrote: “It is not lawful for a Muslim to attach himself as a client to the client of another Muslim without his permission.” Then I was informed that in his document he cursed whoever did that.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-ʿItq (1507), from Muḥammad ibn Rāfiʿ: ʿAbd al-Razzāq narrated to us; Ibn Jurayj informed us; Abū al-Zubayr informed me that he heard Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh, and he mentioned it.
The phrase “in his document he cursed whoever did that” refers to the ḥadīth of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib recorded in the two Ṣaḥīḥs: “Whoever claims lineage to other than his father, or attaches himself to other than his rightful patrons, upon him is the curse of Allah, the angels, and all people.”

8702 — Anas narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ established brotherhood among his Companions. He established brotherhood between Salmān and Abū al-Dardāʾ, and between ʿAwf ibn Mālik and Ṣaʿb ibn Jaththāmah.
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (3404), from Qaṭan ibn Nuṣayr: Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān narrated to us; Thābit narrated to us, from Anas, and he mentioned it.

8703 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ established brotherhood between al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām and ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (2917, 12/179) and al-Ḥākim (3/314).
Al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām and ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd were both from the Muhājirūn of Makkah.

8704 — Anas narrated: The Prophet ﷺ established brotherhood between Ibn Masʿūd and al-Zubayr.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (568) and al-Bayhaqī in al-Kubrā (6/262).

8705 — Zayd ibn Ḥārithah narrated: I said, “O Messenger of Allah, you established brotherhood between me and Ḥamzah ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (1333), Abū Yaʿlā (7211, 7210), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (5/85).

8706 — ʿAmr ibn Qays ibn Thawr ibn Māzin ibn Khaythamah narrated that his grandfather Māzin ibn Khaythamah and Hubayl ibn Kaʿb, the grandfather of Zaml, were sent by Muʿādh ibn Jabal when he settled between al-Sakūn and al-Sakāsik and fought until the people embraced Islam. He then sent the two of them as delegates to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ established brotherhood between al-Sakūn and al-Sakāsik.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Saʿd (7/424), and the wording is his; and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (20/339).
The phrase “al-Sakūn and al-Sakāsik” refers to two well-known tribes in Yemen.
The original brotherhood was between the Muhājirūn and the Anṣār in Madinah, but something of this had also preceded in Makkah between Muslims, meaning between those of higher and lower financial standing, such as Ḥamzah and Zayd ibn Ḥārithah, because Zayd was their freedman and had less wealth than Ḥamzah. Likewise, it occurred between al-Zubayr and Ibn Masʿūd and others.
The Prophet ﷺ also established brotherhood between tribes in order to strengthen brotherhood among them after Islam.

8707 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated: When the Prophet ﷺ was expelled from Makkah, Abū Bakr said, “They have expelled their Prophet. Indeed, we belong to Allah and indeed to Him we will return. They will surely perish.” Then the verse was revealed: “Permission has been given to those who are fought because they have been wronged, and indeed Allah is capable of giving them victory” [al-Ḥajj: 39]. I then knew that fighting would occur. Ibn ʿAbbās said: This was the first verse revealed concerning fighting.
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3085), and the wording is his; al-Tirmidhī (3171); Aḥmad (1865); and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4710).

8708 — Al-Zuhrī said: The first verse revealed concerning fighting, as ʿUrwah informed me from ʿĀʾishah, was: “Permission has been given to those who are fought because they have been wronged, and indeed Allah is capable of giving them victory,” up to His saying: “Indeed, Allah is Powerful, Mighty” [al-Ḥajj: 39-40]. Thereafter, permission to fight was given in many verses of the Qur’an.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Kubrā (11283), through Zakariyyā ibn Yaḥyā → Muḥammad ibn Yaḥyā → Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Abī Rizmah → Salmūyah Abū Ṣāliḥ → ʿAbdullāh → Yūnus → al-Zuhrī.

8709 — Jābir narrated: I participated with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in nineteen expeditions. Jābir said: I did not witness Badr or Uḥud; my father prevented me. But when ʿAbdullāh was killed on the Day of Uḥud, I never stayed behind from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in any expedition.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (145:1813).

8710 — Abū Isḥāq said: I was beside Zayd ibn Arqam, and he was asked, “How many expeditions did the Prophet ﷺ undertake?” He said, “Nineteen.” He was asked, “How many did you participate in with him?” He said, “Seventeen.” I said, “Which of them was first?” He said, “Al-ʿUsayrah or al-ʿUshayr.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3949) and Muslim (143:1254).
Zayd ibn Arqam’s statement appears to restrict the Prophet’s ﷺ expeditions to nineteen. Yet Jābir stated that he participated with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in nineteen expeditions while not witnessing Badr or Uḥud, making the number of the Prophet’s ﷺ expeditions twenty-one. This negates that restriction, and this is the correct position, because what is established among the scholars of sīrah is that the Prophet’s ﷺ expeditions were more than nineteen. Thus, Zayd ibn Arqam’s statement is understood according to what he knew, or as reflecting a difference in how the expeditions were counted.

8711 — Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb narrated that he participated with the Prophet ﷺ in sixteen expeditions.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4473) and Muslim (147:1814).
Muslim also narrated it (146:1814) through another route, in which it states: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ undertook nineteen expeditions and fought in eight of them.

8712 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated: I participated with the Prophet ﷺ in fifteen expeditions.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4472).

8713 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ narrated: I participated with the Prophet ﷺ in seven expeditions, and I went out in nine of the detachments he sent: once Abū Bakr was our commander, and once Usāmah ibn Zayd was our commander.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4270, 4271, 4272, 4273) and Muslim (148:1815).
The scholars of maghāzī and sīrah differed regarding the number of the Prophet’s ﷺ expeditions and detachments. Ibn Saʿd mentioned in his Ṭabaqāt (2/5-6), from al-Wāqidī and others, saying that the reports of some of them entered into the reports of others. They said: The number of military expeditions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in which he personally went out was twenty-seven, and the detachments he sent were forty-seven. The expeditions in which fighting occurred were nine: Badr, Uḥud, al-Muraysīʿ, al-Khandaq, Qurayẓah, Khaybar, the Conquest of Makkah, Ḥunayn, and al-Ṭāʾif. This is what has been gathered for us.
In some of their narrations, it is stated that he fought Banū al-Naḍīr, but Allah made that exclusively for him as fayʾ. He also fought in the expedition of Wādī al-Qurā while returning from Khaybar, and some of his Companions were killed, and he fought in al-Ghābah. End quote.

8714 — Jābir narrated in a long ḥadīth: We traveled with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the expedition of Baṭn Buwāṭ. A man from the Anṣār had a water-camel, so he made it kneel, mounted it, and then urged it forward, but it hesitated somewhat. He said to it, “Move! May Allah curse you.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Who is this cursing his camel?” He said, “I am, O Messenger of Allah.” He said, “Dismount from it, for one that has been cursed must not accompany us. Do not supplicate against yourselves, do not supplicate against your children, and do not supplicate against your wealth, lest you coincide with an hour in which Allah is asked for a gift and He answers you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3009).

8715 — Abū Isḥāq al-Sabīʿī narrated: I was beside Zayd ibn Arqam, and he was asked, “How many expeditions did the Prophet ﷺ undertake?” He said, “Nineteen.” He was asked, “How many did you participate in with him?” He said, “Seventeen.” I said, “Which of them was first?” He said, “Al-ʿUshayr or al-ʿUsayrah.” I mentioned that to Qatādah, and he said, “Al-ʿUshayrah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3949) and Muslim (143:1254).
The phrase “he was asked” refers to the narrator Abū Isḥāq al-Sabīʿī, as explicitly stated in another place in al-Bukhārī (4471): “I asked Zayd ibn Arqam.” The phrase “I mentioned it to Qatādah” was said by Shuʿbah.
Qatādah’s wording “al-ʿUshayrah” is in the diminutive form, with the letter ʿayn/shīn, and with the final hāʾ affirmed, though some omit it. Qatādah’s wording is what the scholars of sīrah agreed upon, and it is correct. Ibn Ḥajar said this in al-Fatḥ (7/281).
Al-ʿUshayrah was a populated village at the lower part of Yanbuʿ al-Nakhl, then later became a station for Egyptian pilgrims. It is the first of the villages of Yanbuʿ al-Nakhl from the coastal side. See al-Maʿālim al-Athīrah, p. 192.
His phrase “Which of them was first?” means: Which expedition did you first participate in with the Prophet ﷺ? He said, “Al-ʿUshayrah,” because two expeditions had preceded it: al-Abwāʾ and Buwāṭ. This order is what al-Bukhārī transmitted from Ibn Isḥāq: al-Abwāʾ, then Buwāṭ, then al-ʿUshayrah. See Fatḥ al-Bārī (7/279-281).

8716 — Jundub ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated from the Prophet ﷺ that he sent a small group and appointed Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ, or ʿUbaydah, over them. When he was about to set out, he wept out of longing for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and sat down. So the Prophet ﷺ appointed ʿAbdullāh ibn Jaḥsh in his place, wrote a letter for him, and ordered him not to read it until he reached such-and-such place. He said, “Do not force any of your companions to travel with you.” When he read the letter, he said, “Indeed we belong to Allah and indeed to Him we will return,” then said, “Hearing and obedience to Allah and His Messenger.” He informed them of the matter and read the letter to them. Two men returned, while the rest continued. They met Ibn al-Ḥaḍramī and killed him, not knowing whether that day was in Rajab or Jumādā. The polytheists said to the Muslims, “You have killed during the sacred month!” So Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed: “They ask you about the sacred month—fighting therein…” [al-Baqarah: 217]. Some said, “If they did not incur sin, then they have no reward.” So Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed: “Indeed, those who believed and those who emigrated and strove in the path of Allah—those hope for the mercy of Allah, and Allah is Forgiving, Merciful” [al-Baqarah: 218].
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (1534), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (2/174), and al-Bayhaqī (9/11-12).

8717 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik narrated: I did not stay behind from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in any expedition he undertook except the expedition of Tabūk. However, I had stayed behind from the expedition of Badr, and no one who stayed behind from it was reproached, for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had only gone out intending the caravan of Quraysh, until Allah brought them together with their enemy without prior appointment. The ḥadīth continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3951) and Muslim (53:2769).
Badr, with fatḥah then sukūn, is a well-known village attributed to Badr ibn Mukhlid ibn al-Naḍr ibn Kinānah, who had settled there. It is also said to be the name of the well located there. Today it is a large populated town approximately 150 kilometers from Madinah. Al-Maʿālim al-Athīrah, p. 44.

8718 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard that Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb was returning from Syria with forty riders from Quraysh, merchants among whom were Makhramah ibn Nawfal and ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ. So the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called the Muslims to go out and said to them, “This is Abū Sufyān returning with the trade goods of Quraysh, so go out to intercept it; perhaps Allah will grant it to you as spoils.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the Muslims set out. Some men went quickly with him, while others were slow, because the call was only for wealth they might obtain, and they did not think they would meet battle. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out with a little over three hundred riders, though most of his Companions were on foot. They had eighty camels and one horse, and some people claim that it belonged to al-Miqdād. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out, and he, ʿAlī, and Marthad ibn Abī Marthad al-Ghanawī shared one camel. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ left through the pass of Banū Dīnār from the lava-field toward al-ʿAqīq. The narrator mentioned his route, until when he was at ʿIrq al-Ẓabyah, he met a man from the Bedouins. They asked him about the people but found no information with him.
When Abū Sufyān drew near to the Ḥijāz, he began seeking news and asking about it until he received information from some riders. He then hired Ḍamḍam ibn ʿAmr al-Ghifārī and sent him to Quraysh to call them to protect their wealth and to inform them that Muhammad had set out against it with his Companions. Ḍamḍam hastened until he reached Quraysh in Makkah and said, “O Quraysh, the trade caravan has been targeted by Muhammad and his Companions”—and al-laṭīmah means the trade goods—“Help! Help! I do not think you will catch it.” Quraysh said, “Does Muhammad and his Companions think this will be like the caravan of Ibn al-Ḥaḍramī?” So they went out, with both the strong and the weak among them, and none of their nobles stayed behind except Abū Lahab, who stayed back and sent in his place al-ʿĀṣ ibn Hishām ibn al-Mughīrah. Quraysh went out as nine hundred and fifty fighters, with two hundred horses that they led, and they brought singing girls with them who beat the tambourines and sang verses mocking the Muslims.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq as in Ibn Hishām (1/606), and by al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalāʾil (3/31-32), and the wording is his.

8719 — Anas narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent Busaysah as a scout to see what the caravan of Abū Sufyān was doing. He returned, and there was no one in the house except me and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He told him the news. Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out and spoke, saying, “We have a pursuit; whoever has his mount ready should ride with us.” Some men began asking his permission to go to their mounts in the upper parts of Madinah, but he said, “No, only whoever has his mount ready.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1901).

8720 — Ibn ʿAbbās and ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr narrated: Three nights before Ḍamḍam reached Makkah, ʿĀtikah saw a dream that frightened her. She sent for her brother al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and said to him, “My brother, by Allah, tonight I saw a dream that terrified me, and I fear that evil and calamity will enter upon your people because of it. So keep secret what I am telling you.” He said to her, “What did you see?” She said, “I saw a rider approaching on his camel until he stopped at al-Abṭaḥ. Then he cried out at the top of his voice: ‘Go forth, O people of treachery, to your places of death in three days!’ I saw the people gather around him. Then he entered the mosque while the people followed him. While they were around him, his camel was made to stand on top of the Kaʿbah, and he cried out the same: ‘Go forth, O people of treachery, to your places of death in three days!’ Then his camel was made to stand on top of Abū Qubays, and he cried out the same. Then he took a rock and threw it, and it came crashing down until, when it reached the base of the mountain, it shattered. There was no house or dwelling in Makkah except that a fragment of it entered it.” Al-ʿAbbās said, “By Allah, this is certainly a dream. Keep it secret, and do not mention it to anyone.”
Then al-ʿAbbās went out and met al-Walīd ibn ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah, who was a friend of his. He mentioned it to him and asked him to keep it secret. Al-Walīd mentioned it to his father ʿUtbah, and the report spread through Makkah until Quraysh were discussing it in their gatherings.
Al-ʿAbbās said: I went early to circumambulate the House, while Abū Jahl ibn Hishām was sitting with a group of Quraysh discussing ʿĀtikah’s dream. When Abū Jahl saw me, he said, “O Abū al-Faḍl, when you finish your ṭawāf, come to us.” When I finished, I came and sat with them. Abū Jahl said to me, “O Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, when did this prophetess arise among you?” I said, “What is that?” He said, “The dream that ʿĀtikah saw.” I said, “What did she see?” He said, “O Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, were you not satisfied that your men claim prophethood until your women claim it too? ʿĀtikah claims in her dream that someone said, ‘Go forth in three days.’ We will wait these three days for you. If what she says is true, then it will happen; but if three days pass and nothing happens, we will write a document against you that you are the most lying household among the Arabs.” Al-ʿAbbās said: By Allah, I did not respond to him much except that I denied it and denied that she had seen anything. Then we dispersed.
When evening came, every woman from Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib came to me and said, “Did you accept that this wicked sinner should speak against your men, then turn to speaking about the women while you heard him, and you had no response to anything you heard?” I said, “By Allah, I did that. I did not respond to him much. By Allah, I will confront him, and if he returns to it, I will deal with him.”
He said: I went early on the third day after ʿĀtikah’s dream, angry and sharp, thinking that I had missed an opportunity with him that I wanted to seize. I entered the mosque and saw him. By Allah, I was walking toward him, seeking to provoke him so that he would repeat some of what he had said and I could attack him. He was a nimble man, sharp-faced, sharp-tongued, and keen-eyed. Suddenly, he rushed toward the door of the mosque. I said to myself, “What is wrong with him, may Allah curse him? Is all this out of fear that I will argue with him?” But he had heard what I had not heard: the voice of Ḍamḍam ibn ʿAmr al-Ghifārī shouting in the valley, standing on his camel, with his camel’s nose cut, his saddle reversed, and his shirt torn, saying, “O Quraysh, the trade caravan! The trade caravan! Your wealth with Abū Sufyān has been attacked by Muhammad and his Companions. I do not think you will catch it. Help! Help!” That distracted me from him, and distracted him from me because of the matter that had come.
The people prepared quickly, saying, “Does Muhammad and his Companions think this will be like the caravan of Ibn al-Ḥaḍramī? No, by Allah, he will learn otherwise.” They were of two types: either a man went out himself, or he sent another man in his place. Quraysh mobilized fully, and none of their nobles stayed behind except Abū Lahab ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. He stayed behind and sent in his place al-ʿĀṣ ibn Hishām ibn al-Mughīrah, who owed him four thousand dirhams and had gone bankrupt. Abū Lahab hired him with that debt to go in his place, so he sent him, and he went out on his behalf, while Abū Lahab stayed behind.
Ibn Isḥāq said: ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Najīḥ narrated to me that Umayyah ibn Khalaf had resolved to stay behind. He was an old, dignified, heavy-bodied man. ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ came to him while he was sitting in the mosque among his people, carrying an incense burner containing fire and incense. He placed it in front of him and said, “O Abū ʿAlī, perfume yourself with incense, for you are only one of the women.” He said, “May Allah disgrace you and disgrace what you have brought.” Then he prepared and went out with the people.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq, and through his route by al-Ḥākim (3/19).
Ibn Isḥāq said: When Abū Sufyān saw that he had secured his caravan, he sent word to Quraysh: “You only came out to protect your caravan, your men, and your wealth. Allah has saved them, so return.” But Abū Jahl ibn Hishām said, “By Allah, we will not return until we reach Badr.” Badr was one of the seasonal gatherings of the Arabs, where they held a market every year. “We will stay there three days, slaughter camels, feed people, serve wine, and have the singing girls play for us. The Arabs will hear of us, our march, and our gathering, and they will continue to fear us forever after that. So go on.”
Sīrat Ibn Hishām (1/618-619).

8721 — Anas narrated that when news reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that Abū Sufyān was approaching, he consulted the people. Abū Bakr spoke, but he turned away from him. Then ʿUmar spoke, but he turned away from him. Then Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah stood and said, “Do you mean us, O Messenger of Allah? By the One in Whose hand is my soul, if you commanded us to plunge into the sea, we would plunge into it; and if you commanded us to drive our mounts to Bark al-Ghimād, we would do so.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then called the people to go out, and they set out until they encamped at Badr. The water-camels of Quraysh came to them, and among them was a black slave belonging to Banū al-Ḥajjāj. They seized him, and the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ began asking him about Abū Sufyān and his companions. He would say, “I have no knowledge of Abū Sufyān, but here are Abū Jahl, ʿUtbah, Shaybah, and Umayyah ibn Khalaf.” When he said that, they would beat him. So he would say, “Yes, I will tell you; this is Abū Sufyān.” When they left him and then asked him again, he would say, “I have no knowledge of Abū Sufyān, but here are Abū Jahl, ʿUtbah, Shaybah, and Umayyah ibn Khalaf among the people.” When he said that again, they beat him, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was standing in prayer. When he saw that, he turned away from prayer and said, “By the One in Whose hand is my soul, you beat him when he tells you the truth and leave him when he lies to you.”
Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “This is the place where so-and-so will fall,” placing his hand on the ground here and here. Anas said: Not one of them moved away from the place where the hand of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had indicated.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (83:1779).
The phrase “the water-camels of Quraysh” means their camels upon which they drew water.
The phrase “not one of them moved away” means that none of them fell far from the place he ﷺ had indicated.

8722 — Anas ibn Mālik reported:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out toward Badr, he consulted the people. Abū Bakr gave him his opinion, then he consulted them again and ʿUmar gave him his opinion. Then he remained silent.
A man from the Anṣār said:
“He is referring to you.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, we will not be like the Children of Israel who said to Mūsā:
‘So go, you and your Lord, and fight. Indeed, we are sitting right here.’
[al-Māʾidah: 24]
Rather, by Allah, even if you were to drive the camels until you reached Bark al-Ghimād, we would certainly be with you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (12022), Abū Yaʿlā (3803), and through him Ibn Ḥibbān (4721).
The phrase “their livers” means the bellies of the camels.
The word al-Ghimād may be pronounced with either ḍammah or kasrah on the letter ghayn. It is a place in the far reaches of Yemen, though other views have also been mentioned.

8723 — Ibn Masʿūd narrated: I witnessed from al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad a scene that I would love to have been the one who did it more than anything equal to it. He came to the Prophet ﷺ while he was supplicating against the polytheists and said, “We will not say as the people of Mūsā said: ‘So go, you and your Lord, and fight; indeed, we are sitting right here.’ Rather, we will fight on your right, on your left, in front of you, and behind you.” I saw the face of the Prophet ﷺ brighten, and he was pleased by what he said.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3952).

8724 — Thābit narrated from Anas ibn Mālik: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent Busaysah as a scout to see what the caravan of Abū Sufyān was doing. He came back, and there was no one in the house except me and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Anas said: I do not know whether he made an exception for some of his wives. He said: Busaysah told him the news. Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out, spoke, and said, “We have a pursuit; whoever has his mount ready should ride with us.” Some men began asking his permission to go to their mounts in the upper parts of Madinah, but he said, “No, only whoever has his mount ready.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions set out until they reached ahead of the polytheists. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “None of you should advance toward anything until I am before him.” When the polytheists drew near, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Rise toward a Paradise whose width is the heavens and the earth.” ʿUmayr ibn al-Ḥumām al-Anṣārī said, “O Messenger of Allah, a Paradise whose width is the heavens and the earth?” He said, “Yes.” He said, “Bakh, bakh!” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “What made you say ‘bakh, bakh’?” He said, “Nothing, by Allah, O Messenger of Allah, except the hope that I will be among its people.” He said, “You are among its people.” So he took some dates from his quiver and began eating from them, then said, “If I live until I finish eating these dates, that would be a long life.” He threw away the dates he had with him, then fought them until he was killed.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (145:1901).
The name “Busaysah” is pronounced with ḍammah on the bāʾ and fatḥah on the sīn. In the Sīrah of Ibn Isḥāq, as found in Ibn Hishām (1/614), it is “Basbas al-Juhanī.” Others attributed him to Dhubyān and said: He is Basbas ibn ʿAmr ibn Thaʿlabah ibn Kharshah ibn ʿAmr ibn Saʿd ibn Dhubyān.
ʿAdī ibn Abī al-Zaghbāʾ was with him, as Ibn Isḥāq mentioned in Sīrat Ibn Hishām (1/617).
He mentioned its story and said: They made their camels kneel near a hill close to the water. Then they took a waterskin of theirs to draw water in it, while Majdī ibn ʿAmr al-Juhanī was at the water. ʿAdī and Basbas heard two slave-girls from the people present disputing at the water. The one being pressed for payment said to her companion: “The caravan will only arrive tomorrow or the day after, so I will work for them and then repay you what I owe you.” Majdī said, “She has spoken the truth,” then he settled the matter between them. ʿAdī and Basbas heard this, mounted their camels, and went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him of what they had heard.

8725 — ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered the bells to be cut from the necks of the camels on the Day of Badr.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (25166) and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4699), both from the hadith of Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar, from Saʿīd, from Qatādah, from Zurārah ibn Awfā, from Saʿd ibn Hishām, from ʿĀʾishah.

8726 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ spoke while he was in a tent on the Day of Badr, and he mentioned the supplication.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Tafsīr (4877), through Isḥāq al-Wāsiṭī → Khālid al-Ṭaḥḥān → Khālid al-Ḥadhdhāʾ → ʿIkrimah → Ibn ʿAbbās. The hadith is cited in full in the Prophet’s ﷺ supplication on the Day of Badr.

8727 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated: Then during the night a light rain fell upon us, so we went under the trees and shields to take shelter from the rain. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ spent the night supplicating to his Lord, Mighty and Majestic, saying, “O Allah, if You destroy this group, You will not be worshiped.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (948), through Ḥajjāj → Isrāʾīl → Abū Isḥāq → Ḥārithah ibn Muḍarrib → ʿAlī.

8728 — Al-Barāʾ narrated: The Companions of Muhammad ﷺ who witnessed Badr told me that they were the same number as the companions of Ṭālūt who crossed the river with him: three hundred and a little over ten. Al-Barāʾ said: No, by Allah, none crossed the river with him except a believer.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3957).

8729 — Al-Barāʾ narrated: Ibn ʿUmar and I were deemed too young on the Day of Badr. The Muhājirūn on the Day of Badr were a little over sixty, and the Anṣār were a little over two hundred and forty.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3956).
The word “nayf” is pronounced with fatḥah on the nūn and a doubled yāʾ, though it may also be lightened. It means a number between two tens. Fatḥ al-Bārī (7/291).

8730 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated: On the Day of Badr, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at the polytheists, and they were one thousand, while his Companions were three hundred and nineteen men.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (58:1763).

8731 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out on the Day of Badr with three hundred and fifteen men. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “O Allah, they are barefoot, so provide mounts for them. O Allah, they are unclothed, so clothe them. O Allah, they are hungry, so feed them.” Allah granted him victory on the Day of Badr, and when they returned, not one of them returned except with one or two camels; they were clothed and fed.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2747), al-Ḥākim (2/132-133, 145), and al-Bayhaqī (6/305).

8732 — Abū Mūsā narrated: The number of the people of Badr was the same as the number of the companions of Ṭālūt on the day of Jālūt: three hundred and seventeen.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār, Kashf al-Astār (1784).
Al-Bukhārī mentioned the names of those from the people of Badr who were named in his Ṣaḥīḥ, arranged alphabetically. They are:
The Prophet Muhammad ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Hāshimī ﷺ. His noble name is placed first due to his rank.
Iyās ibn al-Bukayr.
Bilāl ibn Rabāḥ, the freedman of Abū Bakr al-Qurashī.
Ḥamzah ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib al-Hāshimī.
Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah, an ally of Quraysh.
Abū Ḥudhayfah ibn ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah al-Anṣārī.
Ḥārithah ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Anṣārī, who is Ḥārithah ibn Surāqah; he was among those watching.
Khubayb ibn ʿAdī al-Anṣārī.
Khunays ibn Ḥudhāfah al-Sahmī.
Rifāʿah ibn Rāfiʿ al-Anṣārī.
Rifāʿah ibn ʿAbd al-Mundhir, Abū Lubābah al-Anṣārī.
Al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām al-Qurashī.
Zayd ibn Sahl, Abū Ṭalḥah al-Anṣārī.
Abū Zayd al-Anṣārī.
Saʿd ibn Mālik al-Zuhrī.
Saʿd ibn Khawlah al-Qurashī.
Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl al-Qurashī.
Sahl ibn Ḥunayf al-Anṣārī.
Ẓuhayr ibn Rāfiʿ al-Anṣārī.
And his brother Muẓahhir ibn Rāfiʿ.
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUthmān al-Qurashī.
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd al-Hudhalī.
ʿUtbah ibn Masʿūd al-Hudhalī.
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf al-Zuhrī.
ʿUbaydah ibn al-Ḥārith al-Qurashī.
ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit al-Anṣārī.
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb al-ʿAdawī.
ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib al-Hāshimī.
ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf, an ally of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy.
ʿUqbah ibn ʿAmr al-Anṣārī.
ʿĀmir ibn Rabīʿah al-ʿAnazī.
ʿĀṣim ibn Thābit al-Anṣārī.
ʿUwaym ibn Sāʿidah al-Anṣārī.
ʿItbān ibn Mālik al-Anṣārī.
Qudāmah ibn Maẓʿūn.
Qatādah ibn al-Nuʿmān al-Anṣārī.
Muʿādh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Jamūḥ.
Muʿawwidh ibn ʿAfrāʾ.
And his brother.
Mālik ibn Rabīʿah, Abū Usayd al-Anṣārī.
Murārah ibn al-Rabīʿ al-Anṣārī.
Maʿn ibn ʿAdī al-Anṣārī.
Misṭaḥ ibn Uthāthah ibn ʿAbbād ibn al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Manāf.
Al-Miqdād ibn ʿAmr al-Kindī, an ally of Banū Zuhrah.
Hilāl ibn Umayyah al-Anṣārī. May Allah be pleased with them.
Al-Bukhārī, may Allah have mercy on him, mentioned only forty-four men from the people of Badr, because these were the ones whose names appeared with chains in different places of his al-Jāmiʿ al-Ṣaḥīḥ. For this reason, he titled the chapter: “Naming Those Who Were Named from the People of Badr in al-Jāmiʿ.” As for their actual number, it undoubtedly reaches three hundred and thirteen, and reaches three hundred and fifty when differing names are included. The reason for that goes back to disagreement over some names, as al-Ḥāfiẓ said in al-Fatḥ (7/329).

8733 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated: On the Day of Badr, every three of us shared one camel. Abū Lubābah and ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib were the two who shared with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He said: It was the turn of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. They said, “We will walk for you.” He said, “You two are not stronger than me, and I am not less in need of reward than you.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (3901), Abū Yaʿlā (5359), al-Bazzār — Kashf al-Astār (1759), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4733), al-Ḥākim (2/91), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalāʾil (3/39), all through routes from Ḥammād ibn Salamah → ʿĀṣim ibn Bahdalah → Zirr ibn Ḥubaysh → ʿAbdullāh.
The phrase “the turn of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ” means his turn to ride.

8734 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated: There was no horseman among us on the Day of Badr except al-Miqdād. I saw us all asleep, except the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who was beneath a tree praying and weeping until morning.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1023), Abū Yaʿlā (280), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (899) and Ibn Ḥibbān (2257), all through ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Mahdī → Shuʿbah → Abū Isḥāq → Ḥārithah ibn Muḍarrib → ʿAlī. Its chain is authentic.

8735 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib said to him: We had only two horses with us: a horse belonging to al-Zubayr and a horse belonging to al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad, meaning on the Day of Badr.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/20), and through him al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (3/39), through Ibn Wahb → Abū Ṣakhr → Abū Muʿāwiyah al-Bajalī → Saʿīd ibn Jubayr → Ibn ʿAbbās.

8736 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated: Ibn ʿUmar and I were deemed too young on the Day of Badr.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3956).

8737 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ looked at ʿUmayr ibn Abī Waqqāṣ and deemed him too young when he set out for Badr, then later permitted him. Saʿd said: It is said that his sword failed him. ʿAbdullāh said: He was killed on the Day of Badr.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār — Kashf al-Astār (1770), through Muḥammad ibn Qays → Isḥāq ibn Muḥammad → ʿAbdullāh ibn Jaʿfar → Ismāʿīl ibn Muḥammad ibn Saʿd → ʿĀmir ibn Saʿd → his father Saʿd.
Al-Wāqidī said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ deemed ʿUmayr ibn Abī Waqqāṣ too young on the Day of Badr and wanted to send him back, so he wept. Then he later permitted him, and he was killed that day at the age of sixteen.

8738 — Ḥudhayfah ibn al-Yamān narrated: Nothing prevented me from witnessing Badr except that I went out with my father Ḥusayl, and the disbelievers of Quraysh seized us. They said, “You intend to go to Muhammad?” We said, “We do not intend him; we only intend Madinah.” They took from us Allah’s covenant and pledge that we would go to Madinah and not fight alongside him. We came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him of what happened. He said, “Go back. We will fulfill their covenant, and we will seek Allah’s help against them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-Jihād (1787), through Abū Bakr ibn Abī Shaybah → Abū Usāmah → al-Walīd ibn Jumayʿ → Abū al-Ṭufayl → Ḥudhayfah.

8739 — ʿAlī narrated: We captured two men on the Day of Badr: a man from Quraysh and a freedman of ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ. As for the Qurashī, he escaped, but we captured the freedman of ʿUqbah. We kept saying to him, “How many are the people?” He would say, “By Allah, they are many in number and strong in might.” Whenever he said that, the Muslims would beat him, until they brought him to the Prophet ﷺ. He said, “How many are the people?” He said, “By Allah, they are many in number and strong in might.” The Prophet ﷺ tried hard to get him to tell how many they were, but he refused. Then the Prophet ﷺ asked him, “How many camels do they slaughter?” He said, “Ten every day.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “The people are one thousand; every camel is for one hundred and those who follow them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (948).

8740 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated: I saw us on the Day of Badr taking refuge close to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, while he was the nearest of us to the enemy, and he was among the fiercest of the people that day in battle.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (654, 1042) and Ibn Abī Shaybah (12660) through Isrāʾīl — and the wording is his — and by Aḥmad (1347), al-Nasāʾī in al-Kubrā (8585), and al-Ḥākim (2/143) through Zuhayr; both routes are from Abū Isḥāq al-Sabīʿī → Ḥārithah ibn Muḍarrib → ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.

8741 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr narrated that the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to al-Zubayr on the Day of al-Yarmūk, “Will you not charge so that we may charge with you?” He said, “If I charge, you will not follow through.” They said, “We will.” So he charged against them until he split their ranks, passed beyond them with no one with him, then returned facing them. They grabbed the reins of his horse and struck him twice on his shoulder, between which was a wound he had received on the Day of Badr.
ʿUrwah said: When I was small, I used to put my fingers into those wounds while playing.
ʿUrwah said: ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr was with him that day, and he was ten years old, so he placed him on a horse and assigned a man to watch over him.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (3975), through Aḥmad ibn Muḥammad al-Simsār → ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Mubārak → Hishām ibn ʿUrwah → his father.

8742 — ʿUrwah narrated: Al-Zubayr had three sword wounds, one of them on his shoulder. He said: I used to put my fingers into it. He said: He received two wounds on the Day of Badr and one on the Day of al-Yarmūk. ʿUrwah said: ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān said to me when ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr was killed, “O ʿUrwah, do you recognize the sword of al-Zubayr?” I said, “Yes.” He said, “What is in it?” I said, “It has a notch that was made on the Day of Badr.” He said, “You have spoken the truth.”
“It bears notches from striking against battalions.”
Then he returned it to ʿUrwah. Hishām said: We valued it among ourselves at three thousand, and one of us took it. I wish I had taken it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (3973), through Ibrāhīm ibn Mūsā → Hishām ibn Yūsuf → Maʿmar → Hishām → ʿUrwah.

8743 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated: I will be the first to kneel before the Most Merciful for dispute on the Day of Resurrection. Qays ibn ʿUbād said: Concerning them was revealed: “These are two opposing parties who disputed concerning their Lord” [al-Ḥajj: 19]. He said: They are those who engaged in single combat on the Day of Badr: Ḥamzah, ʿAlī, and ʿUbaydah, or Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Ḥārith; and Shaybah ibn Rabīʿah, ʿUtbah, and al-Walīd ibn ʿUtbah.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3965).
The word “yajthū” means to sit on the knees as a disputant.
The intended meaning of this firstness is that it is restricted to the fighters of this Ummah, because the mentioned duel was the first single combat to occur in Islam.

8744 — Qays ibn ʿUbād narrated: I heard Abū Dharr swear emphatically that this verse: “These are two opposing parties who disputed concerning their Lord” [al-Ḥajj: 19] was revealed concerning those who came out for single combat on the Day of Badr: Ḥamzah, ʿAlī, ʿUbaydah ibn al-Ḥārith, ʿUtbah and Shaybah the two sons of Rabīʿah, and al-Walīd ibn ʿUtbah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3966, 3968, 3969) and Muslim (34:3033).

8745 — Abū Isḥāq narrated: A man asked al-Barāʾ while I was listening, “Did ʿAlī witness Badr?” He said, “He fought in single combat and wore armor over armor.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3970).
His answer, “He fought in single combat and wore armor over armor,” contains an omitted phrase. The implied meaning is: “Yes, he witnessed it, for he fought in single combat there and wore armor over armor,” meaning he wore one coat of armor over another.

8746 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated: He advanced, meaning ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah, and his son and brother followed him. He called out, “Who will come out for single combat?” Some young men from the Anṣār stepped forward to face him. He said, “Who are you?” They told him. He said, “We have no need of you; we only want our cousins.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Stand, O Ḥamzah; stand, O ʿAlī; stand, O ʿUbaydah ibn al-Ḥārith.” Ḥamzah advanced toward ʿUtbah, and I advanced toward Shaybah. Between ʿUbaydah and al-Walīd there were two blows, and each severely wounded the other. Then we turned against al-Walīd, killed him, and carried ʿUbaydah away.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2665), Aḥmad (948), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (3/194).

8747 — Usayd narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to us on the Day of Badr, “When they come close to you, shoot them, and preserve your arrows.”
In another wording: The Prophet ﷺ said on the Day of Badr when we lined up against Quraysh and they lined up against us, “When they come close to you, then use arrows.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3984), and he narrated the second wording as well (2900).
The phrase “when they come close to you” means when they draw near to you.

8748 — Abū ʿImrān al-Tujībī narrated that he heard Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī say: We lined up on the Day of Badr, and a group from us advanced ahead of the row. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at them and said, “With me, with me,” like this.
Maʿmar said: “A group from us rushed forward.” And he said: “We lined up on the Day of Badr.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (23567, 23569).

8749 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said while he was in a tent on the Day of Badr, “I implore You by Your covenant and Your promise. O Allah, if You will, You will not be worshiped after today ever again.” Abū Bakr took his hand and said, “Enough, O Messenger of Allah, for you have persisted in imploring your Lord,” while he was wearing armor. He then came out reciting: “The group will be defeated, and they will turn their backs. Rather, the Hour is their appointment, and the Hour is more disastrous and more bitter.” [al-Qamar: 45-46]
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4877).

8750 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated: On the Day of Badr, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at the polytheists, and they were one thousand, while his Companions were three hundred and nineteen men. The Prophet of Allah ﷺ faced the qiblah, stretched out his hands, and began calling upon his Lord: “O Allah, fulfill for me what You promised me. O Allah, bring what You promised me. O Allah, if You destroy this group from the people of Islam, You will not be worshiped on earth.” He continued calling upon his Lord, stretching out his hands and facing the qiblah, until his cloak fell from his shoulders. Abū Bakr came to him, took his cloak, placed it back on his shoulders, then embraced him from behind and said, “O Prophet of Allah, enough of your imploring your Lord, for He will surely fulfill for you what He promised you.” Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed: “When you were seeking help from your Lord, and He answered you: ‘I will reinforce you with a thousand angels following one another’” [al-Anfāl: 9]. So Allah reinforced him with the angels.
Abū Zumayl said: Ibn ʿAbbās narrated to me: While a Muslim man that day was pursuing a polytheist in front of him, he heard the crack of a whip above him and the voice of a rider saying, “Advance, Ḥayzūm!” He looked at the polytheist in front of him, and he had fallen flat on his back. He looked at him and saw that his nose had been struck and his face split as if by the blow of a whip, and the whole of that had turned green. The Anṣārī came and told that to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he said, “You have spoken the truth. That was from the reinforcement of the third heaven.” They killed seventy that day and captured seventy.
Abū Zumayl said: Ibn ʿAbbās said: When they captured the prisoners, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to Abū Bakr and ʿUmar, “What do you think concerning these prisoners?” Abū Bakr said, “O Prophet of Allah, they are our cousins and kin. I think you should take ransom from them, so it may be a source of strength for us against the disbelievers, and perhaps Allah will guide them to Islam.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “What do you think, O Ibn al-Khaṭṭāb?” I said, “No, by Allah, O Messenger of Allah. I do not see what Abū Bakr sees. Rather, I think you should give us power over them so we strike their necks. Give ʿAlī power over ʿAqīl so he may strike his neck, and give me power over so-and-so, a relative of ʿUmar, so I may strike his neck, for these are the leaders and chiefs of disbelief.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ inclined to what Abū Bakr said and did not incline to what I said.
The next day, I came and found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr sitting and weeping. I said, “O Messenger of Allah, tell me what makes you and your companion weep. If I find reason to weep, I will weep; and if I do not find reason to weep, I will make myself weep because of your weeping.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “I weep because of what was presented to me regarding your companions taking ransom. Their punishment was presented to me closer than this tree,” a tree near the Prophet of Allah ﷺ. Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed: “It is not for a prophet to have captives until he has thoroughly subdued the enemy in the land,” up to His saying: “So eat of what you have taken as spoils, lawful and good” [al-Anfāl: 67-69]. Thus Allah made the spoils lawful for them.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (58:1763). Ḥayzūm is the name of Jibrīl’s horse.

8751 — ʿAlī narrated: When we came to Madinah, we ate from its fruits, but the climate did not suit us and we were afflicted with fever. The Prophet ﷺ was seeking news about Badr. When news reached us that the polytheists had advanced, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ marched to Badr, and Badr was a well. We reached it before the polytheists. We found two men from them there: a man from Quraysh and a freedman of ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ. As for the Qurashī, he escaped; as for the freedman of ʿUqbah, we captured him. We kept saying to him, “How many are the people?” He would say, “By Allah, they are many in number and strong in might.” Whenever he said that, the Muslims would beat him, until they brought him to the Prophet ﷺ. He said to him, “How many are the people?” He said, “By Allah, they are many in number and strong in might.” The Prophet ﷺ tried hard to get him to tell how many they were, but he refused. Then the Prophet ﷺ asked him, “How many camels do they slaughter?” He said, “Ten every day.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “The people are one thousand; every camel is for one hundred and those who follow them.” Then during the night a light rain fell upon us, so we went under the trees and shields to shelter from the rain. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ spent the night supplicating to his Lord, Mighty and Majestic, saying, “O Allah, if You destroy this group, You will not be worshiped.” When dawn rose, he called out, “Prayer, servants of Allah.” The people came out from beneath the trees and shields, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ led us in prayer and urged us to fight. Then he said, “The gathering of Quraysh is beneath this red side of the mountain.” When the people drew near to us and we formed ranks against them, a man among them on a red camel was moving among the people. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “O ʿAlī, call Ḥamzah for me,” and he was the closest of them to the polytheists, “Who is the owner of the red camel, and what is he saying to them?” Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “If there is anyone among the people commanding good, perhaps it is the owner of the red camel.” Ḥamzah came and said, “It is ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah. He is forbidding them from fighting and saying to them: ‘O people, I see a people who are ready to die. You will not reach them while any good remains among you. O people, pin this matter on me today, and say: ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah became cowardly; and you know that I am not the most cowardly among you.’” Abū Jahl heard that and said, “Are you saying this? By Allah, if someone else had said this, I would have made him bite on it. Your lungs have filled your belly with fear.” ʿUtbah said, “Are you mocking me, O one whose backside is yellowed? Today you will know which of us is the coward.”
Then ʿUtbah, his brother Shaybah, and his son al-Walīd came out in pride and said, “Who will fight in single combat?” Six young men from the Anṣār came out, but ʿUtbah said, “We do not want these; rather, let those from our cousins, from Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, fight us.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Stand, O ʿAlī; stand, O Ḥamzah; stand, O ʿUbaydah ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-Muṭṭalib.” Allah Most High caused ʿUtbah and Shaybah, the two sons of Rabīʿah, and al-Walīd ibn ʿUtbah to be killed, while ʿUbaydah was wounded. We killed seventy of them and captured seventy. A short man from the Anṣār brought al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib as a prisoner. Al-ʿAbbās said, “O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, this man did not capture me. Rather, I was captured by a bald-fronted man with one of the most beautiful faces among people, riding a piebald horse. I do not see him among the people.” The Anṣārī said, “I captured him, O Messenger of Allah.” He said, “Be silent, for Allah Most High supported you with a noble angel.” ʿAlī said: We captured from Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib: al-ʿAbbās, ʿAqīl, and Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (948) and al-Bazzār, Kashf al-Astār (1761). The wording is Aḥmad’s.
His phrase “I would have made him bite on it” comes from biting with the molars, meaning: I would have said to him, “Bite the private part of your father.”
His phrase “O one whose backside is yellowed”—al-ist means the backside. That is, he accused him of effeminacy or of applying saffron to his backside. It is also said that it is a phrase said to a pampered, indulgent person who has not been tested by experience and hardship. Ibn al-Athīr said this.

8752 — Rifāʿah ibn Rāfiʿ al-Zuraqī narrated: Jibrīl came to the Prophet ﷺ and said, “How do you regard the people of Badr among you?” He said, “Among the best of the Muslims,” or a similar phrase. He said, “And likewise are those angels who witnessed Badr.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3992).

8753 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said on the Day of Badr, “This is Jibrīl, holding the head of his horse, wearing the equipment of war.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3995).

8754 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me and to Abū Bakr on the Day of Badr, “With one of you is Jibrīl, and with the other is Mīkāʾīl, and Isrāfīl is a mighty angel who witnesses the battle, or is present in the battle.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1257), Abū Yaʿlā (340), al-Bazzār in his Musnad (729), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (3/134).

8755 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Thaʿlabah ibn Ṣuʿayr narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ dozed briefly in the shelter, then woke and said, “Receive glad tidings, O Abū Bakr. This is Jibrīl, wearing his turban, holding the reins of his horse and leading it, with dust upon its front teeth. Allah’s victory and His promise have come to you.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then commanded, took a handful of pebbles in his hand, came out, faced the people, and said, “May the faces be disfigured.” Then he threw it at them and said to his Companions, “Charge.” It was then only defeat. Allah killed those He killed among their chiefs and caused those who were captured to be captured.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq, as in al-Bidāyah wa al-Nihāyah (5/126).

8756 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ raised his hand on the Day of Badr and said, “O Lord, if You destroy this group, You will never be worshiped on earth.” Jibrīl, peace be upon him, said to him, “Take a handful of dust.” So he took a handful of dust and threw it into their faces. There was not a single one of the polytheists except that dust from that handful struck his eyes, nostrils, and mouth, so they turned away fleeing.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/86), and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/1673).

8757 — Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām narrated: On the Day of Badr, we heard a sound falling from the sky to the earth, like the sound of a pebble falling into a basin. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ threw those pebbles, and they were defeated. That is the saying of Allah Most High: “And you did not throw when you threw, but Allah threw, so that He might test the believers with a good test from Him.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (3/227), al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/84), and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in al-Tafsīr (5/1672).

8758 — Abū Ṭalḥah narrated: Drowsiness overcame us while we were in our battle rows on the Day of Badr. Abū Ṭalḥah said: I was among those overcome by drowsiness that day. My sword kept falling from my hand, and I would pick it up; it would fall, and I would pick it up again.
Authentic: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (16357).

8759 — Anas ibn Mālik reported:
Abū Jahl said:
“O Allah, if this is indeed the truth from You, then rain down upon us stones from the sky or bring us a painful punishment.”
[al-Anfāl: 32]
Then the following was revealed:
“But Allah would not punish them while you are among them, nor would Allah punish them while they seek forgiveness. And why should Allah not punish them while they prevent people from al-Masjid al-Ḥarām?”
[al-Anfāl: 33–34]
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (2796) and al-Bukhārī (4648).

8760 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Thaʿlabah ibn Ṣuʿayr narrated that Abū Jahl said when the two groups met: “O Allah, whoever among us has severed the ties of kinship more and has come with what is more unknown, destroy him this morning.” Thus he was the one seeking judgment.
Authentic: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (23661), al-Nasāʾī in al-Kubrā (11137), and al-Ḥākim (2/328).
The word “aqṭaʿanā” is a comparative form from cutting/severing.
The word “ātānā” is a comparative form from coming.
The word “fa-aḥinhu” is from “Allah destroyed him,” meaning: destroy him.
Al-Suddī said: When the polytheists left Makkah for Badr, they held onto the curtains of the Kaʿbah and sought Allah’s victory, saying: “O Allah, grant victory to the higher of the two armies, the nobler of the two groups, and the better of the two tribes.” So Allah said: “If you seek judgment, then judgment has come to you” [al-Anfāl: 19], meaning: I have granted victory to what you said—namely Muhammad ﷺ.

8761 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated: When the Muslims encamped at Badr and the polytheists approached, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah while he was upon a red camel and said, “If there is any good with anyone among the people, it is with the owner of the red camel; if they obey him, they will be rightly guided.” He was saying, “O people, obey me regarding these people. If you do this, it will remain in your hearts: each man will look at the killer of his brother and the killer of his father. So put its cowardice upon my head and return.” Abū Jahl said, “By Allah, his lungs have swollen with fear when he saw Muhammad and his Companions. Muhammad and his Companions are only like the meal of one slaughtered camel if we meet.” ʿUtbah said, “You will know who is the coward who ruins his people. By Allah, I see a people who will strike you with blows. Do you not see that their heads are like serpents and their faces are like swords?” Then he called his brother and his son, went out walking between them, and called for single combat.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bazzār, Kashf al-Astār (1762).

8762 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ spent the night supplicating and saying, “O Allah, if You destroy this group, You will not be worshiped on earth.” When dawn rose, he said, “Prayer, servants of Allah.” So we came from beneath the trees and shields. He urged us to fight and said, “It is as if I am looking at the places where they will fall dead.” When the people drew near, there was a man moving among them on a red camel. The Prophet ﷺ said to al-Zubayr, “Call one of your companions and ask him who the owner of the red camel is; if there is anyone among the people commanding good, it is he.” Al-Zubayr asked, “Who is the owner of the red camel?” They said, “ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah. He is forbidding them from fighting and saying: ‘O people, I see a people ready to die. By Allah, I do not think you will reach them until you are destroyed.’” When what he was saying reached Abū Jahl, he came to him and said, “Your lungs have filled with terror when you saw Muhammad and his Companions.” ʿUtbah said to him, “Do you mean me, O one whose backside is yellowed? You will know which of us is more cowardly.” Then he dismounted from his camel, and his brother Shaybah and his son al-Walīd followed him, and they called for single combat. Then he mentioned the full ḥadīth.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (948) and al-Bazzār, Kashf al-Astār (1761).

8763 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf narrated: While I was standing in the row on the Day of Badr, I looked to my right and left, and I found myself between two young boys from the Anṣār, both very young in age. I wished I had been between men stronger than them. One of them nudged me and said, “Uncle, do you know Abū Jahl?” I said, “Yes. What do you need from him, my nephew?” He said, “I have been told that he insults the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. By the One in Whose Hand is my soul, if I see him, my body will not part from his body until the quicker of us dies.” I was amazed at that. Then the other nudged me and said the same thing. It was not long before I saw Abū Jahl moving among the people, so I said, “Do you not see? This is the one you are asking about.” They rushed toward him and struck him with their swords until they killed him. Then they returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him. He said, “Which of you killed him?” Each one said, “I killed him.” He said, “Have you wiped your swords?” They said, “No.” He looked at the two swords and said, “Both of you killed him.” He awarded his spoils to Muʿādh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Jamūḥ. The two men were Muʿādh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Jamūḥ and Muʿādh ibn ʿAfrāʾ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3141) and Muslim (42:1752).

8764 — Anas narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Who will go and see for us what happened to Abū Jahl?” Ibn Masʿūd went and found that the two sons of ʿAfrāʾ had struck him until he had become cold. He took hold of his beard and said, “Are you Abū Jahl?” He said, “Is there anyone greater than a man whom you have killed?” Or he said, “whom his people killed?” Abū Mijlaz said: Abū Jahl said, “If only someone other than a farmer had killed me.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4020) and Muslim (118:1800). The wording is Muslim’s.

8765 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that he came to Abū Jahl while there was still some life in him on the Day of Badr. Abū Jahl said, “Is there any man greater than a man whom you have killed?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3961).
The word “aʿmad,” with the un-dotted ḥāʾ absent and the letter ʿayn, is a comparative form from “ʿamida,” meaning: perished.

8766 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr narrated: Al-Zubayr said: On the Day of Badr I met ʿUbaydah ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. He was fully armored, and nothing could be seen of him except his eyes. He was known as Abū Dhāt al-Karsh. He said, “I am Abū Dhāt al-Karsh.” I charged at him with a short spear and stabbed him in the eye, and he died. Hishām said: I was informed that al-Zubayr said: I placed my foot on him and then pulled with effort, and it was difficult for me to pull it out, while its two ends had bent. ʿUrwah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked him for it, and he gave it to him. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed away, he took it. Then Abū Bakr requested it, and he gave it to him. When Abū Bakr passed away, ʿUmar asked him for it, and he gave it to him. When ʿUmar passed away, he took it. Then ʿUthmān requested it from him, and he gave it to him. When ʿUthmān was killed, it came into the possession of the family of ʿAlī. Then ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr requested it, and it remained with him until he was killed.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3998).
“Fully armored” means covered with weapons and armor so that nothing of him was visible.

8767 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf narrated: I made a written agreement with Umayyah ibn Khalaf that he would protect my property and dependents in Makkah, and I would protect his property and dependents in Madinah. When I mentioned “al-Raḥmān,” he said, “I do not know al-Raḥmān. Write to me with your name that you had in the Jāhiliyyah.” So I wrote to him as “ʿAbd ʿAmr.” When it was the Day of Badr, I went out toward a mountain to protect him while the people were asleep. Bilāl saw him and went out until he stood before a gathering of the Anṣār and said, “Umayyah ibn Khalaf! May I not survive if Umayyah survives.” A group from the Anṣār went out with him following us. When I feared they would catch us, I left his son behind for them to occupy them, so they killed him. Then they refused except to continue following us, and Umayyah was a heavy man. When they caught up with us, I said to him, “Kneel down.” He knelt, and I threw myself over him to protect him. They covered him with swords from beneath me until they killed him, and one of them struck my foot with his sword. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf used to show us that scar on the top of his foot.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2301).

8768 — Ibn Masʿūd narrated: The first sūrah in which a prostration was revealed was “By the star” [al-Najm]. He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ prostrated, and those behind him prostrated, except for one man whom I saw take a handful of dust and prostrate upon it. I later saw him killed as a disbeliever, and he was Umayyah ibn Khalaf.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4863) and Muslim (105:576).

8769 — Ibrāhīm narrated: Al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays wanted to appoint Masrūq to a position, so ʿUmārah ibn ʿUqbah said to him, “Will you appoint a man from the remnants of the killers of ʿUthmān?” Masrūq said to him, “ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated to us—and in our view he was reliable in ḥadīth—that when the Prophet ﷺ wanted to kill your father, he said, ‘Who will take care of the children?’ He said, ‘The Fire.’ So I am pleased for you with what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was pleased for you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2686) and al-Ḥākim (2/124).

8770 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ accepted ransom for the prisoners of Badr, and the ransom for each man among them was four thousand. He killed ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ before the ransom. ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib stood over him and killed him as a captive. He said, “Who will take care of the children, O Messenger of Allah?” He said, “The Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq (9394), and through him al-Ṭabarānī (11/406-407).
The meaning of the Prophet’s ﷺ statement, “The Fire,” is: You are destined for the Fire. As for the children, leave them, for Allah will take care of them. This is because ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ was the wretched one who placed the entrails of a slaughtered camel on the back of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was praying in the House of Allah. It was also said that “the Fire” referred to that time, because ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ and his children were in a state of disbelief then; when his children later accepted Islam, they exited this threat, and the statement of Masrūq would not be in its proper place.
I say: None of the prisoners was killed as a captive except ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ and al-Naḍr ibn al-Ḥārith. As for Ṭuʿaymah ibn ʿAdī, he was killed in the battle. This is what Abū ʿUbayd said in al-Amwāl (171). As for what is narrated about Ṭuʿaymah ibn ʿAdī being killed as a captive, all of it is weak because it is mursal.

8771 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated: We were with ʿUmar between Makkah and Madinah, and we looked for the crescent moon. I was a man with sharp eyesight, so I saw it, while no one else claimed to have seen it. I kept saying to ʿUmar, “Do you not see it?” But he did not see it. ʿUmar said, “I will see it while reclining on my bed.” Then he began telling us about the people of Badr and said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had shown us the places where the people of Badr would fall the day before, saying, “This is the place where so-and-so will fall tomorrow, if Allah wills.” ʿUmar said: By the One Who sent him with the truth, they did not miss the boundaries that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had marked. He said: They were placed in a well, one upon another. Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went until he reached them and said, “O so-and-so son of so-and-so, and O so-and-so son of so-and-so, have you found what Allah and His Messenger promised you to be true? Indeed, I have found what Allah promised me to be true.” ʿUmar said, “O Messenger of Allah, how do you speak to bodies that have no souls?” He said, “You do not hear what I say better than they do, except that they are unable to reply to me.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (76:2873).

8772 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ faced the House and supplicated against six men from Quraysh, among them Abū Jahl, Umayyah ibn Khalaf, ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah, Shaybah ibn Rabīʿah, and ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ. By Allah, I saw them lying dead at Badr, changed by the sun, and it was a hot day.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3960) and Muslim (110:1794). The wording is Muslim’s.
His statement, “He supplicated against six men from Quraysh,” mentions only five. The sixth was al-Walīd ibn ʿUtbah, as in al-Bukhārī.

8773 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated: The chiefs of Quraysh gathered in al-Ḥijr and made a pact by al-Lāt, al-ʿUzzā, Manāt the third, the other, Nāʾilah, and Isāf: “If we see Muhammad, we will rise against him as one man and will not leave him until we kill him.” His daughter Fāṭimah, may Allah be pleased with her, came crying until she entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “These chiefs of Quraysh have made a pact against you. If they see you, they will rise against you and kill you. There is not a man among them except that he has known his share of your blood.” He said, “My daughter, bring me water for wuḍūʾ.” He performed wuḍūʾ, then entered the mosque upon them. When they saw him, they said, “There he is.” They lowered their gazes, their chins fell to their chests, and they were fixed in their seats. None of them raised his gaze to him, and not one of them stood up to him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came until he stood over their heads, took a handful of dust, and said, “May the faces be disfigured,” then threw it at them. No man among them was struck by a pebble from that dust except that he was killed as a disbeliever on the Day of Badr.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2672), Ibn Ḥibbān (6502), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (1/163).
