8774 — Abū Ṭalḥah narrated that on the Day of Badr, the Prophet of Allah ﷺ commanded that twenty-four men from the chiefs of Quraysh be thrown into one of the wells of Badr, an evil and foul well. Whenever he prevailed over a people, he would remain in the open ground for three nights. When it was the third day at Badr, he ordered his mount to be prepared and its saddle tied. Then he walked, and his Companions followed him, saying, “We do not think he is setting out except for some need of his.” He came to the edge of the well and began calling them by their names and the names of their fathers: “O so-and-so son of so-and-so, and O so-and-so son of so-and-so, would it have pleased you if you had obeyed Allah and His Messenger? Indeed, we have found what our Lord promised us to be true, so have you found what your Lord promised to be true?” ʿUmar said, “O Messenger of Allah, what are you saying to bodies that have no souls?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “By the One in Whose Hand is the soul of Muhammad, you do not hear what I say better than they do.” Qatādah said: Allah brought them to life until He made them hear his statement as rebuke, humiliation, punishment, regret, and remorse.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3976) and Muslim (78:2875). The context is al-Bukhārī’s.
His phrase “at the edge of the rakī” means the edge of the well. “Rakī,” with fatḥah on the rāʾ, kasrah on the kāf, and a doubled final letter, refers to a well before it has been lined with stones.
His phrase “in one of the ṭawī wells of Badr”: a ṭawī is a well that has been lined and built with stones so that it remains firm and does not collapse.
The two narrations are reconciled by saying that it had been lined with stones, then it collapsed and became like an unlined well. See al-Fatḥ (7/302).
His phrase “he remained in the open ground for three nights”: al-ʿarṣah means a courtyard or a wide open place.
The number of polytheists killed was seventy, so perhaps the others were buried in other places.

8775 — Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ left the dead of Badr for three days. Then he came to them, stood over them, and called them, saying, “O Abū Jahl ibn Hishām, O Umayyah ibn Khalaf, O ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah, O Shaybah ibn Rabīʿah, have you not found what your Lord promised to be true? Indeed, I have found what my Lord promised me to be true.” ʿUmar heard the Prophet’s ﷺ statement and said, “O Messenger of Allah, how can they hear and how can they answer when they have decayed?” He said, “By the One in Whose Hand is my soul, you do not hear what I say better than they do, but they are unable to answer.” Then he ordered that they be dragged and thrown into the well of Badr.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (77:2874).
His phrase “they were thrown into the well of Badr”: Umayyah ibn Khalaf was not in the well, because he was large-bodied, swelled up, and his limbs came apart after being dragged. So stones and dirt were thrown over him until he was covered. However, he was near the well, so he was called among those who were called.
The remaining ḥadīths and the conclusion regarding the hearing of the dead will come in the tafsīr of Sūrat al-Ṭūr.

8776 — Abū Isḥāq narrated: I heard al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib, may Allah be pleased with him, narrating: On the Day of Uḥud, the Prophet ﷺ appointed ʿAbdullāh ibn Jubayr over the foot soldiers, and they were fifty men. He said, “If you see birds snatching us away, do not leave this place of yours until I send for you. And if you see that we have defeated the people and trampled them, do not leave until I send for you.” They defeated them. He said: By Allah, I saw the women running quickly, their anklets and legs visible, lifting their garments. The companions of Ibn Jubayr said, “The spoils, O people! The spoils! Your companions have prevailed, so what are you waiting for?” ʿAbdullāh ibn Jubayr said, “Have you forgotten what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to you?” They said, “By Allah, we will go to the people and take from the spoils.” When they went to them, their faces were turned back, and they came fleeing in defeat. That was when the Messenger was calling them from behind them, and no one remained with the Prophet ﷺ except twelve men. They inflicted upon us seventy casualties. The Prophet ﷺ and his Companions had inflicted one hundred and forty upon the polytheists on the Day of Badr: seventy prisoners and seventy killed. The ḥadīth.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Jihād wa al-Siyar (3039), from ʿAmr ibn Khālid: Zuhayr narrated to us; Abū Isḥāq, that is al-Sabīʿī, narrated to us: I heard al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrating, and he mentioned it.

8777 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said on the Day of Badr, “Whoever among Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib you are able to capture, then capture them, for they came out unwillingly.”
Authentic: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (676).

8778 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated: A short man from the Anṣār came with al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib as a prisoner. Al-ʿAbbās said, “O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, this man did not capture me. Rather, I was captured by a bald-fronted man with one of the most beautiful faces among people, riding a piebald horse. I do not see him among the people.” The Anṣārī said, “I captured him, O Messenger of Allah.” He said, “Be silent, for Allah Most High supported you with a noble angel.” ʿAlī said: We captured from Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib: al-ʿAbbās, ʿAqīl, and Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (948).

8779 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated: When they captured the prisoners, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to Abū Bakr and ʿUmar, “What do you think concerning these prisoners?” Abū Bakr said, “O Prophet of Allah, they are our cousins and kin. I think you should take ransom from them, so it may be a source of strength for us against the disbelievers, and perhaps Allah will guide them to Islam.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “What do you think, O Ibn al-Khaṭṭāb?” I said, “No, by Allah, O Messenger of Allah. I do not see what Abū Bakr sees. Rather, I think you should give us power over them so we strike their necks. Give ʿAlī power over ʿAqīl so he may strike his neck, and give me power over so-and-so, a relative of ʿUmar, so I may strike his neck, for these are the leaders and chiefs of disbelief.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ inclined to what Abū Bakr said and did not incline to what I said. The next day, I came and found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr sitting and weeping. I said, “O Messenger of Allah, tell me what makes you and your companion weep. If I find reason to weep, I will weep; and if I do not find reason to weep, I will make myself weep because of your weeping.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “I weep because of what was presented to me regarding your companions taking ransom. Their punishment was presented to me closer than this tree,” a tree near the Prophet of Allah ﷺ. Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed: “It is not for a prophet to have captives until he has thoroughly subdued the enemy in the land” [al-Anfāl: 67] up to His saying: “So eat of what you have taken as spoils, lawful and good” [al-Anfāl: 69]. Thus Allah made the spoils lawful for them.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (58:1763).

8780 — Ibn ʿUmar narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ consulted Abū Bakr concerning the prisoners, and he said, “They are your people and your kin, so let them go.” He consulted ʿUmar, and he said, “Kill them.” He said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ accepted ransom for them. Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed: “It is not for a prophet to have captives until he has thoroughly subdued the enemy in the land,” up to His saying: “So eat of what you have taken as spoils, lawful and good” [al-Anfāl: 67-69]. He said: The Prophet ﷺ met ʿUmar and said, “A calamity nearly struck us because of differing from you.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/329).

8781 — Anas narrated, mentioning a man narrating from al-Ḥasan, who said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ consulted the people concerning the prisoners on the Day of Badr. He said, “Allah has given you power over them.” ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb stood and said, “O Messenger of Allah, strike their necks.” The Prophet ﷺ turned away from him. Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned and said, “O people, Allah has given you power over them, and they were only your brothers yesterday.” ʿUmar stood and said, “O Messenger of Allah, strike their necks.” The Prophet ﷺ turned away from him. Then the Prophet ﷺ returned and said the same to the people. Abū Bakr stood and said, “O Messenger of Allah, perhaps you should pardon them and accept ransom from them.” Then the grief that had been on the face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ disappeared. He pardoned them and accepted ransom from them. Then Allah revealed: “Had it not been for a decree from Allah that preceded, you would have been touched for what you took by a great punishment” [al-Anfāl: 68].
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (13555).

8782 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated: The Prophet ﷺ accepted ransom for the prisoners of Badr, and the ransom for each one of them was four thousand.
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq (9394) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (11/406-407).

8783 — ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr narrated: Quraysh had mourned loudly over their dead, then regretted it and said, “Do not mourn over them, lest Muhammad and his Companions hear of that and rejoice over your misfortune.” Among the prisoners was Abū Wadāʿah ibn Ṣubayrah al-Sahmī. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “He has in Makkah a son who is a clever merchant and wealthy. It is as if you will soon see him coming to you with the ransom of his father.” When Quraysh said what they said regarding ransom, al-Muṭṭalib said, “By Allah, you have spoken the truth. If you do that, he will gain the upper hand over you.” Then he slipped away at night, came to Madinah, and ransomed his father for four thousand dirhams.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (14/203-204).

8784 — Anas narrated that some men from the Anṣār asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for permission and said, “Permit us to leave the ransom of our sister’s son, al-ʿAbbās.” He said, “By Allah, you will not leave even a single dirham of it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4018).

8785 — ʿĀʾishah narrated: Al-ʿAbbās said, “I was a Muslim, O Messenger of Allah.” He said, “Allah knows best about your Islam. If it is as you say, Allah will reward you. So ransom yourself, your two nephews Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith and ʿAqīl ibn Abī Ṭālib, and your ally ʿUtbah ibn ʿAmr.” He said, “I do not have that, O Messenger of Allah.” He said, “Then where is the wealth that you and Umm al-Faḍl buried, when you said to her: ‘If I am struck down, this wealth is for my sons: al-Faḍl, ʿAbdullāh, and Qutham’?” He said, “By Allah, O Messenger of Allah, I testify that you are the Messenger of Allah. This is something no one knew except me and Umm al-Faḍl. So count for me, O Messenger of Allah, the twenty ounces of wealth that you took from me.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “I will not.” So al-ʿAbbās ransomed himself, his two nephews, and his ally. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed: “O Prophet, say to whoever is in your hands of the prisoners: If Allah knows any good in your hearts, He will give you better than what was taken from you, and He will forgive you. And Allah is Forgiving, Merciful” [al-Anfāl: 70]. He said: In place of the twenty ounces, He gave me in Islam twenty slaves, each of whom had wealth in his hand with which he traded, along with what I hope for of the forgiveness of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/324) and al-Bayhaqī (6/322).

8786 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated: Some of the prisoners on the Day of Badr had no ransom, so the Messenger of Allah ﷺ made their ransom that they teach the children of the Anṣār writing. He said: One day a boy came crying to his father. He said, “What is the matter with you?” He said, “My teacher hit me.” He said, “The wicked one is seeking revenge for Badr. By Allah, you will never go to him again.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2216) and al-Bayhaqī (6/322).
The word “dhahl” means revenge, enmity, or resentment.

8787 — ʿĀʾishah narrated: When the people of Makkah sent ransom for their prisoners, Zaynab sent wealth as ransom for Abū al-ʿĀṣ, and she sent with it a necklace of hers that had belonged to Khadījah, which she had given her when she married Abū al-ʿĀṣ. She said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw it, he felt deep tenderness for her and said, “Would you consider releasing her prisoner for her and returning to her what belongs to her?” They said, “Yes.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had taken from him, or he had promised him, that he would allow Zaynab to come to him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent Zayd ibn Ḥārithah and a man from the Anṣār and said, “Be in the valley of Yaʾjaj until Zaynab passes by you, then accompany her until you bring her to me.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2692), Aḥmad (26362), and Ibn al-Jārūd (1090).

8788 — Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said concerning the prisoners of Badr: “If al-Muṭʿim ibn ʿAdī had been alive and then spoken to me regarding these foul ones, I would have released them for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4024).
The phrase “al-natnā” is the plural of “natin,” with the letter nūn, and it refers to the prisoners of Badr from the polytheists.
Al-Muṭʿim, the father of Jubayr, was among those under whose protection the Prophet ﷺ entered when he returned from al-Ṭāʾif. Al-Muṭʿim commanded four of his sons to wear weapons, and each of them stood at a corner of the Kaʿbah. When Quraysh heard of that, they said to him, “You are a man whose protection is not violated.” Al-Muṭʿim was also among the strongest of those who worked to annul the document that Quraysh had written against Banū Hāshim and the Muslims with them when they were besieged in the mountain pass.
Al-Muṭʿim ibn ʿAdī died before the Battle of Badr, when he was over ninety years old.
The ḥadīths in the previous chapters contain evidence that the ruler has discretion regarding adult prisoners: if he wishes, he may show favor to them and release them without ransom; if he wishes, he may ransom them for a known amount of wealth; and if he wishes, he may kill them.

8789 — Al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām narrated: On the Day of Badr, one hundred shares were allocated for the Muhājirūn.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4027).
Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said: “The number mentioned here differs from the ḥadīth of al-Barāʾ, in which the Muhājirūn were a little over sixty. They may be reconciled by saying that the ḥadīth of al-Barāʾ refers to those who physically witnessed it, while the ḥadīth of this chapter refers to those who witnessed it physically and legally. It is also possible that the first number refers to the free men, and the second includes their freedmen and followers.” Al-Fatḥ (7/326).

8790 — Qays ibn Abī Ḥāzim narrated: The stipend of the people of Badr was five thousand, and ʿUmar said, “I will certainly give them preference over those who came after them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4022).

8791 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated: I had a she-camel from my share of the spoils on the Day of Badr, and the Prophet ﷺ had given me from what Allah had granted him from the khums that day. The full ḥadīth.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4003) and Muslim (1979).

8792 — Abū Ṭalḥah narrated: Whenever the Prophet ﷺ prevailed over a people, he would remain in the open ground for three nights. When it was the third day at Badr, he ordered that his mount be prepared and its saddle tied. Then he walked, and his Companions followed him, saying, “We do not think he is setting out except for some need of his,” until he stood at the edge of the well and began calling them. Then he mentioned the ḥadīth.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3976) and Muslim (2875).

8793 — Abū Umāmah ibn Sahl narrated: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ finished at Badr, he sent two bearers of glad tidings to the people of Madinah. He sent Zayd ibn Ḥārithah to the people of the lower part, and he sent ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah to the people of the upper part, giving them glad tidings of Allah’s victory for His Prophet ﷺ. Zayd ibn Ḥārithah arrived upon his son Usāmah while he was leveling the soil over Ruqayyah, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. It was said to him, “That is your father who has arrived.” Usāmah said: I came, and he was standing among the people saying, “ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah, Shaybah ibn Rabīʿah, Abū Jahl ibn Hishām, Nabīh, Munabbih, and Umayyah ibn Khalaf have been killed.” I said, “My father, is this true?” He said, “Yes, by Allah, my son.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/217-218).

8794 — Usāmah ibn Zayd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ left ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān and Usāmah ibn Zayd behind to look after Ruqayyah, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, during the days of Badr. Then Zayd ibn Ḥārithah came on al-ʿAḍbāʾ, the camel of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, with the glad tidings.
Usāmah said: I heard the cry of alarm, so I went out, and there was Zayd, who had come with the glad tidings. By Allah, I did not believe it until I saw the prisoners. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ allocated a share for ʿUthmān.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalāʾil (3/130).
“Al-hayʿah” means a cry of alarm.

8795 — Abū ʿAzīz ibn ʿUmayr, the brother of Muṣʿab ibn ʿUmayr, narrated: I was among the prisoners on the Day of Badr, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Treat the prisoners well.” I was with a group of the Anṣār, and whenever they brought their lunch and dinner, they would eat dates and feed me bread because of the instruction of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to them.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (22/393).

8796 — Miqsam, the freedman of ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Ḥārith, narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās that he heard him say regarding: “Not equal are those believers who sit behind” [al-Nisāʾ: 95]: this refers to Badr and those who went out to Badr.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3954).

8797 — Ḥumayd narrated: I heard Anas say: Ḥārithah was struck on the Day of Badr while he was a young boy. His mother came to the Prophet ﷺ and said, “O Messenger of Allah, you know the status of Ḥārithah with me. If he is in Paradise, I will be patient and seek reward; but if it is otherwise, you will see what I do.” He said, “Woe to you—or may you be bereaved! Is it only one Paradise? It is many Gardens, and he is in the Garden of al-Firdaws.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3982).
His phrase “Ḥārithah was struck on the Day of Badr”: he is Ibn Surāqah ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAdī al-Anṣārī. His father Surāqah was a Companion and was martyred on the Day of Ḥunayn, and his mother was al-Rubayyiʿ bint al-Naḍr, the paternal aunt of Anas ibn Mālik.
His phrase “wayḥak” is a word of mercy; it is also said to be a rebuke.
His phrase “or may you be bereaved” means: may you lose a loved one.

8798 — Ḥafṣah reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I truly hope that, if Allah Most High wills, none of those who witnessed Badr and al-Ḥudaybiyyah will enter the Fire.”
She said:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, has Allah not said:
‘And there is none of you except that he will come to it. This is upon your Lord an inevitable decree.’
[Maryam: 71]?”
He replied:
“Have you not heard Him say:
‘Then We will save those who were mindful of Allah and leave the wrongdoers therein on their knees.’
[Maryam: 72]?”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (4281) and Aḥmad (26440), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4800).

8799 — Shaqīq narrated that Ibn Masʿūd told him: The eighteen Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ who were killed on the Day of Badr—Allah placed their souls in Paradise inside green birds that roam freely in Paradise. While they were in that state, your Lord looked upon them and said, “My servants, what do you desire?” They said, “Our Lord, is there anything above this?” He said again, “My servants, what do you desire?” On the fourth time they said, “That our souls be returned to our bodies so that we may be killed as we were killed.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (10/249) and Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Jihād (198).

8800 — Ibn Shihāb narrated: When the defeated remnants of the polytheists returned to Makkah, ʿUmayr ibn Wahb came and sat with Ṣafwān ibn Umayyah in al-Ḥijr. Ṣafwān said, “May Allah make life ugly after those killed at Badr.” He said, “Yes. By Allah, there is no good in life after them. Were it not for a debt upon me that I cannot repay, and dependents whom I would leave with nothing, I would travel to Muhammad and kill him if my eyes could fill with sight of him. I have an excuse with him by which I can approach him: I will say, ‘I came because of this son of mine who is a prisoner.’”
Ṣafwān rejoiced and said to him, “Your debt is upon me, and your dependents will be like my dependents in maintenance. I will not be able to provide anything for mine while being unable to provide for them.” They agreed. Ṣafwān mounted him, prepared him, and ordered that a sword of Ḥimyar be polished and poisoned. ʿUmayr said to Ṣafwān, “Conceal my news for some days.”
ʿUmayr came to Madinah, dismounted at the door of the mosque, tied his mount, took the sword, and headed toward the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. ʿUmar saw him while he was among a group of the Anṣār, became alarmed, and entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, saying, “O Prophet of Allah, do not trust him with anything.” He said, “Bring him in to me.” ʿUmar went out and ordered his companions to enter upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and guard against ʿUmayr. ʿUmar and ʿUmayr came forward until they entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, while ʿUmayr had his sword. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to ʿUmar, “Step back from him.” When ʿUmayr drew near, he said, “May your morning be pleasant.” This was the greeting of the people of Jāhiliyyah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has honored us above your greeting, and has made our greeting the greeting of the people of Paradise, and it is salām.” ʿUmayr said, “Your time with it is recent.” He said to him, “What brought you, O ʿUmayr?” He said, “I came because of my prisoner who is with you, so deal kindly with us concerning our prisoners, for you are kin and family.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “What is the matter with the sword around your neck?” He said, “May Allah make these swords ugly. Did they benefit us at all? I only forgot it around my neck when I dismounted.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Tell me the truth: what brought you?” He said, “I came only to seek my prisoner.” He said, “Then what did you stipulate with Ṣafwān in al-Ḥijr?” ʿUmayr was startled and said, “What did I stipulate with him?” He said, “You undertook to kill me for him on condition that he support your children and pay off your debt. Allah will come between you and that.” ʿUmayr said, “I testify that you are the Messenger of Allah, and I testify that there is no god but Allah. O Messenger of Allah, we used to deny you regarding revelation and what came to you from heaven. This conversation was between me and Ṣafwān in al-Ḥijr exactly as you said; no one knew of it, and Allah informed you of it. Praise be to Allah who brought me to this path.” The Muslims rejoiced over him, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him, “Sit, O ʿUmayr, so we may keep you company.” He said to his Companions, “Teach your brother the Qur’an.” And he released his prisoner for him.
ʿUmayr said, “Permit me, O Messenger of Allah, to return to Quraysh and call them to Allah and to Islam, perhaps Allah will guide them.” He permitted him, and he returned to Makkah. Ṣafwān had been saying to Quraysh, “Rejoice in a victory that will make you forget the event of Badr.” He kept asking every rider who came from Madinah, “Has anything happened there?” until a man came to him and said, “ʿUmayr has accepted Islam.” The polytheists cursed him. Ṣafwān said, “It is upon me before Allah that I will never speak to him and will never benefit him with anything.” Then ʿUmayr came and called them to Islam and advised them with all his effort, and many people accepted Islam because of him.
Good: Narrated by Mūsā ibn ʿUqbah in his Maghāzī as in al-Iṣābah (7/531-533), and by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (17/63).

8801 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ defeated Quraysh on the Day of Badr and returned to Madinah, he gathered the Jews in the market of Banū Qaynuqāʿ and said, “O assembly of Jews, accept Islam before there strikes you the like of what struck Quraysh.” They said, “O Muhammad, do not be deceived by yourself because you killed a group of Quraysh who were inexperienced and did not know fighting. If you fought us, you would know that we are truly the people, and that you have not met the like of us.” So Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed concerning that: “Say to those who disbelieve: You will be overcome,” up to His saying: “One group fighting in the path of Allah and another disbelieving” [Āl ʿImrān: 12-13], regarding Badr.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3001).

8802 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Anṣārī narrated: I saw the Prophet ﷺ in the Battle of Anmār praying voluntary prayer on his mount while facing toward the east.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4140).

8803 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Anṣārī narrated: We went out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the Battle of Banū Anmār. Jābir said: While I was resting beneath a tree, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came. I said, “O Messenger of Allah, come to the shade.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ dismounted. I went to a sack of ours and searched in it for something. I found in it a small cucumber, broke it, and brought it near to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He said, “Where did you get this?” I said, “We brought it with us from Madinah, O Messenger of Allah.” Jābir said: We had a companion with us whom we had prepared to go and graze our riding animals. I prepared him, and he turned away to go to the riding animals, wearing two old worn cloaks of his. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at him and said, “Does he not have two garments other than these?” I said, “Yes, O Messenger of Allah. He has two garments in the chest; I clothed him with them.” He said, “Call him and tell him to wear them.” I called him, and he wore them. Then he turned away to go. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “What is the matter with him? May Allah strike his neck. Is this not better for him?” The man heard him and said, “O Messenger of Allah, in the path of Allah?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “In the path of Allah.” He said: The man was then killed in the path of Allah.
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik (1), Ibn Ḥibbān (5418), al-Bazzār, Kashf al-Astār (2963), and al-Ḥākim (4/183).
His phrase “jarw qiththāʾ”: “jarw” here means small cucumbers. “Al-ʿaybah” is like a chest in which clothes are placed.

8804 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Who will deal with Kaʿb ibn al-Ashraf? He has harmed Allah and His Messenger.” Muḥammad ibn Maslamah stood and said, “O Messenger of Allah, would you like me to kill him?” He said, “Yes.” He said, “Then permit me to say something.” He said, “Say it.” Muḥammad ibn Maslamah came to him and said, “This man has asked us for charity, and he has burdened us. I have come to you seeking a loan.” He said, “And more than that, by Allah, you will grow tired of him.” He said, “We have followed him, and we do not wish to leave him until we see where his matter will end. We want you to lend us a wasq or two.” ʿAmr narrated to us more than once and did not mention “a wasq or two,” or I said to him, “Does it contain ‘a wasq or two’?” He said, “I think it contains ‘a wasq or two.’” He said, “Yes, give me collateral.” They said, “What do you want?” He said, “Pledge your women to me.” They said, “How can we pledge our women to you when you are the most handsome of the Arabs?” He said, “Then pledge your sons to me.” They said, “How can we pledge our sons to you, then one of them would be insulted and it would be said: He was pledged for a wasq or two? This would be shame upon us. But we will pledge our weapons to you.” Sufyān said: Meaning weapons. He made an appointment to come to him. He came to him at night with Abū Nāʾilah, who was Kaʿb’s brother through nursing. He called them to the fortress, and Kaʿb came down to them. His wife said to him, “Where are you going out at this hour?” He said, “It is only Muḥammad ibn Maslamah and my brother Abū Nāʾilah.” Someone other than ʿAmr said: She said, “I hear a voice as if blood is dripping from it.” He said, “It is only my brother Muḥammad ibn Maslamah and my foster brother Abū Nāʾilah. If a noble man were called to a stab at night, he would answer.” Muḥammad ibn Maslamah entered with two men. It was said to Sufyān: Did ʿAmr name them? He said: He named some of them. ʿAmr said: Two men came with him. Someone other than ʿAmr said: Abū ʿAbs ibn Jabr, al-Ḥārith ibn Aws, and ʿAbbād ibn Bishr. ʿAmr said: Two men came with him. He said, “When he comes, I will take hold of his hair and smell it. When you see that I have gained control of his head, then strike him.” And he said once: “Then I will let you smell it.” Kaʿb came down to them wearing his garment, and the scent of perfume was coming from him. He said, “I have never smelled a scent better than today’s.” Someone other than ʿAmr said: He said, “I have the most fragrant and most perfect women of the Arabs.” ʿAmr said: He said, “Do you permit me to smell your head?” He said, “Yes.” He smelled it, then let his companions smell it. Then he said, “Do you permit me?” He said, “Yes.” When he had gained control of him, he said, “Now!” So they killed him. Then they came to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4037) and Muslim (1801).
His killing was in Rabīʿ al-Awwal of the third year, as Ibn Saʿd said in al-Ṭabaqāt (2/31).

8805 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ walked with them to Baqīʿ al-Gharqad, then sent them forth and said, “Go in the name of Allah.” And he said, “O Allah, help them.” Meaning the group he sent to Kaʿb ibn al-Ashraf.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2391), al-Ṭabarānī (11/222), al-Bazzār, Kashf al-Astār (1801, 1802), and al-Ḥākim (2/98).

8806 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn Kaʿb ibn Mālik narrated from his father, who was one of the three whose repentance was accepted: Kaʿb ibn al-Ashraf used to satirize the Prophet ﷺ and incite the disbelievers of Quraysh against him. When the Prophet ﷺ came to Madinah, its people were mixed: among them were Muslims, polytheists who worshiped idols, and Jews. They used to harm the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions, so Allah, Mighty and Majestic, commanded His Prophet to be patient and pardon. Concerning them Allah revealed: “And you will surely hear from those who were given the Scripture before you…” [Āl ʿImrān: 186]. When Kaʿb ibn al-Ashraf refused to stop harming the Prophet ﷺ, the Prophet ﷺ ordered Saʿd ibn Muʿādh to send a group to kill him. So he sent Muḥammad ibn Maslamah, and he mentioned the story of his killing. When they killed him, the Jews and polytheists were terrified, and they came to the Prophet ﷺ in the morning and said, “Our companion was attacked at night and killed.” The Prophet ﷺ mentioned to them what he used to say, and the Prophet ﷺ called them to write a document between him and them to which they would adhere. So the Prophet ﷺ wrote a document between himself, them, and the Muslims in general.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3000).
His phrase “from his father” means his grandfather, because his grandfather Kaʿb ibn Mālik was one of the three.

8807 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent a group to Abū Rāfiʿ. ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAtīk entered his house at night while he was sleeping and killed him.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4038).

8808 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent some men from the Anṣār to Abū Rāfiʿ the Jew and appointed ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAtīk over them. Abū Rāfiʿ used to harm the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and support others against him, and he had a fortress in the land of the Ḥijāz. When they came near him, the sun had set and the people had returned with their livestock. ʿAbdullāh said to his companions, “Sit in your place. I am going and will act gently with the gatekeeper; perhaps I will be able to enter.” He came forward until he drew near to the gate, then covered himself with his garment as if relieving himself, while the people had entered. The gatekeeper called out to him, “O ʿAbdullāh, if you want to enter, then enter, for I intend to close the gate.” So I entered and hid. When the people had entered, he closed the gate and hung the keys on a peg. He said: I went to the keys, took them, and opened the gate. Abū Rāfiʿ used to have people sit with him at night, and he was in an upper chamber of his. When those who had spent the evening with him left, I climbed up to him, and every time I opened a door, I locked it behind me from the inside. I said: If the people become aware of me, they will not reach me until I have killed him. I reached him, and he was in a dark room among his family. I did not know where he was in the room, so I said, “O Abū Rāfiʿ.” He said, “Who is this?” I moved toward the voice and struck him with the sword while I was startled, but I did not do anything effective. He cried out, so I left the room and stayed not far away. Then I entered upon him again and said, “What is that sound, O Abū Rāfiʿ?” He said, “May your mother be bereaved; a man in the house struck me with a sword just now.” He said: I struck him with a blow that wounded him severely but did not kill him. Then I placed the tip of the sword in his belly until it reached his back, and I knew that I had killed him. I began opening the doors one by one until I reached a stairway of his. I placed my foot down, thinking I had reached the ground, but I fell on a moonlit night and broke my leg. I tied it with a turban, then went and sat at the gate. I said: I will not leave tonight until I know whether I killed him. When the rooster crowed, the announcer of death stood on the wall and said, “I announce the death of Abū Rāfiʿ, the merchant of the people of the Ḥijāz.” I went to my companions and said, “Escape, for Allah has killed Abū Rāfiʿ.” I came to the Prophet ﷺ and told him. He said, “Stretch out your leg.” I stretched out my leg, and he wiped it, and it was as though I had never complained of it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4039).

8809 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik narrated: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ instructed us, while we were at Khaybar, not to kill a child or a woman.
Authentic: Narrated by Isḥāq ibn Rāhwayh, al-Maṭālib al-ʿĀliyah (1959).

8810 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “I saw as if I were inside a fortified coat of armor, and I saw cattle being slaughtered. I interpreted the fortified armor as Madinah, and the cattle as—by Allah, good.” He said: He said to his Companions, “If we remain in Madinah, then if they enter upon us there, we will fight them.” They said, “O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, they never entered upon us there in Jāhiliyyah, so how could they enter upon us there in Islam?” ʿAffān said in his narration: He said, “Then it is your affair.” He put on his armor. The Anṣār then said, “We have rejected the opinion of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” They came and said, “O Messenger of Allah, then do as you wish.” He said, “It is not for a prophet, once he has put on his armor, to take it off until he fights.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (14787) and al-Nasāʾī in al-Kubrā (7600).

8811 — Abū Mūsā narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I saw in a dream that I was emigrating from Makkah to a land filled with date palms. At first I thought it was al-Yamāmah or Hajar, but it turned out to be Madinah, Yathrib.
In that same dream I shook my sword, and its blade broke. I interpreted that as what befell the believers on the Day of Uḥud.
Then I shook it again, and it returned even better than before. I interpreted that as the victory Allah granted and the reunion of the believers.
I also saw cattle—and Allah is the source of all good. They represented the believers on the Day of Uḥud, while the good referred to the goodness Allah later brought and the reward for truthfulness that He granted us after Badr.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3622) and Muslim (2272).

8812 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ awarded himself the sword Dhū al-Fiqār on the Day of Badr.
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
This was the sword concerning which he saw the dream before Uḥud.
When the polytheists came for the Battle of Uḥud, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ preferred to remain inside Madinah and fight them there.
But some people who had not witnessed Badr said:
“O Messenger of Allah, take us out to meet them at Uḥud so that we may attain the virtue attained by the people of Badr.”
They continued urging the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until he put on his armor.
Then they regretted it and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, remain here, for the decision is yours.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“It is not appropriate for a prophet to remove his armor after putting it on until Allah judges between him and his enemy.”
He also said:
“I saw myself inside a fortified coat of armor, and I interpreted it as Madinah.
I saw that I had a ram riding behind me, and I interpreted it as the commander of the army.
I saw that my sword Dhū al-Fiqār had a notch in it, and I interpreted it as a calamity that would befall you.
I also saw cattle being slaughtered—and the cattle, by Allah, are good.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/128–129), and in abbreviated form by al-Tirmidhī (1561), Ibn Mājah (2808), and Aḥmad (2445).

8813 — Al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ wore two coats of armor on the Day of Uḥud.
When he attempted to climb a large rock, he was unable to do so.
Ṭalḥah sat beneath him, allowing the Prophet ﷺ to step upon him until he reached the top of the rock.
Al-Zubayr said:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Ṭalḥah has secured Paradise.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1692, 3738), Aḥmad (1417), Ibn Ḥibbān (6979), and al-Ḥākim (3/374).
The statement “Ṭalḥah has secured Paradise” means that he performed a deed deserving Paradise.

8814 — Al-Sāʾib ibn Yazīd narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ wore two coats of armor together on the Day of Uḥud.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (15722), al-Shāfiʿī in al-Umm (4/252), and Saʿīd ibn Manṣūr (2858).

8815 — Anas narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ took a sword on the Day of Uḥud and said:
“Who will take this sword from me with its due right?”
Many stretched out their hands, each saying:
“I will! I will!”
He then asked:
“Who will take it with its due right?”
The people hesitated.
Then Simāk ibn Kharashah, Abū Dujānah, said:
“I will take it with its due right.”
He took it and split open the heads of the polytheists with it.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2470).
Abū Dujānah was a courageous warrior. Whenever he tied on his distinctive red headband, people knew he was entering fierce combat. After receiving the Prophet’s ﷺ sword, he put on that red headband and strode proudly between the battle lines.

8816 — Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
ʿAlī came carrying his sword on the Day of Uḥud after it had become bent.
He said to Fāṭimah:
“Take this sword with praise, for it has satisfied me.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“If you have fought well with your sword, then Sahl ibn Ḥunayf, Abū Dujānah, ʿĀṣim ibn Thābit al-Aflaḥ, and al-Ḥārith ibn al-Ṣimmah have also fought excellently.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (3/24) and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (3/283–284).

8817 — Abū ʿUqbah, a freedman of Persian origin, said:
I fought with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at Uḥud.
I struck one of the polytheists and said:
“Take that from me—I am the Persian youth!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ turned toward me and said:
“Why did you not say: ‘Take that from me—I am the Anṣārī youth’?”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (5123), Ibn Mājah (2784), and Aḥmad (22515).

8818 — ʿĀʾishah narrated:
When the Day of Uḥud came, the polytheists were initially defeated.
Then Iblīs—may Allah curse him—shouted:
“O servants of Allah, those behind you!”
So the front ranks of the Muslims turned back toward those behind them, and they began fighting one another.
Ḥudhayfah then saw that one of those being attacked was his own father, al-Yamān.
He cried out:
“O servants of Allah! My father! My father!”
But, by Allah, they did not stop until they killed him.
Ḥudhayfah then said:
“May Allah forgive you.”
ʿUrwah said:
By Allah, there remained in Ḥudhayfah a remnant of goodness until he met Allah.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4065).

8819 — Al-Zubayr said: “By Allah, that day I could see the women’s anklets as they ran with their garments raised after the enemy had been routed. I saw nothing standing between us and capturing them. We thought that the enemy had been killed and that none of them would ever return to us. The standard-bearers had been struck down, [but they remained steadfast around the standard until it passed to an Abyssinian slave of theirs named Sawab. Then Sawab was killed and the standard fell]. No one among Allah Most High’s creation would approach it until ʿAmrah bint ʿAlqamah al-Harithiyyah sprang toward it, raised it for them, and the people rallied around it.
“By Allah, we remained in that condition, having gained the upper hand over them and defeated them, when the archers disobeyed the command of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. They went down toward the camp when they saw it left exposed after we had driven the enemy away from it. They desired the spoils and abandoned their covenant with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and began taking the belongings. The cavalry then came upon us from behind and crushed our ranks. The people turned back in flight, and someone—whom they believed to be Satan—shouted, ‘Muhammad has been killed!’ The people were struck with tremendous alarm and trampled over one another. They became three groups: one-third wounded, one-third killed, and one-third fleeing. The battle had reached its height.
“The archers had disagreed among themselves. One group, seeing the people collecting the spoils after Allah Most High had defeated the polytheists and the Muslims had taken the booty, said, ‘What are you waiting for?’ Another group said, ‘The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave you explicit instructions and forbade you from leaving your position, whether the battle went against him or in his favor.’ They disputed over this. Then the first group of archers insisted on joining the army, so the men dispersed and abandoned their position. It was then that the cavalry of the polytheists charged.”
Good: Narrated by Ishaq in his Musnad, as cited in al-Matalib al-ʿAliyah (4260).

8820 — Al-Baraʾ ibn ʿAzib reported that on the day of Uhud, the Prophet ﷺ appointed ʿAbdullah ibn Jubayr as commander over the infantry, who numbered fifty men. He said: “Even if you see birds snatching us away, do not leave this position until I send for you. And if you see us defeat and trample the enemy, do not leave until I send for you.”
The Muslims defeated them. Al-Baraʾ said: “By Allah, I saw the women running swiftly with their garments raised, their anklets and lower legs exposed. The men under Ibn Jubayr said, ‘The spoils, people! The spoils! Your companions have prevailed, so what are you waiting for?’ ʿAbdullah ibn Jubayr said, ‘Have you forgotten what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ told you?’ They replied, ‘By Allah, we will go to the people and obtain a share of the spoils.’
“When they reached them, the tide was turned against them, and they began fleeing. That was when the Messenger was calling to them from behind. Only twelve men remained with the Prophet ﷺ. The enemy killed seventy of us, whereas the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions had inflicted one hundred and forty casualties upon the polytheists at Badr: seventy captives and seventy killed.
“Abu Sufyan then called three times, ‘Is Muhammad among the people?’ The Prophet ﷺ forbade them from answering him. He then asked three times, ‘Is the son of Abu Quhafah among the people?’ Then he asked three times, ‘Is the son of al-Khattab among the people?’ He returned to his companions and said, ‘These men have been killed.’
“ʿUmar could no longer restrain himself and said, ‘You have lied, by Allah, enemy of Allah! All those whom you named are alive, and there still remains for you that which will distress you.’ Abu Sufyan said, ‘A day in return for the day of Badr; war has its alternating fortunes. You will find mutilation among your dead. I did not order it, but it did not displease me.’
“He then began chanting, ‘Rise high, Hubal! Rise high, Hubal!’ The Prophet ﷺ said, ‘Will you not answer him?’ They asked, ‘Messenger of Allah, what should we say?’ He said, ‘Say: Allah is higher and more majestic.’ Abu Sufyan said, ‘We have al-ʿUzza, whereas you have no ʿUzza.’ The Prophet ﷺ said, ‘Will you not answer him?’ They asked, ‘Messenger of Allah, what should we say?’ He said, ‘Say: Allah is our Protector, and you have no protector.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3039).

8821 — Ibn ʿAbbas said: “Allah, Blessed and Exalted, did not grant victory at any place as He granted victory on the day of Uhud.” We found that difficult to accept, so Ibn ʿAbbas said: “The Book of Allah, Blessed and Exalted, will judge between me and whoever rejects this. Allah Almighty says concerning the day of Uhud:
‘Allah certainly fulfilled His promise to you when you were killing them by His permission.’
“Ibn ʿAbbas said that al-hass means killing. Allah then says:
‘Until, when you lost courage…’ through His saying: ‘He has already pardoned you, and Allah is the Possessor of bounty toward the believers.’ [Ali ʿImran: 152]
“This referred specifically to the archers. The Prophet ﷺ stationed them in a particular position and said, ‘Protect our rear. If you see us being killed, do not come to our aid; and if you see us taking spoils, do not join us.’
“When the Prophet ﷺ gained the spoils and the camp of the polytheists was laid open, all the archers rushed down and entered the camp to plunder it. The ranks of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had become intermingled like this”—and he interlaced the fingers of his hands—“and they became confused. When the archers abandoned the opening where they had been stationed, the cavalry entered through that position and attacked the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ. They struck one another, became confused, and many Muslims were killed.
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions had held the advantage at the beginning of the day, to the point that seven or nine of the polytheists’ standard-bearers were killed. The Muslims then recoiled toward the mountain. They did not reach the cave, as people claim; rather, they were beneath al-Mihras. Satan cried out, ‘Muhammad has been killed!’ No one doubted that it was true. We continued in that condition, having no doubt that he had been killed, until the Messenger of Allah ﷺ appeared between the two Saʿds. We recognized him by the swaying motion of his walk.
“We rejoiced as though nothing of what had befallen us had happened. He climbed toward us, saying, ‘The wrath of Allah has become severe against a people who bloodied the face of His Messenger.’ On another occasion, he said, ‘O Allah, it is not for them to rise above us,’ until he reached us.
“After some time, Abu Sufyan began shouting from the foot of the mountain, ‘Rise high, Hubal! Rise high, Hubal!’—referring to his deity—‘Where is the son of Abu Kabshah? Where is the son of Abu Quhafah? Where is the son of al-Khattab?’
“ʿUmar said, ‘Messenger of Allah, may I answer him?’ He said, ‘Yes.’ When Abu Sufyan said, ‘Rise high, Hubal,’ ʿUmar replied, ‘Allah is higher and more majestic.’ Abu Sufyan said, ‘Son of al-Khattab, you have certainly gladdened its eye, so leave it—or refrain from it.’ He then asked, ‘Where is the son of Abu Kabshah? Where is the son of Abu Quhafah? Where is the son of al-Khattab?’
“ʿUmar replied, ‘Here is the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, here is Abu Bakr, and here I am, ʿUmar.’
“Abu Sufyan said, ‘A day in return for the day of Badr. Days alternate, and war has its changing fortunes.’ ʿUmar replied, ‘They are not equal. Our dead are in Paradise, whereas your dead are in the Fire.’ Abu Sufyan said, ‘If that is what you claim, then we have indeed failed and lost.’
“Abu Sufyan then said, ‘You will find mutilation among your dead, but it was not done by the decision of our leaders.’ Then the zeal of the Age of Ignorance overtook him, and he said, ‘It did happen, and we did not dislike it.’”
Good: Narrated by Ahmad (2609), al-Tabarani in al-Kabir (10731), and al-Hakim (2/296–297).

8822 — Anas ibn Malik reported that on the day of Uhud, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would say: “O Allah! If You so will, You will not be worshipped upon the earth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (23:1743). Al-Nawawi said: “What is well known in the books of prophetic biography and military campaigns is that he said it on the day of Badr, while this narration states that he said it on the day of Uhud. There is no contradiction between the two, for he said it on both days. Allah knows best.”

8823 — Anas reported that Abu Talhah said: “Drowsiness overcame us while we were standing in our battle lines on the day of Uhud. My sword kept falling from my hand; I would pick it up, then it would fall again, and I would pick it up.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4562).
Al-Tirmidhi (3007) also narrated through another route from Abu Talhah that he said: “I raised my head on the day of Uhud and began looking around. There was not a single one of them that day who was not swaying beneath his leather shield because of drowsiness.” That is the meaning of the statement of Allah Almighty:
“Then, after the distress, He sent down upon you a sense of security—a drowsiness.” [Ali ʿImran: 154]

8824 — Anas reported that Abu Talhah said: “Drowsiness overcame us while we were in our battle lines on the day of Uhud.” He related that he was among those overcome by drowsiness that day, saying: “My sword kept falling from my hand and I would pick it up; then it would fall from my hand and I would pick it up again. The other group were the hypocrites, whose only concern was themselves—the most cowardly of people, the most panic-stricken, and the most forsaking of the truth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhi (3008), who said: “Good authentic.”

8825 — Al-Zubayr said: “I remember being with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of Uhud when fear became intense upon us and sleep was sent over us. Every one of us had his chin—or he said, his chin—upon his chest. By Allah, I could hear, as though in a dream, Muʿattib ibn Qushayr saying:
‘If we had possessed any authority in this matter, we would not have been killed here.’
“I memorized those words, and Allah Almighty revealed concerning them:
‘Then, after the distress, He sent down upon you a sense of security—a drowsiness,’ through His saying, ‘We would not have been killed here,’ because of the words of Muʿattib ibn Qushayr. He then said:
‘Say: Even if you had remained in your homes,’ until He reached: ‘And Allah knows well what is within the breasts.’” [Ali ʿImran: 154]
Good: Narrated by Ishaq ibn Rahawayh in his Musnad, as cited in al-Matalib al-ʿAliyah (4260).

8826 — ʿUthman ibn Mawhab reported: A man who had performed the pilgrimage came and saw a group of people sitting together. He asked, “Who are these people sitting here?” They replied, “They are Quraysh.” He asked, “Who is the elder?” They replied, “Ibn ʿUmar.”
He approached him and said, “I am going to ask you about something. Will you answer me? I adjure you by the sanctity of this House: do you know that ʿUthman fled on the day of Uhud?” Ibn ʿUmar replied, “Yes.” He asked, “Do you know that he was absent from Badr and did not attend it?” He replied, “Yes.” He asked, “Do you know that he remained behind from the Pledge of al-Ridwan and did not attend it?” He replied, “Yes.” The man then proclaimed, “Allahu Akbar!”
Ibn ʿUmar said: “Come here, so that I may inform you and clarify the matters about which you asked me. As for his flight on the day of Uhud, I testify that Allah pardoned him. As for his absence from Badr, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was his wife, and she was ill. The Prophet ﷺ therefore told him, ‘You will receive the reward and the share of a man who attended Badr.’
“As for his absence from the Pledge of al-Ridwan, had there been anyone in the valley of Makkah possessing greater influence and protection than ʿUthman ibn ʿAffan, the Prophet ﷺ would have sent that person in his place. Instead, he sent ʿUthman. The Pledge of al-Ridwan took place after ʿUthman had gone to Makkah. The Prophet ﷺ indicated his right hand and said, ‘This is the hand of ʿUthman.’ He then struck it against his other hand and said, ‘This pledge is for ʿUthman.’ Now take this explanation with you.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4066).

8827 — ʿAbdullah ibn al-Zubayr said: I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say, “Talhah has secured Paradise,” after Talhah had done what he did in defense of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The people had fled from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until some of them reached al-Munaqqa, short of al-Aʿwas. ʿUthman ibn ʿAffan fled, as did ʿUqbah ibn ʿUthman and Saʿd ibn ʿUthman—the latter two being men of the Ansar from Banu Zurayq—until they reached al-Jalʿab, a mountain in the vicinity of Madinah. They remained there for three days and then returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “You certainly went very far in your flight.”
Good: Narrated by Muhammad ibn Ishaq in his Sirah (514).

8828 — Shaqiq reported: ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn ʿAwf met al-Walid ibn ʿUqbah. Al-Walid said to him, “Why do I see that you have distanced yourself from the Commander of the Believers, ʿUthman?” ʿAbd al-Rahman replied, “Convey to him that I did not flee on the day of ʿAynayn”—ʿAsim said that he meant the day of Uhud—“I did not remain behind on the day of Badr, and I did not abandon the way of ʿUmar.”
Al-Walid went and informed ʿUthman of this. ʿUthman said: “As for his statement, ‘I did not flee on the day of ʿAynayn,’ how can he reproach me for a sin that Allah has already pardoned?” Allah said:
“Indeed, those of you who turned away on the day the two armies met were only caused to slip by Satan because of some of what they had earned. Yet Allah has certainly pardoned them.” [Ali ʿImran: 155]
“As for his statement that I remained behind on the day of Badr, I was nursing Ruqayyah, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, until she died. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ assigned me my share, and anyone to whom the Messenger of Allah ﷺ assigned a share is counted among those who attended.
“As for his statement, ‘I did not abandon the way of ʿUmar,’ neither I nor he is capable of following it fully. Go to him and relate this to him.”
Good: Narrated by Ahmad (490), al-Bazzar in Kashf al-Astar (2512), and al-Tabarani in al-Kabir (1/45).
His statement, “the day of ʿAynayn”: ʿAynayn is a hill belonging to Mount Uhud in Madinah. It is also said that the name refers to two hills near Uhud.
His statement, “the way of ʿUmar”: This refers to his asceticism, his fairness toward those who were wronged, and his forceful treatment of oppressive evildoers. Allah Most High had granted him strength and awe. Thus, ʿUthman said: “Neither I nor he is capable of following it fully. Go to him and relate this to him.”
It appears that their excuse for fleeing was the report that had spread that the Prophet ﷺ had been killed—for what reason, then, would they continue fighting? Allah therefore pardoned them and accepted their excuse. The first person to announce the glad tidings that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was alive was Kaʿb ibn Malik, as related in the following hadith:

8829 — ʿAbdullah ibn Kaʿb ibn Malik reported that Kaʿb was the first person to recognize the Messenger of Allah ﷺ after the defeat, when the people were saying, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has been killed.”
Kaʿb said: “I recognized his eyes gleaming beneath his helmet, so I called out at the top of my voice, ‘Muslims, rejoice! This is the Messenger of Allah ﷺ!’ He motioned to me to remain silent.
“When the Muslims recognized the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, they rose with him and headed toward the mountain pass. Accompanying him were Abu Bakr, ʿUmar, ʿAli, Talhah, al-Zubayr, al-Harith ibn al-Simmah, and a group of Muslims.
“When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ took shelter in the mountain pass, Ubayy ibn Khalaf caught up with him, saying, ‘Muhammad, may I not survive if you survive!’ The men asked, ‘Messenger of Allah, should one of us turn upon him?’ He replied, ‘Leave him.’
“When Ubayy drew near, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ took the spear from al-Harith ibn al-Simmah. One of the men, according to what was related to me, said: ‘When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ took it, he shook himself with such force that we scattered away from him like biting flies scattering from the back of a camel when it shakes itself. He then faced Ubayy and struck him with the spear, causing him repeatedly to tumble from the back of his horse.’”
Good: Narrated by Abu Nuʿaym in al-Dalaʾil (2/619–620).

8830 — Al-Baraʾ ibn ʿAzib reported: “On the day of Uhud, the Prophet ﷺ placed ʿAbdullah ibn Jubayr in command of the archers. The polytheists killed seventy of us, whereas the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions had inflicted one hundred and forty casualties upon the polytheists on the day of Badr: seventy captives and seventy killed. Abu Sufyan said, ‘A day in return for the day of Badr; war has its alternating fortunes.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3986).

8831 — Qatadah said: “We do not know of any clan among the Arab clans having more martyrs, or being more honored on the Day of Resurrection, than the Ansar.”
Qatadah said: “Anas ibn Malik related to us that seventy of them were killed on the day of Uhud, seventy on the day of Biʾr Maʿunah, and seventy on the day of al-Yamamah. Biʾr Maʿunah occurred during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, while the day of al-Yamamah occurred during the caliphate of Abu Bakr al-Siddiq, on the occasion of Musaylimah the Liar.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4078).

8832 — Jabir reported: “Some people drank wine on the morning of Uhud and were all killed as martyrs that same day. This occurred before wine was prohibited.”
Among them were Hamzah ibn ʿAbd al-Muttalib, al-Yaman—the father of Hudhayfah—Anas ibn al-Nadr, and Musʿab ibn ʿUmayr.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4618).

8833 — Food was brought to ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn ʿAwf while he was fasting. He said: “Musʿab ibn ʿUmayr was killed, and he was better than I. He was shrouded in a cloak: when his head was covered, his feet became exposed, and when his feet were covered, his head became exposed.”
The narrator said: “I think he also said, ‘Hamzah was killed, and he was better than I.’”
ʿAbd al-Rahman continued: “Then the worldly provisions that were spread out for us were spread out—or he said: we were given of this world what we were given—and we fear that the rewards for our good deeds may have been hastened for us in this world.” He then began to weep until he left the food untouched.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4045). His statement, “He was better than I,” was said out of humility.

8834 — Khabbab ibn al-Aratt reported: “We emigrated with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the cause of Allah, seeking the countenance of Allah, and our reward became assured with Allah. Some of us passed away without consuming anything of their reward in this world. Among them was Musʿab ibn ʿUmayr, who was killed on the day of Uhud. Nothing could be found in which to shroud him except a short striped cloak. Whenever we placed it over his head, his feet became exposed, and whenever we placed it over his feet, his head became exposed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ therefore said, ‘Place it over the side of his head and cover his feet with idhkhir grass.’
“Others among us lived until their fruits ripened, and they are now gathering them.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4047) and Muslim (44:940).

8835 — Jabir ibn ʿAbdullah reported that on the day of Uhud, a man asked the Prophet ﷺ, “Tell me: if I am killed, where will I be?” He replied, “In Paradise.” The man threw away the dates he was holding and fought until he was killed.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4046) and Muslim (143:1899).

8836 — Anas reported: “My paternal uncle Anas ibn al-Nadr was absent from the fighting at Badr. He said, ‘Messenger of Allah, I was absent from the first battle in which you fought the polytheists. If Allah allows me to witness another battle against the polytheists, Allah will certainly see what I shall do.’
“When the day of Uhud came and the Muslims retreated, he said, ‘O Allah, I apologize to You for what these people have done’—meaning his companions—‘and I disassociate myself before You from what these people have done’—meaning the polytheists.
“He then advanced and encountered Saʿd ibn Muʿadh. He said, ‘Saʿd ibn Muʿadh! Paradise, by the Lord of al-Nadr! I can smell its fragrance from this side of Uhud.’
“Saʿd later said, ‘Messenger of Allah, I could not accomplish what he accomplished.’”
Anas continued: “We found more than eighty wounds upon him—blows from swords, thrusts from spears, and shots from arrows. We found that he had been killed and that the polytheists had mutilated him. No one could recognize him except his sister, who recognized him by his fingertips.
“We used to believe—or suppose—that the following verse was revealed concerning him and others like him:
‘Among the believers are men who have been true to the covenant they made with Allah.’” [Al-Ahzab: 23]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (2805) and Muslim (48:1903).

8837 — Anas ibn Malik reported: “That day, we found seventy wounds upon Anas ibn al-Nadr. No one recognized him except his sister, who recognized him by his fingertips.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Ishaq in his Sirah (510).

8838 — Jabir reported: “My father was brought on the day of Uhud after he had been mutilated. He was placed before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and covered with a cloth. I went to uncover him, but my people stopped me. I went again to uncover him, but my people stopped me. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then ordered that he be carried away.
“He heard the voice of a woman crying and asked, ‘Who is this?’ They replied, ‘The daughter of ʿAmr’—or: ‘the sister of ʿAmr.’ He said, ‘Why is she weeping?’—or: ‘Do not weep. The angels continued shading him with their wings until he was lifted away.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (1293) and Muslim (129:2471).

8839 — Jabir reported: “When the time of Uhud approached, my father called me during the night and said, ‘I believe that I will be among the first of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ to be killed. Apart from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ himself, I leave behind no one dearer to me than you. I have a debt, so repay it, and treat your sisters well.’
“When morning came, he was the first person killed. Another man was buried with him in the same grave. My heart was not content to leave him with the other man, so I exhumed him six months later. He was just as he had been on the day I buried him, except for a slight change near his ear.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (1351).

8840 — Abu Qatadah, who was present on that occasion, reported: ʿAmr ibn al-Jamuh came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked, “Messenger of Allah, if I fight in the cause of Allah until I am killed, will I walk in Paradise with this leg of mine restored to health?” His leg was lame.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied, “Yes.”
He, his nephew, and one of their freedmen were killed on the day of Uhud. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed by him and said, “It is as though I can see you walking in Paradise with this leg of yours restored to health.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered that he and his freedman be placed together in one grave.
Good: Narrated by Ahmad (22553).

8841 — Anas ibn Malik reported that on the day of Uhud, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was left isolated with seven men from the Ansar and two men from Quraysh. When the enemy closed in upon him, he said, “Who will repel them from us in return for Paradise—or so that he may be my companion in Paradise?”
A man from the Ansar advanced and fought until he was killed. The enemy then closed in upon them again, and the Prophet ﷺ said, “Who will repel them from us in return for Paradise—or so that he may be my companion in Paradise?”
Another man from the Ansar advanced and fought until he was killed. This continued until all seven were killed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said to his two companions, “We did not deal fairly with our companions.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (100:1789).

8842 — Ubayy ibn Kaʿb reported: “On the day of Uhud, sixty-four men from the Ansar and six from the Muhajirun were killed, among them Hamzah. The polytheists mutilated their dead.
“The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, ‘If we are ever given a day against the polytheists like this day, we will certainly inflict more upon them.’
“When the day of the Conquest came, an unidentified man said, ‘There will be no Quraysh after today!’ The herald of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ then proclaimed, ‘Everyone, black and white, has been granted safety except for so-and-so and so-and-so,’ naming certain people.
“Allah Almighty then revealed:
‘If you retaliate, then retaliate with the equivalent of what you were made to suffer. But if you remain patient, that is certainly better for those who are patient.’ [Al-Nahl: 126]
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, ‘We shall remain patient and shall not retaliate.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhi (3129) and ʿAbdullah ibn Ahmad in his father’s Musnad (21229, 21230); authenticated by Ibn Hibban (487).

8843 — Abu Hurayrah reported that ʿAmr ibn Uqaysh had money owed to him through usury during the Age of Ignorance, and he was unwilling to embrace Islam until he had collected it.
On the day of Uhud, he came and asked, “Where are my cousins?” They replied, “At Uhud.” He asked, “Where is so-and-so?” They replied, “At Uhud.” He asked, “Where is so-and-so?” They replied, “At Uhud.”
He put on his armor, mounted his horse, and headed toward them. When the Muslims saw him, they said, “Keep away from us, ʿAmr!” He replied, “I have believed.”
He fought until he was wounded and was carried back to his family. Saʿd ibn Muʿadh came and told his sister, “Ask him whether he fought out of tribal zeal for his people, out of anger on their behalf, or out of anger for Allah.”
He replied, “Rather, it was out of anger for Allah and His Messenger!”
He died, entered Paradise, and had never performed a single prayer for Allah.
Good: Narrated by Abu Dawud (2537) and al-Hakim (2/113).

8844 — Abu Hurayrah would say: “Tell me about a man who entered Paradise without ever having performed a prayer.” When the people did not know the answer, they would ask, “Who was he?” He would reply, “The Usayrim of Banu ʿAbd al-Ashhal: ʿAmr ibn Thabit ibn Waqsh.”
Al-Husayn said: “I asked Mahmud ibn Labid, ‘What was the story of al-Usayrim?’ He replied:
“‘He had refused to accept Islam despite his people having done so. When the day of Uhud came and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ departed for Uhud, Islam became appealing to him and he embraced it. He took his sword, went out in the morning until he reached the people, entered the midst of the fighting, and fought until his wounds incapacitated him.
“‘While the men of Banu ʿAbd al-Ashhal were searching for their dead on the battlefield, they suddenly found him. They said, “By Allah, this is al-Usayrim! What brought him here? We left him behind while he was still opposed to this matter.”
“‘They asked him what had brought him. They said, “What brought you here, ʿAmr? Was it concern for your people, or a desire for Islam?”
“‘He replied, “Rather, it was a desire for Islam. I believed in Allah and His Messenger and embraced Islam. I then took my sword, went out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and fought until what has befallen me befell me.”
“‘He died in their arms shortly afterward. They mentioned him to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who said, “He is indeed among the people of Paradise.”’”
Good: Narrated by Ahmad (23634).

8845 — ʿAʾishah reported: “On the day of Uhud, the polytheists were defeated. Iblis—may Allah’s curse be upon him—then cried out, ‘Servants of Allah, beware of those behind you!’ The foremost ranks turned back, and they began fighting with those behind them.
“Hudhayfah looked and saw that the man was his father, al-Yaman. He cried out, ‘Servants of Allah, my father! My father!’ But by Allah, they did not stop until they had killed him. Hudhayfah said, ‘May Allah forgive you.’”
ʿUrwah said: “By Allah, a lasting remnant of goodness continued to remain in Hudhayfah until he returned to Allah.”
“Basurtu” means “I knew or recognized,” deriving from insight into a matter. “Absartu” refers to seeing with the eye. It is also said that basurtu and absartu have the same meaning.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4065).

8846 — Saʿd ibn Abi Waqqas reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed by a woman from Banu Dinar whose husband, brother, and father had been killed while fighting alongside the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at Uhud.
When she was informed of their deaths, she asked, “What happened to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
They replied, “He is well, mother of so-and-so. Praise be to Allah, he is as you would wish him to be.”
She said, “Show him to me so that I may look at him.” They pointed him out to her, and when she saw him, she said, “Every calamity after seeing you safe is insignificant!” By jalal, she meant something small and insignificant.
Good: Narrated by Muhammad ibn Ishaq, as mentioned by al-Hafiz Ibn Kathir in al-Bidayah wa-al-Nihayah (3/47).

8847 — Jaʿfar ibn ʿAmr ibn Umayyah al-Damri reported:
“I travelled with ʿUbaydullah ibn ʿAdi ibn al-Khiyar. When we reached Hims, ʿUbaydullah ibn ʿAdi said to me, ‘Would you like to visit Wahshi and ask him about the killing of Hamzah?’ I replied, ‘Yes.’
“Wahshi was living in Hims, so we asked about him. We were told, ‘There he is, sitting in the shade of his palace, looking like a full waterskin.’
“We approached until we stood a short distance from him. We greeted him, and he returned the greeting. ʿUbaydullah had wrapped his turban around his head and face, so Wahshi could see nothing of him except his eyes and feet.
“ʿUbaydullah asked, ‘Wahshi, do you recognize me?’
“Wahshi looked at him and said, ‘No, by Allah. However, I know that ʿAdi ibn al-Khiyar married a woman named Umm Qital bint Abi al-ʿIs. She bore him a boy in Makkah, and I used to seek a wet nurse for him. I carried that boy with his mother and handed him to her. It is as though I can still see your feet.’
“ʿUbaydullah uncovered his face and said, ‘Will you not tell us about the killing of Hamzah?’
“He replied, ‘Yes. Hamzah had killed Tuʿaymah ibn ʿAdi ibn al-Khiyar at Badr. My master, Jubayr ibn Mutʿim, said to me, “If you kill Hamzah in retaliation for my uncle, you will be free.”
“‘When the people set out in the year of ʿAynayn—ʿAynayn being a mountain opposite Uhud, with a valley between them—I went out with the people to fight. When they formed their battle lines, Sibaʿ came forward and called, “Is there anyone who will duel me?”
“‘Hamzah ibn ʿAbd al-Muttalib came out against him and said, “Sibaʿ, son of Umm Anmar, the circumciser of women! Do you oppose Allah and His Messenger ﷺ?” He then charged him, and Sibaʿ became like yesterday that had passed away.
“‘I concealed myself from Hamzah beneath a rock. When he came near me, I threw my spear at him and lodged it in his lower abdomen until it emerged from between his hips. That was the end of him.
“‘When the people returned, I returned with them. I remained in Makkah until Islam became widespread there, after which I departed for al-Taʾif. Its people sent an envoy to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and I was told that he did not harm envoys. I therefore went with them until I arrived before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
“‘When he saw me, he asked, “Are you Wahshi?”
“‘I replied, “Yes.”
“‘He asked, “Did you kill Hamzah?”
“‘I replied, “What occurred is as you have been informed.”
“‘He said, “Can you keep your face out of my sight?”
“‘I then left. After the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed away and Musaylimah the Liar emerged, I said, “I will certainly go out against Musaylimah. Perhaps I will kill him and thereby compensate for Hamzah.”
“‘I went out with the people, and what happened took place. I suddenly saw a man standing in a breach in a wall, looking like an ash-colored camel, with dishevelled hair. I threw my spear at him and lodged it between his breasts until it emerged from between his shoulder blades. A man from the Ansar then sprang upon him and struck him on the crown of his head with his sword.’”
ʿAbdullah ibn al-Fadl said: Sulayman ibn Yasar informed me that he heard ʿAbdullah ibn ʿUmar say: “A slave girl standing upon the roof of a house cried out, ‘Alas, Commander of the Believers! The black slave has killed him!’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4072).
Regarding the statement that a slave girl standing upon a roof cried out, “Alas, Commander of the Believers! The black slave has killed him”: There is reason to question the application of the title “Commander of the Believers” to Musaylimah the Liar. Musaylimah claimed to be a commissioned prophet, and his followers used to address him as “Messenger of Allah” and “Prophet of Allah.” The title “Commander of the Believers” arose afterward. The first person to be given that title was ʿUmar, some time after Musaylimah had been killed. This is stated in Fath al-Bari (7/371).

8848 — Anas ibn Malik reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed by Hamzah after his body had been mutilated. He said, “Were it not that Safiyyah would be deeply grieved, I would have left him until the beasts and birds of prey consumed him, so that he would be resurrected from their bellies.”
Good: Narrated by Abu Dawud (3136), al-Tirmidhi (1016), Ahmad (12300), and al-Hakim (1/365).

8849 — ʿUqbah ibn ʿAmir reported: “Eight years after the battle, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed for those killed at Uhud, like one bidding farewell to both the living and the dead. He then ascended the pulpit and said, ‘I will precede you, and I shall be a witness over you…’” The remainder of the lengthy hadith follows.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4042) and Muslim (30:2296).

8850 — Jabir ibn ʿAbdullah reported: “When news of Hamzah’s death reached the Prophet ﷺ, he wept. When he saw him, he sobbed.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzar in Kashf al-Astar (1794).

8851 — Ibn ʿUmar reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed by the women of ʿAbd al-Ashhal as they wept for those of their families who had died on the day of Uhud. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “But Hamzah has no women to weep for him.”
The women of the Ansar then came and wept for Hamzah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ awoke and said, “Woe to them! Have they still not returned home? Tell them to return, and they must not wail over anyone who dies after today.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Majah (1591), Ahmad (4984), and al-Hakim (3/195).

8852 — Yahya ibn ʿAbbad ibn al-Zubayr reported from his father, from his grandfather, who said:
“I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say—after the people had fled from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until some of them reached a mountain near Madinah, short of al-Aʿrad, and then returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“‘Hanzalah ibn Abi ʿAmir encountered Abu Sufyan ibn Harb. When Hanzalah overpowered him, Shaddad ibn al-Aswad saw him and struck Hanzalah from above with his sword until he killed him. Hanzalah had nearly killed Abu Sufyan.
“‘The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “The angels are washing your companion Hanzalah. Ask his wife about him.”
“‘She said, “He went out while in a state of major ritual impurity when he heard the alarm calling the people to battle.”
“‘The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “That is why the angels washed him.”’”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Hibban (7025) and al-Hakim (3/204–205).

8853 — Zayd ibn Thabit reported: “When the Prophet ﷺ set out for Uhud, some of those accompanying him turned back. One group said, ‘We should kill them,’ while another group said, ‘We should not kill them.’ Allah then revealed:
‘Why are you divided into two groups concerning the hypocrites?’ [Al-Nisaʾ: 88]
“The Prophet ﷺ said, ‘It expels men just as fire removes impurities from iron.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (1884) and Muslim (6:2776).
Ibn Hisham said: “The Prophet ﷺ appointed Ibn Umm Maktum to lead the people in prayer.”

8854 — Saʿd ibn Abi Waqqas reported: “On the day of Uhud, I saw two men dressed in white on the right and left of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, fighting in his defense with the fiercest fighting. I never saw them before that day or afterward.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4045) and Muslim (47:2306).
His statement, “two men dressed in white,” refers to Jibril and Mikaʾil, peace be upon them, as explicitly stated in Sahih Muslim (2306).

8855 — Anas reported: “On the day of Uhud, the people fled from the Prophet ﷺ, while Abu Talhah stood before him, shielding him with a leather shield.
“Abu Talhah was a powerful archer with a strong draw. He broke two or three bows that day. Whenever a man passed carrying a quiver of arrows, the Prophet ﷺ would say, ‘Empty them out for Abu Talhah.’
“The Prophet ﷺ would raise himself to look toward the enemy, and Abu Talhah would say, ‘May my father and mother be sacrificed for you! Do not raise yourself, lest an arrow from the enemy strike you. Let my neck be struck instead of yours.’
“I saw ʿAʾishah bint Abi Bakr and Umm Sulaym with their garments raised. I could see the anklets upon their lower legs as they carried waterskins upon their backs and poured water into the mouths of the men. They would then return, refill the waterskins, come back, and pour the water into the mouths of the men.
“The sword fell from Abu Talhah’s hands twice or three times.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4064) and Muslim (136:1811).

8856 — ʿAli ibn Abi Talib reported: “I never heard the Prophet ﷺ offer both his parents as a ransom for anyone except Saʿd ibn Malik. I heard him say on the day of Uhud, ‘Saʿd, shoot! May my father and mother be sacrificed for you.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4059) and Muslim (41:2411).
“Saʿd ibn Malik” is Saʿd ibn Abi Waqqas.

8857 — Saʿd ibn Abi Waqqas reported: “On the day of Uhud, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ offered both of his parents as a ransom for me.” He meant the occasion when the Prophet ﷺ said, “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you,” while Saʿd was fighting.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4057) and Muslim (42:2412). This wording is al-Bukhari’s.

8858 — Saʿd ibn Abi Waqqas reported that on the day of Uhud, the Prophet ﷺ offered both of his parents as a ransom for him.
Saʿd said: “A man from among the polytheists had inflicted severe harm upon the Muslims. The Prophet ﷺ said to me, ‘Shoot! May my father and mother be sacrificed for you.’
“I drew an arrow that had no arrowhead and struck the man in his side. He fell, and his private parts became exposed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed until I could see his molar teeth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (42:2412).
His statement, “He had burned the Muslims,” means that the man had inflicted many casualties upon them.

8859 — Saʿd ibn Abi Waqqas reported: “On the day of Uhud, the Prophet ﷺ emptied his quiver out for me and said, ‘Shoot! May my father and mother be sacrificed for you.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4055).
“Nathala” means that he took out and emptied its contents. A kinanah is a container for arrows.
