8860 — Abu ʿUthman—Abu ʿUthman al-Nahdi, ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn Mull—reported: “During one of those days on which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ fought, no one remained with him except Talhah and Saʿd.” This was reported on their own authority.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3722, 3723) and Muslim (47:2414).

8861 — Al-Saʾib ibn Yazid reported: “I accompanied ʿAbd al-Rahman ibn ʿAwf, Talhah ibn ʿUbaydillah, al-Miqdad, and Saʿd—may Allah be pleased with them. I did not hear any of them narrate from the Prophet ﷺ, except that I heard Talhah narrating concerning the day of Uhud.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4062).

8862 — Qays ibn Abi Hazim reported: “I saw that Talhah’s hand was paralyzed. He had used it to shield the Prophet ﷺ on the day of Uhud.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4063).

8863 — Anas ibn Malik reported that on the day of Uhud, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was left isolated with seven men from the Ansar and two men from Quraysh. When the enemy closed in upon him, he said, “Who will repel them from us in return for Paradise—or so that he may be my companion in Paradise?”
A man from the Ansar advanced and fought until he was killed. The enemy then closed in upon them again, and the Prophet ﷺ said, “Who will repel them from us in return for Paradise—or so that he may be my companion in Paradise?”
Another man from the Ansar advanced and fought until he was killed. This continued until all seven were killed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said to his two companions, “We have not dealt fairly with our companions.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1789).
Regarding his statement, “We have not dealt fairly with our companions”: This means that the two men of Quraysh had not dealt fairly with the Ansar, because the two Qurashis did not go out to fight, while the men of the Ansar went out one after another. Al-Nawawi mentioned this explanation.

8864 — Jabir ibn ʿAbdullah reported: “When the people turned away on the day of Uhud, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was standing to one side with twelve men from the Ansar, among whom was Talhah ibn ʿUbaydillah. The polytheists caught up with them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ turned and asked, ‘Who will confront these people?’
“Talhah said, ‘I will.’
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, ‘Remain where you are.’
“A man from the Ansar said, ‘I will, Messenger of Allah.’
“He said, ‘Then you go.’ The man fought until he was killed.
“The Prophet ﷺ turned again and saw the polytheists. He asked, ‘Who will confront these people?’
“Talhah said, ‘I will.’
“He said, ‘Remain where you are.’
“Another man from the Ansar said, ‘I will.’
“He said, ‘Then you go.’ The man fought until he was killed.
“The Prophet ﷺ continued asking the same question, and each time a man from the Ansar went out and fought as the man before him had fought until he was killed. Eventually, only the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Talhah ibn ʿUbaydillah remained.
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked, ‘Who will confront these people?’
“Talhah replied, ‘I will.’
“Talhah then fought with the strength of all eleven men. His hand was struck and his fingers were severed, causing him to exclaim, ‘Hass!’ The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, ‘Had you said, “In the name of Allah,” the angels would have lifted you while the people watched.’
“Allah then repelled the polytheists.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasaʾi (3149), and through him by Ibn al-Sunni in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (670).

8865 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khattab distributed woolen garments among some of the women of Madinah. One fine garment remained, so some of those with him said, “Commander of the Believers, give this to the daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ who is married to you.” They meant Umm Kulthum bint ʿAli.
ʿUmar replied, “Umm Sulayt has a greater right to it.” Umm Sulayt was one of the women of the Ansar who had pledged allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. ʿUmar said, “She used to carry the waterskins for us on the day of Uhud.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4071).
Umm Sulayt was the mother of Abu Saʿid al-Khudri. She had been married to Abu Sulayt, who died before the Hijrah. Malik ibn Sinan al-Khudri then married her, and she bore him Abu Saʿid. Al-Hafiz Ibn Hajar stated this in Fath al-Bari.

8866 — Anas reported: “On the day of Uhud, the people fled from the Prophet ﷺ, while Abu Talhah stood before him, shielding him with a leather shield.” He then related the hadith.
It contains the following: “I saw ʿAʾishah bint Abi Bakr and Umm Sulaym with their garments raised. I could see the anklets upon their lower legs as they carried waterskins upon their backs and poured water into the mouths of the men. They would then return, refill the waterskins, and come back to pour the water into the mouths of the men.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4064) and Muslim (136:1811).

8867 — Abu Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “The wrath of Allah became severe against a people who did this to His Prophet”—and he pointed to his incisor tooth—“and the wrath of Allah became severe against a man whom the Messenger of Allah kills in the cause of Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4073) and Muslim (106:1793).

8868 — Abu Hurayrah reported that the Prophet ﷺ said, “The wrath of Allah became severe against a people who shattered the helmet upon the head of their Prophet while he was calling them to Allah.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzar in Kashf al-Astar (1793).

8869 — ʿAbdullah ibn Masʿud reported: “It is as though I can see the Messenger of Allah ﷺ relating the story of a prophet whose people struck him and made him bleed. As he wiped the blood from his face, he said, ‘O Allah, forgive my people, for they do not know.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (3477) and Muslim (1792).
In another narration of Muslim: “While he was wiping the blood from his forehead.”

8870 — Ibn ʿAbbas reported: “The wrath of Allah became severe against the person whom the Prophet ﷺ killed in the cause of Allah, and the wrath of Allah became severe against the people who bloodied the face of Allah’s Prophet ﷺ.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4074).

8871 — Al-Zubayr reported: “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ instructed ʿAli ibn Abi Talib to go to al-Mihras. ʿAli brought him water in his shield. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ intended to drink from it, but he detected an odor in it and found it distasteful. He therefore used it to wash from his face the blood that had struck him, while saying, ‘The wrath of Allah became severe against the one who bloodied the face of the Messenger of Allah.’
“The person who bloodied him that day was ʿUtbah ibn Abi Waqqas.”
Good: Narrated by Ishaq ibn Rahawayh, as cited in al-Matalib al-ʿAliyah (4260).

8872 — Sahl ibn Saʿd was asked about the wound of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He replied: “By Allah, I know who washed the wound of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who poured the water, and what was used to treat him.
“Fatimah, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, washed the wound, while ʿAli poured water from a shield. When Fatimah saw that the water only caused the bleeding to increase, she took a piece of matting, burned it, and pressed it against the wound, and the bleeding stopped. His incisor tooth was broken that day, his face was wounded, and the helmet upon his head was shattered.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4075) and Muslim (102:1790).

8873 — Regarding the verse:
“Those who responded to Allah and the Messenger after suffering injury—for those among them who did good and remained mindful of Allah there is a magnificent reward.” [Ali ʿImran: 172]
ʿAʾishah said to ʿUrwah: “Son of my sister, both of your fathers were among them: al-Zubayr and Abu Bakr. After what befell the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of Uhud and the polytheists withdrew from him, he feared that they might return. He therefore asked, ‘Who will pursue them?’ Seventy men volunteered, among whom were Abu Bakr and al-Zubayr.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4077) and Muslim (51:2418).

8874 — Anas reported that the incisor tooth of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was broken on the day of Uhud and his head was wounded. As he wiped the blood from himself, he said, “How can a people succeed when they have wounded the head of their Prophet and broken his incisor tooth while he is calling them to Allah?”
Allah Almighty then revealed:
“You have no authority whatsoever in this matter.” [Ali ʿImran: 128]
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (104:1791).

8875 — Jabir ibn ʿAbdullah reported: “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would place two of those killed at Uhud together in a single cloth. He would then ask, ‘Which of them had learned more of the Qurʾan?’ When one of them was pointed out, he would place him first in the grave.
“He said, ‘I shall be a witness over these people on the Day of Resurrection.’ He ordered that they be buried with their blood upon them. They were neither washed nor was the funeral prayer performed over them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4079).

8876 — ʿUbayd ibn Rifaʿah al-Zuraqi reported from his father:
“When the day of Uhud came and the polytheists withdrew, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, ‘Stand in orderly rows so that I may praise my Lord Almighty.’
“They formed rows behind him, and he said:
“‘O Allah, all praise belongs entirely to You. O Allah, none can withhold what You have expanded, none can bring near what You have placed far away, none can distance what You have brought near, none can give what You have withheld, and none can withhold what You have given.
“‘O Allah, spread over us Your blessings, mercy, bounty, and provision. O Allah, I ask You for lasting bliss that neither changes nor disappears. O Allah, I ask You for bliss on the day of destitution and security on the day of war. O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the evil of what You have given us and the evil of what You have withheld from us.
“‘O Allah, make faith beloved to us and beautify it within our hearts. Make disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience hateful to us, and make us among those who are rightly guided.
“‘O Allah, cause us to die as Muslims, keep us alive as Muslims, and join us with the righteous, neither disgraced nor subjected to trials.
“‘O Allah, fight the disbelievers who turn others away from Your path and deny Your messengers, and place upon them Your punishment and torment. O Allah, fight the disbelievers who were given the Scripture, O God of truth.’”
ʿAli said: “I also heard it from Muhammad ibn Bishr, who narrated it with a connected chain, but I will not relate it here.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ahmad (15492), al-Bukhari in al-Adab al-Mufrad (699), and al-Bazzar in Kashf al-Astar (1800).

8877 — Regarding the verse:
“Those who responded to Allah and the Messenger after suffering injury—for those among them who did good and remained mindful of Allah there is a magnificent reward.” [Ali ʿImran: 172]
ʿAʾishah said to ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr: “Son of my sister, both of your fathers were among them: al-Zubayr and Abu Bakr. After what befell the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the day of Uhud and the polytheists withdrew from him, he feared that they might return. He therefore asked, ‘Who will pursue them?’ Seventy men volunteered, among whom were Abu Bakr and al-Zubayr.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari (4077).

8878 — Abu Hurayrah reported:
“The Prophet ﷺ sent a reconnaissance detachment and appointed ʿAsim ibn Thabit as its commander. He was the maternal grandfather of ʿAsim ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khattab.
“They travelled until they reached a place between ʿUsfan and Makkah. News of them reached a clan of Hudhayl known as Banu Lihyan, who pursued them with nearly one hundred archers. They followed their tracks until they reached a campsite at which the Muslims had stopped. There they found date stones from provisions the Muslims had brought from Madinah. They said, ‘These are dates from Yathrib,’ and continued following their tracks until they caught up with them.
“When ʿAsim and his companions realized that they had been overtaken, they sought refuge upon a raised piece of ground. The enemy surrounded them and said, ‘You have our solemn covenant and pledge that if you come down to us, we will not kill any one of you.’
“ʿAsim replied, ‘As for me, I will not descend under the protection of a disbeliever. O Allah, inform Your Prophet about us.’
“They shot arrows at them until they killed ʿAsim and seven other men. Khubayb, Zayd, and one other man remained. The enemy gave them their solemn covenant and pledge, so they descended to them. Once they had gained control of them, they unstrung their bows and tied the three men with the bowstrings.
“The third man said, ‘This is the first act of treachery.’ He refused to accompany them. They dragged him and tried to force him to go with them, but when he continued to refuse, they killed him.
“They took Khubayb and Zayd away and sold them in Makkah. Banu al-Harith ibn ʿAmir ibn Nawfal purchased Khubayb, because Khubayb had killed al-Harith on the day of Badr.
“Khubayb remained their prisoner until they unanimously decided to kill him. He borrowed a razor from one of the daughters of al-Harith so that he could remove his pubic hair, and she lent it to him.
“She later said: ‘I became distracted from one of my young children, who crawled over to Khubayb. Khubayb placed him upon his thigh. When I saw him there, I became so terrified that Khubayb recognized my fear, for the razor was still in his hand.
“‘He said, “Do you fear that I will kill him? I would never do that, Allah willing.”
“‘I never saw a prisoner better than Khubayb. I once saw him eating from a bunch of grapes when there was no fruit in Makkah at that time, while he was bound in iron chains. It could only have been provision that Allah had granted him.’
“They took him outside the Sacred Precinct to kill him. He said, ‘Allow me to pray two units of prayer.’ When he finished, he returned to them and said, ‘Were it not that you might think I was prolonging the prayer out of fear of death, I would have prayed more.’ He was the first person to establish the practice of praying two units before being killed.
“He then said, ‘O Allah, count them one by one.’ He recited:
“I do not care, when I am killed as a Muslim,
 On which side, for Allah, my death may fall.
“That is entirely for the sake of God; and if He wills,
 He will bless the severed limbs of a body torn apart.”
“ʿUqbah ibn al-Harith then approached and killed him.
“Quraysh sent men to retrieve a recognizable part of ʿAsim’s body, because ʿAsim had killed one of their great leaders on the day of Badr. Allah sent over his body a swarm of hornets resembling a protective canopy. They protected him from the men Quraysh had sent, and they were unable to take anything from his body.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhari in al-Maghazi (4086), from Ibrahim ibn Musa: Hisham ibn Yusuf informed us, from Maʿmar, from al-Zuhri, from ʿAmr ibn Abi Sufyan al-Thaqafi, from Abu Hurayrah, who narrated it.

8879 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Unays reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned me and said: “I have been informed that Khālid ibn Sufyān ibn Nubayḥ al-Hudhalī is gathering people to attack me. He is at ʿUranah, so go to him and kill him.” I said, “O Messenger of Allah, describe him to me so that I may recognize him.” He said: “When you see him, you will experience a shudder.”
He said: I set out with my sword strapped across me until I came upon him at ʿUranah, accompanied by women traveling in litters, while he was searching for a place for them to camp. It was then the time of the afternoon prayer. When I saw him, I experienced the shudder that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had described. I advanced toward him, fearing that an encounter between us might distract me from the prayer, so I prayed while walking toward him, indicating the bowing and prostration by motions of my head.
When I reached him, he asked, “Who are you?” I replied, “A man of the Arabs who heard about you and the forces you are gathering against this man, and so has come to you for that purpose.” He said, “Yes, I am engaged in that.” I walked with him for a while. When I gained the opportunity, I attacked him with my sword until I killed him. I then departed, leaving his women bent over him.
When I returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and he saw me, he said: “The face has prospered.” I said, “I killed him, O Messenger of Allah.” He replied: “You have spoken the truth.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then rose with me, took me into his house, and gave me a staff, saying: “Keep this with you, O ʿAbdullāh ibn Unays.”
I went out among the people carrying it, and they asked, “What is this staff?” I replied, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave it to me and instructed me to keep it.” They said, “Why do you not return to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and ask him about it?” I returned and said, “O Messenger of Allah, why did you give me this staff?” He replied: “It will be a sign between you and me on the Day of Resurrection, for few people on that day will be carrying staffs.”
ʿAbdullāh therefore fastened it alongside his sword, and it remained with him. When he died, he instructed that it be placed with him inside his shroud, and the two were buried together.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (16047) and Abū Yaʿlā (905); authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (983) and Ibn Ḥibbān (7160).

8880 — Anas reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent seventy men on a mission. They were known as “the Reciters.” Two clans of Banū Sulaym—Riʿl and Dhakwān—intercepted them at a well known as Biʾr Maʿūnah. The men said, “By Allah, we did not come for you; we are merely passing through on a mission for the Prophet ﷺ.” Nevertheless, they killed them. The Prophet ﷺ supplicated against them for a month during the dawn prayer. That was the beginning of the qunūt supplication, as we had not previously performed it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4088), from Abū Maʿmar, from ʿAbd al-Wārith, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, from Anas.
The words “on a mission” refer to teaching, as expressly stated in the narration of Muslim (677/147). Anas ibn Mālik reported: Some people came to the Prophet ﷺ and said, “Send men with us to teach us the Qur’an and the Sunnah.” He sent seventy men of the Anṣār with them who were known as “the Reciters.” Among them was my maternal uncle Ḥarām. They recited and studied the Qur’an together at night in order to learn it. During the day, they would bring water and place it in the mosque. They would also gather firewood and sell it, using the proceeds to purchase food for the people of al-Ṣuffah and the poor.
The Prophet ﷺ sent them to those people, but they intercepted and killed them before they reached their destination. The martyrs said: “O Allah, convey to our Prophet on our behalf that we have met You, that we are pleased with You and You are pleased with us.”
Anas said: A man approached Ḥarām, the maternal uncle of Anas, from behind and thrust a spear through him. Ḥarām exclaimed: “I have triumphed, by the Lord of the Kaʿbah!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to his Companions: “Your brothers have been killed. They said: ‘O Allah, convey to our Prophet on our behalf that we have met You, that we are pleased with You and You are pleased with us.’”

8881 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that Riʿl, Dhakwān, ʿUṣayyah, and Banū Liḥyān asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for reinforcements against an enemy. He supported them with seventy men of the Anṣār whom we called “the Reciters” in their time. They would gather firewood by day and pray by night. When they reached Biʾr Maʿūnah, those people treacherously attacked and killed them. When the news reached the Prophet ﷺ, he performed qunūt for a month, supplicating during the dawn prayer against several Arab clans: Riʿl, Dhakwān, ʿUṣayyah, and Banū Liḥyān.
Anas said: We used to recite concerning them a passage of the Qur’an that was subsequently withdrawn: “Convey to our people on our behalf that we have met our Lord; He is pleased with us and has made us pleased.”
Qatādah also related from Anas ibn Mālik that the Prophet of Allah ﷺ performed qunūt for a month during the dawn prayer, supplicating against several Arab clans: Riʿl, Dhakwān, ʿUṣayyah, and Banū Liḥyān.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4090), from ʿAbd al-Aʿlā ibn Ḥammād, from Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ, from Saʿīd, from Qatādah, from Anas ibn Mālik.
The words “they asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for reinforcements against an enemy” apparently indicate that they came to the Prophet ﷺ seeking military assistance against their enemy, which differs from the preceding account. The two reports may be reconciled by understanding that they asked the Prophet ﷺ for both things: instruction and then assistance. The Prophet ﷺ therefore sent the seventy Reciters for both teaching and support.

8882 — Anas reported that the Prophet ﷺ sent his maternal uncle—the brother of Umm Sulaym—among seventy mounted men. The leader of the polytheists was ʿĀmir ibn al-Ṭufayl. He was given a choice of three options, and said: “The people of the plains will be yours and the people of the settled highlands will be mine; or I will be your successor; or I will attack you with one thousand and another thousand men of Ghaṭafān.” ʿĀmir was then afflicted by a plague-swelling in the house of a certain woman. He exclaimed: “A swelling like that of a young camel, and in the house of a woman from such-and-such a family! Bring me my horse.” He died upon the back of his horse.
Ḥarām, the brother of Umm Sulaym, then went forward, together with a lame man and another man from a certain clan. He said to them, “Remain nearby while I approach them. If they grant me safety, remain where you are; but if they kill me, return to your companions.” He asked the enemy, “Will you grant me safety so that I may convey the message of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?” He began speaking to them, but they signaled to a man who approached him from behind and stabbed him—Hammām said, “I think”—until the spear passed through him. Ḥarām exclaimed: “Allah is the Greatest! I have triumphed, by the Lord of the Kaʿbah!”
The enemy then caught up with the men and killed all of them except the lame man, who was on the top of a mountain. Allah revealed concerning them a passage that was later withdrawn: “We have met our Lord; He is pleased with us and has made us pleased.” The Prophet ﷺ supplicated against them for thirty mornings—against Riʿl, Dhakwān, Banū Liḥyān, and ʿUṣayyah, who had disobeyed Allah and His Messenger ﷺ.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4091).
In the wording, “Ḥarām, the brother of Umm Sulaym—who was a lame man—and a man from such-and-such a clan went forward,” the description “lame” appears to refer to Ḥarām. This is incorrect, for the lame man was someone else. As Ibn Ḥajar states, it appears that the conjunction “and” was inadvertently omitted by the copyist and should be moved, so that the correct wording becomes: “He went forward, together with a lame man.” The lame man was Kaʿb ibn Zayd of Banū Dīnār ibn al-Najjār, while Ḥarām was Ibn Milḥān.

8883 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: When Ḥarām ibn Milḥān—who was his maternal uncle—was stabbed on the day of Biʾr Maʿūnah, he took some of his blood like this and splashed it over his face and head. He then exclaimed: “I have triumphed, by the Lord of the Kaʿbah!”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4092).

8884 — ʿĀʾishah reported: When the persecution of Abū Bakr became severe, he sought the Prophet’s ﷺ permission to depart. The Prophet ﷺ told him: “Remain.” Abū Bakr asked, “O Messenger of Allah, do you hope that permission will be granted to you?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would say: “I do indeed hope for that.”
She said: Abū Bakr therefore waited for him. One day at noon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to him and called out to him, saying: “Have everyone with you leave.” Abū Bakr replied, “They are only my two daughters.” The Prophet ﷺ said: “Do you realize that I have been granted permission to depart?” Abū Bakr asked, “O Messenger of Allah, may I accompany you?” The Prophet ﷺ replied: “You may accompany me.”
Abū Bakr said, “O Messenger of Allah, I have two she-camels that I prepared for the journey.” He gave one of them—the she-camel called al-Jadʿāʾ—to the Prophet ﷺ. They mounted and departed until they reached the cave at Mount Thawr, where they concealed themselves.
ʿĀmir ibn Fuhayrah had been an enslaved servant of ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Ṭufayl ibn Sakhbarah, ʿĀʾishah’s maternal half-brother. Abū Bakr had a milch animal, which ʿĀmir would take out in the evening and bring to them in the morning. He would travel to them during the latter part of the night, then take the animal out to pasture, so that none of the shepherds noticed him. When the Prophet ﷺ and Abū Bakr departed, ʿĀmir went with them, riding behind them in turns, until they reached Madinah. ʿĀmir ibn Fuhayrah was later killed on the day of Biʾr Maʿūnah.
Abū Usāmah also reported that Hishām ibn ʿUrwah said: My father informed me that when the men at Biʾr Maʿūnah were killed and ʿAmr ibn Umayyah al-Ḍamrī was taken captive, ʿĀmir ibn al-Ṭufayl pointed to one of the slain men and asked him, “Who is this?” ʿAmr ibn Umayyah replied, “This is ʿĀmir ibn Fuhayrah.” He said, “After he was killed, I saw him raised toward the sky, until I could see the sky between him and the earth; then he was set down.”
The news reached the Prophet ﷺ, and he announced their deaths, saying: “Your companions have been slain. They asked their Lord, saying: ‘Our Lord, inform our brothers on our behalf that we are pleased with You and You are pleased with us.’ So He informed them concerning their brothers.”
Among those killed that day was ʿUrwah ibn Asmāʾ ibn al-Ṣalt, after whom ʿUrwah was named; and also Mundhir ibn ʿAmr, after whom al-Mundhir was named.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4093).

8885 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: The Prophet ﷺ supplicated for thirty mornings against those who had killed his Companions at Biʾr Maʿūnah. He would supplicate against Riʿl and Liḥyān, saying: “And ʿUṣayyah disobeyed Allah and His Messenger ﷺ.”
Anas said: Allah Most High revealed to His Prophet ﷺ concerning those who were killed—the Companions of Biʾr Maʿūnah—a passage of the Qur’an that we recited until it was later abrogated: “Convey to our people that we have met our Lord; He is pleased with us and we are pleased with Him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4095).

8886 — ʿĀṣim al-Aḥwal reported: I asked Anas ibn Mālik about qunūt in prayer, and he said, “Yes.” I asked, “Was it before bowing or after it?” He replied, “Before it.” I said, “But a certain person informed me that you said it was after it.” He replied, “He has lied. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed qunūt after bowing only for one month. He had sent some men known as the Reciters, numbering seventy, to a group of polytheists. Some people who had a treaty with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ betrayed them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ therefore performed qunūt after bowing for one month, supplicating against them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4096).
Al-ʿAynī said: “The implied meaning is that he sent them to some polytheists who were not party to a treaty, while another group among them—the counterparts of those to whom the Reciters had been sent—had a treaty with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Those treaty-bound people gained the upper hand, betrayed the agreement, and killed the Reciters who had been sent to assist them against their enemy.” ʿUmdat al-Qārī (17/176).

8887 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed qunūt for one continuous month during the noon, afternoon, sunset, night, and dawn prayers. He did so at the end of each prayer, after saying, “Allah hears the one who praises Him,” in the final unit. He supplicated against a clan of Banū Sulaym—Riʿl, Dhakwān, and ʿUṣayyah—while those behind him said, “Āmīn.” He had sent people to them to invite them to Islam, but they killed them.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1443) and Aḥmad (2746); authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (618) and al-Ḥākim (1/225–226).

8888 — Anas reported: A man would assign some palm trees from his land to the Prophet ﷺ. When the lands of Qurayẓah and al-Naḍīr were conquered for him, the Prophet ﷺ began returning to each man what he had given him.
Anas said: My family instructed me to go to the Prophet ﷺ and ask him to return what they had given him, or part of it. The Prophet of Allah ﷺ had given it to Umm Ayman, but when I went to him, he gave the palm trees back to me. Umm Ayman came, placed a cloth around my neck, and said, “By Allah, we shall not give them to you after he has given them to me!” The Prophet of Allah ﷺ said: “Umm Ayman, let him go, and you shall have such-and-such.” She replied, “No, by Him besides whom there is no deity!” He continued increasing his offer until he gave her ten times their value, or nearly ten times their value.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4120) and Muslim (1771/71).

8889 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: When the Emigrants arrived in Madinah from Makkah, they came possessing nothing, whereas the Anṣār owned land and palm groves. The Anṣār entered into an arrangement with them whereby they would give them half the annual produce of their properties and spare them the labor and expenses.
The mother of Anas ibn Mālik, known as Umm Sulaym, was also the mother of ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Ṭalḥah, Anas’s maternal brother. She had given the Messenger of Allah ﷺ some of her palm trees, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave them to his freedwoman Umm Ayman, the mother of Usāmah ibn Zayd.
Ibn Shihāb said: Anas ibn Mālik informed me that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had finished fighting the people of Khaybar and returned to Madinah, the Emigrants returned to the Anṣār the grants of fruit that the Anṣār had provided them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ therefore returned my mother’s palm trees to her and gave Umm Ayman others in their place from his own orchard.
Ibn Shihāb said: Umm Ayman, the mother of Usāmah ibn Zayd, had been an enslaved servant of ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and was originally from Abyssinia. When Āminah gave birth to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ after his father had died, Umm Ayman cared for him until he grew up. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then emancipated her and married her to Zayd ibn Ḥārithah. She died five months after the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2630) and Muslim (1771). This wording is Muslim’s.
The word al-ʿaqār here means palm trees. Al-Zajjāj said: “ʿAqār is anything having firmly rooted foundations.” It has also been said that the term refers specifically to palm trees.
The word ʿidhāq is the plural of ʿadhq, meaning a palm tree.
The words “when he had finished fighting the people of Khaybar” refer generally to the Emigrants returning the grants to the Anṣār. The particular incident involving Umm Ayman, however, occurred when Banū al-Naḍīr were expelled, as previously related.
The words “the Emigrants returned to the Anṣār the grants of fruit that they had provided them” demonstrate that these were grants of produce, not transfers of ownership of the palm trees themselves. Had ownership of the trees been given as a gift, they would not have taken them back, since a gift may not be reclaimed after the recipient has taken possession of it. Permission to benefit from something, however, may be withdrawn whenever the owner wishes.

8890 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb reported: The property of Banū al-Naḍīr was among what Allah bestowed upon His Messenger ﷺ as spoils for which the Muslims had spurred neither horses nor riding camels. It therefore belonged exclusively to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. From it, he would provide his family with their maintenance for a year, then place what remained into weapons and riding animals as equipment in the cause of Allah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4885) and Muslim (1757).

8891 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: The Prophet ﷺ fought Banū al-Naḍīr and Qurayẓah. He expelled Banū al-Naḍīr but allowed Qurayẓah to remain and showed them favor. Later, when Qurayẓah fought, he had their fighting men killed and divided their women, children, and property among the Muslims. Some of them, however, joined the Prophet ﷺ, who granted them safety, and they embraced Islam. He expelled all the Jews of Madinah: Banū Qaynuqāʿ—the clan of ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām—the Jews of Banū Ḥārithah, and all the other Jews of Madinah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4028) and Muslim (1766).

8892 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported: The Prophet ﷺ burned and cut down the palm trees of Banū al-Naḍīr at al-Buwayrah. Then the following was revealed:
“Whatever palm trees you cut down or left standing upon their trunks was by Allah’s permission.”
 [Al-Ḥashr 59:5]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4031) and Muslim (1746).

8893 — Ibn ʿUmar reported that the Prophet ﷺ burned the palm trees of Banū al-Naḍīr. He said: Concerning this, Ḥassān ibn Thābit composed:
How lightly the chiefs of Banū Luʾayy took
 the sweeping blaze at al-Buwayrah!
Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith replied:
May Allah perpetuate that deed of yours,
 and may flames consume its surrounding lands.
 You will come to know which of us remains far from its harm,
 and which of our two lands is truly injured.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4032) and Muslim (1746/30).
He also said: Concerning this, the following was revealed:
“Whatever palm trees you cut down or left standing upon their trunks was by Allah’s permission.”
 [Al-Ḥashr 59:5]
“Which of our two lands” refers to the land of Banū al-Naḍīr and the land of the Anṣār. Nuzh means distance or remoteness, while taḍīr means to harm.

8894 — Mālik ibn Aws ibn al-Ḥadathān reported: While I was sitting with my family after the day had advanced, a messenger from ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb came to me and said, “Answer the summons of the Commander of the Faithful.” I went with him and entered upon ʿUmar. He was sitting upon the woven cords of a bed with no mattress between him and it, leaning against a leather cushion. I greeted him and sat down.
He said, “Mālik, some needy households from your people have come to us, and I have ordered that they be given a small allotment. Take it and distribute it among them.” I said, “Commander of the Faithful, if only you would assign that to someone else.” He replied, “Take it, man.”
While I was sitting with him, his chamberlain Yarfāʾ came and asked, “Will you receive ʿUthmān, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf, al-Zubayr, and Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ, who are seeking permission to enter?” He replied, “Yes.” Permission was given, and they entered, greeted him, and sat down.
After a short while, Yarfāʾ asked, “Will you receive ʿAlī and al-ʿAbbās?” He replied, “Yes.” He permitted them to enter, and they greeted him and sat down. Al-ʿAbbās said, “Commander of the Faithful, judge between me and this man.” They were disputing over the property of Banū al-Naḍīr that Allah had bestowed upon His Messenger ﷺ.
The group—ʿUthmān and his companions—said, “Commander of the Faithful, judge between them and relieve each of the other.” ʿUmar said, “Wait! I adjure you by Allah, by whose permission heaven and earth remain standing: Do you know that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, ‘We prophets are not inherited from; whatever we leave behind is charity,’ referring to himself?” They replied, “He did say that.”
ʿUmar turned to ʿAlī and al-ʿAbbās and said, “I adjure you both by Allah: Do you know that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said that?” They replied, “He did say that.”
ʿUmar said: “I shall inform you concerning this matter. Allah granted His Messenger ﷺ an exclusive right regarding this fayʾ that He granted to no one else.” He then recited:
“Whatever Allah bestowed upon His Messenger from them—you spurred neither horses nor riding camels for it. But Allah gives His messengers authority over whomever He wills, and Allah has power over all things.”
 [Al-Ḥashr 59:6]
“This property belonged exclusively to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. By Allah, he neither seized it for himself to your exclusion nor kept it from you. He gave it to you and distributed it among you until only this property remained. From it, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would provide his family with their maintenance for the year, then take what remained and place it where the wealth of Allah was to be placed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ followed this practice throughout his life. I adjure you by Allah: Do you know this?” They replied, “Yes.”
He then said to ʿAlī and al-ʿAbbās, “I adjure you both by Allah: Do you know this?” They replied, “Yes.”
ʿUmar continued: “Allah then took His Prophet ﷺ in death, and Abū Bakr said, ‘I am the successor entrusted with the affairs of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.’ Abū Bakr took possession of it and administered it as the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had done. Allah knows that in doing so he was truthful, righteous, rightly guided, and a follower of the truth.
“Allah then took Abū Bakr in death, and I became Abū Bakr’s successor. I administered it during the first two years of my rule according to the practice of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Abū Bakr. Allah knows that in doing so I was truthful, righteous, rightly guided, and a follower of the truth.
“Then you both came to speak to me. Your claim was one and your matter was the same. You, al-ʿAbbās, came asking me for your share from your nephew, while this man”—meaning ʿAlī—“came seeking his wife’s share from her father. I told you both that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: ‘We prophets are not inherited from; whatever we leave behind is charity.’
“When I decided to entrust it to you, I said, ‘If you wish, I shall entrust it to you on the condition that you accept Allah’s covenant and pledge to administer it as the Messenger of Allah ﷺ administered it, as Abū Bakr administered it, and as I have administered it since assuming responsibility for it.’ You said, ‘Entrust it to us.’ It was upon that condition that I delivered it to you.”
He then said, “I adjure you by Allah: Did I entrust it to them on that condition?” The group replied, “Yes.” He turned to ʿAlī and al-ʿAbbās and said, “I adjure you both by Allah: Did I entrust it to you on that condition?” They replied, “Yes.”
He said: “Do you now seek from me a judgment other than that? By Allah, by whose permission heaven and earth remain standing, I shall give no judgment concerning it other than this. If you are unable to administer it, return it to me and I shall relieve you of its responsibility.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3094) and Muslim (1756/49).

8895 — A man among the Prophet’s Companions reported that, before the Battle of Badr, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was in Madinah, the disbelievers of Quraysh wrote to ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl and the idolaters among the Aws and Khazraj, saying: “You have given refuge to our man. We swear by Allah that you must fight him or expel him; otherwise, we shall march against you in our entirety, kill your fighting men, and violate your women.”
When this reached ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy and the idolaters with him, they assembled to fight the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions. When the Prophet ﷺ learned of this, he met them and said: “The threat of Quraysh has profoundly affected you. They could not harm you more than you intend to harm yourselves. Do you mean to fight your own sons and brothers?” When they heard this from the Prophet ﷺ, they dispersed.
News of this reached the disbelievers of Quraysh. After the Battle of Badr, they wrote to the Jews: “You possess weapons, armor, and fortresses. You must fight our man, or we shall do such-and-such to you, and nothing will stand between us and the anklets of your women.”
When news of their letter reached the Prophet ﷺ, Banū al-Naḍīr resolved upon treachery. They sent word to the Prophet ﷺ: “Come out to us with thirty of your Companions, and we shall come out with thirty of our rabbis. We shall meet at a place midway between us so that they may hear from you. If they affirm your truthfulness and believe in you, we shall all believe.”
The Prophet ﷺ set out with thirty of his Companions, and thirty Jewish rabbis came out to meet him. When they reached an open tract of land, some of the Jews said to the others, “How can you get to him when thirty of his Companions are with him, every one of whom would love to die before him?”
They therefore sent word to him: “How can we understand one another while we are sixty men? Come out with three of your Companions, and three of our scholars will come to you. Let them listen to you; if they believe in you, we shall all believe and affirm your truthfulness.”
The Prophet ﷺ set out with three of his Companions, while three Jews came out with daggers concealed beneath their garments, intending to assassinate the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. A sincere woman from Banū al-Naḍīr sent word to her brother, a Muslim man of the Anṣār, and informed him of Banū al-Naḍīr’s intended betrayal of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Her brother hurried forward until he caught up with the Prophet ﷺ. He privately informed him of their plot before the Prophet ﷺ reached them, so the Prophet ﷺ returned.
The following morning, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ marched against them with military detachments and besieged them. He said: “By Allah, you will have no security with me unless you enter into a covenant with me.” They refused to give him a covenant, so he and the Muslims fought them that day.
The following morning, he marched with cavalry and military detachments against Banū Qurayẓah, leaving Banū al-Naḍīr. He called upon Qurayẓah to enter into a covenant with him, and they did so. He then withdrew from them and marched against Banū al-Naḍīr with his detachments. He fought them until they agreed to depart, with permission to take whatever their camels could carry except weapons.
Banū al-Naḍīr departed, carrying whatever their camels could bear of their belongings, the doors of their houses, and their timber. They demolished their own houses and carried away whatever timber suited them.
Their expulsion was the first gathering and removal of people toward Syria. Banū al-Naḍīr belonged to one of the tribes of the Children of Israel and had not suffered expulsion since Allah decreed exile upon the Children of Israel. For this reason, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ expelled them. Had Allah not decreed exile for them, He would have punished them in this world as Banū Qurayẓah were punished.
Allah then revealed:
“Whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth glorifies Allah, and He is the Almighty, the All-Wise,”
continuing until:
“And Allah has power over all things.”
 [Al-Ḥashr 59:1–6]
The palm groves of Banū al-Naḍīr belonged exclusively to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ; Allah bestowed them upon him and reserved them for him. He said:
“Whatever Allah bestowed upon His Messenger from them—you spurred neither horses nor riding camels for it.”
 [Al-Ḥashr 59:6]
That is, they were acquired without battle.
The narrator said: The Prophet ﷺ gave most of it to the Emigrants and divided it among them. He also gave a share to two needy men of the Anṣār but to no other Anṣārī. The remainder became the charitable endowment of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that remained in the hands of the descendants of Fāṭimah.
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq (9733).

8896 — Ibn ʿAwn reported: I wrote to Nāfiʿ asking him about calling people to Islam before fighting them. He wrote back: “That was only during the early period of Islam. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ raided Banū al-Muṣṭaliq while they were unaware, as their livestock were being watered. He killed their fighting men and took their captives. Juwayriyyah bint al-Ḥārith was captured that day. ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar, who was present with that army, related this to me.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2541) and Muslim (1730). This wording is Muslim’s.

8897 — Ibn Muḥayrīz reported: I entered the mosque and saw Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī, so I sat with him and asked him about coitus interruptus. Abū Saʿīd said: We accompanied the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the campaign of Banū al-Muṣṭaliq and captured some Arab women. We desired women, celibacy had become difficult for us, and we wished to practice coitus interruptus. We intended to do so but said, “Shall we practice it while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ is among us without first asking him?” We therefore asked him, and he replied: “There is no harm if you do not do so, for every soul destined to exist until the Day of Resurrection will inevitably come into existence.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4138) and Muslim (1438/125).

8898 — ʿĀʾishah reported: Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intended to travel, he would draw lots among his wives. Whichever wife’s lot was drawn would accompany him. On one campaign he drew lots among us, and my lot was selected. I therefore accompanied the Messenger of Allah ﷺ after the command of veiling had been revealed.
I was carried inside my litter and would remain in it when it was set down. We traveled until the Messenger of Allah ﷺ completed that campaign and began the return journey. When we had drawn close to Madinah, orders were given one night to depart. I rose when the departure was announced and walked beyond the army to relieve myself. When I had finished, I returned toward my mount and touched my chest, only to discover that a necklace of black onyx from Ẓafār had broken and fallen. I went back to search for it, and the search delayed me.
The men responsible for preparing my mount came and lifted my litter onto the camel I had been riding, assuming that I was inside it. Women at that time were light and had not become heavy with flesh, since they ate only small amounts of food. The men therefore did not find the litter’s lightness unusual when they raised and carried it. I was also a young girl. They sent the camel forward and departed.
I found my necklace after the army had moved on. I returned to their campsite, but there was no one there to call or answer. I remained at the place where I had been, thinking that when they discovered my absence they would return for me. While I was sitting there, sleep overcame me.
Ṣafwān ibn al-Muʿaṭṭal al-Sulamī, of Dhakwān, had remained behind the army. In the morning he reached my stopping place and saw the dark figure of a sleeping person. When he saw me, he recognized me, for he had seen me before the command of veiling. I awoke when he recognized me and said, “Indeed, we belong to Allah, and to Him we shall return.” I covered my face with my outer garment. By Allah, we did not speak a single word, nor did I hear anything from him other than his words of return to Allah.
He lowered himself, made his camel kneel, and placed his foot upon its foreleg. I rose and mounted it. He then led the camel on foot until we reached the army during the intense heat of midday, while they were encamped.
Some people were ruined by what followed, and the one who assumed the greatest share of the slander was ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl.
ʿUrwah said: I was informed that the slander would be circulated and discussed in his presence, and he would approve of it, listen to it, and encourage its spread. ʿUrwah also said: Among those involved in the slander, no one was specifically named except Ḥassān ibn Thābit, Misṭaḥ ibn Uthāthah, and Ḥamnah bint Jaḥsh, together with others whose names I do not know. They were a group, as Allah Most High stated. The man said to have borne its greatest share was ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl.
ʿUrwah said: ʿĀʾishah disliked anyone insulting Ḥassān in her presence, and would say that he was the one who composed:
My father, his father, and my honor
 are a shield protecting Muhammad’s honor from you.
ʿĀʾishah continued: When we reached Madinah, I fell ill for a month. The people were engrossed in circulating the slander, but I knew nothing of it. What made me uneasy during my illness was that I did not see from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ the tenderness he usually showed me when I was ill. He would enter, greet me, and merely ask, “How is that one?” He would then leave. This made me uneasy, though I still knew nothing of the evil being said.
When I had begun to recover, I went out with Umm Misṭaḥ toward al-Manāṣiʿ, which was where we relieved ourselves. We would go out only from night to night. This was before privies were constructed close to our houses. We followed the custom of the early Arabs of going into open country to relieve ourselves, and we disliked having privies beside our homes.
I went with Umm Misṭaḥ, who was the daughter of Abū Ruhm ibn al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Manāf. Her mother was the daughter of Ṣakhr ibn ʿĀmir and the maternal aunt of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq. Her son was Misṭaḥ ibn Uthāthah ibn ʿAbbād ibn al-Muṭṭalib. When Umm Misṭaḥ and I had finished and were returning toward my house, she stumbled over her cloak and exclaimed, “May Misṭaḥ perish!”
I said to her, “What a terrible thing you have said! Are you insulting a man who witnessed Badr?” She replied, “My dear girl, have you not heard what he said?” I asked, “What did he say?” She then told me what the slanderers were saying, and my illness became even worse.
When I returned home, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered, greeted me, and asked, “How is that one?” I said, “Will you permit me to go to my parents?” I wanted to verify the news through them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ granted me permission.
I asked my mother, “Mother, what are the people saying?” She replied, “My daughter, take it easy. By Allah, there is hardly ever a beautiful woman beloved by her husband who has co-wives without them speaking much against her.” I exclaimed, “Glory be to Allah! Have the people truly been saying this?”
I wept throughout that night until morning. My tears did not cease, nor did I taste sleep. When morning came, I continued weeping.
As revelation had been delayed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and Usāmah ibn Zayd to ask their opinion about separating from his wife. Usāmah advised the Messenger of Allah ﷺ according to what he knew of his wife’s innocence and the affection he knew the Prophet ﷺ had for her. He said, “They are your family, and we know nothing but good about them.”
ʿAlī said, “Messenger of Allah, Allah has not restricted matters for you, and there are many women besides her. Ask the maidservant, and she will tell you the truth.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned Barīrah and asked: “Barīrah, have you ever seen anything about her that made you suspicious?”
Barīrah replied: “By Him who sent you with the truth, I have never seen anything in her that I could fault, except that she is a young girl who sometimes falls asleep while watching her family’s dough, so a domestic animal comes and eats it.”
That same day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood upon the pulpit and publicly sought support against ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy. He said: “Muslims, who will excuse me in dealing with a man whose abuse of my family has reached me? By Allah, I know nothing but good concerning my family. They have also mentioned a man about whom I know nothing but good and who never entered upon my family except in my company.”
Saʿd ibn Muʿādh of Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal rose and said, “Messenger of Allah, I shall excuse you and deal with him. If he belongs to the Aws, I will strike off his head; and if he belongs to our brothers of the Khazraj, command us and we shall carry out your order.”
A man of the Khazraj then stood up. He was Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah, the leader of the Khazraj, and Umm Ḥassān was his cousin from the same clan. He had previously been a righteous man, but tribal zeal overcame him. He said to Saʿd, “You have lied! By Allah, you will not kill him and cannot kill him. Had he belonged to your own clan, you would not have wanted him killed.”
Usayd ibn Ḥuḍayr, Saʿd ibn Muʿādh’s cousin, then rose and said to Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah, “You have lied! By Allah, we shall certainly kill him. You are behaving like a hypocrite, arguing in defense of the hypocrites.”
The two tribes, the Aws and Khazraj, became inflamed until they nearly fought one another, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ remained standing upon the pulpit. He continued calming them until they settled down and fell silent.
I wept throughout that entire day. My tears did not stop, nor did I taste sleep. My parents remained with me the next morning. I had wept for two nights and a day without my tears ceasing or tasting sleep, until I thought that my weeping would split my liver.
While my parents were sitting with me and I was weeping, a woman of the Anṣār sought permission to enter. I allowed her in, and she sat and wept with me. While we were in that state, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered, greeted us, and sat down. He had not sat with me since the rumors began, and a month had passed without any revelation concerning me.
After sitting down, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ praised Allah and recited the testimony of faith, then said: “To proceed: ʿĀʾishah, such-and-such has reached me concerning you. If you are innocent, Allah will declare your innocence. If you have committed a sin, seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him, for when a servant acknowledges a sin and repents, Allah accepts the servant’s repentance.”
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ finished speaking, my tears stopped so completely that I could not feel a single drop. I said to my father, “Answer the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on my behalf.” My father replied, “By Allah, I do not know what to say to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” I said to my mother, “Answer the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concerning what he has said.” She replied, “By Allah, I do not know what to say to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Being a young girl who had not memorized much of the Qur’an, I said: “By Allah, I know that you have heard this story until it has settled in your minds and you have believed it. If I tell you that I am innocent, you will not believe me; but if I confess to something—and Allah knows that I am innocent of it—you will believe me.
“By Allah, I can find no comparison for my situation with you except the father of Yūsuf when he said:
‘So beautiful patience is most fitting, and Allah alone is whose help is sought against what you describe.’”
 [Yūsuf 12:18]
I turned away and lay down upon my bed. Allah knew that I was innocent and that He would declare my innocence. Yet, by Allah, I never imagined that Allah would reveal recited revelation concerning me. In my own estimation, my affair was too insignificant for Allah to speak about me in revelation. I merely hoped that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would see a dream through which Allah would declare my innocence.
By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had not left his seat, nor had anyone departed from the house, when revelation descended upon him. The severe condition that accompanied revelation overtook him, and drops of perspiration like pearls streamed from him despite the coldness of the day, because of the weight of the words being revealed.
When the revelation ended, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was smiling. His first words were: “ʿĀʾishah, Allah has declared you innocent.” My mother said, “Rise and go to him.” I replied, “By Allah, I will not rise to go to him. I shall praise none but Allah Almighty.”
Allah Most High revealed:
“Indeed, those who came with the slander are a group from among you.”
 [Al-Nūr 24:11]
He revealed the ten verses declaring my innocence.
Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq, who used to support Misṭaḥ ibn Uthāthah because of their kinship and Misṭaḥ’s poverty, said: “By Allah, I shall never again spend anything upon Misṭaḥ after what he said about ʿĀʾishah.”
Allah then revealed:
“Let not those among you who possess virtue and wealth swear not to give…”
through His words:
“…and Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
 [Al-Nūr 24:22]
Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq said: “Yes, by Allah, I certainly love that Allah should forgive me.” He resumed the support he had provided to Misṭaḥ and said: “By Allah, I shall never withdraw it from him.”
ʿĀʾishah continued: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had asked Zaynab bint Jaḥsh concerning me, saying: “What do you know or what have you seen?” She replied: “Messenger of Allah, I guard my hearing and sight from speaking without knowledge. By Allah, I know nothing but good.”
ʿĀʾishah said: Among the Prophet’s wives, she was the one who competed with me in status, but Allah protected her through piety. Her sister Ḥamnah, however, began defending her and became ruined among those who were ruined by the slander.
Ibn Shihāb said: This is what reached me from the reports of those narrators.
ʿUrwah then related that ʿĀʾishah said: By Allah, the man about whom those things were said used to declare: “Glory be to Allah! By Him in whose hand is my soul, I have never uncovered the garment of any woman.” She said: He was later killed in the cause of Allah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4141) and Muslim (2770).

8899 — Umm Rūmān, the mother of ʿĀʾishah, reported: While ʿĀʾishah and I were sitting together, a woman of the Anṣār entered and exclaimed, “May Allah do such-and-such to so-and-so!” Umm Rūmān asked, “What has happened?” She replied, “My son is among those who have repeated the story.” She asked, “What story?” The woman then related what had been said.
ʿĀʾishah asked, “Has the Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard it?” She replied, “Yes.” She asked, “Has Abū Bakr heard it?” She replied, “Yes.” ʿĀʾishah collapsed unconscious. When she regained consciousness, she had a fever accompanied by shivering, so I placed her garments over her and covered her.
The Prophet ﷺ came and asked: “What is wrong with her?” I replied, “Messenger of Allah, she has been seized by a shivering fever.” He asked: “Is it perhaps because of a story being circulated?” She replied, “Yes.”
ʿĀʾishah sat up and said: “By Allah, if I swear an oath, you will not believe me, and if I offer an explanation, you will not excuse me. My situation with you is like that of Yaʿqūb with his sons:
‘Allah alone is whose help is sought against what you describe.’”
The Prophet ﷺ departed without saying anything. Allah then revealed her vindication. She said: “It came by Allah’s praise, not through the praise of anyone else, nor through yours.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4143).

8900 — Masrūq reported: We entered upon ʿĀʾishah, while Ḥassān ibn Thābit was with her reciting poetry and praising her in his verses. He said:
Chaste and dignified, never accused of anything suspicious;
 she rises hungry, having never consumed the flesh of heedless women.
ʿĀʾishah said to him, “But you were not like that.” Masrūq said: I asked her, “Why do you permit him to enter upon you when Allah Most High says:
‘The one among them who assumed the greatest share of it will suffer a tremendous punishment’?”
 [Al-Nūr 24:11]
She replied: “What punishment could be more severe than blindness?” She also said: “He used to defend the Messenger of Allah ﷺ—or engage in poetic combat on his behalf.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4146) and Muslim (2488/155).
“Ḥaṣān” means chaste.
“Razān” means a woman of dignity and composure.
“Tuẓannu” means to be accused.
“Gharthā” means hungry: she does not backbite people and thereby fill herself with their flesh.

8901 — ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr reported: I began insulting Ḥassān in the presence of ʿĀʾishah, but she said: “Do not insult him, for he used to defend the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
ʿĀʾishah also related: Ḥassān sought the Prophet’s ﷺ permission to satirize the polytheists. The Prophet ﷺ asked: “What about my ancestry among them?” Ḥassān replied: “I shall extract you from among them as a hair is drawn out of dough.”
ʿUrwah said: I insulted Ḥassān because he had been among those who spoke extensively against her.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4145) and Muslim (2487/154).
The words “he defended the Messenger of Allah ﷺ” mean that he defended him through his poetry.

8902 — ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ distributed the captives of Banū al-Muṣṭaliq, Juwayriyyah bint al-Ḥārith fell to the share of Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās, or to one of his cousins. She concluded a contract with him to purchase her freedom. She was a beautiful and attractive woman whom no one could see without being captivated by her.
She came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ seeking his assistance in paying her emancipation contract. ʿĀʾishah said: By Allah, as soon as I saw her at the door of my apartment, I disliked her presence, for I knew that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would notice the same beauty in her that I had noticed.
She entered and said: “Messenger of Allah, I am Juwayriyyah, daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Abī Ḍirār, the chief of his people. The hardship that has befallen me is not hidden from you. I fell to the share of Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās—or one of his cousins—and entered into an emancipation contract with him. I have come to seek your assistance in paying it.”
He asked: “Would you like something better than that?” She asked, “What is it, Messenger of Allah?” He replied: “I shall pay your emancipation contract and marry you.” She said, “Yes, Messenger of Allah.” He replied: “I have done so.”
News spread among the people that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had married Juwayriyyah bint al-Ḥārith. The people said, “They are now the in-laws of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ!” They therefore released the captives in their possession.
ʿĀʾishah said: Through his marriage to her, one hundred households of Banū al-Muṣṭaliq were set free. I know of no woman who brought a greater blessing to her people than she did.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3931) and Aḥmad (26265); authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4054) and al-Ḥākim (4/26). This wording is Aḥmad’s.

8903 — ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, reported: As for Zaynab bint Jaḥsh, Allah protected her through her religion, and she said nothing but good. Her sister Ḥamnah, however, was ruined among those who were ruined. Those who spoke about the matter included Misṭaḥ, Ḥassān ibn Thābit, and the hypocrite ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl. He was the one who encouraged and gathered the slander, and he assumed its greatest share, together with Ḥamnah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (2770/58) and al-Bukhārī (4757).
“Yastawshīhi” means that he cultivated, managed, and promoted it.

8904 — ʿĀʾishah reported: When the revelation vindicating me descended, the Prophet ﷺ stood upon the pulpit, mentioned the matter, and recited the Qur’an concerning it. When he descended from the pulpit, he ordered that the two men and the woman receive their prescribed punishment, and it was carried out upon them.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4474), al-Tirmidhī (3181), Ibn Mājah (2567), and Aḥmad (24066).
The two men were Ḥassān ibn Thābit and Misṭaḥ ibn Uthāthah. It was also said that the woman was Ḥamnah bint Jaḥsh. Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4475).

8905 — Al-Zuhrī reported: Al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik asked me, “Has it reached you that ʿAlī was among those who accused ʿĀʾishah?” I replied, “No. However, two men from your people—Abū Salamah ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān and Abū Bakr ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḥārith—have informed me that ʿĀʾishah told them that ʿAlī had remained silent and did not involve himself in the accusation concerning her.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4142), from ʿAbdullāh ibn Muḥammad; Hishām ibn Yūsuf dictated it to him from memory, from Maʿmar, from al-Zuhrī.

8906 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: The Prophet ﷺ inspected him for participation on the day of Uḥud, when he was fourteen years old, but did not permit him to fight. He inspected him on the day of the Trench, when he was fifteen, and permitted him.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4097) and Muslim (1868/91).

8907 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported: The first battle I witnessed was the Battle of the Trench.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4107).

8908 — Ibn ʿUmar reported: I entered upon Ḥafṣah while her braids were dripping. I said, “You see what has happened among the people, yet I have been given no part in the matter.” She replied, “Go and join them, for they are waiting for you. I fear that your remaining away from them may cause division.” She continued urging him until he went.
When the people dispersed, Muʿāwiyah delivered an address and said: “Whoever wishes to speak concerning this matter should show himself to us, for we have a greater right to it than he and his father.”
Ḥabīb ibn Maslamah asked Ibn ʿUmar, “Why did you not answer him?” ʿAbdullāh replied: I loosened the cloth binding my legs and was about to say, “The one who fought you and your father for the sake of Islam has a greater right to this matter than you.” But I feared uttering a statement that would divide the community, lead to bloodshed, and be interpreted as meaning something other than what I intended. I therefore remembered what Allah has prepared in Paradise.
Ḥabīb said: “You were protected and preserved.” Maḥmūd related from ʿAbd al-Razzāq the wording: “her braids.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4108).
The word nasawātuhā is a scribal transposition; the correct reading is nawāsātuhā, meaning her braids. “Dripping” indicates that she had apparently bathed.
The words “You see what has happened among the people” refer to the fighting between ʿAlī and Muʿāwiyah at Ṣiffīn.
The ḥibwah was a cloth placed around the back, with its ends tied around the legs after the knees were drawn together.
The words “the one who fought you and your father for the sake of Islam” refer to Muʿāwiyah’s father, Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb, who was one of the leaders of the Confederates at the Battle of the Trench.

8909 — ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, said concerning Allah’s words:
“They came upon you from above and from below; your eyes grew wild, your hearts rose into your throats, and you entertained various thoughts about Allah.”
 [Al-Aḥzāb 33:10]
“That occurred on the day of the Trench.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4103) and Muslim (3020/12).

8910 — Yazīd ibn Sharīk al-Tamīmī reported: We were with Ḥudhayfah when a man said, “Had I lived in the time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I would have fought alongside him and distinguished myself.” Ḥudhayfah asked, “Would you really have done that?”
“I saw us with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the night of the Confederates, when we were struck by a fierce wind and bitter cold. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: ‘Will any man bring me news of the enemy, so that Allah may place him with me on the Day of Resurrection?’ We remained silent, and none of us answered him.
“He repeated: ‘Will any man bring me news of the enemy, so that Allah may place him with me on the Day of Resurrection?’ We remained silent, and no one answered. He said it a third time, but we remained silent and no one answered.
“He then said: ‘Rise, Ḥudhayfah, and bring us news of the enemy.’ Once he had called me by name, I had no choice but to rise. He said: ‘Go and bring me news of them, but do nothing to alarm them against me.’
“When I departed from him, it was as though I were walking inside a heated bath, until I reached them. I saw Abū Sufyān warming his back at the fire. I placed an arrow at the center of my bow and intended to shoot him, but then I remembered the Messenger of Allah’s words: ‘Do nothing to alarm them against me.’ Had I shot, I would certainly have struck him.
“I returned, again feeling as though I were walking in a heated bath. When I reached the Prophet ﷺ, I informed him of the enemy’s situation. Once I had finished, I became cold, so the Messenger of Allah ﷺ covered me with the extra portion of a cloak in which he had been praying. I remained asleep until morning. When morning came, he said: ‘Rise, sleepyhead!’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1788/99).
“Fierce wind and qurr”: qurr means intense cold.
“It was as though I were walking inside a heated bath” means that he no longer felt the cold experienced by the others. The word ḥammām is derived from ḥamīm, meaning hot water.

8911 — ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr reported: On the day of the Trench, ʿUmar ibn Abī Salamah and I were with the women inside Ḥassān’s fortified tower.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (2416) and al-Bukhārī (3720).
Ibn Saʿd said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would dispatch Salamah ibn Aslam with two hundred men and Zayd ibn Ḥārithah with three hundred men to guard Madinah and proclaim the takbīr aloud. He feared that Banū Qurayẓah might attack the women and children. Al-Ṭabaqāt (3/67).

8912 — Abū Hurayrah reported: Al-Ḥārith al-Ghaṭafānī came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “Muhammad, give us half the dates of Madinah, or we shall fill it against you with cavalry and men.” The Prophet ﷺ replied: “Let me first consult the Saʿds”—meaning Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah and Saʿd ibn Muʿādh.
They said: “No, by Allah! We did not accept humiliation during the pre-Islamic period, so how could we accept it now that Allah has brought Islam?” Al-Ḥārith returned and was informed of their response, so he said, “You have betrayed us, Muhammad!”
Ḥassān responded:
Ḥārith, whoever betrays the protection granted his neighbor
 is one of you—for Muhammad never betrays.
 If you betray, betrayal is among your customs,
 and baseness grows deep within the roots of al-sakhbar.
 The pledge of a man of Murrah, wherever you encounter it,
 is like shattered glass whose fracture can never be repaired.
Al-Ḥārith said: “Muhammad, restrain Ḥassān’s tongue from us! If his words were mixed with the water of the sea, they would permeate it.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1803).

8913 — Sahl ibn Saʿd reported: We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at the Trench. They were digging while we carried the soil upon our backs. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“O Allah, there is no true life except the life of the Hereafter,
 so forgive the Emigrants and the Anṣār.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4098) and Muslim (1804/126).
The word aktādinā is the plural of katid, meaning the area extending from the withers toward the back. Muslim’s version reads “upon our shoulders.”
The trench was situated along the northwestern side of Madinah because that was the only exposed approach. The other sides were protected by palm groves, dense cultivated lands, mountains, and other natural barriers.

8914 — Anas reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out to the Trench and found the Emigrants and the Anṣār digging on a cold morning. They had no servants to perform that labor for them. When he saw their exhaustion and hunger, he said:
“O Allah, the true life is the life of the Hereafter,
 so forgive the Anṣār and the Emigrants.”
They answered him:
We are the ones who pledged allegiance to Muhammad
 to strive as long as we remain alive.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4099) and Muslim (1805/130).

8915 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: The Emigrants and the Anṣār began digging the trench around Madinah and carrying soil upon their backs while chanting:
We are the ones who pledged allegiance to Muhammad
 to remain upon Islam for as long as we live.
The Prophet ﷺ answered them:
“O Allah, there is no true good except the good of the Hereafter,
 so bless the Anṣār and the Emigrants.”
A quantity of barley filling two cupped hands would be brought and prepared for them with rancid fat. It would be placed before the men while they were hungry, though it tasted repulsive in the throat and had a foul odor.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4100).
Ihālah means fat used as a condiment, whether oil, clarified butter, or animal fat.
Sanikhah means that its taste and color had changed because of age.
Bashiʿah means foul-tasting and unpleasant in the throat.

8916 — Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“O Allah, there is no true life except the life of the Hereafter,
 so forgive the Anṣār and the Emigrants.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3795) and Muslim (1805/127).

8917 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: On the day of the Trench, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would say:
By Allah, had Allah not guided us, we would never have found guidance,
 nor given charity, nor performed prayer.
 So send tranquility down upon us.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1804) and Abū Yaʿlā (3395).

8918 — Umm Salamah reported: I have never forgotten what he said on the day of the Trench as he handed the workers bricks, while the hair upon his chest had become covered with dust. He was saying:
“O Allah, the true good is the good of the Hereafter,
 so forgive the Anṣār and the Emigrants.”
He then saw ʿAmmār and said: “Alas for you, son of Sumayyah! The rebellious faction will kill you.”
The narrator said: I mentioned this report to Muḥammad—meaning Ibn Sīrīn—and he asked, “Did he narrate it from his mother?” I replied, “Yes. She used to associate closely with Umm Salamah and enter upon her.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (26482) and Abū Yaʿlā (1645). This wording is Aḥmad’s.

8919 — Al-Barāʾ reported: I saw the Prophet ﷺ on the day of the Trench carrying soil until the dust covered the hair of his chest—and he was a man with abundant body hair. He was chanting the martial verses of ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah:
O Allah, had it not been for You, we would never have found guidance,
 nor given charity, nor performed prayer.
 So send tranquility down upon us,
 and make our feet firm when we meet the enemy.
 The enemies have indeed transgressed against us;
 whenever they desire sedition, we refuse.
He raised his voice while reciting it. Another narration reads: “Those people have indeed transgressed against us.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3034) and Muslim (1803/125). This wording is al-Bukhārī’s.

8920 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: While the trench was being dug, I noticed that the Prophet ﷺ was suffering from severe hunger. I returned to my wife and asked, “Do you have anything? I have seen that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ is suffering from intense hunger.” She brought out a leather bag containing one ṣāʿ of barley. We also had a young domesticated animal, so I slaughtered it while she ground the barley. She finished at the same time as I did, and I cut the meat and placed it in a cooking pot.
When I was about to go to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, my wife said: “Do not disgrace me before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and those with him by bringing more people than the food can feed.” I went to him and spoke privately, saying: “Messenger of Allah, we have slaughtered a young animal and ground a ṣāʿ of barley that we had. Come with a few people.”
The Prophet ﷺ called aloud: “People of the Trench! Jābir has prepared a meal, so come along quickly!” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then instructed me: “Do not remove your pot from the fire, and do not bake your dough until I arrive.”
I returned home while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came leading the people. When I reached my wife, she rebuked me, saying, “What have you done!” I replied, “I did exactly what you told me: I spoke to him privately.”
She brought the dough to the Prophet ﷺ. He spat lightly into it and invoked Allah’s blessing. He then went to the cooking pot, spat lightly into it, and invoked Allah’s blessing. He said: “Call another woman to help you bake. Ladle food from your pot, but do not take it off the fire.”
They numbered one thousand. By Allah, they continued eating until they were satisfied and departed. Our pot continued bubbling as before, and our dough continued being baked as though none had been taken from it.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4102) and Muslim (2039/141).
“Khamaṣ” means hunger—the emptiness of the stomach from food.
A jirāb is a leather container in which provisions and similar things are stored.
“Ḥayyahala bikum” is an expression of invitation and encouragement, meaning: “Come quickly.”
The statement that they numbered one thousand is the correct version because it contains additional information. The differing numbers cannot be understood as referring to separate incidents, since this event occurred only once.

8921 — Jābir reported: While we were digging on the day of the Trench, we encountered a mass of extremely hard earth. The men came to the Prophet ﷺ and said, “A hard obstruction has appeared in the trench.” He replied: “I shall go down.” He rose with a stone tied against his stomach. We had remained for three days without tasting any food.
The Prophet ﷺ took the pickaxe and struck the obstruction, whereupon it became a loose mound of sand that flowed apart.
I said, “Messenger of Allah, permit me to go home.” I went to my wife and said, “I have seen the Prophet ﷺ in a condition that I cannot patiently bear. Do you have anything?” She replied, “I have some barley and a young goat.” I slaughtered the goat, and she ground the barley. We then placed the meat in the cooking pot.
I then went to the Prophet ﷺ after the dough had become ready and the pot, set upon its three stones, was nearly cooked. I said, “I have prepared a small meal. Come, Messenger of Allah, with one or two men.” He asked: “How much is there?” I told him, and he said: “That is abundant and good.”
He instructed me: “Tell her not to remove the pot from the fire or the bread from the oven until I arrive.” He then said to the people: “Rise.” The Emigrants and the Anṣār all rose.
When Jābir entered upon his wife, he said, “What shall we do! The Prophet ﷺ has come with the Emigrants, the Anṣār, and those accompanying them.” She asked, “Did he ask you how much food we had?” I replied, “Yes.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Enter without crowding one another.” He began breaking the bread, placing meat upon it, and covering the pot and oven each time he took food from them. He presented it to his Companions, then returned for more.
He continued breaking bread and ladling food until everyone was satisfied, and some food still remained. He then said to Jābir’s wife: “Eat from this and give some away as gifts, for the people have been afflicted by hunger.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4101).
“A loose mound of sand” means that it became flowing sand that would not hold together. Ah-yam has the same meaning as ahyal.
Jābir’s wife was Sahlah bint Masʿūd ibn Aws al-Anṣāriyyah, may Allah be pleased with her.
A burmah is a cooking pot.
Athāfī is the plural of uthfiyyah: one of the three stones erected to support a cooking pot.
“Do not press against one another” means: Do not crowd together.

8922 — A man among the Prophet’s Companions reported: When the Prophet ﷺ ordered that the trench be dug, the workers encountered a rock that prevented them from continuing. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ rose, took the pickaxe, placed his cloak beside the trench, and recited:
“The word of your Lord has been perfected in truth and justice. None can alter His words, and He is the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing.”
 [Al-Anʿām 6:115]
A third of the rock broke away. Salmān al-Fārisī was standing and watching, and a flash of light appeared with the Messenger of Allah’s blow.
He struck a second time and recited:
“The word of your Lord has been perfected in truth and justice. None can alter His words, and He is the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing.”
Another third of the rock broke away, and a flash appeared that Salmān saw.
He struck a third time and recited the same verse. The remaining third broke away. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ climbed out, took his cloak, and sat down.
Salmān said, “Messenger of Allah, whenever you struck, I saw a flash accompanying the blow.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked: “Salmān, did you see that?” He replied, “Yes, by Him who sent you with the truth, Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ said: “When I struck the first blow, the cities of Chosroes and their surroundings, along with many other cities, were displayed before me until I saw them with my own eyes.”
The Companions present said: “Messenger of Allah, pray that Allah will grant us their conquest, give us their lands as spoils, and bring about the destruction of their power at our hands.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated for that.
